# Completed and Locked Decisions

> Relocated from CLAUDE.md Rev 32 §13.5 to slim the always-loaded core. This is the authoritative narrative log of completed/locked decisions.
>
> **APPEND-ONLY.** Never edit or delete an existing entry — this is a log, not a status page. An entry records what was decided *and believed* at the time it was written; rewriting one destroys the evidence of how the understanding changed, which is the thing the log exists to preserve. When a later event invalidates part of an earlier entry, **append a new entry** that names the entry it corrects and says what changed. Superseded entries stay exactly as written.
>
> **Ordering (changed 2026-09-01, CLAUDE.md Rev 40).** The log is now **chronological**: entries carrying their own date run oldest-first, and the 30 entries that carry no date of their own are collected in a labelled block ahead of them, in the relative order they previously had. This one reordering moved entries; **no entry's text was changed, and none was added or removed** — the reordering script asserted the entry set was identical before and after, and the previous order is recoverable from git history. Append new entries at the **end**, as before: that keeps the file chronological as long as an entry's date is the date it is written. If you ever log an entry with an older date, place it by its date rather than at the end.
>
> **Note on overlap:** many entries here also appear in the formal DHF index (`docs/np_dhf_001.md`) and in git history. Content was relocated faithfully, not deduped — the dedup pass against the DHF is **`OI-CONV-07`** (owning entry in `docs/np_conv_001.md`, tracked in `pending-decisions.md` §13.3), and deleting authoritative memory stays a reviewed, per-item decision. **Where this file and the DHF disagree, the DHF wins.** Referenced from CLAUDE.md → Document Map.

## How to read this file

**~226 KB in ~200 lines — individual entries run to 14 KB on one line. Never read it whole.**
It is an append-only log, one `- ` bullet per entry, in two blocks: **Undated entries** (30 entries
with no date of their own) and then **Dated entries — oldest first**, 2026-05-09 → 2026-09-01, with
the newest at the end. Later entries correct earlier ones rather than editing them, so **a later
entry wins** — always grep for a later one before quoting an entry as current.

Recipes:

- Was X decided, and how: `grep -n 'X' docs/status/completed-decisions.md` then
  `sed -n '<line>p' docs/status/completed-decisions.md | fold -w 120`.
- Everything from one date: `grep -n '(2026-08-12)' docs/status/completed-decisions.md`.
- What was later overturned: `grep -n 'SUPERSEDED\|REVERSED\|corrects' docs/status/completed-decisions.md`.
- The newest decisions: `tail -20 docs/status/completed-decisions.md`.
- Everything after a date: `sed -n '/^- .*2026-08-11/,$p' docs/status/completed-decisions.md` —
  reliable now that the dated block is sorted.

For the narrative of what each CLAUDE.md revision changed, read
`docs/reference/claude-md-revision-history.md` instead — it is far smaller and usually the answer.

## Undated entries (no date recorded in the entry text)

These predate dated logging or were written without a date stamp, so they cannot be placed on
the timeline below. They keep the relative order they had before the Rev 40 reordering. A
struck-through entry is here because the only date it carries is its *retirement* date, not the
date the decision was recorded.

- Two-tier product strategy with shared platform
- ~~600-LED FPC zone modules from launch (no Rev 1/Rev B)~~ **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-28** — the fixed 5-zone/600-LED hardware model is retired; see `docs/np_hex_zm_001.md` (hex-tile lattice) and this file's SMART-1 entry below.
- 400 mW/cm² peak pulsed via firmware (Path A, $0 BOM) — pending regulatory opinion
- 21-channel EEG montage with FC3/FC4/Oz/A1/A2
- STM32G071 safety MCU (not G031)
- No NFC anywhere — optical code + resistive pogo authentication
- USB-C wired-first, BT/Wi-Fi toggle, antennas in hub not headset
- Boa dial fit system with spring-decoupled pods
- 5-layer passive + active Helmholtz EMF shielding with palladium fabric
- UHDR/SHDR terminology framework — complete separation, never linked by design
- Clinical consent engine: use case library → minimum necessary data mapper → plain-language consent document
- Research partnership: a priori consent (4 layers), results opt-in, suggestion portal, crowdfunding
- POA workflow with human document review
- Service network: Tier A optical + Tier B electronics + Tier C retail + depot
- All interface protection covers tethered to headset
- Predictive maintenance system via SHDR fleet telemetry
- Charger policy: auto-include correct charger at every upgrade + $19 upfront 65W option at checkout
- All durability changes listed in durability-maintenance.md (§7)
- **FPC 20-pin pinout locked:** Pin 18 = ZONE_ID (resistor per zone, 1% 0402: ZM-01=10kΩ, ZM-02=22kΩ, ZM-03=47kΩ, ZM-04=100kΩ, ZM-05=220kΩ); Pin 19 = PD2_CATHODE (scalp-side reference photodiode, RISK-14 Option B)
- **Zone module dual photodiode (RISK-14 Option B):** PD2 on scalp-facing surface, pin 19; PD1/PD2 ratio separates fouling from LED aging; T1/T2 identical mold
- ~~Five-layer zone module keying (RISK-15): mechanical key + ZONE_ID resistor + braille/numeral + shell tactile dots + bone conduction audio; works for color-blind and blind users~~ **REDESIGNED 2026-07-28** — all sockets share one identical shape (SMART-1); mechanical type/position keying and ZONE_ID are both gone, replaced by an orientation-only mating feature plus software placement validation (`np_module_map`). Color-blind/blind accessibility requirement is unchanged. **Accessible position-ID gap CLOSED 2026-07-31 by NP-HFE-002 Rev 1** (`docs/np_hfe_002.md`) — see the dedicated entry below. **Bone-conduction audio cannot substitute** — confirmed 2026-07-28 that it requires head contact, which is unavailable at the moment of module insertion (helmet must be off-head to access a socket); insertion-time confirmation moves to the companion phone/tablet/computer app, which already receives module status over BLE during setup, and bone conduction is retained for while-worn readback only. See `docs/np_hfe_002.md`, `docs/np_hfe_001.md` CT-01, `docs/np_hex_zm_001.md` §4a, `docs/superseded/np_fw_za_001.md`.
- **Self-sealing gasket (RISK-16 Option A):** co-molded Shore 40–50A silicone, no user RTV, IPX4 rated after 10 swap cycles
- **Sliding eject lever (RISK-22 Option A):** 10–12mm, 3:1 mechanical advantage, ≤1N extraction force; accessibility target: Parkinson's Hoehn & Yahr II–III
- **ZONE_ID firmware debounce:** 3× ADC reads at 100ms intervals before FAULT; ≥2/3 must pass (RISK-18)
- **PDMS bonding process:** SiO₂ 75nm interlayer + O₂ plasma, ≥150 N/m peel strength; 200-cycle IEC 60068-2-14 qualification BLOCKING before production (RISK-04)
- ~~**Multi-FPC bundle management (RISK-17):** ≥2mm inter-FPC separation; ≥15mm FPC-to-EEG (or grounded Al foil barrier); 3 anchor bosses per FPC; all 5 Hub ZIF connectors on same PCB edge~~ **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-29** — sized for 5 large discrete FPC tails. Hex tiles have no tail at all (back-face pad array), so there are no adjacent FPCs to separate and no per-module anchor bosses. See the NP-DRV-SHELL-002 entry below; the ≥15mm FPC-to-EEG figure specifically is shown to be unachievable in the hex architecture and is replaced (not merely relaxed) — the <5 µVpp artifact threshold it served is retained.
- **Risk register documented:** 26 risks total (RISK-01 through RISK-26); 23 MITIGATED; 3 OPEN: RISK-03 (regulatory opinion, external), RISK-20 (CFRP Ra confirmation, external), and RISK-26 (fan/heatsink-loss scalp overheat, FMEA-G07-01 — Path B1 mitigation selected, SW01-M04 implementation + THERM-1b pending); RISK-25 (cervical VNS cardiac reflex) MITIGATED pending FAI-CV02 hardware bench
- **HRV biofeedback protocol (software only):** Resonance frequency breathing pacer (6 breaths/min default, personalized sweep); real-time coherence score; four protocols (standalone, HRV+taVNS synchronized, HRV+EEG dual biofeedback, HRV+PBM); no additional hardware; bone conduction delivers breathing cue; uses existing VNS clip PPG. Locks the VNS+HRV modality as the only NeurOne modality with multi-modal trial evidence (2025 RCT: PBM + qEEG NF + HRV biofeedback simultaneously).
- **Cervical VNS (T2 accessory):** Neck-worn tcVNS module, carotid sheath stimulation; safety MCU owns enable with cardiac monitor interlock; 510(k) predicate = electroCore gammaCore K163334/K173323; gel pad consumable; +$35–55 BOM.
- **Research data anonymization architecture:** On-device, per-study, fresh per request. NeurOne never holds or accesses raw UHDR at any point (biometric-derived key never leaves device). Consent withdrawal is immediately effective for all future data flows from any time period. Irreversibility notice given at L3 blanket consent + per-project invitation. Audit trail of contributed studies in SHDR (user-readable, never shared with researchers).

Every entry below carries its own date. Range: 2026-05-09 → 2026-09-01.
Entries sharing a date keep the relative order they had before the reordering.

- **EEG cable routing (RISK-21, OI-09 CLOSED — NP-DRV-SHELL-001 Rev 2, 2026-05-10):** Dedicated 8×5mm main trunk + 6×4mm lateral branch channels on outer CFRP inner surface (opposite side from FPC bundle, scalp-side). Routing: Hub PCB EEG connector → occipital arch main trunk (~120mm) → crown junction → left branch (P3/C3/F3/Fp1) and right branch (P4/C4/F4/Fp2), each ~90–200mm arc. Shell wall routing achieves 18–22mm point-to-point FPC-to-EEG separation — DRC-18 PASSES without barrier. Snap-in retention clips at 80mm intervals. Channel spec handed to shell tooling supplier. - **Lens and goggle assembly tooling (NP-TOOL-LENS-001 Rev 2, 2026-05-10):** All five locked design changes consolidated: (1) Sliding rail lens mount F-01/F-02 — T-slot groove in lens rim + tongue in goggle arm, user self-install, ≤5N extraction, 500-cycle rated, eliminates Tier A alignment jig; (2) N42 magnet pockets F-03/F-04 — 6× per lens rim, ≥1mm polymer wall all faces (BLOCKING verification), 10,000-cycle snap durability, polarity pattern enforces shade orientation; (3) EC driver contacts F-05/F-06 — 2× hard gold ≥0.5µm pads in lens rim + BeCu pogo pins in goggle arm, ≤50mΩ, EC transition time >3s flags contact degradation in SHDR; (4) AgNW outer coating P-01 — replaces ITO (ITO 0.5% strain-to-failure vs. AgNW ≥5%), 20–30Ω/sq, ≥82% transmission at 660nm and 808nm (Mode F PBM wavelengths), supplier qualification BLOCKING; (5) Hard coat P-02 — 3–5µm silicone, ≥3H pencil hardness, applied over AgNW (outer) and EC film (inner on EC lens); (6) Inner PDMS diffuser P-03 — plasma-activated anti-fouling, SiO₂ interlayer on PC substrate (separate qualification from PI substrate result); (7) Lens rim guard anchor hooks F-07 (Issue #18) — integral to goggle arm at superior-posterior corner, 4mm ID loop; shade system clearance ≥4mm from hook to S1/S2/S3 shade body confirmed in CAD assembly (MR-19); tether lanyard ≤45mm free length routed along posterior arm surface, must not cross visual field (MR-20); OI-10 tracks CAD sign-off. 20-item mold design review checklist (Rev B adds MR-19 and MR-20); lens rim + goggle arm reviews concurrent; 10 open items tracked.
- **sLORETA-guided HD-tDCS (T2, NP-FW-HD-001 Rev 1, 2026-05-11):** Full firmware specification baselined. Scalar sLORETA weight matrix W (2447 voxels × 21 channels, 7mm MNI grid, ≈205KB) precomputed offline; loaded into LPSDR4 at session start. On-device runtime: 64-epoch covariance accumulation (C = 21×21 symmetric, 1.8KB SRAM) → voxel source power map (P[v] = W_v^T C W_v, 2447 floats, 9.6KB LPSDR4) → peak detection → MNI→10-20 electrode mapping. Electrode MNI lookup table from Jurcak et al. (2007). 4×1 ring selection: E_center = nearest electrode to MNI target; 4 cathodes = nearest neighbours with ≥2 quadrant angular spread constraint. Current distribution: anode I, each cathode −I/4 (charge balanced). Ramp state machine: 30s ramp-up → steady → 30s ramp-down, 100ms tick. Safety MCU owns all enable GPIO; impedance check at 1kHz AC before each session; charge density software monitor (95% of 40µC/cm² triggers abort). Concurrent EEG with 200µs blanking on current step edges; SNR ≥20dB specification. FAI-HD01 (15mm localization error, phantom), FAI-HD02 (electrode mapping, software PASS), FAI-HD03 (FWHM ≤25mm, phantom), FAI-HD04 (SNR ≥20dB, bench). Hardware FAI blocking for G3-07 full closure. 7 predefined clinical targets: DLPFC_L/R, VLPFC_L, ACC, MPFC, M1_L/R. Bilateral 4×1 and standard 2-electrode fallback also implemented. BOM delta $0 (Ag/AgCl dual-rated already in T2 cap spec; tACS driver existing T2 hardware). Closes Issue #23.
- **Cervical VNS safety interlock (NP-FW-CVNS-001 Rev 1, 2026-05-11):** Dual-processor cardiac interlock architecture. Safety MCU (STM32G071) owns CVNS_ENABLE_L/R GPIO independently of main processor. R-peak signal from main processor PPG detection drives RPEAK_IN GPIO pulse (5ms active-high) to safety MCU. Safety MCU measures R-R interval via TIM2 (1MHz timer); computes instantaneous HR; compares rolling 5s window mean to pre-stimulation baseline. HR change >15 BPM → TIM6 ISR (5ms) asserts enable GPIO LOW within <5.1ms worst-case (spec: <100ms FAI-CV02). Baseline cross-validation: main processor PPG-derived HR and safety MCU GPIO-timer-derived HR must agree within 5 BPM; mismatch blocks enable. Re-enable requires all three: 30s lockout elapsed + explicit app confirmation + repeat impedance check + baseline re-established. Three firmware modules: np_cvns_interlock + np_cvns_stim (biphasic charge-balanced, 10s ramp-up, 5s ramp-down) + np_cvns_session (UHDR/SHDR record). RISK-25 MITIGATED. Closes Issue #24 software gate (G3-08 SOFTWARE BASELINED).
- **Cervical VNS 510(k) Q-Sub (NP-REG-CVNS-001 Rev 1, 2026-05-11):** Substantial equivalence argument baselined. Predicate: electroCore gammaCore K163334 + K173323. SE based on same intended use (cluster headache, migraine) and same/safer technology (biphasic charge-balanced, ≤2 mA vs predicate ≤24 mA). Key differences: lower current (more conservative), bilateral option (≤2 mA supported by literature), cardiac interlock (safety enhancement). 6 Q-Sub questions drafted. Type B meeting target Month 20. Standards: IEC 60601-1, IEC 60601-2-10, IEC 62304 Class C + Class B, IEC 62366-1, 21 CFR 820.
- **eMMC partition architecture and encryption (NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1, 2026-05-11):** 9-partition GPT user area (8,192 MiB total) + dual hardware boot partitions. Partitions: Firmware Bank A (128 MiB, write-protected production) · Firmware Bank B (128 MiB, OTA target) · Safety MCU FW staging (4 MiB) · Config/Calibration (16 MiB) · SHDR (512 MiB) · UHDR (6,903 MiB, ~84% of user area) · Scratch (500 MiB, zeroed on boot). UHDR: AES-256-XTS, Argon2id key derivation (64 MiB memory, 4 iterations, p=1, 64-byte output → K1+K2), biometric/PIN input, 32-byte TRNG salt in Config partition — NeurOne never holds the key. SHDR: AES-256-XTS, HKDF-SHA256 from NeurOne manufacturing root key + eMMC CID hash, stored in Config under SNVS OTPMK — NeurOne can decrypt for warranty/fleet analytics. LittleFS on UHDR, SHDR, and Config (4 KiB blocks, power-loss resilient, wear-leveled). Scratch: raw blocks, no filesystem, no encryption, no persistent data. Dual-bank OTA: bootloader in eMMC hardware boot partitions 1+2 (primary + backup); Ed25519 signature verification on all firmware images before boot; SNVS_LPGPR0 boot bank flag; 9-step OTA sequence with readback verification and automatic revert; USB-C DFU recovery always available. Safety MCU firmware: never automated-updated via OTA — explicit user confirmation + page-by-page SPI flash with readback required. Session data classification: 27-element table (§12 of NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1) governs all UHDR vs SHDR routing decisions. UHDR backup: nightly incremental on USB-C power, same Argon2id-derived AES-256-XTS key, to USB-C local or E2E encrypted cloud — NeurOne cannot decrypt backup archives. Write endurance monitoring: EXT_CSD wear indicator + LittleFS block utilisation logged to SHDR every boot and every 100 sessions; PRE_EOL_INFO=0x03 blocks session start. **NP-COORD-001 G1-16 CLOSED** — NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1 satisfies the G1 gate requirement that firmware storage architecture spec is baselined before any storage firmware code is authored. Closes Issue #19.
- **Zone module bone conduction announcement firmware (firmware/zone_announce/, 2026-05-11):** Implements RISK-15 Layer 5 (five-layer keying). Five parallel slot state machines drive ZONE_ID LPADC1 detection (10 kΩ pull-up, midpoint ADC thresholds for ZM-01–05) with 3×100 ms debounce (≥2/3 majority, RISK-18). DDS audio synthesis from 256-entry Q15 sine LUT outputs zone-specific tone sequences via SAI3 I2S at 8 kHz to bone conduction piezo: N beeps (440 Hz, N = zone position) + 880 Hz confirmation; unknown zone plays descending 200→150 Hz error pair. Announcement queue serialises simultaneous multi-zone insertions. SHDR auth log written on every debounce exit (no user biology). Insert callback fires twice: before audio (enables PBM zone immediately) and after audio (UX complete). Platform HAL stubs OI-ZA-01 through OI-ZA-04 for LPADC + SAI3 + eDMA. G2-10 CLOSED. **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-28 — the ZONE_ID resistor-ladder detection mechanism (5 fixed slots) is retired by np_module_map's UID-based auto-inventory; the DDS audio engine and debounce algorithm remain reusable but need re-triggering from module-map events. See `docs/superseded/np_fw_za_001.md` supersession note.**
- **HRV biofeedback protocol firmware (firmware/hrv_biofeedback/, 2026-05-11):** Implements all four HRV protocols from CLAUDE.md §3 modality 6. Six-module static library: np_hrv_ppg (adaptive PPG peak detection, RR buffer, RMSSD), np_hrv_coherence (256-pt Welch PSD, radix-2 DIT FFT, LF/HF band extraction, coherence score = LF_peak/(LF_total+HF_total)×10), np_hrv_pacer (breathing pacer, RF personalization sweep 4–7 BPM in 0.5 BPM steps, closed-loop rate set), np_hrv_tavns_sync (RSA slope detection: sliding 6-interval dRR window, ±5 ms/beat hysteresis, inspiration-phase gate, 6 s failsafe, safety MCU veto path), np_hrv_eeg_biofeedback (1024-pt EEG Welch bands, frontal alpha/theta ratio, adaptive step adjusts pacer rate every 30 s to maximise alpha/theta while sustaining coherence ≥ 5.0), np_hrv_session (protocol orchestration, UHDR session record, SHDR coherence trend slope). UHDR data: full RR series + per-update coherence array + np_hrv_session_record_t. SHDR data: coherence trend slope only (no user biology). Platform HAL stubs (taVNS enable/disable, pacer phase notify, Config partition persist) await FW team implementation (OI-HRV-01–05 in NP-FW-HRV-001 Rev 1). **NP-COORD-001 G2-12 CLOSED.** Closes Issue #21.
- **Dual-bank OTA bootloader firmware (firmware/bootloader/, 2026-05-11):** First firmware code authored for NeurOne. Implements complete B-1 through B-9 boot sequence per NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1 §8.1–§8.4. Boot selector uses SNVS_LPGPR0 (persistent through warm resets) with bit fields: [1:0] BOOT_BANK, [7:2] BOOT_ATTEMPTS (6 bits, max 3), [8] OTA_PENDING, [16] DFU_FORCED. eMMC driver targets USDHC2 peripheral with partition switching (boot part 1/2/user area), 8-bit bus, HS timing. Ed25519 verifier is self-contained (no external crypto library): RFC 8032 §5.1.7 verify over message = sha256 || version_le32 || size_le32; includes SHA-256, SHA-512, CRC32, GF(2^255−19) field arithmetic, and constant-time final comparison. OTA state machine: 9-step flow with streaming sector-by-sector SHA-256 (avoids loading full 128 MiB image into SRAM), write-with-readback, np_ota_state_t persisted to Config partition at two mirrored sectors, rollback after 3 failed boot attempts of OTA candidate. DFU recovery: USB DFU 1.1 class on USB1 (USB-HS OTG), writes to Scratch then verifies before writing to inactive bank, Ed25519 gated — no unauthenticated firmware can be flashed. Manufacturing root public key is a placeholder; must be replaced at secure build step before any production image is signed. Closes Issue #20.
- **1064nm smart zone module — Path B architecture locked (NP-FW-PBM1064-001 Rev 1, 2026-05-12; hardware NP-HW-FPC-001 Rev 5, 2026-05-13):** On-module driver IC selected: Microchip ATtiny402 (I2C slave, SOT-23-8, programmed via UPDI before assembly) + 3× Infineon IRLML6344TRPBF N-channel MOSFETs (SOT-23, RDS(on)=27mΩ, VGS_th fully enhanced at 3.3V). Driver BOM $1.00 — below $2.00 target. Smart module FPC part number NP-FPC-ZM-SM-01; 550 LEDs in tri-wavelength interspersed pattern (200×660nm CH_A + 200×808nm CH_B + 150×1064nm CH_C); 66×78mm footprint unchanged from base module. InGaAs PDs: Hamamatsu G12180-010A (0.90 A/W at 1064nm, <1nA dark current), annular ring PD1/PD2 at X=33.0mm, Y=39.0mm. 1064nm emitters: EPITEX L1064-02AU via Marubeni. BOM delta vs base module: +$23–28. Estimated retail $149–199 per zone / $599–699 all-5-zone kit. mold variant: NP-TOOL-ZM-SM-001 Rev 1 (rigidizer cavity 24×16×3.5mm internal; mechanical key +2.0mm lateral face width prevents insertion into unmodified hub without TIA gain switch). Smart module ZONE_ID = 3.3kΩ resistor on pin 18 → ADC < 1100 counts (12-bit, 10kΩ pull-up, 3.3V ref). Hub enables LPI2C3 per slot independently (5 separate buses, 400kHz fast mode); I2C probe to address 0x30; ACK within 5ms required. Hub PCB requires Vishay DG2788A analog switch per slot to switch TIA Rf 47kΩ→22kΩ for InGaAs PD gain (OI-PBM-HW-01, BLOCKING). Base modules (ZM-01..ZM-05, ADC 1100–4000 counts) continue via direct-PWM path — unchanged. Duty cycle ceiling 25% unconditionally enforced at hub firmware layer (DUTY_MAX_REG = 0x32). InGaAs PD dose-metering: K_PD1, K_PD2, K_ratio_nom per zone per wavelength in Config partition (factory cal) or firmware defaults (cal_source=DEFAULT in SHDR). Session dose limits: 660/808nm = 60 J/cm², 1064nm = 36 J/cm². Aggregate irradiance ceiling 600 mW/cm² (OI-PBM-05: pending RISK-03 scope expansion). Session descriptor schema v4. EEG-adaptive: uniform mode (default) and cortical gradient mode (Research, OI-SES-02). FAI-SM-01,-02,-03,-05,-09,-10,-11: Software PASS (PR #57). FAI-SM-04,-06,-07,-08: Hardware bench PENDING (require calibrated 1064nm optical bench). Firmware: firmware/pbm/. Issues: #53 (firmware), #54 (hardware, closed PR #58), #55 (T2 combined), #56 (regulatory/bibliography). **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-28 — the 5-zone-slot addressing (ZM-01..05, 5 LPI2C3 buses, 66×78mm footprint, 150-of-550-LED count, "all-5-zone kit" pricing) described here is retired by NP-HEX-ZM-001's hex-tile architecture; the on-module ATtiny402+FET driver concept and InGaAs PD choice remain the relevant precedent for today's T1-C. See supersession notes in `docs/np_fw_pbm1064_001.md`, `docs/superseded/np_hw_fpc_001.md`, `docs/np_proc_fpc_1064_001.md`.**
- **T2 combined 1064+1170nm session architecture locked (NP-SES-1064-001 Rev 1, 2026-05-12):** 1064nm zone modules + 1170nm T2 laser coordinated by np_pbm_t2_combined. Three-tier penetration: 660nm surface / 1064nm cortical / 1170nm deep. Thermal throttle priority: 1170nm first → CH_C → CH_B, CH_A last. sLORETA coordination for T2 depression protocol (§6.4 NP-SES-1064-001, blocking on T2 prototype). Combined UHDR record: per-zone per-wavelength J/cm² + 1170nm dose + sLORETA MNI coordinate.
- **QMS established at company formation (Issue #33, 2026-05-13):** Six QMS documents released simultaneously — NP-QMS-001 (manual), NP-DHF-001 (DHF index), NP-QMS-DC-001 (design controls), NP-RM-001 (ISO 14971 risk management plan), NP-SW-001 (IEC 62304 software development plan), NP-QMS-CAPA-001 (CAPA procedure). - **Hub PCB Rev B TIA gain switch architecture locked (NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 2, 2026-05-13):** Vishay DG2788A dual-SPDT analog switch per slot (×5) switches TIA feedback Rf 47kΩ→22kΩ when smart module detected. GAIN_SEL[n] GPIO (i.MX RT1062 GPIO_B0_04..08, one per slot) asserted HIGH after ZONE_ID debounce confirms ADC < 1100, and before I2C mux enable (sequencing requirement, NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 2 §5.1). NXP PCA9546A (0x70) provides 4-channel I2C isolation for slots 0–3; slot 4 via GPIO mux. Boot init drives GAIN_SEL[0..4] LOW before zone detection task. On removal: I2C mux disable then GAIN_SEL deassert (HIGH). Firmware: np_tia_gain_t enum, np_sm_slot_ctx_t.tia_gain field, np_pbm_hal_tia_gain_set(), np_pbm_hal_tia_gain_boot_init() stub. Hub PCB Rev B BOM delta $1.41–1.91. OI-PBM-HW-01 SPECIFIED (hub Gerber build pending). OI-PBM-HW-02 SPECIFIED. FAI-SM-04 and FAI-SM-06 unblocked pending Gerber build. **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-28 — sized for 5 zone slots; SMART-1 (below) requires every socket in the ~30–80-socket hex lattice to be I2C/TIA-capable. → Rev 3 written 2026-07-29 (see the "Hub PCB Rev C" entry below); Rev 2 archived verbatim as `docs/np_hw_hub_001.md` Appendix A. Rev 2's DG2788A topology and TIA-saturation analysis are carried forward unchanged; its `GAIN_SEL[0..4]` GPIO scheme, PCA9546A slot map, ZONE_ID sequencing, and open items OI-HUB-01…05 are retired.**

Direct remediations completed: NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 (warranty token architecture, factory reset, two-layer key scheme, Scratch encryption, EDF+ header policy, Mode F spec), NP-SEC-BR-001 Rev 1 (Breach Response Plan), NP-PROC-POA-001 Rev 1 (POA Upload Procedure), NP-APP-TELEMETRY-001 Rev 1 (App Telemetry Policy). - **Mode F consent and safety — locked (2026-06-02):** Mode F default-off. Requires separate explicit consent distinct from session consent. Ambient indicator: right temple amber LED triple-pulse pattern (3×150ms on/off, 2s pause) when active — not suppressible by stealth mode. IEC 62471 cumulative daily retinal dose calculated across all Mode F wear time per calendar day (not per session). Firmware build flag `NP_MODE_F_REGULATORY_CLEARED = 0` — gated on RISK-03 Q-13 opinion letter. Spec: NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §F.

- **UHDR two-layer key scheme — locked (2026-06-02):** UKMD (32-byte TRNG master key) stored in Config partition encrypted by WKMD (Argon2id-derived from biometric/PIN). Biometric change re-encrypts only the 32-byte wrapper — UHDR partition ciphertext unchanged. Spec: NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §C. Supersedes single-layer spec in NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1 §6 (incorporated into NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 2).
- **Device factory reset — locked (2026-06-02):** eMMC SANITIZE on UHDR + zero SHDR + clear Config + new TRNG salt + new warranty token. Power-loss resilient (reset_in_progress flag in SNVS_LPGPR1). Spec: NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §B.

Opcode wire values frozen: initiate=0x01, chunk=0x02, verify=0x03, commit=0x04, abort=0x05, safetyMCUBegin=0x10, safetyMCUChunk=0x11, safetyMCUCommit=0x12. `firmwareVersion` GATT UUID `4E455550-0011-1000-8000-00805F9B34FB` (READ/NOTIFY 4B LE, NOT in NPUUID.all). `applyConnected()` test helper in `NeurOneGATTManager`. 27/27 iOS OTA tests + 21/21 firmware host tests pass. ISA ISC-107–113 VERIFIED (progress: 36/164).
- **NPPS lexer/serializer bug fixes + regression tests (2026-06-10):** Four lexer bugs corrected in `NPPSLexer` (`app/ios/NeurOne/Protocol/NPProtocolScripting.swift`) and one serializer round-trip corruption fixed: **(1) Compound digit-ident (`660_808nm`, `660_808_1064nm`):** `-` after digit entered `readNumber()` even when followed by a letter; unit-reading backtrack left pos at `_`; outer loop read `_808nm` as a spurious field key → parser threw "Expected ':' or '{'". Fix: after unit backtrack, if pos is at `_`, read a compound ident consuming letters, digits, and `_` (e.g. `"660_808nm"`). **(2) Hyphenated tags (`wind-down`, `all-modalities`):** `-` after an ident unconditionally entered the number branch; `Double("-")` is nil → threw "Invalid number: -". Fix: only enter number branch when `-` is immediately followed by a digit (`pos + 1 < source.count && source[pos + 1].isNumber`). **(3) `mW_cm2` unit reading:** Unit loop stopped at digit `2` producing `"mW_cm"` (not in units set), then backtracked and outer loop re-read `mW_cm2` as an ident field key → parse error. Fix: extend unit loop to include `source[pos].isNumber` in addition to `.isLetter` and `== "_"`. **(4) `NPPSFieldValue.asIdent` broadened:** Extended to also return `.string` cases so quoted wavelength values (`wavelength: "660_808nm"`) work in user-authored scripts. **(5) Serializer round-trip corruption (MEDIUM):** Tags with hyphens were stored as split tokens (`["wind","down"]`) and re-emitted unquoted by the serializer, permanently corrupting the tag across save/reload cycles. Fix: `serializeTag()` helper in `NPPSSerializer` quotes any tag containing characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]` (and escapes embedded double-quotes). Both tag emission sites (single protocol and composite) updated to use `serializeTag()`. **4 regression tests added** to `app/ios/NeurOneTests/NPProtocolLibraryTests.swift`: `testLexerCompoundDigitIdentRoundTrip` (660_808nm + mW_cm2 in pbm_transcranial block), `testLexerTriWavelengthCompoundIdent` (660_808_1064nm), `testHyphenatedTagsDoNotThrow` (wind-down, all-modalities), `testHyphenatedTagRoundTripPreservesValue` (serialize→reparse preserves "wind-down" as a single tag). All 19 bundled NPPS protocols now parse without errors. ISA ISC-39 VERIFIED (NPProtocolLibrary loads all 19 templates). ISA ISC-122–125 VERIFIED (PhoneSessionManager WC bridge: isSupported guard, state forwarding, graceful unreachable, no UHDR-class fields in WC payload). ISA progress: 44/164 → 49/164. No UHDR/SHDR impact — lexer changes operate on protocol structure only.
- **UHDRKeyManager — Argon2id + PHC reference library implementation locked (2026-06-11):** PBKDF2 placeholder fully replaced. PHC `phc-winner-argon2` (CC0/Apache 2.0) vendored into `app/NeurOneShared/Sources/NeurOneArgon2/phc/` as an SPM C target. `np_bridge.c` wraps `argon2id_hash_raw()` into `np_argon2id_hash()` API. `Argon2Bridge.swift` exports `npArgon2idDeriveKey(password:salt:outputLength:)` with fixed params m=65536 KiB, t=4, p=1 per RFC 9106. `ARGON2_NO_THREADS` compile flag routes to single-threaded path (spec-compliant for p=1). `UHDRKeyManager.swift` derives WKMD → Argon2id → memory-only `UHDRKey(k1:k2:)` (64 bytes = K1+K2 for AES-256-XTS); key never persisted to Keychain or transmitted. Biometric credential: 32-byte `SecRandomCopyBytes` CSPRNG seed protected by `.userPresence` + `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` + `kSecAttrSynchronizable: false`. Salt: separate 32-byte CSPRNG Keychain entry (non-synchronizable, device-bound). Auth via `.deviceOwnerAuthentication` (biometric preferred, passcode fallback) — supports Parkinson's/post-stroke/no-biometric users. `bzero` in `UHDRKey.deinit` + credential defer block. Release build verified: `nm libNeurOneArgon2.a` confirms `np_argon2id_hash_custom` absent in release binary (gated by `#ifdef NP_ARGON2_CUSTOM_ENABLED` in `np_bridge.c`, `.when(configuration: .debug)` in `Package.swift`). PHC SOUP entry + SHA-256 file inventory in `phc/VENDOR.md`. 6/6 `UHDRKeyManagerTests` pass.  Supersedes compile-gate placeholder from PR #112.
- **Two consent subjects — locked (2026-06-16):** Warranty owner consent (SHDR) and user research consent (UHDR) are entirely separate. Warranty owner is the entity that registered the device — may be a clinic, not the user. A clinic activating warranty has NOT consented on behalf of any patient. `SHDRUploader` (gated by `WarrantyAnalyticsGate`) and `ConsentStore` (manages `ResearchAnalyticsGate`) are code-structurally independent. Blanket research consent withdrawal (L3) additionally revokes research analytics (`ResearchAnalyticsGate`); partial research withdrawals do not. Warranty consent withdrawal has no effect on research analytics. See CLAUDE.md §6.0 for full invariant table. Spec: `app/ios/NeurOne/Consent/ConsentStore.swift` header comment and `docs/np_priv_notice_001.md`.
- **Geo-gated privacy protections universalized — locked (2026-07-10):** Per the durable principle (most privacy-protecting approach; any jurisdiction's user protection applies to all users everywhere, never geo-gated), two previously jurisdiction-scoped USER protections are now universal. **(1) BIPA biometric written release** — the biometric written-release screen + public retention/destruction policy ship to ALL users (iOS `BIPADisclosureView` unconditional; Android `BipaConsentScreen` locale gate removed; `RegionHelper.isLikelyIllinois` deleted). **(2) Washington MHMD** — universal no-sale of SHDR behavioral data + standalone (non-HIPAA) warranty authorization obtained from all warranty owners. Regulation citations retained as the WHY; controls applied globally. Legal opinions (BIPA "possession" defense; WA counsel scope confirmation) remain open — they confirm scope, they do NOT gate shipping the protection. Deliberately LEFT jurisdictional (transfer *mechanisms*, not user protections): EU-US Data Privacy Framework self-certification and EU data residency (inherently tied to EU data). Docs updated: CLAUDE.md, `docs/np_priv_rem_001.md` (STEP-34/35), `docs/np_priv_notice_001.md` (Rev C — unconditional biometric-release + no-sale sections), `docs/np_priv_audit_001.md`. App code (iOS + Android BIPA) was already universal.
- **Hub ↔ cervical VNS library integration + cardiac-interlock config — locked (2026-07-11):** `firmware/hub_control/modules/np_mod_cvns.c` is the sole integration layer between the hub module framework (`np_module_registry` / `np_session_runner` / `np_safety_spi`) and the standalone `np_cervical_vns` safety library (`firmware/cervical_vns/`: session + interlock + stim). Replaces the prior broken stub in `np_mod_intranasal.c`, which called a nonexistent API (`np_cvns_interlock_init()` no-arg, `np_cvns_stim_start(&sp)`, `np_cvns_stim_params_t`) and could never compile against the real library. Owns the three library contexts; wires the fault callback (`np_cvns_fault_cb_t` → unified `NP_SAFETY_EN_CVNS` disable + `np_log_shdr_fault` with **suppressed (0) timestamp** per the fault-latch privacy gate), the session-end callback (enable teardown), and the display callback (caches non-biology current/impedance for telemetry; **no HR cached**). **Safety-critical cardiac-interlock config** centralized in `cvns_interlock_config()`: PPG rate pinned to `NP_CVNS_PPG_SAMPLE_RATE_HZ` (compile-time `_Static_assert`); **cardiac baseline is mandatory and cannot be waived by the descriptor** (`baseline_req` is deliberately not consulted — the interlock always requires a valid baseline before any enable, and this driver never requests the unified enable bit before the library reports a stimulation-delivery stage); HR-change limit / observation window / cross-validation tolerance stay resident in `np_cvns_config.h` (library + Class C safety MCU enforce). Descriptor→config validator `np_mod_cvns_build_config()` clamps frequency (mHz→Hz, 1–25 Hz), current (≤2000 µA), pulse width (200–1000 µs, 0→250 default), maps side {right/left/bilateral}→electrode_config, and bounds session duration to `NP_CVNS_SESSION_MAX_S`. Unified `NP_SAFETY_EN_CVNS` enable bit is asserted in delivery stages (RAMP_UP/ACTIVE/RAMP_DOWN) **and while the interlock is PRE_SESSION** (the library has requested enable and is awaiting the safety-MCU grant — required so the MCU can see the request; OI-CVNS-HUB-08), and dropped on fault / stop / terminal / session-end. Scheduler entry points `np_mod_cvns_tick(now_ms,now_s)` (100 ms) + `np_mod_cvns_push_ppg(sample,ts)` (PPG ISR) + `np_mod_cvns_request_reenable(now_s)`; HAL stubs OI-CVNS-HUB-04/05/06 (accessory-present, now_ms, now_unix); scheduler wiring OI-CVNS-HUB-07 (CLOSED — see below). **Registry symbol set completed:** `np_mod_cvns.c` + previously-uncompiled `np_mod_t2_stubs.c` both added to `HUB_SOURCES` (the registry's CVNS + T2 externs were unresolved at link before). **Blocking library bug fixed:** `np_cvns_session_start()` passed `NULL` as `safety_cb` to `np_cvns_stim_init()`, which intentionally rejects NULL (FAI-tested) → `session_start` was dead (always `INVALID_ARG`); the session now passes an internal no-op `session_stim_safety_cb` (the session advances by polling stim state, so behaviour is unchanged). Verified: `np_mod_cvns_tests` host suite + full firmware host suite **13/13 ctest green**; `np_mod_cvns.c` clean under ARM Cortex-M7 `-Werror`; `np_cervical_vns` library rebuilds clean. **Wire-format change (2026-07-11):** `np_mod_cvns_params_t.pulse_width_us` widened from `uint8_t` to `uint16_t` (struct 9→10 bytes) so the full clinical 200–1000 µs range is expressible from a descriptor (a `uint8_t` capped it at 255 µs). The only binary mirror — `app/web/src/lib/hubCompiler.ts` `encodeCervicalVNS()` — was updated in lockstep (10-byte buffer; `pulse_width_us` u16 at offset 5; `ramp_s` u16 shifted to 7; `baseline_req` u8 to 9). Android/iOS emit NPPS **text** (no binary struct), so no change there. `np_mod_cvns_build_config()` already clamped to 200–1000; the 1000 µs ceiling is now reachable and tested. **Discovered, NOT fixed (flagged):** `firmware/cervical_vns/tests/np_cvns_fai_tests.c` uses C++ lambda syntax (`[](...)`) in a `.c` file — pre-existing, never compiled, not in CI ctest. **OI-CVNS-HUB-08 CLOSED (2026-07-11): unified heartbeat → library SPI responses bridged.** The `np_cervical_vns` library advances its enable/impedance state machine only when fed `np_cvns_interlock_spi_response()` / `np_cvns_stim_safety_mcu_response()`; in the real hub there is no dedicated CVNS SPI link, so `cvns_apply_heartbeat()` in `np_mod_cvns.c` translates the unified 200 ms heartbeat reply into those calls. **Single-writer handoff:** the safety-heartbeat task (`task_safety_heartbeat` in `np_hub_control_main.c`) only publishes a snapshot via `np_mod_cvns_set_heartbeat_status(granted_mask, mcu_status)` (two naturally-atomic `volatile` word stores using `np_safety_spi_get_granted_mask()` + `np_safety_spi_get_status()` AFTER each `np_safety_spi_heartbeat()`); ALL library mutation happens on the control-task tick, which calls `cvns_apply_heartbeat()` **before** `np_cvns_session_tick()` so a grant/impedance verdict lands the same tick the library consumes it. **Translation:** (1) advisory `NP_CVNS_STAGE_IMPEDANCE` is satisfied with a nominal in-window value (`NP_CVNS_HB_IMPEDANCE_NOMINAL_KOHM`) so the flow reaches baseline — the authoritative impedance gate is the MCU's enable refusal; (2) `granted_mask & NP_SAFETY_EN_CVNS` while the interlock is `PRE_SESSION` → `NP_CVNS_SPI_RSP_ENABLE_GRANTED` + stim granted; (3) a NON-cardiac adverse status (`NP_SAFETY_STATUS_IMPEDANCE`/`_CUTOFF`/non-recoverable) with the grant absent while the hub is still requesting it → `NP_CVNS_SPI_RSP_ENABLE_REJECTED` (interlock FAULT + `cvns_fault_cb` drops the unified enable and logs SHDR, suppressed timestamp). **The cardiac cutoff path (`NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC`) is deliberately excluded** — it stays owned by the hub re-enable manager (`np_cvns_reenable`, OI-CVNS-HUB-01) and the main-processor PPG path (`np_mod_cvns_push_ppg` → `cvns_fault_cb`); feeding it here too would double-drive the fault and double-log SHDR. The snapshot is invalidated at init, session start, and stop so no stale grant crosses a session boundary. **+17 host assertions** (impedance-advance, enable-grant, enable-reject-impedance, cardiac-left-to-reenable, snapshot-reset-on-start); full firmware host suite **13/13 ctest green**; module + `np_hub_control_main.c` clean under Cortex-M7 `-Werror`. **OI-CVNS-HUB-09 CLOSED (2026-07-11): genuine hub-side per-electrode impedance measurement.** The advisory `NP_CVNS_STAGE_IMPEDANCE` step now drives an async hub-side measurement HAL (`np_cvns_hal_impedance_measure_start()`/`_poll(float kohm_out[])`, mirroring the re-enable manager's gate-3 start/poll pattern but returning per-electrode kΩ, not a pass/fail bit) via a new `cvns_advisory_impedance_step()`; the measured `kohm[NP_CVNS_ELECTRODE_COUNT]` ([0]=left,[1]=right) is fed to `np_cvns_stim_safety_mcu_response()` so the library records **real** values into its UHDR/SHDR impedance fields (and app display) instead of `NP_CVNS_HB_IMPEDANCE_NOMINAL_KOHM`. Retry-until-pass while in the stage: an out-of-window reading holds the session at IMPEDANCE (fail-closed) and re-measures on the next tick (recovers if contact is corrected); a HAL that cannot start, or a measurement exceeding `NP_CVNS_HB_IMPEDANCE_MEASURE_TIMEOUT_MS` (= `NP_CVNS_REENABLE_IMP_TIMEOUT_MS`, same hardware), falls back to the nominal placeholder so liveness is preserved — the safety MCU's enable refusal remains the authoritative impedance gate. A per-session measurement sub-state (`np_mod_cvns_imp_state_t` IDLE→MEASURING→DONE) is re-armed at `control()` start; `cvns_apply_heartbeat()` gained a `now_ms` arg for the timeout. **+4 host tests** (measured per-electrode kΩ recorded not-placeholder; out-of-window hold + re-measure; start-fail nominal fallback; timeout nominal fallback) — the pre-existing single-tick advance test updated to the two-phase (start-then-poll) async flow; full firmware host suite **13/13 ctest green**; `np_mod_cvns.c` clean under `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`. **Residual:** a safety-MCU enable refusal surfaces through the library's generic fault path, which `np_cvns_session_tick()` records with `cutoff_occurred=1` though it was not cardiac (SHDR `fault_reason` is correctly `NP_CVNS_FAULT_SAFETY_MCU`) — fixing the library's fault taxonomy is a separate library change. **OI-CVNS-HUB-07 CLOSED (2026-07-11): scheduler wiring of `np_mod_cvns_tick`/`push_ppg` into the hub scheduler.** The module's two entry points now have real callers. **(1) Tick:** `np_runner_run()` (`np_session_runner.c`) drives `np_mod_cvns_tick(now_ms, now_s)` once per loop iteration whenever the new production accessor `np_mod_cvns_active()` reports a CVNS command is running (command start through terminal stage), and caps its loop sleep to `NP_CVNS_STIM_TICK_MS` (100 ms) while active so the cardiac interlock, unified-enable handoff, and current ramp advance in real time; `now_ms`/`now_s` are read from the CVNS HAL clocks (`np_mod_cvns_hal_now_ms`/`_now_unix`) — the SAME free-running ms domain the PPG feed stamps with, not the runner's session-relative clock — matching what `np_mod_cvns_control()` passed to `np_cvns_session_start()`. Ticks continue through a recoverable cardiac hold (the runner loop keeps running; abort is suppressed by the heartbeat task), so the in-session re-enable flow advances. **(2) PPG feed:** new hub seam `np_hub_ppg_isr_sample(uint32_t sample)` (`np_hub_control_main.c`, alongside the existing `np_hub_zone_*_cb` platform-callback idiom) forwards to `np_mod_cvns_push_ppg(sample, np_mod_cvns_hal_now_ms())` — the platform PPG ADC ISR calls it at `NP_CVNS_PPG_SAMPLE_RATE_HZ` (200 Hz); `push_ppg` self-guards (no-op unless a CVNS session is active), so the ISR may route every sample unconditionally, ISR-safe (append-only, no blocking/alloc). Without this caller the interlock's Pan-Tompkins pipeline never received samples and the <100 ms cardiac cutoff never armed. Added `bool np_mod_cvns_active(void)` production accessor to `np_mod_cvns.c` (distinct from the NPTEST-only `np_mod_cvns_test_active()`). Verified: `np_mod_cvns_tests` host suite **ALL TESTS PASSED** (full firmware host suite 13/13 ctest green); all three touched files — `np_mod_cvns.c`, `np_session_runner.c`, `np_hub_control_main.c` — **cross-compile clean under the ARM Cortex-M7 toolchain (`arm-none-eabi-gcc` 14.2.1) with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`** (compiled via the target's exact `compile_commands.json` flags; the full `np_hub_control` link is separately blocked by a PRE-EXISTING `np_mod_pbm.c` ↔ `np_pbm1064` API mismatch, unrelated to this change — see the pbm build-break task). **OI-CVNS-HUB-11 CLOSED (2026-07-13): numeric hub-vs-MCU per-electrode impedance cross-validation.** Mirrors the cardiac main-vs-MCU baseline cross-check (`NP_CVNS_BASELINE_CROSSVAL_BPM`) for impedance. **Class C (safety MCU):** `np_impedance_check` (SW01-M06) now additionally reads per-electrode CVNS impedance (new HAL `np_hal_impedance_read_cvns_electrode_ohm`, electrode 0=left/1=right) and `np_impedance_check_build_cvns_report()` packs it into a new 8-byte `np_safety_imp_report_t` (`firmware/common/include/np_spi_wire_types.h`: magic 0x5A + valid flag + `cvns_kohm_x100[2]` + checksum). The per-electrode read is **purely additive** — the existing single-value enable GATE is unchanged, so the Class C interlock decision is untouched. **Wire transport:** the report rides the SPARE bytes [8..15] of the existing 38-byte heartbeat MISO window (the MCU stages a full 38-byte reply via new HAL `np_hal_spi_send_reply`; `np_safety_main.c`) — NO new SPI transfer, NO new NSS length, the 8-byte base reply frame and its checksum untouched. **Hub:** `np_safety_spi.c` parses + magic/checksum-validates the report into `s_cvns_imp_kohm[2]`/`s_cvns_imp_valid`, exposed via `np_safety_spi_get_cvns_impedance()`; the heartbeat task publishes it to the CVNS module via `np_mod_cvns_set_mcu_impedance()` (single-writer snapshot, like the granted-mask/status snapshot). **Cross-check + fail-closed:** in `cvns_advisory_impedance_step()` (OI-CVNS-HUB-09 path), when the hub's genuine in-window per-electrode reading completes it is compared against the MCU report; a disagreement beyond `NP_CVNS_IMPEDANCE_CROSSVAL_KOHM` (1.0 kΩ, `np_cvns_config.h`) on any electrode is **fail-closed**: log ONE SHDR diagnostic (`NP_CVNS_SHDR_EV_IMP_CROSSVAL` 0xC7, suppressed timestamp) and fault the library so the session never reaches delivery (`crossval_faulting` flag suppresses `cvns_fault_cb`'s generic log to avoid a double record). When the MCU report is not yet available, no cross-check is possible and the flow proceeds unchanged — the safety MCU's own enable refusal stays the AUTHORITATIVE gate; this is an additional main-processor block. **Privacy:** raw per-electrode kΩ is UHDR (tissue), transferred device-internally (MCU→hub) for cross-validation only and NEVER written to SHDR — only the divergence FLAG (device-condition, no biology) is. **+5 host tests** (`np_mod_cvns_tests.c`: agree→advance, diverge→fail-closed+one SHDR log, tolerance boundary strict-`>`, no-MCU-report→proceed) **+4 MCU host tests** (`np_impedance_report_tests.c`: report validity/rounding/checksum/gate-independence/re-invalidation) wired into `firmware/safety_mcu/CMakeLists.txt` + `firmware/CMakeLists.txt`; full firmware host suite **14/14 ctest green**. All changed C files clean under ARM `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` (`np_mod_cvns.c`/`np_safety_spi.c`/`np_hub_control_main.c` Cortex-M7; `np_impedance_check.c`/`np_safety_main.c` Cortex-M0+). No app-side mirror (the report is a device-internal MCU→hub reply; `hubCompiler.ts` only encodes hub→MCU descriptors). Doc sync: NP-FMEA-001 (SW01-M06 delta), NP-DHF-001; the `np_fw_emmc_001.docx` §12 classification-table row for the impedance report + divergence flag is a pending docx sync (classification captured in code + CLAUDE.md §5.1). **Residual — OI-CVNS-HUB-10:** HRV biofeedback consumes the same PPG stream via its own HAL path; a unified hub PPG fan-out (one ISR seam feeding both CVNS + HRV) is a future consolidation, not required here.
- **Session-log eMMC backing (OI-LOG-01..04) + BLE/USB-C protocol transport (OI-HUB-MAIN-01) — implemented + host-tested (2026-07-11):** The two remaining hub-control HAL stubs are now real code. **OI-LOG-01..04** (`firmware/hub_control/src/np_log_backend.c` + `include/np_log_backend.h`): the four `np_log_hal_{uhdr,shdr}_{append,flush}` entry points are a per-partition, block-coalescing append writer. Sub-block records buffer in a `NP_LOG_STAGE_BYTES` (512) staging block; whole blocks auto-commit (large EEG sample blocks bypass staging straight to the HAL); `flush` commits the exact buffered tail (no padding — append-mode files never rewrite a committed byte) then issues a durable block-device sync. Per-partition capacity/endurance tracking returns `NP_HUB_ERR_LOG_FULL` (→ `NP_ABORT_LOG_FAIL`) and a torn write latches the partition faulted so no partial record follows. **Encryption boundary:** this layer writes PLAINTEXT; the UHDR/SHDR partitions are already AES-256-XTS mounted (UHDR under the biometric-derived UKMD via `np_uhdr_key`; SHDR under the HKDF manufacturing key), so ciphertext is produced at the block-device layer without the logger ever touching key material. Residual hardware glue is the LittleFS-file open/append/sync HAL **OI-LOG-05..07** (host-modeled under `NPTEST_HOST`). **OI-HUB-MAIN-01** (`firmware/hub_control/src/np_transport.c` + `include/np_transport.h`): `np_hal_proto_queue_receive()` is backed by a length-prefixed (4-byte LE) reassembler + single-slot mailbox. `np_transport_feed()` (called by the BLE GATT write handler / USB-C CDC RX task) accumulates fragments into one complete signed protocol blob (framing validated against `NP_HUB_PROTO_BLOB_MAX`, malformed length discarded); a completed blob backpressures further feeds (`NP_HUB_ERR_SESSION_ACTIVE`) until the consumer picks it up. Consumer wait/signal + critical section are HAL **OI-HUB-MAIN-01a..e** (target: FreeRTOS binary semaphore + `taskENTER/EXIT_CRITICAL`, with a `UINT32_MAX`→`portMAX_DELAY` block-forever mapping so `pdMS_TO_TICKS` cannot overflow the `portMAX_DELAY` sentinel that `task_protocol_rx` passes; host: deterministic model under `NPTEST_HOST`). `NP_HUB_PROTO_BLOB_MAX` centralized in `np_hub_types.h` (replaces the local `PROTO_BUF_LEN` in `np_hub_control_main.c`). Both modules wired into `np_hub_control_app_main()` bring-up (`np_transport_init()`, `np_log_backend_init()`). **Tests:** 34 `np_log_backend_tests` + 32 `np_transport_tests` host checks (append/flush fidelity, coalescing, large-append passthrough, UHDR/SHDR isolation, fault latch, capacity; single/fragmented/malformed/busy/reset/oversized-buffer/max-size framing) — full firmware host suite **12/12 binaries green**. CMake wired (`hub_control/CMakeLists.txt` cross sources + two `NP_BUILD_TESTS` targets; root `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` `add_test`; CI `firmware-host-tests.yml` already triggers on `firmware/hub_control/**`). Target-branch FreeRTOS usage verified by a POSIX-port `-Werror` compile. See `firmware/hub_control/`.
- **Fault latch read privacy gate — locked (2026-07-14):** For any future dedicated fault-latch SPI read command, the latch's `count` field → **SHDR** unconditionally (distinct-fault-transition tally, no biology/timestamp; mirrors device session count) and `tick_ms` → **SHDR but SUPPRESSED to 0 when the latched fault carries `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC`** (a CVNS cardiac cutoff co-locates the event time with a UHDR-class health event). Enforced in code via `np_fault_latch_report_tick_ms()`/`_report_count()` (`firmware/safety_mcu/src/np_fault_latch.c`) — a future read command must marshal through these, not read `s_latch` directly. 18 host tests (`np_fault_latch_privacy_tests.c`). Both elements added to NP-FW-EMMC-001 §12 classification table (now 32 elements). The current 8-byte reply frame is unchanged; `tick_ms`/`count` still never leave SRAM today. See pending-decisions.md §13.4 (RESOLVED).
- **Fault-latch SHDR reporting made unconditional — reverses the conditional `tick_ms` suppression in the "Fault latch read privacy gate — locked (2026-07-14)" entry above (NEW 2026-08-12).** That entry is left exactly as written; this one records what changed. **What was believed then:** that reporting `tick_ms` for a cardiac fault would co-locate a device fault time with a UHDR-class health event, so zeroing it only for `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC` protected the wearer, and that this "mirrored" the OI-CVNS-HUB-11 divergence flag's suppressed timestamp. **What is now known:** all three limbs were wrong. (1) **The suppression created the signal it was meant to remove.** `count` was reported unconditionally by a *separate* helper, so `count > 0 && tick_ms == 0` distinguished a valid cardiac latch from both a valid non-cardiac latch and an invalid latch — a one-bit cardiac oracle readable with certainty. It is *strictly worse than not suppressing*: a raw `tick_ms` is a relative SysTick value that is meaningless until joined against a session record SHDR structurally does not hold (no session identifier, no wall clock), whereas the redaction pattern is **self-interpreting** — it needs no join, no other field, no correlation. The redaction converted a value requiring a join into a flag requiring nothing. (2) **It was never protecting the cardiac predicate anyway.** The hub already writes the cardiac cutoff to SHDR as a first-class named event — `NP_CVNS_SHDR_EV_CUTOFF` 0xC1 → `fault_log.fault_type = 'CVNS_HR_CUTOFF'` with `fault_source = 'SAFETY_MCU'` (`ci/shdr/shdr_fleet_schema.sql`) — under the locked "safety interlock log → SHDR" boundary rule. The predicate is published by design; hiding it in one field while naming it in another protected nothing. (3) **The cited precedent is unconditional and was mis-cited.** `NP_CVNS_SHDR_EV_IMP_CROSSVAL` is not conditionally redacted: `np_log_shdr_fault()` discards its `session_ms` argument for **every** caller (`(void)session_ms;`, `firmware/hub_control/src/np_session_log.c`), and the `0U /* ts suppressed */` literals at the CVNS call sites are belt-and-braces on a logger that already drops the field for all fault types. The sibling has no oracle; the fault latch took a correct unconditional rule as authority for a conditional one. **General principle now binding (recorded in CLAUDE.md §5.1):** *a redaction applied conditionally on a sensitive predicate leaks that predicate.* The redaction must be unconditional, or the predicate must not be inferable from the pattern of redaction — the same failure shape as a login form answering "no such user" versus "wrong password". **Resolution:** `tick_ms` is not SHDR-reportable at all — fault event timing is UHDR, which the fleet schema already stated (`fault_log` has no timing column; "Precise fault event timing, where it exists at all, is UHDR under the user's key") and which `np_log_shdr_fault()` already enforced for every caller. Option (a), reporting `tick_ms` unconditionally, was rejected because it would contradict that schema statement and require a new column with no consumer; options (b)/(c) were rejected because a dead helper or a bucketed value invites a future consumer for a field nothing needs — `count`, not timing, is what fleet predictive maintenance consumes (NP-PMS-001 §5, "fault latch counts"). The two independent accessors `np_fault_latch_report_tick_ms()` / `_report_count()` are **deleted** and replaced by one fixed-shape marshaller, `np_fault_latch_build_report()` → `np_fault_latch_report_t {status, slot, count}` (`np_safety_protocol.h`): the oracle existed *because* the accessors were independent, so the fix is structural — a struct whose shape is fixed at compile time cannot have a field present for one fault kind and absent for another — rather than a different constant. `tick_ms` stays in the SRAM latch for MCU-internal timing. **Verification:** 23 host assertions (was 18), including a regression guard asserting that with identical history at the same tick the reported records for a cardiac and a non-cardiac fault differ **only** in `status`; **falsified** by reintroducing the retired conditional, which fails exactly that assertion (exit 1), then reverted to green. `count`/`slot` are pinned to positive expected values so a do-nothing marshaller cannot satisfy the guard. Firmware host suite 28/28. **IEC 62304 Class C:** reporting path only — no stimulation enable, interlock threshold, cutoff timing, or latch-commit behaviour changed; `np_fault_latch_commit()`/`_clear()`/`_init()` untouched. No QMS/CAPA record opened (development mode; no hardware, no bench data, no SHDR fault records exist, so nothing was encoded under the retired rule and no consumer required migration). Docs synced: CLAUDE.md §5.1, NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 2 §12, `pending-decisions.md` §13.4, `document-register.md`, NP-DHF-001. **Reported, not resolved:** whether publishing `CVNS_HR_CUTOFF` to SHDR at all is the right call is a separate live question (it is a locked decision, CLAUDE.md §5.1 / NP-FW-CVNS-001 §8.2); and NP-FW-CVNS-001 §8.2 contradicts itself, routing "Time-of-cutoff offset (s after stim onset)" to **UHDR** two rows above routing "Safety MCU fault log (offset, reason, no HR)" — whose §5.6 struct contains `cutoff_offset_ms` — to **SHDR**.
- **clinical-03 lateralization — option (b), narrow the zone (2026-07-20):** `clinical-03-pbm-cognitive-1064` retargeted from `Frontal Right` (11 sockets, includes midline 1/5/13) to a new `Frontal Right (excl. midline)` zone (8 sockets: 3, 6, 9, 10, 14, 15, 18, 19). Rationale: the protocol's entire Grade-A evidence base (Gonzalez-Lima / Barrett) is right-lateralized, and Monte Carlo optical modelling (NP-OPT-PSF-001) shows the inclusive zone puts **16.3% of its cortical energy contralateral**, falling to **3.6%** when narrowed — a 78% reduction (74-80% across the model's full sensitivity sweep). Cost: one zone definition, zero firmware. This is exactly the mechanism the PR #210 membership ruling designates (dis-inclusion = author a narrower zone; there is no runtime exclusion filter), and it depends on nothing gated by REG-1, since midline status derives from the validated socket row structure rather than from 10-20 registration. Option (a) accept-the-spill was rejected because the deviation is quantified, avoidable, and free to fix. Option (c) partial-module inclusion remains deferred and is now shown to be strictly dominated for this purpose — see below.
- **Partial-module inclusion (option (c)) — deferral now evidence-backed, still deferred (2026-07-20):** NP-OPT-PSF-001 §4.1 models an aperture lighting only the ipsilateral half of a midline module: it still spills **22.1%** contralateral versus exactly 50% lit whole, i.e. it removes ~56% of the leak, while simply omitting the module takes it to zero. The mechanism is the 1064nm spatial resolution floor — an edge-spread function of **~26 mm** (10-90%) at cortical depth versus a 20 mm mask feature, so most of the masked half is re-illuminated by scatter from the lit half. Note the honest form of the argument: the optics does not prove masking useless, it proves masking **cannot reach zero** — and since zone narrowing reaches zero for free, that is enough to close the question. Partial-module inclusion must not be revisited as a lateralization tool; any future proposal needs a justification other than spatial selectivity at depth. Implementation still requires a fresh owner ruling.
- **Lateralized-protocol audit encoded in tooling (2026-07-20):** the point-in-time 72-file survey that found clinical-03 is now a build check in `scripts/sync-socket-map.ts`. Any protocol naming a bare single-hemisphere zone fails the run until it is either narrowed or explicitly added to `ACCEPTED_LATERALIZED` with a reason; unresolvable zone names also fail. Bilateral pairs, whole-lobe and whole-region zones, and `zones: clinician_selected` pass. tDCS/TMS/tACS/VNS/audio/visual are documented as out of scope (they do not address sockets by zone).
- **BN-boss conductive thermal export ADOPTED as base thermal design (NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001, 2026-07-22):** Module heat is exported from the BN thermal boss through a solid conductive via to an **external** fan-cooled heatsink, with the inter-bowl cavity left **stagnant** — the shielded, sealed interior is never ventilated. THERM-1a first-pass FD (perfect-sink bound) shows this carries ~90% of module heat and holds the LED junction throttle-free (54 °C < 62 °C) at full T2-peak flux, preserving the EMF-shield / IP-seal differentiator that cavity ventilation would breach (apertures through mu-metal + RF liners). Chosen over inter-bowl-cavity ventilation, rejected on shield-integrity, ingress/hygiene, optics-fouling, acoustic/EEG-noise, power/MTBF, and safety-case-inversion grounds (NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001 §5). **Scope note:** the via is a *healthy-state* junction-cooling strategy only — it gives **zero benefit under heatsink/fan loss** (fault face 60.3 °C with or without it), so it does **not** replace the fan-health interlock. Formalized as **NP-DT-001 DI-SAFE-13** (Rev B, 2026-07-22).
- **THERM-1a fan-health path selection — Path A REJECTED, Path B1 SELECTED (NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001, 2026-07-22):** First-pass THERM-1a analysis (1D closed-form + rough non-verification-grade axisymmetric FD; cases C2/C3/C6) rejected Path A: the 62 °C junction throttle leaves the scalp-facing face ≈ 60 °C and scalp skin ≈ 52–55 °C at the worst fault (fan off, 43.3 °C ambient, low perfusion) — **14–21 °C over the IEC 60601 42 °C applied-part limit**, 1D and 2D in agreement, and unchanged by the export via. **Path B1 (scalp-facing NTC co-located with PD2) committed** (NP-REQ-FANHEALTH-001 §4a). Provisional constants: SR-FAN-03 natural-convection-safe ceiling ≈ **4.5 mW/cm² at 43.3 °C ambient** (halt/trickle PBM on fan loss, → 0 as ambient → 42 °C); SR-FAN-04 τ_face ≈ 35–45 min, t₄₂ ≈ minutes (≤ 10 s response target met by 2+ orders of magnitude — the steady ceiling, not response speed, governs). The face ≤ 42 °C ceiling at extreme ambient is fundamentally ambient-bounded and owned by the firmware ambient/duty gate (NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001 / NP-FW-POE-001). All constants provisional pending verification-grade 2D CFD (NP-THERM-CFD-001 §9 step 2) + THERM-1b bench; **path selection is firm.** FMEA-G07-01 closure path logged in the feeder (NP-FMEA-GEOM-001) and assigned **RISK-26** in the NP-RISK-001 register (Rev B→C, status OPEN pending SW01-M04 implementation + THERM-1b); SR-FAN-01…06 accepted into **NP-SW-001 §6.2** (Rev B).
- **clinical-03 evidence-grade claim gated behind REG-1 (option iii, 2026-07-27):** the ~8× area deviation between clinical-03's 8-module zone and the literature's single-site aperture (`docs/status/pending-decisions.md` §13.2c) was resolved by choosing option (iii) over the alternatives: (i) accept the ~8× deviation while keeping the "Grade A"/gold-standard claim, or (ii) shrink the module count now on the still-provisional lattice and accept placement uncertainty. Both were rejected as insufficiently conservative — (i) keeps an overstated claim, (ii) authors a precise-sounding zone against boundaries not yet confirmed by REG-1. Decision: **withhold the evidence-grade/"Grade A"/gold-standard characterization from `clinical-03-pbm-cognitive-1064.npps` until REG-1 (10-20 socket registration, NP-HEX-ZM-001 §7) lands and the zone is re-authored to the true 1–2 module Fp2/F4 footprint.** REG-1's row in NP-HEX-ZM-001 §7 now lists this claim gate as a consumer.
- **clinical-03 duty/dose shortfall — honest relabel, not a firmware change (2026-07-27):** the same investigation surfaced that the protocol's `frequency: 0Hz` + `duty_cycle: 25%` declaration was self-contradictory as authored, and the root cause is a genuine hardware limit, not a typo: the 1064nm channel's duty cycle is firmware-capped at 25% (`np_pbm_drive_set_duty()`, NP-FW-PBM1064-001 §5, "cannot be bypassed by the session layer" — the npps schema itself only accepts `duty_cycle: 1–25`), and the channel's session dose is separately capped at 36 J/cm² (OI-SES-01) versus the literature's 60 J/cm²/site. True CW at literature intensity is not achievable on current hardware — offered explicitly as a choice, the owner declined to raise the firmware duty/dose ceilings (which would require its own safety and regulatory review — RISK-03, FAI-SM re-test, OI-SES-01 re-confirmation) and chose instead to correct the protocol file to honestly state what it delivers: ~0.10 W/cm² average (2.5× below literature CW) within the 36 J/cm² ceiling (well below the literature's 60 J/cm²/site). `protocols/predefined/clinical-03-pbm-cognitive-1064.npps` bumped to Rev 1.1 with both quantified gaps stated in the description and an inline comment explaining the firmware ceiling. This gap is independent of the REG-1/area gate above — closing REG-1 alone does not restore the "gold-standard" claim while this shortfall remains.
- **Hub enclosure tooling locked (NP-TOOL-HUB-001 Rev 1, 2026-07-27):** Four molded features on the single hub housing tool (no separate mold from a base hub, unlike the smart zone module variant which required NP-TOOL-ZM-SM-001): (1) F-01 intranasal probe dock — Y-junction saddle sized to junction body OD + two silicone-padded bilateral probe-tip receptacles, ≤2N retention, prevents Y-junction fracture in storage (CLAUDE.md §3); (2) F-02 two tethered anchor posts for the hub's own USB-C and DFU/service port covers, ≤20mm tether bounded so a detached cover geometrically cannot reach the F-04 fan intake grille; (3) F-03 large-radius Boa cable channel continuing the shell's PTFE-lined 50,000-cycle channel through the hub housing, ≥12×-lace-OD minimum bend radius (exact radius pending supplier datasheet, OI-HTOOL-02); (4) F-04 tool-free fan/heatsink service door — two diagonally-opposed quarter-turn captive fasteners, ≤6mm louvre slot width for finger-probe safety, giving the user a way to act on the SR-FAN-05 predictive-maintenance alert (RISK-26 / FMEA-G07-01) before the safety derate engages. **Hub placement (occipital arch, external to the 5-layer shielded shell) is inferred, not newly asserted**, from three already-locked/adopted facts: the EEG harness routing origin at "Hub PCB EEG connector" (NP-DRV-SHELL-001 Rev 2), hub-resident antennas (CLAUDE.md §4.1), and the external fan-cooled heatsink terminating the BN-boss thermal export (NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001 §5) — the exact hub-to-shell mechanical interface remains open (OI-HTOOL-01). 11-item mold design review checklist; 4 FAI items (FAI-HTOOL-02 BLOCKING on the tether-reach-vs-fan-intake test); 6 open items (OI-HTOOL-01–06). Closes `docs/status/pending-decisions.md` §13.4 "Hub tooling" item. See `docs/np_tool_hub_001.md`.
- **SMART-1 decided — every hex-lattice socket is I2C/TIA-capable, not a subset (NP-HEX-ZM-001 §4a/§7, 2026-07-28):** Full coverage over cheaper partial coverage. Rationale: NeurOne's research mission (SBIR trials, IRB-approved custom studies, the research suggestion portal — CLAUDE.md §6.3) depends on placing any current or future smart module type at any socket for arbitrary montage/protocol design; subset coverage would permanently bake a placement constraint into shell/Hub PCB tooling, for every smart module type this platform will ever ship, not just T1-C. **Known unresolved cost:** the Hub PCB Rev B I2C/TIA architecture (this file, "Hub PCB Rev B TIA gain switch architecture locked") was sized for five zone slots — one PCA9546A 4-channel I2C mux plus one GPIO-muxed fifth. That does not scale to the ~30–80-socket hex lattice (NP-HEX-ZM-001 §3) without a materially different I2C fan-out design and a much larger per-socket analog-switch count. This is real Hub PCB NRE, not yet scoped or costed — flagged for EE Lead. See `docs/np_hex_zm_001.md` §4a, §7 (SMART-1). **→ RESOLVED 2026-07-29 by NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 (next entry).**
- **Shell socket interconnect architecture — cluster carriers (NP-DRV-SHELL-002 Rev 1, 2026-07-29):** Replacement for the retired 5-zone-slot FPC routing of NP-DRV-SHELL-001 Rev 2. **Status DRAFT, deliberately not BASELINED** — the ~80-socket lattice it routes to is itself PROVISIONAL pending REG-1/ACT-1, and an interconnect cannot be more certain than its lattice; the architecture is therefore expressed per *cluster* (≤8 sockets) rather than per lattice count, so a REG-1 re-cut changes how many carriers are populated and nothing else.
  - **The rejected options, with reasons.** Per-socket tails: 80 ZIF receptacles need ~960 mm of Hub PCB connector edge and put 1,600 conductors on one board — rejected on arithmetic. Monolithic L1 backplane: makes the socket layer a single non-repairable part, fights the compound curvature, and maximizes conductive mass on the one layer NP-HELMET-GEOM-001 §3.2 constrains to non-magnetic/low-eddy because the fluxgates mount there — rejected.
  - **Selected: cluster carriers.** The 3–7-tile cluster (**now 3–6 under CONTIG-1**; the 3-hex triad this range originally spanned is excluded by CLUSTER-1) that NP-HEX-ZM-001 §5.4a invented for a purely *mechanical* reason (one clamp actuator instead of 42 levers) is also the correct *electrical* aggregation boundary, for an independent reason: high-impedance analog (PD1/PD2 at 14–72 µA, EEG at sub-µV) cannot travel, and the cluster is the nearest conversion point that already exists. Five networks split by physics — N1 power, N2 control, N3 local sense (**≤50 mm, never leaves its carrier**), N4 electrode, N5 safety.
  - **The inherited assumption that was retired:** *"the Hub PCB is where module signals are conditioned."* Retiring it is what collapses SMART-1's residual. Analog front ends now scale with **cluster count** (stated here as ~12; **now 18** — see the SUPERSEDED-FIGURES note at the end of this entry), not socket count (~80) — the same order as the 5 they replace. Hub PCB Rev C consequently needs **no** ~80 DG2788A gain switches, **no** ~160 high-impedance channels crossing the shell, **no** ~80 `GAIN_SEL` GPIO and **no** cascaded muxing. The DG2788A per-socket topology itself survives unchanged; it is replicated onto the carriers, exactly as NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 2's own supersession note predicted.
  - **Hub PCB Rev C interface contract (coordination surface):** 12 cluster connectors × 12 pins, **16 positions provisioned** (8 I2C branches × 2 clusters); one 8-channel host branch switch; 12 `SAFE_EN_n` GPIO **sourced by the Safety MCU**, default LOW at reset; **⚠ every count in this bullet is superseded — 18 clusters exceed both the 16 provisioned positions and the 8×2 tree, and `SAFE_EN_n` ownership is itself contested; see the note at the end of this entry;** one broadcast `SYNC`; PDN feed rated at the vault ceiling. Two-level tree arithmetic is 8 × 2 × 8 = **128 = `NP_HEXMAP_MAX_SOCKETS`**, so the physical bus tree and the firmware 7-bit addressing ceiling agree with no arbitrary sub-ceiling.
  - **Conductor count, stated honestly:** 144 conductors at the Hub PCB (EEG included) vs 110 for the retired design (100 + 10 EEG cables) and 1,600 for per-socket tails. That is ~1.3× the retired count for 16× the sockets — *not* a reduction, and the document does not claim one.
  - **Modules lose their tail.** A hex tile presents an 18-contact back-face pad array seated by the §5.4a clamp plunger. Consequence: **the module interconnect has zero dynamic-flex paths** — the retired architecture's dominant failure mode (fatigue cracking at a stiffener edge over 1,000 swap cycles) is designed out rather than mitigated. Non-obvious coupling recorded: contact count drives clamp-plate load (~38–63 N est. at 18 contacts per 7-tile cluster), so **contact count is a RISK-22 accessibility variable**, closed jointly with MECH-2 (OI-SHELL2-03).
  - **Safety: hardware enable per cluster, software enable per socket.** **⚠ Contested — NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 §7.2 instead specifies a single `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` bit; see the note at the end of this entry.** 80 sockets cannot each own a Safety MCU GPIO, and routing enable authority over I2C would put the main processor in the path — destroying the CLAUDE.md §4.2 property. `SAFE_EN_n` gates each cluster's high-side load switch, so no module firmware state, stuck I2C transaction or main-processor hang can produce emission from a de-energized cluster, and the <50 ms watchdog cutoff stays a rail cut rather than a message. Granularity **improves** 5 → ~12. This is the hardware half of OI-HUB-SOCKET-01; the socket-indexed control registry remains open there.
  - **≥15 mm EEG separation is unachievable and is not claimed.** The retired 18–22 mm came from putting PBM FPCs and EEG cables on opposite sides of the shell wall. EEG electrodes now live *inside* T1-B tiles on the same L1 carrier as LED drive current; the best geometric separation left is the ~2–3 mm L1 laminate. Separation was always the *mechanism*; **<5 µVpp injected artifact is the requirement, and it is retained verbatim** (retired DRC-18c → SH2-DRC-16) and met by four mechanisms instead: shorten the analog path, DRL-driven guard plane, deterministic subtractable artifact, and loop-area minimization.
  - **The therapeutic band cannot be filtered off the bus — arithmetic, not opinion.** Holding a 40 Hz/25 % pulse at 0.25 A within 100 mV needs **15.6 mF per tile**; the PWM carrier needs ~62 µF and the edges ~10 µF. So on-tile decoupling kills the carrier and the edges and cannot touch the 0.5–100 Hz envelope, which *is* the therapy and sits in exactly the EEG band and the ELF band the Helmholtz loop cancels. It must be handled geometrically (broadside `VLED+`/`PGND` pairs, ≤25 mm² loop area per cluster feed — ~37 nT at 30 mm vs ~1.9 µT for a 5 mm-spaced pair) and computationally, never by filtering.
  - **Three prohibitions locked.** (1) The shell, shield stack, mu-metal and any DRL-driven structure **may not** carry LED or tES return current — the shell is a *driven* EEG shield (CLAUDE.md §4.3), so returning therapeutic-band current through it injects that current into the EEG reference. (2) N1 **may not** form a closed ring on L1 — a bowl-scale loop antenna and ground loop. (3) Spread-spectrum/dithered PWM is **prohibited**, inverting standard EMC practice: dither smears bus energy into broadband noise across the EEG band that neither the notch nor subtraction can remove, whereas a deterministic phase-locked artifact is a known, subtractable line.
  - **One aperture, segregated returns.** The interconnect shares the existing posterior-centre blind-mate boss (no second aperture to defend against the §5.3a λ/20 slot criterion), but the boss must present segregated contact groups with **independent returns** — the Helmholtz coils are the actuator of a loop whose fluxgate sensors sit on L1, and a shared return impedance would let the actuator modulate its own sensor's reference, nulling at the fluxgate while the field at the brain diverges (same failure shape as FMEA-G07-01: sensor reads nominal while the hazard grows). **Time-boxed: cheap now, expensive once MECH-1 tools the boss (OI-SHELL2-02).**
  - **Cancellation calibration is now configuration-dependent.** Because any tile type may occupy any socket, bus current distribution — and therefore self-field at each fluxgate — changes per configuration, so NP-HEX-ZM-001 §5.3.1 reason 4's "fixed coil-drive→field transfer" no longer holds unconditionally. Recalibration fires on `np_module_map` rebuild. Feed-forward self-field subtraction must bind to **measured PD/dose telemetry, not commanded current**, because LED aging, PD-metered compensation and the 62 °C throttle all make actual current diverge from commanded.
  - **BOM/manufacturing:** est. +$125–216 per headset at L1 (EE Lead confirmation required, OI-SHELL2-05) — a real increase, ~$1.30–2.40 per socket against ~$4–6 per socket for the retired five large modules; per socket cheaper, per headset not. L1 becomes an **electro-mechanical assembly**, requiring a new rigid-flex-into-molded-carrier supplier category in NP-PROC-SUP-001 (OI-SHELL2-04). The retired per-zone molded FPC channels (REQ-ST-01..07) are deleted from shell tooling; complexity moves into L1 lamination. Cluster-level rework replaces bowl scrap.
  - 28-item design review checklist (SH2-DRC-01–28, vs 23 in the retired document); 10 open items (OI-SHELL2-01–10). OI-SHELL2-09 records the controlled-document updates this architecture *implies but does not make* — NP-HEX-ZM-001 §5.3.1/§5.4a, NP-HELMET-GEOM-001 §2 (which still assumes a "20-pin FPC" in the L1 module depth budget) and §3.2, plus a new FMEA entry for the shared-return failure. Those are separate controlled revisions, deliberately not edited here.
  - **⚠ SUPERSEDED FIGURES (2026-08-04) — cluster count is 18, not 12.** This entry was written against
    a cluster count of ~12 derived from CLUSTER-1 alone. Two later principal decisions change it:
    **SYM-1** (partition mirror-symmetric about the sagittal midline) and **CONTIG-1** (petals must form
    a contiguous arc — no pendant petal). Under CLUSTER-1 + SYM-1 + CONTIG-1 the count at the shipped
    80-socket lattice is **18, provably minimal** (six forced midline clusters + six lateral mirror
    pairs; derivation at NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 §8.2.1, diagram at `docs/diagrams/np_hextile_cluster_map.svg`).
    Consequences for the figures above, none of which are corrected in NP-DRV-SHELL-002 itself yet
    (**OI-HEXTILE-14**): cluster-tier BOM $76.08 → **$114.12**; analog front ends scale with 18 not ~12;
    and **18 exceeds both the 16 provisioned connector positions and the `8 branches × ≤2` I2C tree** —
    interconnect options and a recommendation are recorded at NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 §8.2.2. Separately, this
    entry's "hardware enable per cluster" is **contested, not settled**: NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 §7.2
    specifies a single `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` bit (all-or-nothing cranial cut, 1 GPIO) against this
    document's per-cluster `SAFE_EN_n` (18 GPIO) — a 17-GPIO and safety-granularity divergence tracked as
    **OI-HEXTILE-13**, with a proposed resolution at NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 §8.4.1 (split the enable by IEC
    62304 class: Class B per-cluster gates for availability, one Class C bit in series as the hard
    interlock). **The five-network split, the cluster-carrier selection and the zero-dynamic-flex
    conclusion are unaffected** — only the counts and the enable ownership are.
- **Hub PCB Rev C — distributed cluster-controller tier replaces the 5-slot star (NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3, 2026-07-29, DRAFT not yet baselined):** Closes the SMART-1 residual above. **Key finding: the binding constraint is interconnect, not I2C mux count.** Rev 2 is a star — every socket's analog *and* digital lines terminate at the hub — so scaling it to the 80-socket lattice needs ~11 conductors × 80 ≈ **880 conductors** through the single posterior blind-mate boss (NP-HEX-ZM-001 §5.3c), 160 TIA channels, 160 ADC channels against ~32 available on the RT1062, and 80 `GAIN_SEL` GPIO. A deeper PCA9546A mux tree (the obvious answer) fixes address collision and leaves all of that untouched, so it was rejected along with per-socket address translation (LTC4316 ≈ $200) and a hub-side scanning MCU (both still stars). **Decision: a cluster-controller tier on the inner bowl, one small board per 8 sockets** (`ceil(n/8)`; 10 boards at n=80, 16 max — and 16 × 8 = 128 = the full 7-bit `NP_HEXMAP_SOCKET_BITS` domain, which is why 8 is the right cluster size). Each board: STM32G071 (same part as the safety MCU — reuses toolchain, BSP, errata and IEC 62304 qualification lineage rather than saving ~$7 on a second family), one PCA9548A for I2C isolation (exact 8-channel fit; tier-1 controllers have unique strapped addresses so no mux is needed there at all), one 16:1 low-leakage mux feeding PD1/PD2 currents into **one shared switchable-gain TIA**, and the per-socket LED drive.
