Hub↔inner-bowl is one differential I2C bus (PCA9615-class) plus an ATTN line, with a firmware bus-quiet window in the ADS1299 inter-conversion gaps — differential chosen chiefly because a single-ended 400 kHz open-drain bus running past µV EEG leads would repeat the mistake NP-HEX-ZM-001 §5.3.1 rejects for the Helmholtz coil. **Rev B's component-level work is carried forward unchanged** (DG2788A dual-SPDT, Rf 47 kΩ ↔ 22.1 kΩ, and the Si-vs-InGaAs saturation analysis); only the count and control source change. **Four consequences worth recording:** (1) **the hub PCB no longer encodes the socket count at all** — no per-socket footprint, net, or `n_sockets`-derived constant — so **REG-1 no longer blocks hub tooling** and can move the lattice anywhere in 30–128 sockets, re-tooling only inner-bowl boards; (2) **zero `GAIN_SEL` GPIO** — gain is a cluster-MCU pin set per *sample* from live UID inventory inside the scan loop, which also removes Rev 2's stale-gain-after-hot-swap hazard, with no shift register or GPIO expander needed; (3) **PD1/PD2 ratio accuracy improves** — both PDs now traverse the same mux, Rf, op-amp and ADC, so gain error is common-mode and cancels exactly in the ratio the fouling-vs-aging discriminant rests on, retiring Rev 2's ≤0.5% per-slot matching requirement; (4) the safety enable mask **cannot** hold per-cluster bits (16-bit frame, `NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK = 0x3FFF`, 2 spare; 16 cluster bits + 9 modality bits = 25 > 16), so bits 0–4 collapse to a single `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` with 1–4 reserved-not-reused, gated physically per cluster on each LED rail — accepted consequence is all-or-nothing safety cuts, routed to safety review (OI-HUB-C07). Cluster-controller firmware is a new IEC 62304 **Class B** unit (`firmware/cluster_ctrl/`), conditional on HUB-REQ-C02: the safety-MCU enable gates each cluster's LED rail **upstream** of the cluster MCU, preserving CLAUDE.md §4.2 dual-processor isolation. **Also caught:** re-indexing `K_PD1`/`K_PD2`/`K_ratio_nom` by socket (the migration NP-FW-PBM1064-001's supersession note proposes) would *preserve* a defect — those coefficients characterise a swappable module, so they must be keyed to **module UID** in the existing UID-keyed `np_module_map` NVRAM record, else a module swap silently applies the wrong calibration while `cal_source` still reads `NP_CAL_FACTORY` (OI-HUB-C06). **BOM:** $6.34/cluster board → **$63.40 at n=80** (`$6.34 × ceil(n/8)`; $25.36 at 30, $101.44 at 128), hub PCB itself ≈ **+$1.09** net. Comparable to naively scaling Rev 2 (~$67) but buildable, at ~100 conductors across the parting plane instead of ~880 — the same money spent where it works. Not yet netted against the retired 5-zone-module drive electronics already inside the $405 Home Standard BOM (OI-HUB-C08). Rev 2 is archived verbatim as Appendix A. 12 open items OI-HUB-C01…C12 (largest: the per-socket FPC pinout + LED drive stage, still unwritten); 15 review items HUB-DRC-C01…C15. **Status: DRAFT — the socket count and every part number are explicitly open.** See `docs/np_hw_hub_001.md`.
- **★ CLUSTER-1 — the 7-hex flower is the cluster unit (principal direction, 2026-07-30):** wherever the
  lattice allows a full flower, use one; where it does not, use a **partial flower** — not a larger patch
  and not a 3-hex triad. The reason is mechanical: the flower minimises the cluster's longest dimension,
  and both clamp-plate stress and stored curvature scale off that. An 8th tile must attach on the
  perimeter, forcing span 122.2 → 161.8 mm, bending stress ×1.75, plate deflection ×3.07 and dome depth
  25.1 → 55.0 mm. Recorded in `docs/np_hex_zm_001.md` §5.4a. (Logged here 2026-08-04 — the decision itself
  predates this entry and was previously only in the brief.)
- **Accessible zone-module position identification — layered design, app-primary (NP-HFE-002 Rev 1, 2026-07-31):** replaces RISK-15 Layers 3/4/5 rather than re-scaling them. **Why the old layers are unsalvageable, in order of decisiveness:** (b) the hex lattice *tessellates*, so the only shell surface left between two sockets is a structural web a few millimetres wide, and an ISO 17049 braille cell needs a ~6 × 10 mm footprint — the artefact does not physically fit, independent of any psychophysics; (a) an 80-member tactile-symbol set is not discriminable by touch, and multi-cell braille numerals require braille literacy that most late-blind adults do not have; (c) with one universal module mould, a position marking on the module would be a manufacturing lie. Additionally the socket count is provisional (78 → 80 already, gated on REG-1/ACT-1), so any per-socket moulded artefact re-tools on every lattice re-cut. **The design follows the architecture's own identity split** (NP-HEX-ZM-001 §4a: socket = position, module = type): tactile marking follows *type* onto the module, where the set is a closed 3–4 members and symbols work; *position* moves to guidance, because no static marking of an ~80-member open set can work. **Five layers:** (L1) a tactile landmark grid on the shell — a dashed midline spine whose six gaps are exactly the six midline sockets including Oz, three transverse band ridges at the frontal/central-sulcus/parieto-occipital boundaries coded 1/2/3 bumps where they cross the spine, and a uniform "standard electrode site" marker at the 8 T1 montage sites plus Oz (this last one gated on REG-1); counting depth is ≤2 rows and ≤4 sockets, verified against the generated socket map; (L2) **companion-app guided placement as the primary channel** — exception-structured work list, landmark-grammar instructions, errors spoken as corrective movements not socket ids, native VoiceOver/TalkBack including refreshable-braille routing, offline over BLE; (L3) 1–4 raised bars on the module's non-mating face for type; (L4) `np_module_map_check_placement()` as the already-delivered backstop; (F) a tactile placement card for shipped standard maps as a phone-free fallback. **Rejected:** per-socket tactile symbols (geometry + discriminability + re-tooling); per-socket touch-to-speak (needs ~80 new sense channels on a Hub PCB whose I2C fan-out is already unscoped NRE — `np_module_map` only sees a module once it is electrically seated and answering, so there is no pre-insertion sensing to build on). **App-primary is the equal path, not an accommodation:** a tessellating field of identical hex tiles has nothing printed on it and nowhere to print it, so a sighted user has no better channel either — and a deaf-blind user has *no* other channel at all, since neither audio nor static tactile marking can carry dynamic protocol-specific guidance but a braille display driven by the platform screen reader can. **Two consequences recorded rather than buried:** (1) the wrong-site-dosing path RISK-15 guarded is now closed in software, so residual harm from a placement error is a blocked session and rework — re-rating to S2 is *recommended and flagged, pending a change order* under NP-QMS-DC-001 §8.1, not applied; (2) that argument depends on maps being authored honestly, so a required map must not use DONT-CARE at any socket where module type materially changes delivered wavelength (HFE-R-15, enforceable as a build check over `protocols/predefined/`). Requirements HFE-R-01..15 enter NP-HFE-001's formative and summative plans; **no human-factors data exists yet**, and the primary empirical risk — tactile counting on a concave dome rather than a flat page — must be tested on helmet-representative curvature or the result will be falsely reassuring (NP-HFE-002 OI-HFE2-03, NP-HFE-001 OI-HFE-08).
- **★ SYM-1 — the cluster partition is mirror-symmetric about the sagittal midline (principal direction,
  2026-08-04):** every cluster is either self-symmetric (centred on a midline socket) or one of a left/right
  mirror pair. Constrains *which* sockets group together, not the cluster unit; CLUSTER-1 is unchanged.
  **Forced consequence:** a cluster holding a midline socket must equal its own mirror image, so its centre
  is on the midline; only odd-width rows carry one (sockets {2, 13, 29, 46, 62, 74}) and a flower spans only
  rows r−1…r+1, so **six midline clusters are forced**, absorbing exactly 30 sockets. The residual two
  lateral bands of 25 need 6 clusters each by exhaustive search → **18 total, provably minimal**, against 12
  unconstrained. **Cost: 6 clusters and ~$38.** Accepted because the cluster is simultaneously the D-8
  safety-cut domain and the D-7 I2C segment, so an asymmetric partition would make a per-cluster safety cut
  asymmetric across hemispheres and split the inclusive-midline zone-membership rule across two bus
  segments — against which every lateralized protocol in the tree is written, including NP-OPT-PSF-001 §4's
  exactly-50/50 midline model. `docs/np_hex_zm_001.md` §5.4a; NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 §8.2.1.
- **★ CONTIG-1 — a cluster's petals must form a contiguous arc (principal direction, 2026-08-04):** no
  **pendant petal** (a petal whose only in-cluster contact is the centre, with foreign sockets on both
  flanks). Dropping *outer* petals per CLUSTER-1 gives this for free; only a count-minimising search
  violates it, and the first computed 18-cluster partition did — four pendants (sockets 27, 31, 77, 80).
  **The reason is the clamp plate, not the tiles:** tiles are independent modules in independent sockets, so
  the cluster's structural member is the §5.4a plate, and the plate cannot bridge a gap because the gap
  socket belongs to a different cluster actuated independently. It must instead reach on a **40 mm
  cantilever arm** necked to at most **23.09 mm** (one hex edge) following **2.33 mm** of dome — which
  under-seats that plunger, puts a re-entrant notch on the sole load path, and creates a withdrawal snag
  path whose failure modes are breaking the arm or levering a neighbouring module out of its socket. Note
  this **defeats the metric CLUSTER-1 optimised** (plate stress/deflection via longest dimension) without
  breaking its stated rule, since a pendant does not lengthen the cluster. **Resolved at zero cost in
  cluster count** by reassignment — 73→C16, 75→C18, 27→C8, 31→C9 — leaving 18 clusters, mirror-symmetric,
  max size 6, zero pendants. Verified by HT-DRC-21. `docs/np_hex_zm_001.md` §5.4a; NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 §8.2.1.
- **Safety-MCU cranial PBM enable collapsed to one bit — firmware now matches NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 §7.2 (NEW 2026-08-05)** — §7.2 decided at Rev 3 that `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_ZONE_0..4` becomes a single `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` bit; the firmware never followed, so five per-zone bits survived two revisions in both `np_safety_protocol.h` and `np_hub_config.h`. This was doubly wrong: five was the retired slot count, and *zone* is a noun firmware must not model at all — a zone is a human-authored socket set in `protocols/predefined/00-zones.npps`, and under the §4.5.1 discriminator firmware may hold a socket grouping only if changing its membership requires re-tooling hardware. Cluster membership qualifies; zone membership does not (gate REG-1 will re-cut zone boundaries with no hardware change). Because these symbols gate PBM and tES elements, a stale grouping is a **wrong-site stimulation path**, not a data-quality problem. Implemented: bit 0 is `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL`, bits 1–4 are reserved holes **excluded** from `NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK` (`0x3FFF` → `0x3FE1`), so a hub that still sets a retired zone bit has it stripped in `np_spi_watchdog_tick` rather than enabling something else; `NP_EN_PBM_ZONE0..4_PORT/PIN` collapse to `NP_EN_PBM_CRANIAL_PORT/PIN` on PA0. **The word was deliberately NOT compacted.** §7.2 justified the reservation solely by SHDR fault records, and no such records exist yet (principal, 2026-08-04), so that rationale does not currently bind — but a second, previously unstated one does: enable-bit position ≡ `current_ua[]` slot ≡ charge-accumulator index is a three-way Class C identity that the 40 µC/cm² limit rests on. §7.2 has been amended to record it (was OI-HEXTILE-14). **Latent defect found and fixed in passing:** `np_thermal_interlock.c` used the NTC channel index as an enable-bit position (`granted_mask &= ~(1U << ch)`), so after the collapse an over-temp on cranial sense domains 1–4 would have cleared reserved bits and cut **nothing** while reporting a thermal cutoff. Every cranial domain now clears the one cranial bit. Also clears the **PA4 double-assignment** (SPI1 NSS vs `NP_EN_PBM_ZONE4_PIN`, OI-HEXTILE-13 note (c) / FMEA-M08-04). New coverage: `np_thermal_interlock_tests` (the module had none — it builds only into the ARM target), plus `test_enable_word_layout` and `test_enable_word_matches_hub` in `np_safety_spi_proto_tests`, the latter comparing both headers through a test-only mirror TU so the "must match" contract is checked rather than asserted in a comment. **HUB-DRC-C12 closes.** **Explicitly still open:** whether the safety layer can cut one cluster or only the whole cranial lattice — the accepted all-or-nothing consequence — remains **OI-HUB-C07** / **OI-HEXTILE-13**; per-cluster policy bits would need the enable word widened to `uint32_t` (18 cluster + 9 modality = 27 > 16) and were not implemented. Safety-MCU package selection (OI-HEXTILE-13 finding 1) unaffected and untouched.
- **ACC reclassified as a DEEP (indirect-modulation) HD-tDCS target (NEW 2026-08-05)** — FAI HD02-A required all 7 clinical targets within 35 mm of their nearest cap electrode. ACC (0, 28, 28) is 47.1 mm from Fz and had been failing since the suite was written. It is not a mapping bug: ACC sits on the medial wall, its 2nd/3rd nearest electrodes are 49.1 mm, and no 10-10 scalp position gets closer than ~37.9 mm (Fpz) — the criterion is unsatisfiable for ACC by **any** electrode placement. The six other targets are cortical-surface at 11–29 mm. Root cause was a conflation in `np_hd_clinical_target_t` between *sLORETA source-localization* targets (deep sources localize fine) and *4×1 stimulation* targets (§2.3 claims ~1.5 cm FWHM at 10 mm depth). Resolution: targets now carry `NP_HD_TARGET_DEPTH_SURFACE`/`_DEEP` (`np_hd_clinical_target_depth()`); ACC stays selectable and keeps the anxiety indication, but the spec states plainly that a 4×1 there is **indirect network modulation, not focal stimulation of the structure**, and callers must not present it as the latter. HD02-A is now checked in both directions — surface targets must be ≤35 mm, deep targets must be >35 mm — so reclassifying ACC to SURFACE to silence the test fails instead of passing vacuously (verified). **Deliberately NOT resolved by raising the 35 mm threshold**, which would have made the FAI assert focal reach the physics does not support and weakened the criterion for the six targets that pass with real margin. See NP-FW-HD-001 §2.3, §4.3, §12. **Open:** app-side disclosure — the UI must surface the DEEP classification when a clinician selects ACC; the firmware exposes the class but nothing consumes it yet.
- **T2 clinical tACS driver: 21 channels, one per cap electrode (NEW 2026-08-05, supersedes "16-ch arbitrary waveform")** — the 16-channel figure in CLAUDE.md §3 T2 had no sourcing document, no BOM line, and no named silicon; `NP_HD_DRIVER_CHANNELS` was defined and never referenced. The wiring spec that would have pinned it (NP-HW-TCAP-001) does not exist, so `k_driver_channel[]` was a placeholder that wrapped electrodes 16–20 back onto channels 11–15. That wrap aliased geometric *neighbours* (Cz↔Pz, C4↔P4, T8↔P8, P7↔O1, P3↔O2), and since a 4×1 ring draws its cathodes from the electrodes nearest its anode, it guaranteed collisions — C4↔P4 made the M1_R ring undeliverable. 16 was never the binding number: a bilateral 4×1 energises 10 electrodes, so 16 channels were always sufficient; every collision came from the mapping, not the count. The T2 cap already carries 21 conductors (Ag/AgCl electrodes dual-rated for EEG + stimulation, §3 T2), so only the driver was 16-channel, never the harness. Effect: all rings now take their geometrically nearest cathodes — M1_R reverts from the workaround `C4[FC4,F4,Cz,Fz]` to `C4[FC4,F4,Cz,P4]`, and the bilateral M1 mirror rings no longer reach to T8/T7 on temporal bone. **Constraint of record if cost ever reintroduces sharing: never alias two electrodes within one ring radius (~60 mm).** See NP-FW-HD-001 §6.4. **Open:** NP-HW-TCAP-001 still requires authoring to become the controlled hardware source of truth; the firmware table is the interim authority. BOM delta (5 additional precision current sources + sense) not yet costed against the T2 BOM.
- **NP-FW-CVNS-001 §5 reconciled with the Class C safety-MCU firmware — Rev 1 → Rev 2 (2026-08-05):** §5 ("Safety MCU Bare-Metal Cardiac Interlock", BASELINED) contradicted `firmware/safety_mcu/` on every hardware row and on the baseline/monitoring behaviour. **Root cause: §5 documented the safety MCU using the *main processor's* constants and behaviours** — `NP_CVNS_RR_WINDOW_SIZE` (20), `NP_CVNS_BASELINE_BEATS_MIN` (5), the 300–2000 ms R-R validity window, and the "<3 intervals → warning → 10 s soft cutoff" rule all live in `firmware/cervical_vns/include/np_cvns_config.h`, the SW-02 module specified in §6 of the same document. The safety MCU has its own header and its own values (`NP_CARDIAC_BASELINE_BEATS` = 8, `NP_RR_BUF_SIZE` = 8, no validity filter, no soft-cutoff timer). The two sides are *meant* to be independent implementations — they cross-validate at ±5 BPM (`NP_CVNS_BASELINE_CROSSVAL_BPM`) — so attributing one side's constants to the other is an error independent of which values are correct. **Corrected in Rev 2:** §5.3 now names the safety MCU's own constants with a table and a stated independence rationale; §5.3 step 2 and §5.4.3 are marked as main-processor behaviour not implemented on the MCU; §5.4.2a no longer instructs asserting `CVNS_ENABLE_L` **and** `CVNS_ENABLE_R` (the firmware has one `NP_EN_CVNS` line, so the Rev 1 instruction was unimplementable) and instead describes the single-line cutoff, the signed `int16_t` delta comparison (FMEA-M05-02), and the strict `>` threshold; §5.1's pin map is retained verbatim but marked **UNVERIFIED — do not build to this table**, since no schematic exists in-tree to settle it. **Deliberately NOT resolved — four new open items, each with two candidate resolutions, the evidence for each, and a stated settlement condition:** OI-CVNS-08 (STM32G071 pin map — every row disagrees; settled only by the G1 layout package), OI-CVNS-09 (single vs dual CVNS enable — a clinical/regulatory question against the gammaCore predicate K163334/K173323, not a code-style one), OI-CVNS-10 (baseline window 8 on the MCU vs 5 on the main processor — **explicitly do not harmonise by editing one number**), OI-CVNS-11 (the MCU applies no R-R validity filter — intended upstream filtering, or a gap?). **No number in either the doc or the firmware was changed to make the other side agree, and no firmware behaviour changed.** **Test gap closed:** `firmware/safety_mcu/tests/np_cardiac_interlock_tests.c` (`np_cardiac_interlock_tests`, 35 assertions) is the first host coverage of the Class C cardiac interlock — the unit that actually owns the enable GPIO. The suite registered as "NP-FAI-CVNS-001: cardiac interlock FAI suite" tests the main-processor module on the other side of the SPI boundary. New suite pins current behaviour and was falsified against four deliberately-broken variants before acceptance (unsigned delta → 2 failures, 5-beat baseline → 2, truncating `rr_to_bpm` → 1, no prime-beat guard → 3). `ctest` 23/24 → **24/24**, existing 23 unchanged. **Evidence noted for OI-CVNS-08:** `NP_EN_PBM_ZONE0..4` (the code side of the doc's SPI-on-PA1–PA4 conflict) is already stale — a zone is a named socket set authored in `protocols/predefined/00-zones.npps`, and NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 §7.2 has already decided bits 0–4 collapse to a single `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` (OI-HUB-C07), freeing PA1–PA4; that conflict may dissolve without anyone resolving it. Per development-mode posture (no hardware, no bench data, no UHDR records) **no QMS/CAPA record was opened** — this entry is document control for a BASELINED spec, not a defect filing. See `docs/np_fw_cvns_001.md` Rev 2.
- **CORRECTION to the NP-FW-CVNS-001 Rev 2 entry above — OI-CVNS-08's evidence, after NP-HW-HUB-001 §7.2 landed (2026-08-05):** The Rev 2 entry above closes with "Evidence noted for OI-CVNS-08", written before the cranial-enable collapse merged. That paragraph stands as written and is **superseded by this one**; four of its claims are no longer true, and every one of them had overstated the doc-vs-code conflict. (1) "`NP_EN_PBM_ZONE0..4` is the code side of the SPI-on-PA1–PA4 conflict" — those macros are retired; **PA1–PA3 are released**. (2) The pin map was described as a *three-way* disagreement because `np_safety_config.h`'s SPI1 comment claimed PA4 for NSS while `NP_EN_PBM_ZONE4_PIN` also claimed it — **§7.2 resolved that double-assignment; PA4 is now SPI1 NSS only** (OI-HEXTILE-13 note (c) / FMEA-M08-04). (3) "That decision has not yet propagated into `np_safety_config.h`" — **it has**. (4) "Resolve after OI-HUB-C07 lands" — **discharged**; OI-CVNS-08 is now blocked on the G1 layout package alone. **Net effect runs opposite to the original framing:** with PA1–PA3 free and PA4 unambiguous, the whole PA-bank half of NP-FW-CVNS-001 Rev 1's §5.1 map (SPI1 on PA1–PA4, `CVNS_ENABLE_L`/`_R` on PA5/PA6, `HEARTBEAT_WATCHDOG` on PA7) is internally coherent as a board, which it was not when Rev 2 was written — so "one of the two documents a board that cannot exist" is too strong. **`RPEAK_IN` on PB0 remains the one row that cannot work in either reading**, PB0 still being `NP_EN_BES_PIN` — a cardiac input on a stimulation output. §14.1 of NP-FW-CVNS-001 Rev 2 has been updated in place (the doc is a specification, not a log); candidate A's "evidence against" is now the header's own self-declaration that GPIO bank assignments are "provisional pending PCB layout (G1 gate)", so "the code is right" is an assumption rather than a finding. Anyone re-opening OI-CVNS-08 should not resurrect "SPI1 collides with the PBM zone enables" — that collision is gone. No firmware, test, or open-item *status* changed; evidence only.
- **`docs/status/completed-decisions.md` is append-only — convention recorded in the file header (2026-08-05):** Established after the correction above was first written as an in-place edit of the Rev 2 entry, which silently destroyed the record of what had been believed when Rev 2 was authored. The header now states the rule: never edit or delete an existing entry; when a later event invalidates one, append a new entry naming the entry it corrects. The log's value is the trail of how understanding changed, so a superseded entry is evidence, not clutter. Note this differs from the specification documents (`docs/np_*.md`), which ARE edited in place and carry their revision history in a Rev header — the two file types have opposite update rules on purpose.
- **NP-DRV-SHELL-002 Rev 1 → Rev 2 — five positions replaced by peer decisions (2026-08-11, DRAFT):** Rev 1 (2026-07-29) had one commit in the window that took `NP-HW-HUB-001` to Rev 3 and `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` to Rev 2, and other documents had begun inheriting its stale model. **This entry corrects, but does not replace, the "Shell socket interconnect architecture — cluster carriers (Rev A)" entry above; that entry stands exactly as written.** Five changes: **(1) The cluster carrier gains a local MCU** — principal direction 2026-08-11 that NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 §3.1/§8.3 prevails, so the board becomes a *cluster controller*: STM32G071 UFQFPN32, PCA9548A, 16:1 PD current mux, one shared switched-gain TIA (DG2788A), 8:1 NTC mux + ADC, cluster power gate. **HUB-001 §3.2's stated justification was deliberately NOT carried forward** — it argues the tier needs intelligence because "T1-A base tiles carry no on-module driver; their LED current comes from the hub", but HEXTILE **D-3** (2026-08-04) fitted an on-module driver to *every* tile type and marked it irreversible, so no LED drive current crosses the socket at all; OI-HUB-C15 already directs the removal. The **surviving** justification is recorded instead: the controller retains a switched-gain TIA, a 16:1 PD mux, an 8:1 NTC mux and an ADC, and scanning/sequencing that set — per-sample channel and gain selection, settling, conversion, per-socket dose accumulation, phase-aligned to the emitter ON-window and scheduled around the ADS1299 bus-quiet window — is an MCU's job; driving those selects from the hub over I2C would reinstate the per-socket hub-side signal count the architecture exists to delete. **The coupling this creates is stated rather than left to be discovered: keeping the AFE on the carrier effectively resolves OI-HUB-C17c against HEXTILE D-4**, since Rev 1's passive carrier and HUB-001 §7.5.3 (which inherited it verbatim) were both conditional on D-4 moving TIA + ADC on-module. It is the **conservative** side of C17c's own stated worry — ADC drift 25 → 62 °C against the ±15 % dose claim, FAI-SM-06, which a cooler carrier-mounted ADC never faces — and the cost is stated too: N3 survives, three spring contacts sit in the photocurrent path, and D-4's signal-integrity argument is outweighed rather than refuted. **C17c's other half — continuous on-tile dissipation inside the 42 °C face / 62 °C junction envelope — is untouched and remains open.** **(2) N1 bus rail 12 V → 24 V, closing OI-SHELL2-01.** OI-HUB-C17b ADOPTED HEXTILE **D-6**; OI-HUB-C18 requested exactly this propagation. Vault bus current ~2.9 A → **~1.46 A**, I²R quartered, N1 conductor sizing and the cluster power-gate part class re-rated. The 2-contact `VLED+` budget is re-checked: HUB-001 §7.5.5 shows it had **zero** derating at 12 V and ~2× at 24 V, i.e. Rev 1's contact count and its rail assumption were mutually inconsistent, and adopting 24 V is what repairs it. 20 V PD-native was considered and rejected (Mode 3 runs from any PD source, Standard T1 from 15 V, so the rail must be regulated independently of PD — a conversion stage returns regardless). Boost siting is OI-HUB-C19 (Hub PCB, provisional 2026-07-30). Second-order benefit neither peer document states: at equal delivered power the §9.3 self-field figures **halve** (broadside pair ~37 → ~19 nT). New finding in passing — the worst-case **per-cluster feed is the entire vault budget (~1.46 A), not 1/18 of it**, because HEXTILE §9.2 caps concurrency at ~6 tiles vault-wide and nothing stops a protocol placing all six in one cluster. **(3) Cluster count 12 → 18; the §7.1 connector shortfall was real.** Under CLUSTER-1 (7-hex flower) + SYM-1 (mirror-symmetric, 2026-08-04) + CONTIG-1 (contiguous petal arc) the count is 18 and provably minimal — six forced midline clusters plus six mirror pairs (HEXTILE §8.2.1; diagram `docs/diagrams/np_hextile_cluster_map.svg`). Rev 1's 12 was `ceil(80/7)` from CLUSTER-1 alone and does not satisfy SYM-1. **18 does not fit 16 positions, and Rev 1's `8 branches × ≤2 clusters = 16` I2C tree cannot reach 18 either** — this is OI-HEXTILE-14. Adopted its §8.2.2 recommendation: **provision 20 connector positions** (adding tails is the cheap axis — §7.3 notes an extra *conductor* costs 16, now 18–20, hub pins, so widening a tail is expensive while adding tails is not) and **adopt D-7's 32-segment tree** (4 × LPI2C × PCA9548A, 18 of 32 used, 14 spare). Tails 12 → 18, interface pins 144 → 216. Rev 1's `8 × 2 × 8 = 128 = NP_HEXMAP_MAX_SOCKETS` coincidence is lost and was never load-bearing — the firmware ceiling stands on NP-HEX-ZM-001 §3.4's full-7-bit-domain justification. §8.2.2 **option 4 recorded as conditional, not decided**: if the single Class C `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` enable is accepted, `SAFE_EN_n` leaves the tail (12 → 11 conductors), clusters tap a multi-drop trunk instead of each running a star leg, connector count becomes largely insensitive to a REG-1 re-cut, and safety-MCU I/O demand drops ~38–40 → ~23 — **gated on OI-HUB-C07**; until it closes, Rev 3 builds against 20 positions and 12-conductor tails. Rev 1's claim that the STM32G071 has "ample GPIO for 12 enables" is **withdrawn**: the safety-MCU package is unspecified anywhere in the tree and demand at 18 enables is ~38–40 I/O. **(4) Bezel is 1.0 mm** where it binds (principal direction 2026-08-11) — NP-THERM-BEZEL-001 §4.5's calculated value; the competing 2.5 mm was never derived and appears in NP-HEX-ZM-001 §3.1 only as a table column header. No figure in SHELL-002 depends on it; recorded because peers split on it. **(5) New unowned thermal input — OI-SHELL2-11.** Eighteen *active* controller boards dissipate **continuously** (emitters are duty-cycled; this is not) into the inter-bowl gap, which NP-THERM-CFD-C2-001 §7 characterises as **stagnant air at 0.231 m²K/W — ~59 % of the entire outward resistance path** (outward ≈ 0.393 vs inward ≈ 0.108 m²K/W), the analysis that already concluded the outward path is ~4× more resistive than the inward path to the perfused scalp and forced Path B1. Heat added gap-side therefore sits *behind* the dominant outward resistance, and Rev 1's "components out of the scalp thermal path" is **not established**. Two inputs do not exist: a budgeted per-controller dissipation, and a CFD case with the source on the gap-facing side of L1 rather than at the LED junction plane. **This is the load that moved sides when C17c resolved against D-4, and the two must be assessed as one budget.** **Socket contact count deliberately left OPEN, with changed terms.** It is *not* resolved by the MCU decision, but **HUB-001 §7.5.2's 14–15 synthesis figures are void** — they follow from one premise, "deleting N3 removes `PD1_K`, `PD2_K`, `NTC` outright", and N3 now survives. Reconciling the surviving networks against HEXTILE **§7.1–7.2 (D-5, 16-position pogo, 2.00 mm pitch)** signal by signal gives **17**: `SEAT_N` **adopted from D-5** (a partially-seated tile can answer I2C while `PD1_K` sits at elevated contact resistance, producing a silent *under-read* that firmware compensates by driving harder — a dose-integrity failure against a stated claim, and stronger now that N3 survives); `SYNC` **retained** (REQ-EMI-03 needs a deterministic phase reference that I2C broadcast cannot give, and OI-HUB-C05 independently records the same hole); `DGND` **retained**, not for ground bounce (~52 mV against a 0.99 V threshold) but because REQ-EMI-07 requires `PGND` to be LED return *only* for the §9.3 broadside pair to cancel; the 2 reserved contacts **dropped** (the "grow-to-4" extensibility they were held for is already served by HEXTILE §1's union rule, and D-3 means a fourth wavelength adds no contact). The **one open sub-question is `VLED+` redundancy** — 2 (0.52 A nominal, **1.04 A on loss of one contact = exactly the rating, zero margin**, into the fretting→resistance→heating→fretting runaway SH2-DRC-09 exists to bound) vs 4 (0.26 A / 0.35 A, ~3×, but eight contacts on power alone against a live RISK-22 one-handed-force budget) vs **3, recommended** (0.35 A / 0.52 A, ~2× under single-contact loss, at half the contact cost of four) → 17 or 19 total. Left open under OI-SHELL2-03 because it trades bench-measurable contact degradation (new **SH2-DRC-10a**) against a human-factors force budget (MECH-2 + the §5.4a HFE formative) and cannot be closed from electrical reasoning alone. Two consequences recorded: the force target must be **restated against a 6-tile plate** (no cluster reaches 7 under the 18-cluster partition, which *lowers* worst-case plate load ~14 % — the SYM-1 correction that raised the cluster count reduced the plate load), and above ~17 contacts the pad array should go to **two staggered rows**, because a 34–38 mm single row at 2.00 mm pitch tapers into the hex corners exactly where §7.1's ±0.4 mm blind-mate tolerance over a doubly-curved cluster is hardest to hold. Also deleted: the module-side UID EEPROM BOM line (~$8.50), redundant under D-3. **BOM restated $125–216 → $175–225** (~$2.20–2.80/socket): the count rose 50 % and the board gained a $1.50 MCU, so Rev 1 understated by ~40 % and its "$1.30–2.40 per socket" has been quoted downstream. DRC 28 → 32 items; open items 10 → 11 (OI-SHELL2-01 closed, 02/03/04/05 re-scoped, 11 new). **Status remains DRAFT** — REG-1 and ACT-1 are still open and an interconnect cannot be more certain than its lattice.
- **CORRECTION to the NP-DRV-SHELL-002 Rev 2 entry above — socket contact count CLOSED at 19; `VLED+`/`PGND` = 3+3 (principal decision, 2026-08-11):** The Rev 2 entry above records the socket contact count as *"deliberately left OPEN"* at 17, with `VLED+` redundancy (2 vs 3 vs 4) routed to OI-SHELL2-03 and a new SH2-DRC-10a bench, on the grounds that it trades a bench-measurable contact-degradation distribution against a human-factors force budget and could not be closed from electrical reasoning alone. **The principal closed it the same day, adopting the recommended 3.** That paragraph stays as written; this entry records what changed and why. **Decision: 3 `VLED+` + 3 `PGND` → a 19-contact socket.** Rev 1's 18, `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` D-5's 16, and `NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.5.2's 14–15 (already void — they assumed D-4 deletes N3, which survives) are all superseded. **The rule, recorded so it survives future trimming and so 3 is not carried forward as a bare constant:** *`VLED+` is sized so that the loss of any one contact still leaves ≥2× derating against the contact current rating.* At 24 V and 1.04 A/tile that is 3 — 2 leaves the survivor at **1.04 A, exactly the ≥1.0 A rating with zero margin**, into the fretting → resistance → local I²R heating → more fretting runaway that SH2-DRC-09 exists to bound, and a marginal contact is not a clean open; 4 buys ~3× but spends eight contacts on power alone against a live RISK-22 one-handed-force constraint. **The asymmetry is the argument:** the failure mode at 2 is silent, thermal and self-accelerating, while the accessibility cost of 3 is small, measurable, and recoverable through the §5.4a over-centre actuator's mechanical advantage. If the rail, tile peak power or contact rating changes, re-derive from the rule. **Three consequences.** (1) **REQ-SKT-01 is new and binding, not advisory** — a 19-contact single row spans 38 mm at D-5's 2.00 mm pitch, which fits a 40 mm hex only along the long diagonal and tapers into the corners exactly where the ±0.4 mm lateral blind-mate tolerance over a doubly-curved cluster is hardest to hold; the array becomes **two staggered rows** (nominally 9 + 10, ~18 mm span), which also gives the mis-key asymmetry somewhere to live and keeps `SEAT_N` at a genuine mechanical extreme so the last-to-mate sequencing is real. Verified by new **SH2-DRC-05a**. (2) **Clamp-plate load falls despite two more contacts.** 19 contacts on a 6-tile plate is **34.2–57.0 N**, *below* Rev 1's 18 contacts on a 7-tile plate (37.8–63.0 N) — because no cluster reaches 7 tiles under the 18-cluster partition (sizes 3–6, `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.2.1). The SYM-1 correction that *raised* the cluster count more than paid for the contact increase. OI-SHELL2-03's count half is therefore **closed**; what remains is purely the human-factors question — whether 34–57 N is one-handed-achievable at Parkinson's H&Y II–III — and its original target ("18 → 12 cuts plate load by a third") was written against a 7-tile plate and must be restated. (3) **SH2-DRC-10a is re-pointed, not cancelled** — it no longer selects between 2/3/4 but verifies that 3 delivers the asserted ≥2× degraded-case margin on real contacts (pass: survivor ≤0.5 A, no thermal runaway over cycle life). **`NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.1–7.2 must be co-revised** — D-5 fixes a *16-position* interface and its pin table, and neither survives; the 2.00 mm pitch, spring-on-socket choice, ≥0.8 µm hard-gold plating, ≤50 mΩ / ≥500-cycle specs and §7.3 mating sequence all carry over unchanged. Raised as **OI-SHELL2-09(i)** and flagged as **blocking socket tooling**, since it is a tooled interface rather than a paper inconsistency. BOM: socket spring-contact array 17 → 19 contacts, $28–56 → $32–64; headset total unchanged at **$175–225**. DRC 32 → 33 items.
- **NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 2 → Rev 3 — socket interface re-cut 16 → 19 positions (2026-08-11):** Closes `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` Rev 2 **OI-SHELL2-09(i)**, which flagged D-5's 16-position interface as **blocking socket tooling** rather than a paper inconsistency — it is a tooled part. **Two independent causes, deliberately not conflated.** *Cause 1 — a decision reversal elsewhere:* `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.3a resolved **OI-HUB-C17c against D-4** (principal direction, 2026-08-11), keeping the switched-gain TIA, PD mux, NTC mux and ADC on the cluster controller instead of moving them on-module, so **network N3 survives** and `PD1_K`, `PD2_K`, `NTC` return to the socket (+3). *Cause 2 — requirements this document had not accounted for:* `SYNC` (+1), because REQ-EMI-03 requires a deterministic phase reference phase-locked to the EEG/fluxgate sample frame and an I2C broadcast carries arbitration and segment-switch jitter — this also gives **OI-HUB-C05**'s "T1-C PWM phase sync routing not yet defined" a home; and `DGND` (+1), because REQ-EMI-07 requires `PGND` to be **LED return only** for the §9.3 broadside supply/return pair to cancel (the ground-bounce argument, ~52 mV against a 0.99 V I2C threshold, is real but is *not* the reason). Against those five, **`VLED`/`PGND` drops 4+4 → 3+3** under the rule *loss of any one contact must still leave ≥2× derating* — net 16 → 19. **D-4's rationale is explicitly NOT withdrawn.** §5.3's TIA-saturation physics is unchanged and its case for on-module conversion — deleting the ~80× DG2788A NRE, design-time-fixed gain, removing the longest high-impedance path — was **outweighed, not refuted**, on the conservative ground that a controller-mounted ADC never faces the 25 → 62 °C drift question a tile-mounted one must answer against the ±15 % dose claim (FAI-SM-06). **The cost is carried openly rather than hidden:** three spring contacts now sit in the photocurrent path, so contact-resistance drift and fretting become dose-metering error terms, and §7.1's ≤50 mΩ / ≥500-cycle spec is binding on pins 13–15 as well as on the electrode pin. The `SEAT_N` argument strengthens correspondingly — with N3 restored, a partially-seated tile answering I2C while `PD1_K` sits at elevated resistance returns an *under-read* photocurrent that firmware compensates for by driving harder, a silent dose-integrity failure. **Pad layout: two staggered rows (nominally 9 + 10, ~18 mm span), forced not preferred** — 19 positions at D-5's 2.00 mm pitch span 38 mm in a single row, which fits a 40 mm flat-to-flat hex only along the 46.19 mm vertex diagonal and tapers into the corners exactly where the ±0.4 mm lateral blind-mate tolerance over a doubly-curved cluster is hardest to hold; two rows sit inside the 20.0 mm inradius. This is `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` **REQ-SKT-01**. A useful side effect: the 9-vs-10 row-length difference *is* the mis-key asymmetry §7.1 requires, which a symmetric single row needed a deliberate feature to carry. §7.3 mating sequence updated — `DGND` promoted into group 1 so **every return leads**, N3 sense lines added to group 3, and a new two-row constraint recorded (the length stagger must be consistent *between* rows, or one row can seat fully while the other is partial — precisely the state `SEAT_N` exists to detect). §8.1 rail table recomputed at ×3: 0.35 A/contact nominal (~3×), 0.52 A on single-contact loss (~2×); the 5 V option is now excluded twice over, since it exceeds the contact *rating* at 3 contacts rather than merely exceeding comfortable derating at 4. **§7.2 now agrees pin for pin with `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1.4** — new **HT-DRC-23** exists so that stays true across either document's next revision, and **HT-DRC-22** verifies the two-row array. HT-DRC-08 re-scoped to check the degraded case, not just nominal. **Two new open items, and the first is the honest residual of this change: OI-HEXTILE-15** — §5.3, §6.2's U2 dual-TIA line and §6.4's BOM still read as though D-4 holds, and were deliberately not rewritten, because that change reaches the driver topology, the per-tile BOM and OI-HEXTILE-06's PD-population options; affected are U2 (~$26 at 80 tiles, may return to the controller) and U1's 12-bit-ADC-with-PGA requirement (may relax, possibly reopening the ATtiny402-vs-tinyAVR-2-series choice), while the InGaAs PD selection and co-location (D-2) are **unaffected**, being optical decisions independent of where the transimpedance stage sits — so OI-HEXTILE-06's ~$10/tile PD question remains the dominant and still-orthogonal term. **Not tooling-blocking**: the socket interface is already correct. **OI-HEXTILE-16** — **two** conductors carry four names between the documents: pin 18 is `ELEC_SHLD` here and `GUARD` there, and pin 12 is `/ALERT` here and `ALERT#` there. Both are the same conductor under two spellings; harmless in prose and not harmless in a netlist, a test fixture or a firmware pin table, where a silent duplicate net or an unconnected pin is the failure mode. Pick one of each before Hub PCB Rev C release. **Both were found by diffing the two pin tables mechanically rather than by reading them** — the naming mismatch is exactly the class of defect that reads as agreement, which is why HT-DRC-23 should be executed the same way. **Nothing in §4 (emitter lattice), §8.2 (I2C fan-out) or §9 (concurrency ceiling) is affected**, and no firmware change is requested by this revision.
- **Signal naming settled — `ELEC_SHLD` and `ALERT#` adopted; OI-HEXTILE-16 CLOSED (2026-08-11):** Two socket conductors carried two names each across `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1, `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.2 and `NP-HW-HUB-001`. Both settled; **neither choice has technical content**, and both were decided on a stated failure mechanism rather than on preference. **(1) The DRL-driven shield for `ELEC` is `ELEC_SHLD`, not `GUARD`.** `GUARD` collides with a *different conductor these same documents already name* — §3.5, §4.1 and REQ-EMI-02 specify a **DRL-driven guard plane** on L1's scalp-facing face, under which the N4 shared electrode lanes run. The socket pin is driven *from* that plane but is not it; they are different nets with different extents, and naming the pin `GUARD` makes "the guard" ambiguous in the one document that specifies both. `ELEC_SHLD` also names what it shields and pairs typographically with `ELEC`. **(2) The open-drain module fault line is `ALERT#`, not `/ALERT`.** A leading `/` is the **hierarchical-path separator in EDA net naming** (KiCad, Altium): a net literally named `/ALERT` reads as a root-scope hierarchical path and can be silently re-scoped or duplicated on netlist import. That is a mechanism, not a style preference. `ALERT#` was also already the majority usage across the document set (~9 occurrences vs ~4). **Why this needed deciding at all:** a conductor with two names is harmless in prose and is not harmless in a netlist, a test fixture, or a firmware pin table, where the failure mode is a silent duplicate net or an unconnected pin — **a defect that reads as agreement**. Both collisions were found by diffing the two pin tables mechanically rather than by reading them, which is why the verification items were re-specified as diffs rather than reviews: new **SH2-DRC-05b** and amended **HT-DRC-23** both require a mechanical name-for-name comparison. **No firmware symbols existed for either name**, so nothing had to be deferred to and no code changes: `grep` over `firmware/`, `app/`, `scripts/` and `hardware/` found both names only in `docs/`. Propagated to `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.2/§7.3/§7.4, `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1.2/§5.1.4/§5.1.7, and `NP-HW-HUB-001` §125/§7.5.2; historical references retained where a revision is being described, so the rename is traceable rather than silent. **Two residuals recorded, not fixed — OI-HEXTILE-17:** (a) **`SEAT_N` is a third active-low convention** — the 19-position interface now carries `ALERT#` (`#` suffix) beside `SEAT_N` (`_N` suffix), each unambiguous alone but two conventions on one connector; (b) **`NP-HW-HUB-001` §113 calls pin 17 `ELEC_SIG`** where both other documents say `ELEC`. Neither carries the netlist hazard that decided the two above, and renaming them was outside the scope of this decision — **to be settled before schematic capture, which is the point at which a name becomes a net.**
- **OI-HEXTILE-17 CLOSED — `SEAT#` and `ELEC` adopted; active-low convention fixed as `#` (2026-08-11):** The two residuals left by the OI-HEXTILE-16 entry above were recorded there as cosmetic. **The first is not, and checking the codebase rather than reasoning from style is what showed it.** **(a) `SEAT_N` → `SEAT#`.** The item was framed as "a third active-low convention" sitting beside `ALERT#`. In fact **`_N` already means *cardinality* in this codebase**: `firmware/hub_control/tests/np_module_map_tests.c` defines `PBM_TILE_N` and `EEG_TILE_N` as `sizeof(x)/sizeof(x[0])`, so `SEAT_N` reads as *"number of seats"* to anyone who has read the module map. That is a live ambiguity of exactly the kind that decided OI-HEXTILE-16 (`GUARD` colliding with the DRL guard *plane*), not a matter of taste. **This settles the convention toward `#` rather than away from it, so `ALERT#` is confirmed and not reversed** — which is the opposite of where the evidence looked to be heading before the firmware was checked. Supporting precedent: this is an SMBus-style alert line on an I2C bus and the SMBus specification names it `SMBALERT#`; the in-tree vendor header already exposes `I2C_ISR_ALERT` for the same signal. **Rule of record: active-low signals at the socket and cluster-tail interfaces take a `#` suffix — never `_N` (cardinality), never a leading `/` (EDA hierarchical-path separator).** The interface now carries exactly two active-low conductors, `ALERT#` and `SEAT#`, under one convention. **(b) `ELEC_SIG` → `ELEC`**, on two grounds. *Ownership:* the socket interface is defined by `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.2 and `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1.4, and `NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.4 explicitly **adopts** SHELL-002's contract — so HUB-001 is the consumer, and two normative pin tables do not change to match one banner's prose. *Accuracy:* the pin is dual-rated to carry **tES stimulation current** (≤2 mA T1 / ≤4 mA T2), not only a recording signal, and `_SIG` biases the reader toward the recording role — which is the half that is **not** safety-relevant. `ELEC` / `ELEC_SHLD` is asymmetric and costs nothing. **Doc→firmware mapping stated rather than left to whoever writes the driver, since `#` is not a legal identifier character: `<SIGNAL>#` maps to `<SIGNAL>_L`, never to `<SIGNAL>_N`.** **Related, and explicitly NOT closed by this:** `NP-FMEA-001` **OI-FMEA-01** records that the firmware's ten stimulation enable lines are all active-LOW and none carries polarity in its name — `NP_EN_PBM_CRANIAL_PIN`, `NP_EN_BES_PIN`, `NP_EN_TDCS_PIN` and the rest encode it only in header comments (`np_safety_config.h:7`, `np_gpio_mgr.c:5`), which that item calls *"unstated — and on an active-LOW enable line that is a safety question."* This decision does not fix that and does not claim to; it avoids adding two more unmarked active-low names to the set OI-FMEA-01 is already complaining about, and gives that item a convention to converge on if it is ever closed. **No firmware change was made or requested.** Propagated across all three specs (29 `SEAT_N` sites, 2 `ELEC_SIG` sites); pin tables re-verified identical name-for-name by mechanical diff, with the check falsified by reverting a name. **No naming residual remains in this document set.**
- **Naming conventions consolidated into NP-CONV-001; active-low `#` made programme-wide (2026-08-11):** Principal direction: *all* active-low signals terminate with `#`, and conventions should be written down wherever we can have them. **New document `docs/np_conv_001.md` (NP-CONV-001 Rev 1, ACTIVE)** — the first consolidated statement; rules previously lived inline in `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.4 and `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1.7, which now point at it. Discoverable from three surfaces: `docs/status/document-register.md`, a cross-link at the head of `docs/ABBREVIATIONS.md` (that file defines *what terms mean*; NP-CONV-001 defines *how names are formed*), and a row in `CLAUDE.md`'s Document Map. **Applying the rule beyond the socket found two signals that had been missed:** `NP-HW-HUB-001`'s tier-0 shared service-request line, open-drain and pulled LOW by any controller → **`ATTN#`**; and the Class C cranial enable in its §3.1 block diagram → **`PBM_CRANIAL_EN#`**, since `np_safety_config.h:7-8` puts it among the ten active-LOW enable GPIOs. **`SAFE_EN_n` → `SAFE_EN[n]`** (26 sites): the trailing lowercase `_n` was an *index*, not a polarity marker, and is visually indistinguishable from one in a plain-text diff — indices now take bracket notation (§1.3). **§1.2 is the load-bearing part of the new document:** every short suffix is already taken, each rejected on evidence rather than taste — `_N` = cardinality (`PBM_TILE_N`, `EEG_TILE_N` are `sizeof(x)/sizeof(x[0])`), `_L`/`_R` = Left/Right (`CVNS_ENABLE_L` in `NP-FW-CVNS-001` §5.1), `_B` = channel B (`CH_B`, `LED_B`, `NP_BANK_B`), `_LOW` = a threshold (`NP_TIA_GAIN_LOW`), leading `/` = EDA hierarchical-path separator. **CORRECTION to the OI-HEXTILE-17 entry above:** that entry specified `<SIGNAL>#` → `<SIGNAL>_L` as the doc→firmware mapping. **That was wrong — `_L` already means Left.** The mapping is now **`<SIGNAL>_ACTIVE_LOW`**, chosen because every short candidate is taken and because verbosity is a feature for a safety-relevant polarity marker on a device where OI-FMEA-01 already records unstated polarity as a hazard. **Class C boundary held explicitly (§3): no firmware identifier is renamed, and none should be to satisfy this without a change order.** The safety MCU's ten active-LOW enable lines still carry polarity only in header comments; that is Class C code owned by `NP-FMEA-001` **OI-FMEA-01**, and §2 exists only so *new* names converge and OI-FMEA-01 has a convention to adopt. NP-CONV-001 also records, for the first time in one place, the document-ID and new-number-vs-new-revision rule, the no-space `§N` marking, the identifier families (OI/DRC/REQ/D/gates) and their **append-only, never-renumber** rule, the revise-a-locked-position pattern (state what was replaced and why; an outweighed rationale is retained, not deleted), and **verification of cross-document interfaces by mechanical diff rather than review** — because all three of the naming collisions that prompted this survived multiple human readings. Also recorded: **historical records keep the name they were written with** (revision-history rows, `np_dhf_001.md`, this file) — rename forward, never backward, or the evidence of how a name changed is destroyed. **⚠ NEW OPEN ITEM — OI-CONV-01, safety-relevant, raised and NOT resolved.** Making the name carry the polarity exposed an inversion: `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §6 specifies `SAFE_EN[n]` as active-**HIGH** (LOW removes the rail; SH2-DRC-13's "defaults LOW at reset" is therefore the *safe* state), while the safety MCU specifies the opposite for its enable lines — *"Active-LOW open-drain: LOW = stimulation enabled; HIGH = disabled"* (`np_safety_config.h:7-8`, `np_safety_main.c:14`). Both schemes are internally fail-safe on their own terms; together they are inverted, and **implementing `SAFE_EN[n]` to the firmware's house convention would turn SH2-DRC-13's "default LOW at reset" from the safe state into stimulation enabled at power-on reset.** The inversion is now visible in `NP-HW-HUB-001` §3.1's own block diagram, where an active-low `PBM_CRANIAL_EN#` feeds a tier whose per-cluster gate is declared active-high. This is a safety-architecture question, not a naming one; resolving it may require renaming to `SAFE_EN#[n]`. Assess with OI-FMEA-01 and OI-HUB-C07. **OI-CONV-02** tracks sweeping the modality, firmware and app specs, which have not been audited. Verified by a falsifiable probe over the four interface documents: zero bare occurrences of any declared active-low signal, and the probe confirmed to bite when a name is reverted.
- **Master Design Brief re-issued as NP-DB-005 Rev 6 — DRAFT pending approval (2026-08-11):** `docs/np_db_005.docx` re-authored from Rev 5 (2026-05-16). **The brief was rewritten rather than amended because the delta is dominated by retirement, not addition** — Rev 5 asserted as locked design the fixed 5-slot/600-LED/66×78 mm zone-module architecture, ZONE_ID resistor-ladder detection, per-slot LPI2C3 addressing, Hub PCB Rev B's five DG2788A per-slot TIA gain switches and `GAIN_SEL[0..4]`, RISK-15 keying layers 2–5, RISK-17 multi-FPC bundle management including the ≥15 mm FPC-to-EEG separation, the "~500 lines bare-metal" safety-MCU figure, `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_ZONE_0..4`, the 16-channel T2 tACS driver, the 25-risk NP-RISK-001 register, the ≥150 N/m PDMS peel figure, and ACC as a focal 4×1 target. Rev 6 therefore **leads with §0.1, a retirement ledger** pairing each retired mechanism with its replacement and the reason, ahead of any current-state section — a reader who stops after one page still knows not to build to Rev 5. **Issued as DRAFT — PENDING APPROVAL with an unsigned approval block:** 21 CFR §820.40(a) requires review and approval prior to issue, and none has happened, so Rev 5 stays the last approved revision. Marking it issued would have asserted an approval that did not occur; marking Rev 5 superseded in the same stroke would have left the program with no approved brief at all. **The one thing not softened:** Rev 5's retired areas are not a valid design input regardless of approval state — being last-approved does not make retired design correct, and §0.1 is the ledger that says which areas those are. **Identity settled under NP-CONV-001 Rev 6, which Rev 5 predates.** The previously-open question — whether the brief is `NP-DB-001 Rev 6` (its own footer convention) or `NP-DB-006` (the DHF's per-revision serial convention) — resolves to **neither**: it is **NP-DB-005 Rev 6**, a new *revision* of the live serial, because it inherits Rev 5's decisions rather than replacing an architecture (§4, *new number vs new revision*). It is written **in place** at `docs/np_db_005.docx` per §4.0 (the filename is the serial and nothing else) and §4.3 (no revision information in filenames), with Rev 5 retained by version control — **a superseded *revision* of a live serial is not a second file, unlike a superseded *document*, which moves to `docs/superseded/`.** There is no NP-DB-006 and none should be created for a future revision. OI-CONV-06's going-forward-not-retroactively disposition of NP-DB-001…005 is unaffected and not reopened. **References swept against the merged conventions:** every document revision converted from letters to integers **against live front matter rather than by applying the A=1…AA=27 map to stale labels** — which matters, because several documents advanced during the rebase and a positional map would have recorded them one or more revisions behind (NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 is Rev 3, not the Rev 2 that "B" maps to; NP-DHF-001 is Rev 28, not 27). PCB and schema revisions correctly left as letters per §4.2 (`Hub PCB Rev B`/`Rev C` retained, and the document-versus-board distinction is now stated explicitly where both appear in one sentence). `NP-COND-LINKS-001` → `NP-COND-LINK-001`. Retired documents repointed to `docs/superseded/`. **Risk register restated:** NP-RISK-001 is retired and split by artifact into NP-RISK-002/-003/-004, the RISK-NN sequence is closed at 26 and append-only with no number ever reused, new hazards take artifact-prefixed IDs (RISK-HEX/SHELL/HUB-nn), and the open list is corrected to **RISK-03, RISK-16, RISK-20** — **RISK-16 is newly open**, the one risk whose exposure the architecture change *increased* (no seam-length budget for ~30 seals), which the earlier draft of Rev 6 had not carried. RISK-26 is CARRIED to NP-RISK-004 at HIGH/ALARP. **Still true of this revision, and deliberately:** no open decision was resolved and no claim was broadened — RISK-03/-16/-20/-26, OI-LED-W1, REG-1, ACT-1, OI-HEXTILE-14, OI-CHARGE-04 and OI-DOC-01 are reported open with their decision owners; §10 carries every competitive claim next to its gate; and §4 pricing still carries the caveat that BOM/COGS pre-date the hex architecture. **Known residual, flagged not fixed:** NP-DRV-SHELL-002 Rev 2 and NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 3 introduced a 24 V boost rail and a 19-pin socket that Rev 6 does not describe — that is new engineering content, not a reference update, and folding it in silently during a naming pass would have hidden a real content gap. It needs its own revision. Generator: `scripts/create_np_db_005.py` (renamed from `create_design_brief_r6.py` — a generator named for a revision would need renaming on every revision, which is the §4.3 failure mode in miniature). ISA: `MEMORY/WORK/20260810-design-brief-r6/ISA.md`.
- **CORRECTION — "a hub that still sets a retired zone bit" describes an impossible event (2026-08-12):** The 2026-08-05 entry above ("Safety-MCU cranial PBM enable collapsed to one bit") justifies excluding bits 1–4 from `NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK` (`0x3FFF` → `0x3FE1`) on the grounds that *"a hub that still sets a retired zone bit has it stripped in `np_spi_watchdog_tick` rather than enabling something else."* That entry stands as written and is **superseded by this one**; per the append-only rule it is not edited, because it records what was believed when the collapse landed. **The scenario it describes cannot occur, on two independent grounds.** (1) **No hub hardware exists** — development-mode posture, no hardware, no bench data, no fielded device, so there is no deployed or legacy hub to be compatible with. (2) **No source can set those bits either** — the `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_ZONE_0..4` macros were *deleted in that same change*, and every surviving occurrence in the tree is a comment reading `Formerly NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_ZONE_1..4`. Verified by grep across `firmware/` and `app/`: zero definitions, zero setters, comments only. So the mask exclusion is **defence in depth against a future authoring error re-introducing those bit positions**, not backward compatibility with anything that exists. **Why this is worth correcting rather than shrugging at:** a guard described as mitigating a live hazard reads as load-bearing, and a vacuous guard that reads as load-bearing is exactly the kind that survives a later "tidy-up" unchallenged — or, worse, gets cited as evidence that the retired-bit path is *handled*. Naming it as forward-looking defence keeps its actual strength visible. **Nothing about the implementation changes, and the reservation is still right.** `NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK == 0x3FE1` is unchanged, bits 1–4 stay reserved rather than compacted, and `np_spi_watchdog.c:79` (`granted_mask = requested_mask & NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK`) is untouched — because the reservation rests on the *second*, independent rationale already recorded at NP-HW-HUB-001 §7.2 point 2: enable-bit position ≡ `current_ua[]` slot ≡ charge-accumulator index is a three-way **Class C** identity that the 40 µC/cm² limit rests on, and it binds regardless of whether any hub exists. Note the asymmetry with §7.2 reason 1 (SHDR fault records), which explicitly *does not* currently bind because no fault records exist — reason 2 is the one carrying the reservation today, and this correction does not disturb it. **Fixed in three places, left in one by rule:** NP-HW-HUB-001 Rev 3 → **Rev 4** (§7.2 STATUS banner reworded; documentation-only, no hardware/firmware/mask/bit-allocation change), `firmware/safety_mcu/tests/np_safety_spi_proto_tests.c` (`test_enable_word_layout` comment — the assertions are unchanged and still pass), and NP-DB-005 Rev 6 §0.1 and §5.6. The 2026-08-05 entry above keeps its original wording. Raised by the principal, 2026-08-12, from first principles: *no hubs exist, so no hub will ever set a retired zone bit.*
- **Cervical VNS time-of-cutoff offset resolved to UHDR — NP-FW-CVNS-001 §8.2 had classified one datum into two partitions (NEW 2026-08-12).** Found while fixing the fault-latch cardiac oracle (entry above) and reported there as out-of-scope; fixed on request the same day. **The contradiction:** §8.2's routing table classified *Time-of-cutoff offset (s after stim onset)* as **UHDR — "User biology"**, and two rows later routed *Safety MCU fault log (offset, reason, no HR)* to **SHDR** — where §5.6's `np_cvns_fault_log_entry_t` carried that same quantity as `uint32_t cutoff_offset_ms`. §11's summary repeated the SHDR half. So the same physical measurement was specified into both partitions, and **the SHDR copy was the finer-grained one** (milliseconds against the UHDR original's seconds) — the unauthorised copy was strictly more revealing than the authorised one. **Resolved to UHDR**, on three independent grounds rather than one: (1) *substance* — for `reason = HR_CHANGE` the offset is the latency from stimulation onset to the wearer's heart rate deviating past `NP_CARDIAC_HR_DELTA_BPM`, i.e. an **autonomic response latency**, a measurement of the person and not of the device; it fails the §5.1 test ("does this record tell us something about the person?") outright, and is *more* clearly biology than the fault-latch `tick_ms` retired the same day, which was only a relative SysTick value; (2) *precedent* — fault event timing is already UHDR unconditionally across the design: the SHDR fleet schema's `fault_log` has no timing column and states "Precise fault event timing, where it exists at all, is UHDR under the user's key", and `np_log_shdr_fault()` discards `session_ms` for every caller; (3) *nothing is lost* — the UHDR session record (§4.5) **already carried this datum** as `cutoff_time_offset_s`, so the SHDR field was a duplicate, not the only home. **Implementation:** `cutoff_offset_ms` removed from `np_cvns_fault_log_entry_t` (`firmware/cervical_vns/include/np_cvns_types.h`), struct 12 → 8 bytes. The removal is **unconditional** — the field leaves the record shape for every `reason` value. Zeroing it only for the cardiac reason would have recreated precisely the oracle retired from the fault latch on the same date, and would have leaked *doubly* here, because `reason` is itself a member of the same record. `reason` is retained deliberately: the fault **kind** is the already-published SHDR channel (`fault_log.fault_type = 'CVNS_HR_CUTOFF'`) under the locked "safety interlock log → SHDR" rule. **The struct had no producer and no consumer** — declared in the type header, never populated, never written — so no data path, no stored record and no fleet data was affected; as with the fault latch, the fix landed before a consumer existed. **Root cause, and the reason it survived two revisions:** NP-FW-EMMC-001 §12 — the authoritative classification table, which §11 of the CVNS spec claims consistency with — **was silent on this element**. A document that cites an authority for an element the authority does not carry can contradict itself without any cross-check failing. The element is therefore now carried in NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 2 §12 (table 32 → 33 elements), which is the durable half of this fix. **Docs:** NP-FW-CVNS-001 Rev 2 → **Rev 3** (§5.6 struct + rationale, §8.2 row, §11 summary, and §11's stale cross-reference to "NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1 §12, 27 elements"); NP-FW-EMMC-001 §12 + revision-history row amended; NP-DHF-001 §5.x rows and Document History; `document-register.md`. Two further index-row drifts corrected in passing: the DHF row for NP-FW-CVNS-001 read Rev 1 while the document had been at Rev 2 since 2026-08-05, and the register row likewise. **IEC 62304 SW-01 Class C / SW-02 Class B:** record-shape change only — no interlock threshold, cutoff timing, enable path or session behaviour altered. Firmware host suite 28/28; `check-doc-filenames` and `check-section-refs` clean. No QMS/CAPA record opened (development mode; no hardware, no bench data, no SHDR fault records).
- **SHDR accelerometer characterisation programme — NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 2 §H (principal decision, 2026-08-12):** Raw impact data may be collected into SHDR under a **time-boxed, opt-in characterisation programme**, consented by the **warranty owner**, fed **only** into predictive-maintenance model training. The defect it resolves: `NP-PRIV-001` HIGH-01 restricted SHDR to two booleans computed from `NP_ACCEL_DROP_THRESHOLD_G = 15.0f` and `NP_ACCEL_MAINT_THRESHOLD = 3`, both unvalidated guesses, while §G.3 prohibited every field that could validate them — **the spec foreclosed the evidence needed to make itself correct**, so HIGH-01's own Option C ("process on-device") was a destination with no road to it, since the processing it names is exactly what is unknown. Pattern taken from `NP-MOD-ID-001` §7's fleet characterisation programme; CHAR-1…CHAR-4 mirrored, CHAR-A1…CHAR-A5 added. **Two supporting arguments recorded:** (1) *no identifiable subject* — SHDR holds no user identifier, the warranty DB (which SHDR cannot join) names the **registrant**, and nothing in either store records the registrant↔wearer relationship (`NP-MOD-ID-001` §7.5.1: *"the operative distinction is between describing a person and being attributable to one"*), so consent is owed to nobody in particular and the registrant may consent in every configuration; (2) **impact data is a weaker signal than the precedent it follows** — the module duty-map programme accepted registrant consent for data that at least requires the device to be **worn**, and **a drop does not**: the headset can be dropped by a family member, clinic staff, a courier, anyone handling it, so an impact histogram is frequently not a record of the wearer's behaviour and nothing in it marks which entries are. The precedent is followed *a fortiori*, not merely cited. **Four open questions decided.** *(a) Window unit* — **records, not calendar time**: the headset has no battery, coin cell or `VBAT` rail (§4.5 — USB-C powered, Mode 3 runs off a power bank), so the RT1062's SNVS RTC has no backup domain and wall time is lost on every disconnect and re-supplied by the phone; a calendar expiry would be both losable and settable backwards. `NP_ACCEL_CHAR_RECORD_BUDGET = 512` per device, consumed only by emission, monotone by construction, needing no clock — and it *is* the §H.4 statistic, so exhausting the budget and answering the question are the same act (~43 days at 12 sessions/day, ~17 months at 1/day, both inside `NP-MOD-ID-001`'s 24-month intent). *(b) Coarsening* — **yes, on-device**: a fixed 8-bin log-spaced histogram of peak resultant g per session gap; no per-event value, no orientation, no per-event timestamp ever leaves. The review gate needs the *distribution*, never an individual impact, so the histogram carries what is needed and destroys the trajectory that made the raw series health-inferrable. *(c) Review gate output* — an extension requires **all four** of: a fitted threshold–failure relation with effect size and CI (including a decision on the rolling window's unit); a cohort-vs-fleet skew statement per `NP-MOD-ID-001` §7.6; the specific question a further window would answer that this one did not, with its record budget; and a re-run of the enrolment copy against the field set actually collected. **Default is that the programme closes.** *(d) Data at close* — **the extended rows are DELETED from the fleet DB**; the fitted model and aggregate histogram survive. NeurOne keeps the answer, not the evidence. Withdrawal stops collection next gap and deletes that device's rows at next sync; it cannot un-train a model, and the enrolment copy must say so at enrolment rather than at exit. **Implementation landed with the specification** (cheapest possible moment — no SHDR records exist anywhere in the programme): `firmware/shdr/` authored with both modes from the first line, ctest 28 → 29, CI partition 7 + 22 = 29; SHDR schema **Rev D → Rev E**, one new table `shdr_accel_characterisation` and nothing else changed; **`CHAR-01` CI gate** enforcing the firmware↔schema biconditional in both directions, with the `ACCEL-01`/`ACCEL-02` exemption **computed from CHAR-01's own verdict** so it self-revokes — `PERMITTED_ACCEL_COLUMNS` deliberately **not** widened; separate granular revocable warranty-owner opt-in in both apps, code-structurally independent of `ConsentStore` per §6.0. **`NP_ACCEL_CHAR_WINDOW_ENABLED` is 1 on mainline and returns to 0 at window close, in the same change that drops the table** — CHAR-01 makes a half-retired programme fail CI. Six open items raised: OI-EMMC2-09 (placeholder thresholds), OI-EMMC2-10 (**the rolling maintenance window's unit changed from days to session gaps — "7-day" is not implementable on a device with no surviving clock, and this changes what `maintenance_alert` means**), **OI-EMMC2-11 (§G.3's prohibition is already defeated by aggregation over `shdr_accel_records`' per-gap rows — `count(*) FILTER (WHERE drop_detected)` IS the banned drop count and the `gap_index` values IS the banned drop timing; principal decision required, deliberately not fixed because the obvious remedy destroys the sequencing §H needs)**, **OI-EMMC2-12 (enrolment/withdrawal/close copy + legal review — BLOCKING before any device is offered enrolment)**, OI-EMMC2-13 (a general derivation-parameter-provenance rule over ~30 SHDR flags — recommended, not adopted, materially wider than the decision authorising §H), OI-EMMC2-14 (§G provenance discrepancy: Rev 1 front matter cited MEDIUM-06 while §G.1 cited HIGH-01; Rev 2 aligns on HIGH-01, not resolved). Also updated: `NP-PRIV-001` HIGH-01 conclusion + PDF regenerated from `generate_privacy_report.py`; `NP-PRIV-REM-001` Rev 3 (STEP-10 rewritten, G1 Month 6 target unchanged); CLAUDE.md §5.1 boundary row and §5.2 predictive-maintenance section; `NP-DHF-001` Rev 29.
- **OI-LED-W1's electrical premise corrected; NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 3 → Rev 4 (2026-08-16):** `docs/status/pending-decisions.md` §13.2d excluded the only in-window NIR candidate (SFH 4703AS) on one electrical ground — its Vf *"vs. the ~1.6–2.2V the existing 660nm string/driver architecture assumes"*. **Traced: that figure is not specified in any controlled document, and it is not a 660 nm figure.** It is a span welded from two columns of one estimate table — `1.6` is the **NIR** channel's lower bound and `2.2` the **660 nm** channel's upper bound in `NP-PROC-FPC-001` §2.3, both marked *"confirm from datasheet"* — while `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §4.3's 2.10 V / 1.60 V are labelled design targets by **OI-HEXTILE-02** (*"design targets, not datasheet values"*). The 660 nm primary's Vf has still never been read off a datasheet. **The real constraint was a rail, and it is stale:** `NP-PROC-FPC-001` §2.6.2's incompatibility finding is anchored on *"the same **15V** VLED rail at similar string depth"*; D-6 / OI-HUB-C17b set `VLED` = **24 V** (merged — commit `c6b5dfa` verified an ancestor of `origin/main`, not an open branch). Corroboration: §8.1's 1.6 V residual is 6.7 % at 24 V and 10.7 % at 15 V, so §8.1 is self-consistent only at 24 V. **Consequence:** a 3.0–3.6 V part is not categorically incompatible with the current rail; at 7–8 series it lands on residuals §8.1 already accepts, with a ~0.15 V dead band at V_f 3.00–3.15. Option B's stated cost — *"a separate higher-voltage driver stage … and a Hub PCB revision"* — is **not established**; what is established is a per-tile string-count doubling on CH_B, which is a rigidizer-layout and RISK-08 current-matching question. **Real datasheet pulled** (ams-OSRAM *Produktdatenblatt* v1.1): V_F typ **3.3 V** / max 4.0 V at 1 A (not the 3.55 V the doc set carried), I_F max **1000 mA** (the §2.6.2 *"2000mA DC / 11× margin"* row is the **surge** rating at D = 0.005 — same error class as GH CSSRM5.24's "700 mA" binning current), and **centroid wavelength 810 nm** (peak 820 nm), matching `docs/reference/competitive-position.md`'s published 810 nm claim exactly. §13.2d's residual pulse-rating item **resolves PASS at 5.6× margin**, not the 11× claimed. **But the part is listed discontinued** by the ams-osram product page, DigiKey and RS — recorded at that strength, no PCN retrieved. **NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 4** states §8.1's budget as a rule (new §8.1.1), separating the hard functional ceiling `N · V_f ≤ 24 V − V_dropout` from the thermal budget allocation `N · V_f ≥ 22.4 V`, and raises **OI-HEXTILE-18** (the ≤7 % allocation is asserted, not derived; and no minimum driver dropout is specified anywhere) and **OI-HEXTILE-19** (no tolerance is stated on the 24 V rail, so every string figure is a nominal-point calculation, not a corner analysis). **NO DESIGN DECISION WAS TAKEN.** OI-LED-W1, OI-LED-01, OI-HEXTILE-02 and RISK-03 all remain open; per §13.2d the owners are SAB (science), Regulatory Counsel (RISK-03 scope) and EE Lead (cost). This pass corrects the facts they decide against, nothing else. Full trace and residual work: `docs/status/pending-decisions.md` §13.2e.
- **OI-HUB-C07 / OI-HEXTILE-13 — per-cluster safety *policy* DECIDED AGAINST; the cranial PBM enable is one Class C bit (2026-08-16):** Three specifications described the cranial PBM stimulation enable at three granularities: `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §6/§7.1 at 18 per-cluster `SAFE_EN[n]` lines sourced by the Safety MCU (18 GPIO), `NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.2 at a single `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` bit (1 GPIO), with §7.4 already reconciling them as *"12–16 physical enable lines fanned out from one policy bit"* and the earlier "incompatible specs" claim already withdrawn. **The genuinely open question was narrower — whether per-cluster *policy*, i.e. independently commanded cluster bits, is wanted.** `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.4.1 proposed splitting the enable by IEC 62304 class and stated a falsifier: *a hazard where cutting only the faulted cluster is required **and** a whole-lattice cut is itself unacceptable*. **DECISION: the proposal is accepted unchanged.** The cranial PBM safety enable is **one Class C policy bit**; the **18 per-cluster physical gates are retained in full** (nothing leaves the cluster-controller BOM) as **IEC 62304 Class B** availability gates, with the Class C bit **in series**, so **R-11 is preserved** — the Safety MCU still physically owns the enable path and no Class B fault can re-energise a cut lattice. **The falsifier could not be produced, and its absence is structural rather than evidential.** `NP-RISK-003` (zero cluster-domain entries), `NP-RISK-004` (14 entries; the nearest, RISK-18, is explicitly an *availability* failure) and `NP-FMEA-001` §3.1/§3.4/§3.8 were searched. **No hazard in the tree has an extent of one cluster, and none can:** hazard extents are physical — a tile heats (RISK-26, FMEA-M04, answered by the on-module NTC throttle at Class B), a modality accumulates charge (FMEA-M03, answered by its own Class C bit), a rail collapses (watchdog, answered by clearing every bit) — while a **cluster is a clamp-plate and FPC-fan-out boundary**, drawn in `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a for one-handed serviceability under RISK-22 and constrained by CLUSTER-1/SYM-1/CONTIG-1. A control at a granularity matching no hazard's extent is not a safety control. **A future reviewer wishing to reopen this must exhibit a cluster-extent physical process, not merely a scenario.** **A supporting exhaustive case analysis leaves the middle tier with no case of its own:** (1) when the Class B per-socket tier works, it cuts 1 tile of the ~6 that can be concurrently active at all (`NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §9.2) — *strictly better* availability than cutting a cluster; (2) when that tier is untrusted (module MCU wedged, I2C silent), the per-cluster **gate** is the right response — and it is retained, at Class B; (3) when the hub is untrusted, no Class B granularity is reliable and the whole-lattice Class C cut is correct. There is no fourth case: **per-cluster policy is needed only when Class B is untrusted, and precisely then the correct response is the cut it would replace.** **The certification-boundary argument is sufficient on its own:** 18 commanded cluster bits require the Safety MCU to hold a socket→cluster map, which is *topological* and moves with MECH-2/REG-1 — the identical mistake `NP-HW-HUB-001` §4.5.1 already rejects for the logical address, one layer up where the boundary is a **certification** boundary, making a re-clustering a recertification and creating a failure mode that cannot otherwise exist (cutting the wrong cluster from a stale map, leaving the faulted one energised). **What is explicitly NOT claimed:** the availability benefit of per-cluster granularity is **real** — a montage spreading ~6 active tiles over several clusters keeps its remaining tiles where a whole-lattice cut ends the session — and this decision **assigns that benefit to Class B, which delivers it in full**. Both peers' shared concession that coarser cutting is never less safe (`NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.2 *"over-cutting is a usability cost, never a hazard"*; `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §6 *"an availability regression, not a safety one, and it is the conservative direction"*) is **not** restated as a claim that finer cutting is safer; the conservative-claim rule decides the residual, since the single-bit resolution asserts the least safety capability of the options available. **Firmware consequence: none.** The 2026-08-05 collapse already implements the decided layout — `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` is bit 0 and `NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK` is `0x3FE1` in both `firmware/safety_mcu/include/np_safety_protocol.h` and `firmware/hub_control/include/np_hub_config.h`. **The enable word is unchanged and is not widened**, so `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.4.2's `uint16_t`→`uint32_t` re-layout is **not taken** (the frame does not grow, `NP_SAFETY_RX_EXT_FRAME_LEN` stays 38, `current_ua[14]` stays, both checksum spans and all eight `offsetof` assertions are untouched) and its **time-box against the first SHDR fault record is moot**. The enable-bit ≡ `current_ua[]` slot ≡ charge-accumulator index three-way identity is preserved intact, and the bits 1–4 reservation is untouched and not re-litigated. Both headers gained rationale comments recording the decision; **no Class C behaviour changed**, so the verification consequence is comment review at the next design review rather than re-verification. **No new test was added**, because the existing `test_enable_word_layout` / `test_enable_word_matches_hub` already bite: falsified against a synthetic `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CLUSTER_0` at bit 14, **3 assertions fired** (`ALL_MASK == 0x3FE1`, `ALL_MASK == union of the 10 allocated bits`, and the cross-header mirror `union of hub enable bits == NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK`); the mutation was reverted and the suite is 28/28 green. **One new requirement, which neither peer document anticipated — `HUB-REQ-C05` (`NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.2.2):** the Class B per-cluster gate must be commanded from a tier **above** the cluster controller it gates. The `SAFE_EN[n]` high-side switch sits on the cluster controller board (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2) alongside the cluster MCU, so a self-commanded gate would be uncommandable in case (2) — the single scenario per-cluster gating exists for — and the availability benefit would evaporate in the only case it was bought for. Owner: EE Lead + FW. **Consequence for safety-MCU package selection: UNBLOCKED.** Demand falls from ~38–40 I/O (which excluded every ≤32-pin STM32G071 and left LQFP48 without margin) to **~23**: SPI1 slave 4 + R-peak capture 1 + nine non-cranial modality enables + 1 cranial enable + 6 NTC ADC + fault indicator 1 + SWD 2.
