The package remains **unspecified anywhere in the tree** — the only package-qualified STM32G071 is `NP-HW-HUB-001` §8.3's UFQFPN32 *cluster* MCU, a different part — so selection is raised as **OI-HUB-C20**, now the gating item for **G1 layout**, and should be sequenced after **OI-FMEA-06** re-derives `NP_NTC_CHANNEL_COUNT` (today `6 /* 5 zones + 1 hub */`, a retired-architecture count that may reduce demand further). **Two consequences recorded and routed, deliberately not resolved:** (i) **`RISK-SHELL-03` / `OI-RISK4-01`** — the programme's only CRITICAL risk entry — **changes basis**, since the polarity-conflicted line is now a Class B availability gate rather than a Class C stimulation enable; it is **not closed** (a Class B gate defaulting to enabled at reset still energises a rail, and the safety claim now rests on the Class C broadcast line's own unresolved reset polarity) and is **not re-scored**, following the `NP-FMEA-001` Rev 4 precedent that re-scoring is hazard analysis rather than editorial correction; owner remains Safety + EE Lead. (ii) **`OI-HEXTILE-14`** keeps the cluster-tail conductor and connector count: §8.2.2 option 4's condition is now satisfied (a broadcast `SAFE_EN[n]` permits an 11-conductor multi-drop trunk, since N5 was the tail's only star component) but the count is that item's call, and `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §7.1a's instruction to build against **20 positions and 12-conductor tails** stands — now as a baseline rather than a conditional. **Documents revised in lockstep:** `NP-HW-HUB-001` Rev 3 → **4** (§7.2.1 decision, §7.2.2 HUB-REQ-C05, §7.2.3 package, §7.2.4 routed consequences, §7.4 divergence closed and interface row, §11 OI-HUB-C07 closed / OI-HUB-C20 raised); `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` Rev 4 → **5** (§8.4 findings disposed, §8.4.1 decided + falsifier resolution, §8.2.2 option 4, D-8, HT-DRC-13/19, OI-HEXTILE-13 closed); `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` Rev 2 → **3** (§6 layer table + rationale, §7.1 row, §7.1a adopted); `NP-RISK-004` Rev 1 → **2** (§2.1 basis note, OI-RISK4-01, verification map); both firmware headers (comments only); `NP-DHF-001` and `docs/status/document-register.md` updated — **note the DHF was stale, carrying `NP-HW-HUB-001` at Rev 2/"SUPERSEDED" and `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` at Rev 2, and had no `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` row at all; all three corrected rather than silently left.** **Note on the task premise:** the work was briefed as starting from `NP-HW-HUB-001` **Rev 4**; three sources — the file header, `document-register.md`, and `git log` (last touch 20a3c8e, the 2026-08-12 vacuous-guard correction, which did not bump) — establish it was at **Rev 3**, so the bump lands on Rev 4.
- **OI-HUB-C07 closure — three corrections applied at commitment-boundary review, and one new open item raised (2026-08-16, same change set as the entry above):** An advisor pass over the closure found three defects in the *rationale as first written* — none changes the decision, all change how it is recorded. **(1) "No hazard in the tree has a one-cluster extent, and none can" overreached.** What was established is an **empirical null over a closed enumeration** of the hazard list as it stands (`NP-RISK-003` Rev 1, `NP-RISK-004` Rev 2, `NP-FMEA-001` Rev 4) — not an analytic necessity. Upgrading one to the other is the same error as `feedback_absence_of_symptom_is_not_absence_of_hazard`. Both `NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.2.1 and `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.4.1 now state the enumeration, state that **hazard-list completeness is the assumption the closure rests on**, and name `OI-FMEA-06` — whose re-analysis of SW01-M04 and the enable-mask rows against the hex lattice is live — as the work that could disturb it. The physical-extent argument is retained for what it actually supplies: a reason to expect the null to *persist*, which is why reopening condition (a) demands a cluster-extent **physical process** rather than a scenario. **(2) IEC 62304 category error.** §8.4.1's proposal table labels the per-cluster tier "Class B", but **62304 classifies software items, not gate transistors.** Corrected at `NP-HW-HUB-001` §7.2.1 with an explicit assignment table: the 18 gates are **hardware risk control measures carrying no 62304 class**; the **hub software commanding them is Class B**; the cluster-controller firmware stays Class B under HUB-REQ-C02; the safety-MCU software owning `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` stays **Class C**. This is cleaner and more conservative — the gates being external hardware risk controls is part of what *justifies* the commanding software being Class B, rather than the gates carrying a class they cannot hold. **Critically: no item is downgraded from Class C to Class B.** The cluster-controller tier was already Class B; the gates previously had **no stated class at all** (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` Rev 2 §6 had no class column). The decision **states** an unstated classification; it removes no verification activity from anything that previously had one, and invalidates no existing Class C evidence. **A circular reading is also foreclosed**: the Class C broadcast line **is** the risk control for every enumerated hazard, and the 18 gates are **not load-bearing for any Class C hazard** — removing all 18 would not defeat the safety cut. The single enable stands on its own; the gates are availability on top. **(3) "No Class C behaviour change" was hiding two claims and is now split.** (a) *No runtime behaviour change* — true and verified: wire format, enable word, `NP_SAFETY_EN_ALL_MASK`, the bit-to-`current_ua[]`-to-charge-index identity and every GPIO action are byte-for-byte as implemented 2026-08-05, suite 28/28, `test_enable_word_layout` confirmed to bite under mutation. (b) *This is nonetheless a design-control change* — it fixes previously-unstated classifications and adds `HUB-REQ-C05`. Verification consequence is **design-review scope, not re-verification**, discharged at the same review as HUB-REQ-C02 and HT-DRC-13. **NEW OPEN ITEM — `OI-HUB-C21`: a single-bit enable sits at Hamming distance 1 from the unsafe state.** Surfaced by the same review but **orthogonal to OI-HUB-C07 and not introduced by it** — `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` has been one bit since 2026-08-05 and the property holds for **all ten** allocated modality enable bits, not just the cranial one. One stuck-at bit, SEU, partial write or torn read reaches "enabled"; the conventional Class C form is a multi-bit coded pattern plus complement, re-verified periodically, with anything-not-the-pattern meaning disabled. **Urgent rather than merely open**, because `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.4.2's re-layout window — cheap only while no SHDR fault record exists — is the same time-box OI-HUB-C07 declined to spend, and widening one bit to a coded word would spend it for a reason C07 did not have. **Deliberately NOT done in this change set:** it is a genuine Class C *behaviour* change requiring its own hazard analysis, its own verification, and re-checks of **FMEA-M01-01** and **FMEA-M01-03**; it must not ride along on a documentation decision. Owner: Safety + FW, assess with `OI-FMEA-06`. **Cato (cross-vendor audit, mandatory at E4) could NOT run: `codex` is not installed on this machine**, so no independent-family audit was performed and none is claimed — the review above was same-family (advisor) and is recorded as such rather than presented as cross-vendor.
- **A priori research consent goes from four screens to two; L2/L3 merged onto one screen but NOT into one control (2026-08-16, CLAUDE.md Rev 37 §6.2):** The four consent layers L1–L4 are unchanged and remain the units of the data model, the withdrawal surfaces and every downstream citation; only the *presentation* changed. **S1 "What you get back" (L4 + L1) now comes first**, because L1 and L4 were always the same question — L1 asks whether we may contact you, L4 asks what about — and a contact method is the shared precondition for study invitations, per-study engagement notifications and results notifications alike (§6.2.1). **S2 "What you share" (L2 + L3) carries two controls, not one** (§6.2.2). Selecting all nine L2 categories is **not** equivalent to L3 blanket consent: L2 is *scope* (which areas), L3 is *posture* (ask-me-each-time versus pre-approved). "Everything, but ask me" is a real and arguably maximally-engaged position and survives only while both axes do. A full collapse was considered and rejected; had it been adopted it would have destroyed L2's *each project is a fresh decision* property for any user wanting broad scope, and fused the two withdrawal semantics — category withdrawal does not tear down research analytics and blanket withdrawal does (§6.0), so one control means one withdrawal and either every category withdrawal starts tearing down analytics or blanket withdrawal stops. The latter is the regression caught in review on 2026-06-16 and deliberately restored; it was not re-introduced. **Select-all deliberately does NOT auto-enable the blanket toggle (§6.2.3, decided rather than defaulted):** ticking nine boxes expresses breadth of *interest*, the blanket toggle surrenders the *right to be consulted*, and inferring the second from the first attributes to the user a decision they did not make. Auto-enabling also fails silently in both directions and asymmetrically — a user auto-escalated to blanket stops receiving consent requests they wanted with no event to notice, and if they later switch it off they get a research-analytics teardown they never asked for. Per the conservative-claim rule the option that asserts least and preserves the ability to be asked wins. The counter-argument — that only a privacy engineer perceives the distinction — was accepted as genuine and answered **with copy rather than state**: enabling Select all surfaces an inline note ("You'll still be asked before each individual study. To stop being asked, turn on the setting below"), and the blanket toggle's label states what it changes in plain words instead of naming a tier. **L4-first is prevented from reading as an inducement** by three binding copy rules (§6.2.4) reusing `NP-MOD-ID-001` §7.5's honest-exchange framing and §7.5.2's non-coercion invariant: conditional framing ("if your data ever contributes to a study"), the exchange stated as symmetric with null results named explicitly, and an on-screen statement that opting into results grants no data access and declining costs nothing. Documents: CLAUDE.md Rev 34 → 35; `NP-PRIV-NOTICE-001` Rev 3 → 4 (new §5.1 and §5.2 — published notice, external effect); `NP-IRB-001` Rev 1 → 2 (layers-are-not-screens clarification; no consent mechanism or withdrawal right changed); `NP-PRIV-REM-001` Rev 3 → 4 (STEP-36 clarification only — the age gate remains first in the *overall* onboarding chain, which is a different sequence from the research-consent screen order and does not conflict with it).
- **Blanket→analytics teardown moved to the store ingestion point; latent iOS UI bypass closed (2026-08-16, CLAUDE.md §6.2.5):** `ConsentStore.withdrawBlanketResearchConsent()` was correct on both platforms and tested on both (`ResearchAnalyticsGateTests.swift`, `ConsentStoreTests.kt`), but **nothing on iOS called it from the UI.** The only iOS path that turned blanket consent off was Research Preferences → toggle → commit via `updateResearchConsent()`, which performed no teardown; Android routed its dashboard toggle correctly (`Screens.kt`). The platforms were drifted and the 2026-06-16 regression was latent in the iOS UI layer behind a green test suite — the existing tests pinned the named method, not the path the UI used. Merging L2 and L3 onto one commit-at-the-end screen would have made that bypass the ordinary path, so the fix is a precondition of the merge rather than a follow-up. **`updateResearchConsent()` now guards the blanket true→false *transition*** and calls `revokeResearchAnalytics()`, on both platforms. Guarding the transition rather than the value is load-bearing: `blanketConsentGranted == false` holds both for a user who never granted it and for one who just revoked it, so a value guard would tear down analytics on every category-only edit — the same regression inverted. `withdrawBlanketResearchConsent()` is retained as the explicit named path and is idempotent with respect to the guard. Two further latent defects fixed in passing: the iOS onboarding sheet seeded its draft from an empty `ResearchConsentState` rather than committed state, so re-entering Research Preferences and pressing Done silently cleared every prior consent decision (Android had the same defect); and iOS gained an explicit blanket-withdrawal control in `ConsentDashboardView` matching Android's, so withdrawal granularity is now equal on both platforms and strictly greater than before. Withdrawal remains at study, category and blanket granularity with §6.0 scoping unchanged. **Falsification:** three mutations were applied and the suites re-run — (A) guard removed → `commitPathBlanketWithdrawalRevokesResearchAnalytics` fails on both platforms while the pre-existing named-method test still passes, which is the direct evidence the new probe was needed; (B) guard keyed on the value instead of the transition → `commitPathCategoryOnlyEditDoesNotRevokeResearchAnalytics` fails on both platforms; (C) select-all coupled to blanket consent → `selectAllCategoriesDoesNotEnableBlanketConsent`, `everythingButAskMeIsRepresentable` **and** `commitPathCategoryOnlyEditDoesNotRevokeResearchAnalytics` all fail, empirically confirming the §6.2.2 argument that fusing the axes drags the analytics teardown onto category edits. Restored implementation: iOS 406 tests / 0 failures, Android core 148 tests / 0 failures. `SHDRUploader` and `WarrantyAnalyticsGate` gained no reference to `ConsentStore` — the §6.0 code-structural independence invariant is intact.
- **OI-HEXTILE-14 CLOSED — peer documents re-sized to 18 clusters; 20 connector positions provisioned (2026-08-16):** `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.2.1 derives the cluster count at the shipped 80-socket lattice as **18 — provably minimal** under **CLUSTER-1** (7-hex flower is the cluster unit), **SYM-1** (partition mirror-symmetric about the sagittal midline) and **CONTIG-1** (petals form a contiguous arc): six forced midline clusters plus six lateral mirror pairs, sizes 3–6, zero pendant petals, verified by HT-DRC-21. Peers had sized hardware off **12**, or off `ceil(n / 8)` = **10** — which uses board *capacity* as a divisor and is not how a clamp partition is counted. §8.2.2's recommendation (**options 1 + 3**: provision 20 connector positions, adopt D-7's 32-segment I2C tree) is now applied everywhere. **`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` had already taken it at Rev 2 (2026-08-11)**, so it was never the open half — its Rev 3 here is editorial only. **`NP-HW-HUB-001` Rev 5 → 6** is the substantive change: §7.4 interface contract now **18 populated / 20 provisioned** connector positions, **216/240** interface pins and **4 × PCA9548A on LPI2C1–4** (32 segments, 18 used, 14 spare); §6.3 **18 DG2788A**, not 10; §5.2 mux rate restated **per cluster**, and its **16-controller tier-1 ceiling retired because 18 exceeds it** — a bind neither `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.2.2 nor `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` had identified, and the same shortfall as C2 reached from the hub side; §8.2/§8.5 **18 boards / $114.12** and **216** parting-plane conductors, which makes the headline reduction against a star **~4.1×**, not the **8.8×** that section claimed; HUB-DRC-C02 inverted from asserting the `ceil(n/8)` formula valid into the rule that catches its failure. **Why 20 and not 18:** socket count ~80 is PROVISIONAL pending REG-1/ACT-1, and the two axes are not symmetric — one extra *conductor* costs a hub pin on **every** tail (18–20 pins), while two extra *tail positions* cost 12 pins **once**. Everything else is stated **per cluster**, so a lattice re-cut re-derives one number instead of renegotiating the interface. **`NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` Rev 3 → 4:** §8.2.2 PROPOSED → **ADOPTED**, OI-HEXTILE-10's count precondition satisfied, **HT-DRC-20 Open → ✓**. **`NP-HEX-ZM-001` Rev 2 → 3:** §5.4a's MECH-2 table is **kept, not corrected in place** — its three original rows compare cluster *units* under one fixed no-symmetry assumption, and that relative comparison is the reasoning CLUSTER-1 rests on, so overwriting the flower row with a figure derived under a different assumption would have made the column incomparable while still looking like a comparison; instead the design figure (**18 boards / $114.12**) is added as a fourth row, because leaving the correction in prose alone had already demonstrably failed — that is how the peers came to size off 12 and 10. **`NP-DB-005` Rev 6** regenerated from `scripts/create_np_db_005.py`: the COUNT CONFLICT notice becomes **RESOLVED**, and its assertion that `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` was still uncorrected — which was **wrong** — is replaced. **NOT decided here, deliberately: OI-HUB-C07 / `OI-HEXTILE-13`** — whether per-cluster safety *policy* is wanted, and with it §8.2.2 option 4's broadcast `SAFE_EN`, which would drop the tail 12 → 11 conductors, replace the per-cluster star with a multi-drop trunk, and make connector count **insensitive** to cluster count altogether. That is the outcome worth having and it is not claimed; every peer is written to be correct either way, which is what the 20-position provision buys. **Also unchanged:** the five-network split, the cluster-controller selection, the zero-dynamic-flex conclusion, OI-HUB-C17c, and `NP-HW-HUB-001` §5.2's tier-1 *topology* rewrite (remains **OI-HUB-C15**, together with the §8 recost — whose per-board rate is still void from D-3/D-4 and must now be multiplied by 18, not 10). No QMS or CAPA record created — development-mode posture: no hardware, no bench data.
- **Configuration BOM / COGS / GM% re-derived against the hex-tile architecture — all four T1 configurations are gross-margin negative (NP-COST-001 Rev 1, 2026-08-16, CLAUDE.md Rev 38):** The §2.1 cost columns predated the hex-tile decision and were built on the retired five-zone-module design; `NP-DB-005` §4 reproduced them behind a caveat. **Retail is locked and unchanged; margin is the output.** Home Standard moves $405 → **$897–959** BOM, $540 → **$1,196–1,278** COGS, **+36% → −41% to −51%** GM. Core −23% to −45%, Home Lite −50% to −64% (worst), Home Premium −7% to −14%. **Pro is unaffected** (+50% / +73%) because its retail is 3–6× its cost and it absorbs the same absolute delta. **The dominant term is not the cluster tier this work was scoped around.** It is `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §6.4's **$11.53/tile driver + metering**, of which ~$10 is two InGaAs photodiodes: at 30 tiles that is **$346 against a $405 BOM**, i.e. 63–70% of the whole increase, versus $146–208 for the entire L1 tier. **None of `OI-HEXTILE-06`'s three options restores a positive T1 margin** — populate 20 not 30 → −33%; silicon PD on T1-A/T1-B → −8%; both combined → −4%; and option 3 (per-cluster PD) is excluded because `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §4a protects the per-tile "each tile meters itself" claim that CLAUDE.md §3 sells as the primary differentiator over Vielight. The residual is structural: the L1 tier alone is 36–51% of the entire old BOM and no PD decision touches it. **Two corrections of record.** (i) The three deltas this work was commissioned around are **not additive** — `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` **Rev 2** §10.1's "$175–225, all lines" already contains the $114.12 controller tier *and* the $32–64 socket arrays *and* the 24 V-rated high-side switches, and it supersedes Rev 1's $125–216 (which was computed at 12 passive carriers, before the count went to 18 and the carrier gained a $1.50 MCU). Adding them would have treble-counted. (ii) `NP-HW-HUB-001` §8's blanket *"EVERY FIGURE IN §8 IS VOID"* banner is **stale** — it was written when HEXTILE **D-4** moved the TIA and ADC on-module, but `OI-HUB-C17c` was subsequently resolved **against** D-4 (which is why the socket went to 19 contacts), so the TIA / PD mux / NTC mux / ADC lines survive and `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` Rev 2 relies on them. Raised as **OI-COST-06**. **`OI-HUB-C08` is NOT closed and cannot be closed from the record — and it is under-scoped.** It asks for the retired five-zone drive electronics to be netted out before the cluster tier is netted in. `NP-ART-001` §5 records that the 660–670 nm and 808–830 nm emitters are **not selected** (`OI-HEXTILE-02`), leaving *"no incoming-inspection criterion for **the dominant BOM line**"* — so no emitter unit price exists anywhere, for the new tiles or the retired 600, and **both sides of the largest term in the subtraction are unpriced**. Worse, OI-HUB-C08 scopes only *drive electronics*: the **emitter-count delta** (Home Standard 600 → ~2,286; Pro 600 → ~2,814) is a larger term that no open item owned, now **OI-COST-07**. Every published figure is therefore a **FLOOR** excluding term **U**, and U is very likely positive and large — the rows get worse, not better. **Three inputs the model needs do not exist anywhere in the document set, and are recorded as assumptions rather than buried:** per-configuration tile population (**OI-COST-01** — searching every controlled document for the configuration names returns two hits, both merely citing the $405 figure in passing; the adopted split is built from `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §4a's "~8–9 × T1-B + the balance in T1-A" and §6.4's 20–30-tile band); whether every configuration carries the full 18-cluster L1 or only populated clusters (**OI-COST-04**, worth up to ~$140 on Core — the dearer platform-consistent assumption was carried under the conservative-claim rule); and the BOM→COGS relationship (**OI-COST-05**), because **no single rule is recoverable from the six published pairs** — the multiplier is neither constant nor monotonic (1.537 → 1.333 → 1.745) and no fixed markup, adder or linear fit reproduces them, so each configuration keeps its own published multiplier, the only choice that reproduces all six original pairs exactly. GM% confirmed as (Retail − COGS)/Retail against all six published percentages. **CLAUDE.md §2.2 charger policy confirmed unaffected** — the table is keyed to peak draw, §4.5's envelope is unchanged, and `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §9 holds concurrency to ~5–6 tiles however many are populated. `docs/reference/competitive-position.md`: the two LED-count claims stay blocked but for a new reason — they are now *computable* (~2,286 emitters ≈ 190× Vielight, ~1,143/wavelength) yet depend on the unsourced OI-COST-01 assumption and a PROVISIONAL socket count, so they must not be published; **every price comparison there remains factually correct because retail is locked**, and a note now bars adding any claim that characterises T1 pricing as profitable. **This is a programme-level finding, not BOM maintenance**: either retail unlocks, or tile population and PD architecture change substantially, or T1 ships at negative margin — a principal decision, not taken here. Socket count ~80 stays PROVISIONAL pending REG-1/ACT-1 and every derived figure inherits that. `NP-DB-005` §4 recosted via `editscripts/patch_db005_bom_recost.py` (repo patch-script convention — **there is no `scripts/create_np_db_005.py`; NP-DB-005 has no generator of record**), whose locked-retail guard was falsified by tampering a retail cell and confirming it aborts. See `docs/np_cost_001.md`.
- **Retail pricing UNLOCKED — principal decision, 2026-08-16 (NP-COST-001 Rev 2 §8, CLAUDE.md Rev 39):** Taken directly against the entry above, which found all four T1 configurations gross-margin negative and named three exits — unlock retail, change tile population and PD architecture, or ship at negative margin. **The first was chosen. No price is set by this decision.** Unlocking the constraint reverses the arithmetic — GM% becomes the input and retail the output — and what falls out is a **ladder, not a number**: retail = COGS ÷ (1 − original GM target) gives break-even **$1,196–1,278** and target-margin **$1,869–1,997** for Home Standard (**2.20–2.35× the $849 in force**), Core $955–1,121, Home Lite $1,445–1,587, Home Premium $2,475–2,637, Pro Entry $8,881–9,259, Pro Full $19,621–20,189. **The six configurations keep the prices currently in force** until a separate commercial decision is taken; nothing ships at a new price on the strength of this. Every figure inherits the floor status of the recosting — term **U** is still excluded, so this is the *least* retail would have to move. **Break-even binds before margin does, and it is the number to absorb first:** Home Standard cannot be sold below ~$1,196 at *any* margin, already 1.4× its current price, and the per-unit loss at prices in force is −$105 to −$201 (Core), −$297 to −$385 (Home Lite), −$347 to −$429 (Home Standard), −$88 to −$172 (Home Premium). **Three consequences the lock was concealing, none of which is arithmetic.** (i) **The T1 and T2 price ladders collide** — Home Premium at $2,475–2,637 against a Pro Entry at $4,999 is not two tiers, it is one tier with a regulatory footnote, so **CLAUDE.md §1's two-tier structure is a casualty of this ladder** and should be re-examined before any price is set (**OI-COST-08**). (ii) **Pro is where the *target*, not the cost, is the thing to question** — both Pro rows are profitable *today* (+$2,499 and +$10,163 per unit) and appear in the ladder only because holding 73%/81% against a risen COGS demands $9K and $20K; T2 can absorb the delta at its current price at ~50%/~73% instead. **Unlocking retail must not be read as a mandate to raise T2 pricing.** (iii) **Every competitive price claim is live again.** The previous entry recorded that the comparisons in `docs/reference/competitive-position.md` were *"factually correct and safe to use"* **because retail was locked** — the lock was the entire justification and it is gone. At the implied price Home Standard is **~40% of a ~$5K Vielight Neuro Pro 2, not 17%**, and it lands **inside Sens.ai's $1.5–2K band**, converting a differently-priced adjacent product into a head-on competitor that already has EEG, PBM and closed-loop. The claims are flagged rather than rewritten, because they cannot be recomputed until a price exists (**OI-COST-09**). **Binding sequence, and the most actionable output of this decision: `OI-HEXTILE-06` must be decided BEFORE any price is set (OI-COST-10).** Silicon PD on T1-A/T1-B plus a 20-tile build takes Home Standard's COGS from $1,278 to ~$885 and its target-margin retail from **$1,997 to ~$1,383** — a $614 reduction, and the difference between a 2.35× and a 1.63× price increase. Pricing first means pricing against a cost that the PD decision then invalidates. **The unlock therefore does not retire the other two levers; it prices them.** CLAUDE.md §2 heading changed from *"(all locked)"* to *"(🔓 retail UNLOCKED 2026-08-16; charger policy §2.2 still locked)"*, §2.1's Retail column relabelled *"in force, 🔓 unlocked"*, and new **§2.1a** carries the ladder. `NP-DB-005` §4's caveat re-issued via `editscripts/patch_db005_bom_recost.py`; **the script's locked-retail guard is retained deliberately** — unlocking changed no price, so the guard still detects a price that has drifted without a decision behind it, and when new prices are set it becomes the record of what they were changed from. See `docs/np_cost_001.md` Rev 2 §8.
- **UID-keyed PBM dose-metering calibration — OI-HUB-C06's software half closed (NP-FW-PBM1064-001 Rev 4, NP-SES-1064-001 Rev 3, 2026-08-18):** The requested migration was "re-index `K_PD1[0..4][0..2]` by socket, same fix as the session descriptor." **That fix is wrong and was rejected**, per this document's own 2026-07-29 finding and NP-FW-PBM1064-001's ⚠ banner: those coefficients characterise a *physical module*, so under hex tiling — where tiles are universal and swappable — any location-keyed table (5 zones or 128 sockets; the size was never the point) applies the previous occupant's calibration after a swap, **invisibly, because `cal_source` still reads `NP_CAL_FACTORY`**. What shipped instead: calibration keyed to **module UID**. `np_pbm_dose_load_cal(uid, cal_out)` replaces the location-agnostic stub and returns `np_cal_source_t`, failing closed to firmware defaults + `NP_CAL_DEFAULT` on a NULL uid, an all-zero uid, an absent provider or a provider miss — and staging the provider's output so a provider that half-writes before failing leaves no trace, rather than blending one module's factory coefficients with another's defaults under a DEFAULT label. Storage is `np_module_map`'s existing UID-keyed NVRAM record, extended by 36 bytes (9 floats, `3*wl + coeff`), blob version 0x0002 → 0x0003 and 17,804 → 22,412 bytes at 128 sockets; **the version bump is not defensive this time** — a v2 blob read as v3 misaligns every record after the first and hands each socket its neighbour's UID, on a map that gates PBM and tES element addressing. Stale entries needed no new mechanism: `clear_record()` already wipes a socket's whole record on a UID change. **There is deliberately no socket-keyed accessor** anywhere in the new API, so the wrong migration is not reachable by a future caller who has not read this entry. The two libraries are joined by `firmware/hub_control/src/np_pbm_cal_bridge.c` rather than a header include, because hub_control links pbm and not the reverse; the bridge's install point lands with `np_module_map`'s own integration (OI-HEXMAP-02), and until then every socket resolves to the zero UID and uses defaults — an un-bridged build is degraded, never wrong. **One shape change fell out of it:** `cal_source` moved from `np_pbm_shdr_summary_t` (session-uniform) to `np_pbm_socket_shdr_t` (per socket), since one session can now legitimately mix a FACTORY tile and a DEFAULT one. That is what `ci/shdr/shdr_fleet_schema.sql`'s per-(`socket_number`, `module_uid`, `wavelength_nm`) `cal_source` column had assumed since it was written — **firmware could not previously populate it faithfully**, a schema-vs-firmware mismatch this closes. `np_pbm_session_build_shdr_summary()` was split out as the single fixed-shape marshaller for that record (the `np_fault_latch_build_report()` pattern) both to stop provenance drifting back to a session-wide constant across two write sites and because the summary was previously assembled into a local and discarded — an unobservable property no test could assert on. **Verification:** four mutants were run and each was killed by the assertions that name it — UID ignored in the lookup (the socket-keyed store), calibration preserved across an occupant change, provider output published on failure, and session calibration keyed by socket index. Host suite 29 → 30 tests, 0 failures; ARM cross-build clean under `-Werror`; `check-doc-filenames` and `check-section-refs` pass. **Still open under OI-HUB-C06:** the factory *write* path — populating real coefficients at manufacture is §6.6's integrating-sphere procedure on the FAI-SM-06/07/08 bench, unchanged here. **Also recorded:** session descriptor v5 itself needed no work — it shipped in `5c491de` and is unchanged by this revision, including every byte offset the Ed25519 signature covers.
- **NP-TOOL-SHELL-001 opened for the first time since the hex-tile decision — PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED, and the inference-based flag had under-scoped it (Rev 2, 2026-08-18):** The PR #223/#224 documentation cleanup corrected the zone-module-era corpus but explicitly did **not** open `docs/np_tool_shell_001.docx`. It was flagged by inference alone, from `NP-DT-001`'s `DO-HW-06` summary line reading "F-01 zone slot anchor posts ×5", and recorded as **⚠ Needs review** rather than superseded, precisely because nobody had read it. Reading it confirms F-01 and surfaces two defects a citation-level sweep structurally could not find, because **neither is visible in a feature title**. **F-01 — retired outright.** Five colour-coded anchor posts, one per zone slot ZM-01…ZM-05, colours matched to `NP-TOOL-ZM-001` §3.1. `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §3.4 replaced the five position-unique slots with ~80 identical sockets, §4a made the tile a universal SKU with an orientation-only key and no zone identity to colour-code, and §5.1 moved the sockets onto the **inner** bowl while F-01 specifies posts on the outer CFRP surface adjacent to a zone-slot opening — a surface that carries no sockets at all. SH-01–SH-05, SH-23, FAI-CV-01–04 and OI-04 go with it; SH-23 in particular cross-checks against a zone-module mechanical key that `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §4a deleted. **F-03 — premise retired; the threshold it served survives elsewhere.** F-03 routes EEG cables from the Hub PCB to 8 discrete electrode pods in an 8×5 mm channel on the outer CFRP inner surface, kept ≥15 mm from the zone-module FPC bundle (DRC-18) by putting the two on opposite sides of the shell wall. `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §9.1 quotes that exact rule and states it is unavailable — EEG electrodes now live inside T1-B hex tiles on the same L1 carrier as PBM drive current — and §5.3 records the replacement as **"absorbing the EEG harness"**, deleting the retired design's 10 separate EEG cables. There is no harness for the channel to carry. This brings the document into line with two artifacts that had already retired the feature without it: `cad/CAD_PARTS_LIST.md` carries `EEG-ROUTE-CHANNEL` as a RETIRED tombstone noting the `REQ-ST-01..07` features are "deleted from shell tooling", and `NP-RISK-002` disposes of RISK-21 on the ground that "there are no EEG cables to route" — **while F-03 still stood in §2 as a MANDATORY shell feature.** The `<5 µVpp` artifact threshold survives verbatim as `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` SH2-DRC-16 and is now a harder test, because no geometric margin backs it. **Reopening condition stated rather than erased:** `NP-HW-EEGNET-001` Rev 2 §7.3 records that if the electrode moves off the lattice onto a geodesic strut net, RISK-21 reverts and `EEG-ROUTE-CHANNEL` "returns in some form" — but that is the **fallback** option; Rev 2's §1.4/§1.5 make an in-tile electrode offset the lead option specifically because it avoids cables, tail and connector, and the fork is gated on `OI-EEGNET-01` and the blocking measurement `OI-EEGNET-14`. F-03 is retired under the current design, not shown impossible. **F-02 — architecture-independent, but in direct conflict with `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` Rev 1 F-02, and the conflict is safety-relevant.** Both documents are ACTIVE and both specify covers for the same hub ports. This one: three anchor posts on the control hub shell, Ø 4.0 mm × 3.5 mm boss, **50 mm** tether. `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` F-02 — created by this document's own §9 remaining action: two anchor posts, 1.0 mm × 0.5 mm, **≤20 mm free-length** tether, where the maximum is a hazard control so a detached-but-tethered cover cannot reach the F-04 fan intake grille (`FAI-HTOOL-02`, BLOCKING). 50 mm exceeds it by 2.5×. `NP-TOOL-HUB-001`'s back-reference is wrong in both halves — it calls F-02 "the headset's zone-slot and lens-rim accessory ports", but F-02 is on the hub and lens-rim guards belong to `NP-TOOL-LENS-001` F-07. Raised as **OI-ART-07**; the hub document's geometry governs meanwhile, and it is closable now, independently of MECH-1. **F-04 — survives unchanged**, and its PROVISIONAL rationale is untouched (boss at first cut is zero-cost, retrofit is $15,000–40,000 and 6–8 weeks). Two notes: its dust-cover tether is specified as "same anchor post tether spec as F-01" and now dangles onto a retired feature, though the geometry itself survives in §3; and OI-08 tracks HOPE Phase 3 as "expected mid-2026", which is now past and due a status check — no result is supplied here. **Scope is also understated:** §1 says "headset outer CFRP shell", written when the shell was one part. `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5 splits it into L0 (outer, carrying the whole EMF stack) and L1 (inner module carrier). This document governs **L0 only**; L1 has no tooling specification at all (`NP-ART-001` A5, blocked on MECH-1), and three L0 features absent from a §2 checklist claiming to list *all* molded shell features — the four-corner clamp latches with BeCu ground-bond fingers and Hall interlocks, the posterior-centre blind-mate boss, and the parting-plane labyrinth lip and conductive-bead groove — are all MECH-1 and all unretrofittable in exactly the sense §2 warns about. **Treatment:** the document is **not** moved to `docs/superseded/` — `NP-ART-001` §5 retained it deliberately, because it is the only tooling specification artifact **A6** (the L0 outer bowl) has, and retiring it would leave a live artifact with no governing document. Nothing is deleted: all 19 critical dimensions, 23 SH checklist rows, 11 FAI rows and 8 open items are retained verbatim under a per-feature supersession banner, verified by row count before and after. Releasable for **F-04 only**. Re-scope or supersede is **OI-ART-01**, which cannot complete before MECH-1 fixes the clamp and boss geometry. Cross-references resolved in the same pass: `NP-DT-001` DO-HW-06 (the ⚠ flag that prompted this, now confirmed and widened), `document-register.md`, `NP-DHF-001`, `pending-decisions.md` (the shell-tooling item is **reopened** — it was closed against a shell that no longer exists), `NP-ART-001` §5 (whose "F-02, F-03 and F-04 are architecture-independent" claim was written without opening the file and is two-thirds wrong), `docs/superseded/README.md`, and `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` §3 F-02. **The general lesson, recorded because it generalises:** a citation-level sweep can only catch a defect that surfaces in the *title* of the thing cited. "F-01 zone slot anchor posts ×5" carries its own falsifier; "F-03 EEG cable routing channel" and "F-02 accessory port anchor posts ×3" do not, and both were worse. Patch script: `editscripts/patch_hexzm_tool_shell_supersession.py` (idempotent).
- **A zone's only ORIGIN is a `zone` block in a `.npps` file; duplicate definitions are an error (NP-NPPS-REF-001 Rev 13, 2026-08-24):** Three documentation corrections and one implementation change, all about where a zone comes from. **(i)** §1.6 and §4.1 resolved a named `zones` entry against "a loaded `zone` block **or a predefined lobe zone**", naming a second, built-in source that does not exist — all fourteen shipped zones are ordinary `zone` blocks in `00-zones.npps`, and the firmware lobe table was retired at OI-HUB-C14. **Indirection is explicitly permitted**: a generated table or cache is fine provided it derives from `.npps` and regenerates with it; a definition whose *origin* is anything else is not. **(ii)** §8 still claimed the eight lobe zones' membership was "derived from skull geography, not authored" and regenerated by `scripts/sync-socket-map.ts` — a derivation ZONE-1 deleted on 2026-07-30 as circular (on disagreement the file was re-cut from the same four guessed arc constants, so it validated no anatomy). The socket lists are human-authored; the script validates structure and can reject a zone, never author one. **(iii)** §11's grammar summary still documented the retired five-slot `zones` selectors as parseable, three revisions after Rev 7 rejected them — and that is the same defect, since `front`/`rear` named zones no `.npps` file defines and numeric indices bypassed zone definitions entirely. **Duplicate zone/condition names now bind to NEITHER definition and are reported as an error**, replacing last-write-wins: the protocol tree is read recursively with no guaranteed traversal order, so "later" was a property of the file system, and for a zone that is a silent change of which sockets get dosed. Leaving the name unbound is the only order-independent outcome, and a protocol referencing it then fails reference resolution. **A consequence found while implementing this:** `SocketZones.generated.{swift,kt}` held `00-zones.npps` inverted into Swift and Kotlin source, and both mobile runtimes answered zone questions from it. That respected the origin rule but was built at **build** time, so it could hold only the **shipped** zones — a `zone` block a user writes in their own file did not resolve on either platform at all. Both now read the loaded namespace via a new `NPZoneRegistry`, as web already did, with `NPProtocolLibrary` folding user entries in on every mutation; the generated artefact is reduced to the lattice and renamed `SocketLattice.generated.{swift,kt}`. **Verified:** web suite green (285 pass, 4 new namespace tests; the single failure is a pre-existing unrelated `react` resolution error), plus `sync-socket-map --check`, `check-doc-filenames` and `sync-conditions --check`. **Android and iOS are unbuilt** — `dl.google.com` is blocked by this environment's network policy so the Android Gradle Plugin cannot resolve, and there is no Xcode.
- **No runtime may ship a build-time cache of protocol content (NP-NPPS-REF-001 Rev 14, principal direction, 2026-08-24):** Stricter than Rev 13's origin rule and superseding it where they meet. Rev 13 permitted any representation *derived from* `.npps`; that permission does not extend to one built **before the app ships**, because the build cannot bind the future. Nothing stops a `.npps` file changing afterwards, and when one does a build-time cache is read **in place of the edit** — silently and self-consistently, with no error, no mismatch and nothing to notice, leaving an author looking at the file they just edited with no way to tell why their change is not showing up anywhere. That is a support call with no available answer, and for a zone it is also a wrong-site targeting path. **Bundling the `.npps` files themselves is the intended mechanism and is not caching them**; a generated artefact may still carry hardware facts (`SocketLattice.generated.*`, `socketMap.generated.ts`), which do not originate in `.npps` and change only on an inner-bowl re-tool. **Three violations found and closed. (i) Windows** read `NPBundledConditions.cs`, a generated C# transcription of `00-conditions.npps`, tolerated because `app/windows` has no NPPS parser — it does not need one, the condition grammar being a quoted name plus quoted string fields, so the same targeted reader now runs at load time as `NPConditionRegistry` and the csproj copies the library beside the assembly. `scripts/sync-conditions.ts` is retired with its CI step; Rev 10 had already dropped its Swift and Kotlin outputs and Windows was its last target. **(ii) The simulator** baked the parsed library into `protocols.generated.js` and `sockets.generated.js`'s `ZONES`, and had already failed exactly as this rule predicts: the committed copy still carried the `side` field ZONE-1 removed on 2026-07-30, so for a month it showed a lattice the repository had stopped describing. It now fetches and parses at load time via `simulator/js/vendor/npps-runtime.js` — a bundle of the real web parser plus the display transform — and `scripts/build-simulator-runtime.ts` **refuses to emit a bundle containing any definition id from the shipped library**, so the ban is mechanically enforced rather than only documented. **(iii)** `SocketZones.generated.{swift,kt}` had already gone in Rev 13 for a related reason. **Accepted cost, on principal direction:** `fetch()` is blocked at a `file://` origin, so the simulator's documented double-click workflow is removed and it must be served over HTTP with `protocols/predefined/` alongside; `NP-SIM-001-HOWTO` → v2.6. **Verified in a real browser** (Chromium, repo root over HTTP): 65 protocols, 14 zones, `Frontal Left` resolving to the file's own socket list, 4 composites skipped, zero duplicate or unresolved references, and ES live bindings confirmed going 0 → 14 zones / 0 → 65 protocols for a consumer that imported before the load. Web suite 290 pass, including a new 5-test `simulator-runtime.test.ts` covering the transform that moved out of the generator. **Not verified:** the simulator's full boot (Three.js is served from a CDN this environment blocks) and Windows (no `dotnet` available).
- **Cooling accessories: on the roadmap, chiller above ice pack, and the ice pack marketed only for what it does (NP-THERM-COOL-001 Rev 4 D-3, principal, 2026-08-30):** Two optional thermal accessories are accepted onto the roadmap — a **TEC chiller in a mains base station** and a **hip-worn ice/PCM pack** — as *optional purchases, never required*. **Priority ranks the chiller above the ice pack, on value delivered**, and the reason is arithmetic rather than preference: cooling raises only the `thermal` term of `NP-PWRSRC-001` §11's `min(electrical, thermal, dose)`, and on the 45 W brick the **electrical** term binds first at 6.4 tiles. A cooled thermal term changes that number not at all. The chiller arrives with a mains base station and so raises *both* terms; the ice pack raises one. Consequently the ice pack buys the **ambient envelope** — running in a hot room instead of derating or blocking above +43 °C — and buys **no session-length reduction**. **Binding marketing constraint: all marketing claims must be honest, so the ice pack is to be marketed only for what it does; the session-time claim belongs to the base station alone.** Marketing it as a time-saver would be selling a source that changes nothing, which is the prohibition `NP-PWRSRC-001` §12 already states, and this applies to copy, packaging, the store page and the in-app upsell alike. Recorded in `docs/reference/accessories-roadmap.md`. **Raised as a general TODO:** no ordered priority set exists for the accessory roadmap as a whole — D-3 ranked two items and exposed that the roadmap has no relative ordering at all, so a complete ordered set across every accessory and companion-software item, with the ranking criterion written down, is now open at `docs/status/pending-decisions.md` §13.2.
- **A sealed pneumatic cooling loop is OUT of scope, and the criterion is what killed it (NP-THERM-COOL-001 Rev 4 §6.9, D-2, principal, 2026-08-30):** Direction was that the loop is in scope *only if it provides a real benefit, and a benefit not obtainable by other means*. That second limb is the whole decision. The loop's benefit is real — it attacks the stagnant inter-bowl gap, 0.23 m²K/W and 56 % of the outward path, which nothing else in the design touches. But it is **not unique to the loop**: a compliant **thermally-conductive, electrically-insulating gap pad** spanning the same 6 mm shorts the same term conductively at **0.0022 m²K/W**, against 0.067 for a stirred gap — conduction through a solid beating convection across one by two orders of magnitude. Carried through the network, the full static stack reaches **40.6 tiles against the loop stack's 19.7 — 2.1× better, with no blower, no tubes, no acoustic path beside the audio modality, and no penetration of any kind.** **`OI-THCOOL-06` closes with it**, since the ELF magnetic bench measurement was BLOCKING only on the loop's penetration of the posterior boss. Worth recording *why the criterion mattered*: "is it shield-safe?" would have kept the loop alive — §6.2 had established that it was, with every candidate bore below TE11 cutoff above 8.8 GHz — and it was "is the benefit obtainable otherwise?" that retired it. **The gating question moves rather than disappearing**, to `OI-THCOOL-15`: whether a pad achieves real two-face contact across a curved 5–7 mm gap given the tolerance stack, the cluster-clamp hardware already occupying that gap, and compression set over repeated bowl separations for module replacement. It must also be non-magnetic and electrically insulating, because the fluxgates sit on the inner bowl and the Helmholtz coils on the outer. This composes with, rather than competes against, `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.3's second lever — that one is a conductive path to the outer shell via the existing metallic layers, this one a conductive path across the gap that precedes them; they are in series. **Neither this nor D-3 alters any safety requirement**: NP-THERM-COOL-001 §4.1 still governs — with the BN-boss via fitted, no cooling option in the study moves the scalp fraction beyond 7.9 % → 7.5 %.
- **PBM ambient block threshold moves +43 °C → +38 °C (NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001 §2 footnote ‖, NP-THERM-COOL-001 Rev 5 D-1, principal, 2026-08-30):** The T1-A band becomes **full dose ≤ +35 °C, derate +35 → +38, block > +38**, and T1-B, T1-C, the intranasal probe and the protocol roll-ups follow. T2-D (1170 nm laser) is unaffected — its TEC-set bound of +35 was already tighter. **The decisive argument was use case, not thermal:** there is no non-emergency reason to run a head-worn wellness device in a room above +35 °C, so an envelope reaching +43 bought availability nobody wants and paid for it with a derate band the design was never validated across. It also aligns the block with the physics, which +43 did not: `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §7 fits `NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001` §5.1's two published ambients and puts T1-std's full-dose ceiling at **37.9 °C**, so the old threshold sat ~5 °C beyond what the thermal design supports. **A third benefit, recorded as supporting rather than load-bearing:** `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.4 supplies an efficacy *floor* (0.02–0.3 W/cm², 10–120 J/cm², *"under-dosing, not mechanism failure, explains most nulls"*), and near the top of the old 35→43 band the derated duty approached it — so the device could run to completion and report a finished session while delivering a sub-threshold dose. **A null session is indistinguishable from a real one to the person wearing it,** which makes that failure mode worse than a refusal; the narrower band stays clear of it. The exact crossover was *not* computed (it needs the derate curve, itself provisional), which is why this is supporting and not the reason. **These rows lose their `†` "pending THERM-1a" marker and become decided**, carrying a new `‖` marker instead; the derate *curve* within 35 → 38 remains `OI-OPR-01`. With this, D-1, D-2 and D-3 in `NP-THERM-COOL-001` are all closed.
- **T1 PBM block threshold moves +38 °C → +35 °C to match T2, collapsing the T1-A derate band to zero (NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001 §2 footnote ‖, NP-THERM-COOL-001 Rev 6, principal, 2026-08-31):** This **corrects the preceding entry of 2026-08-30**, which set the threshold at +38. T2-D already blocked at +35 — its TEC cannot hold laser setpoint above that — so a customer upgrading T1 → T2 would have met a *tighter* usage limit on the more expensive tier. The two block thresholds are now the same number, and the reason is tier consistency rather than thermal margin. **The consequence is stated plainly rather than discovered later: T1-A now has no derate band at all**, because full dose was already held to ≤ +35 and blocking begins above it, so the reduced-duty region has zero width. That is a simplification, not a loss — the 2026-08-30 entry's third reason records that the derate band was exactly where a *"completed"* sub-threshold session could occur, and a null session is indistinguishable from a real one to the person wearing it. **`OI-OPR-01`'s T1-A derate curve is therefore moot: there is no curve left to specify.** T2-D keeps its own +30 → +35 derate band, so the two envelope *shapes* still differ even though the block thresholds match; only the block was asked to align. One new item: a hard block at a single temperature needs **hysteresis** so an ambient NTC resting on +35 cannot chatter start/stop — `NP-THERM-COOL-001` `OI-THCOOL-16`.
- **Gap-pad geometry specified, and §6.9's full-area assumption corrected (NP-THERM-COOL-001 Rev 6 §6.9.1, 2026-08-31):** §6.9 established that a conductive gap bridge beats the pneumatic loop, using **0.0022 m²K/W** for the pad — a **full-area** figure. Three fixed features forbid a continuous sheet: `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a's **cluster-clamp actuators** occupy the inter-bowl gap (18 clusters under SYM-1/CONTIG-1), §5.3c's **fluxgates** sit there, and §5.1 requires the bowls to **separate for module replacement**, re-compressing anything spanning the gap at every service event. The pattern is therefore **discrete pads, one per tile, co-located with `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §3.2's BN heat-pickup bosses**, in parallel with stagnant air over the uncovered fraction. **Corrected figures:** a realistic 20 mm pad per tile is 22.7 % coverage, R_gap 0.0022 → **0.0094**, tile ceiling **40.6 → 36.3**. That is **1.1× optimistic, not the ~5× a first estimate suggested** — R_gap alone degrades 4.3×, but by then the gap has stopped dominating R_out, which moves only 0.061 → 0.068. **D-2's conclusion is unaffected**: every coverage row down to a 10 mm pad still beats the stirred gap's 0.067, so coverage — not pad conductivity — is the design variable. **One mechanical problem surfaced that is not small:** the pad must compress against something rigid, and the first thing it meets on the outer bowl is the **compressible carbon-loaded absorber foam**, so a pad pressed against foam compresses the foam and never reaches rated conductivity. Either locally omit the absorber where pads land (an EMC question, since the absorber suppresses cavity resonance rather than providing primary shielding — the mu-metal and Pd-polyester do that), or make pad and absorber one part. **This couples `OI-THCOOL-15` to `OI-THCOOL-04`**: the absorber's thermal specification and the pad's land are a single decision.
- **One shared thermal envelope for the whole product line: full dose ≤ +30 °C, derate +30 → +35, block > +35 (NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001 §2 footnote ‖, NP-THERM-COOL-001 Rev 7, principal, 2026-08-31):** **This corrects the preceding entry of the same date**, which set the block at +35 and recorded that T1-A consequently had *no* derate band. Direction: *"consistency makes products easier to understand."* **Every helmet module — T1-A, T1-B, T1-C and T2-D — plus the intranasal probe now carries the identical band.** T2-D's numbers were adopted wholesale, because it was already the tightest and **a shared envelope must be the intersection of what every module can do, never the union**; adopting anything wider would have promised a capability some module cannot deliver. The result is that one sentence now describes the thermal envelope of the entire product line, at any tier, in any configuration — and the §4 module roll-up's *"tightest of all"* annotation on T2-D no longer means anything, because nothing is tighter than anything else. **Two consequences, both recorded rather than left to be discovered. (i) T1's full-dose ceiling tightens +35 → +30 — a real capability reduction**, since full dose in a 32 °C room was previously allowed and now derates. It is **deliberately more conservative than the physics requires**: `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §7 fits `NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001` §5.1 and puts T1-std's full-dose ceiling at **37.9 °C**, nearly 8 °C above the new bound. That margin is paid on purpose, in exchange for a single envelope instead of four. **(ii) `OI-OPR-01` is live again for T1-A**, reversing the preceding entry: that entry called the derate curve *moot* because the band had zero width, and the band is 5 °C wide once more, so the curve must be specified after all. The work is nonetheless smaller than it was before any of this — **one curve now serves every helmet module** rather than one per module. `OI-THCOOL-16` still applies: the block edge at +35 remains a discrete transition needing hysteresis so an ambient NTC resting on it cannot chatter.
- **The SW-02 firmware links: MCUX SDK vendored and `np_application` created (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 11 §4.8, phase 8, 2026-09-01 — closes OI-SWCI-20, OI-SWCI-21 and OI-SWCI-35):** For five phases the main-firmware CI leg could say only that every SW-02 translation unit *compiled*; §4.7 recorded why, and recorded that "zero unresolved symbols" on that leg was therefore **true and vacuous for SW-02** — no SW-02 link ran, so nothing could be unresolved. Both halves are now done. The MCUX SDK 2.16.0 MIMXRT1062 device layer is vendored under the §9 in-tree rule (six files and a licence, byte-exact from tag `MCUX_2.16.000`, per-file SHA-256, Class B so no §7.1.2 anomaly evaluation attaches — a *reachability* claim, recorded with the check that makes it observable rather than asserted), and `firmware/application/` links `np_application.elf`: **0 unresolved symbols out of a starting 102**, `.bin` 48,208 B of the bootloader's 440 KiB reservation, `__isr_vector` at `0x20210000` exactly, 0 warnings. **The link found two defects that no compile could have found, and that is the entry's point.** `np_crypto` and `np_ota_state` were being built as **ARMv4T, ARM ISA, soft-float** — not Cortex-M7, not even Thumb-only. Both deliberately set no CPU flags because they are shared with the Cortex-M0+ build, and the mechanism that supplies them (`CMAKE_C_FLAGS_INIT`) existed in `cmake/stm32g071.cmake` and had never been written into `cmake/arm-none-eabi.cmake`. Nothing had noticed because no ARM image linked either library: the bootloader carries its own `np_signature.c`, and there was no SW-02 application target at all. Separately, `firmware/hub_control/CMakeLists.txt` named a consumer, `np_application`, that did not exist, while `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` called `np_hub_control` "the top-level application target" — two statements in the tree, contradicting each other, compared by nothing. This is §4.4.2's lesson for the third time: *a defect that cannot be observed is not thereby absent*, and closing the visible one is how the next becomes visible. **The platform layer traps; it does not stub, and the distinction is the decision.** §4.4's argument against writing drivers to turn a leg green holds harder on SW-02 than SW-01 — the hub cluster-controller fan-out is unimplemented (OI-HUB-C01..C19), the hex-tile emitters unselected (OI-HEXTILE-02), the EEG net unsized (REG-1). But a stub that answers *plausibly* is the same trade with the failure hidden rather than avoided: `np_mod_visual_hal_hall_lifted()` returning "not lifted" or `np_proto_hal_get_proto_pubkey()` returning a zero key each converts a missing driver into a **defeated interlock**, and at the call site is indistinguishable from a driver that is wrong. So all 93 seams halt — and halting is the state the system is *already designed* to enter: it stops the 200 ms SPI heartbeat and the safety MCU cuts every stimulation enable line within 1.5 s, via a path that is independently implemented, independently Class C, and independently tested. The count is asserted in the build against `NP_SW02_PLATFORM_SYMBOL_COUNT`, as an equality rather than a ceiling, so the distance to a runnable image is a figure the build prints rather than something a reader reconstructs. **The image is not runnable and says so about itself**, in a `.np_build_note` string inside the binary — build logs get separated from artifacts, and "real image or bring-up artifact" is not answerable from a `.bin`'s size. **Four things measured and deliberately not fixed**, recorded as OI-SWCI-39 (nothing configures the FlexRAM partition, and the bootloader script and the SDK disagree about OCRAM's size — 512 KiB against 768 KiB), OI-SWCI-40 (62 of the 63 newly single-sourced seams are still *also* declared by a local `extern`, so the compiler compares the header against the definition and not against the caller — OI-SWCI-18's finding on the SW-02 side), OI-SWCI-41 (no board clock configuration: `SystemInit()` does not bring the PLLs to the 600 MHz `FreeRTOSConfig.h` assumes, so every FreeRTOS tick derives from a clock nothing establishes), and OI-SWCI-42 (SW-02's 162 KiB static footprint exceeds the SDK's 128 KiB DTCM, so `.bss` sits in OCRAM and loses the tightly-coupled path). OI-SWCI-35 — `np_bootloader` setting no `.elf` suffix, so its `Report image size` step had globbed zero files and exited 0 since it was written — was raised at phase 7 and left; phase 8 rewrote that exact step, so it was fixed here, on both sides as phase 7 did on the Class C leg.
- **The SW-02 platform contract is single-sourced against the caller, and a gate holds both SW items to it (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 13 §4.9, 2026-09-02 — closes OI-SWCI-40):** Phase 8 left `np_sw02_platform_hal.h` compared against `firmware/platform/`'s 93 traps but **not** against the eleven `.c` files that call them, each of which still carried its own `extern`. Fifty-nine such declarations are deleted and replaced by an include of the header that already declared the symbol; the three host-test files that *define* doubles for these seams include it too, so drift is a compile error in **both** directions — the caller's and the definition's — which is the half §4.4.1 records as why a header beat a shared test-double translation unit on the SW-01 side. **The item's own count was wrong, and the correction is a measurement, not a preference:** a parsed census finds **56 distinct seams across 59 declaration sites in 11 translation units**, not the 62 across twelve OI-SWCI-40 estimated; seven of the 63 header seams have no caller in `hub_control` at all. Phase 8's figures are left standing with a pointer rather than rewritten, the treatment §4's measured-results table already gets. **The census over all 56 found zero pre-existing disagreements** — every local declaration matched the header verbatim, parameter names included, across all three symbols declared in more than one file. Nothing had drifted *yet*, which is the same result SW-01 gave in August and is the argument for doing this before a driver exists, not evidence that it did not matter. **What it would have cost is measured rather than argued, and this is the entry's point.** On the pre-change tree, declaring `np_mod_eeg_hal_read_impedance()` as returning `int` — the definition returns `float` — built **rc=0** and **linked rc=0** with zero warnings, in the very image CI gates on zero unresolved symbols. C has no mangling and the two declarations sat in different translation units, so nothing in the toolchain was in a position to object. That is not a style defect; it is a silently misread impedance in a seam whose consumers gate contact confirmation. **The image is byte-for-byte what phase 8 measured** — 0 unresolved, `.bin` 48,208 B, `__isr_vector` at `0x20210000`, census `93 seams trapped, 0 driven`, 0 warnings — and host suites are 31/31. That is the expected result and the reason for reporting it: this change moves declarations and nothing else, so movement in any of those figures would have meant it had done something it did not intend. All eleven changed translation units were cross-compiled, not only the three the host suites reach. **Demonstrated by holding a mutation constant across the change**, the method §4.4.1 used: a test double whose parameter type disagrees with the production contract built rc=0, 0 warnings and passed 1/1 before, and is `error: conflicting types` after; the caller-side `float`→`int` drift compiled *and linked* before. **Two controls are recorded because they do NOT discriminate** — a dropped argument and a pointer passed for a scalar were caught before this change too, a local `extern` being still a prototype — and **one negative result is stated as one**: widening a return type in the header is caught at the definition site and in **none** of the three calling modules, because the implicit conversion at each assignment is legal C and `-Wconversion` is off. The header is not a substitute for `-Wconversion`, exactly as §4.4.1 said. **Two defects surfaced by the deletion rather than sought:** `np_led_state_t` was still defined in `np_hub_control_main.c` after phase 8 moved it into the header, because nothing compared the two; and OI-CVNS-HUB-09's asynchronous start/poll contract prose sat beside one of three callers, so it moved to the declaration where the other callers and the definition can see it. **The property is now a check rather than a claim in a document.** `scripts/check-platform-seam-decls.ts` covers **both** contracts — the 63 SW-02 seams and the 25 SW-01 seams whose zero had been asserted only in prose since 2026-08-10 — 88 seams over 39 translation units, with the seam names **parsed from the headers at run time and never listed in the checker**, because a restated list would be the one-value-two-places shape of Defects C and D inside a checker written to forbid it, and would fail the wrong way by going quiet as seams were added. Eight self-test cases falsify it in both directions, including a commented-out `extern` that must not be reported and the two vacuity cases — a missing header and an empty source tree — which fail rather than pass on finding nothing. It exits 1 on the pre-change tree with all 59 violations named by file and line, and 0 after. **A larger family was found and deliberately left, as OI-SWCI-43:** 84 symbols declared by an `extern` that **no header declares at all** — the `np_mod_*_{init,detect,control,shutdown,telemetry}` registry entry points of 17 module drivers, plus `np_ed25519_verify`, which does have a header. That is the same hazard with the mitigation *absent* rather than unused, but there is no declaration to compare against, so the only assertion a build could make is "a header ought to exist" — a design decision about how the registry publishes its modules, not a build property, and one that interacts with the hub fan-out (OI-HUB-C01..C19) still being unimplemented.
- **The i.MX RT1062 FlexRAM partition is now a NeurOne decision rather than whatever the eFuses said (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 14 §4.10, phase 9, 2026-09-02 — closes OI-SWCI-39, re-scopes OI-SWCI-42, raises OI-SWCI-44):** Phase 8 measured that **nothing in this repository configured the FlexRAM partition** and that the two in-tree declarations of the resulting memory map disagreed — `bootloader_imxrt1062.ld` says OCRAM is 512 KiB at `0x20200000`, the MCUX script says 768 KiB at the same address — and left it, on the reasoning that the bootloader's figure was "the conservative one" so nothing was currently wrong. **Both halves of that needed correcting, in opposite directions.** The disagreement is not one: `0x20200000` is the **dedicated OCRAM2 array**, 512 KiB, *not part of FlexRAM*, and the FlexRAM OCRAM aperture begins exactly where it ends at `0x20280000`; the SDK's 768 KiB is the composite of the two under the **default eFuse split** (512 + 256). The two numbers never described the same memory, so "conservative" was true for a reason other than the one given. What was genuinely wrong is the clause the item stated second and under-weighted: **both scripts, and `np_config.h`, asserted a partition that no code chose.** A fuse-set partition is not thereby incorrect — the defect is that it was not a decision, was not written down, and could not be checked. §4.4.2's lesson in a fourth setting, one turn worse: the defect could not even be *stated*, because the quantity it is about had no name in the tree. **The partition is derived, not preferred: ITCM 0 banks · DTCM 16 banks (512 KiB) · FlexRAM OCRAM 0 banks.** FlexRAM is 16 banks of 32 KiB (from the vendored `MIMXRT1062_features.h`, so the figure is checkable in-tree rather than read off a datasheet), and exactly three regions can consume them. Two consume nothing: no section in either linker script is in ITCM — the bootloader executes from OCRAM2 where the ROM loaded it, the application executes in place from the OCRAM2 staging area — and nothing addresses `0x20280000`, nor needs to, since the 440 KiB staging reservation is 10.7% used. DTCM is the only claimant, so it takes all sixteen; assigning banks to *unused* instead would be a decision to shrink the device's RAM to fit a bring-up image, which is exactly the reasoning §4.8.5 rejected when it declined to shrink `configTOTAL_HEAP_SIZE`. **A consequence closes the item's own complaint:** with zero FlexRAM OCRAM banks, OCRAM2 is the only OCRAM, so the bootloader's 512 KiB stops being "right by being conservative" and becomes exact by construction. **The code is `firmware/bootloader/src/np_flexram.c`, called first in `Bootloader_Reset()` as a new step B-0** — ahead of the watchdog disable, so "no code runs before the memory map is established" is checkable by reading the top of one function rather than arguable about every preceding statement. It must be the bootloader: the application's `Reset_Handler` sets MSP from a DTCM address and copies `.data` into DTCM before its first C statement. **Reassigning banks from running code is safe here for a reason that is a property of the linker script, not a promise** — `bootloader_imxrt1062.ld` declares exactly one `MEMORY` region and it is OCRAM2, so no section of the bootloader's can be in a bank the call reassigns; the script now says that where an edit would see it. **The module is split the way `np_app_image.c` is:** two pure encoders (the GPR17 bank-config word, the GPR14 window codes), checked on a host against hand-worked oracles, and three register stores compiled out on a host rather than replaced by a fake register file — a fake would let the suite pass while the real layout was wrong. Notably **the three GPR fields being written are absent from the vendored MCUX 2.16.0 header** (`MIMXRT1062.h` defines two GPR16 fields and no `CM7_CFG*TCMSZ`), so they are transcribed from IMXRT1060RM, corroborated against shipping RT1062 firmware, and the transcription itself is asserted. **One fact, four declarations, and disagreement fails a check rather than a device:** a new host suite (`np_bootloader_flexram_tests`, the 25th Class B target), five new cases in `np_app_link_agreement_tests` — which now `#include`s `np_config.h` rather than parsing it — three link-time `ASSERT`s, and two CI steps on the artifacts (that the call survived `--gc-sections`, and that `__StackTop` sits at the top of the DTCM the partition creates, recomputed in the step so it cannot become a fifth copy). Every one mutation-tested before commit. **`.bss` deliberately did NOT move into the new DTCM, and that is the judgement worth recording.** Its stated blocker is gone and 162,436 B would fit 524,288 B three times over, but DTCM exists only if three register writes to silicon nobody has run behave as documented; with `.bss` in the staging area a partition that fails to take effect costs `.data` and the stack and fails loudly inside `Reset_Handler`, whereas with `.bss` in DTCM it would also take `ucHeap`, every task stack and every static buffer, and present as arbitrary corruption later. **OI-SWCI-42 is therefore re-scoped, not closed**: what gates it is now one bench confirmation, not a decision nobody has made. **Two findings raised in passing.** `build-all.yml`'s `NP_TOTAL_TEST_COUNT` read 30 against a real total of 31 — phase 8 updated two of three copies — so the weekly backstop had been failing two of its four assertions, unseen, since 2026-09-01; that is OI-SWCI-37's shape recurring and OI-SWCI-10's reason for nobody noticing, and it is corrected here to 7 + 25 = 32 with the history left in the file. And `np_config.h`'s `NP_SCRATCH_SRAM_BASE`/`_SIZE` describe a 64 KiB OCRAM window that overlaps `.app_staging` and the bootloader stack, referenced by nothing — flagged in place as **OI-SWCI-44** rather than deleted in a phase about a different map, but exactly the shape of unowned declaration this phase exists to be suspicious of.
- **The SW-02 core clock is verified against the silicon before the scheduler starts (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 15 §4.11, 2026-09-02 — OI-SWCI-41 partially closed):** Phase 8 recorded the missing board clock configuration as prose in §4.8.5 — "not a measurement but is the same species" — and left it there. That was wrong on two counts, and both are failure modes this programme has already named. **First, it was the one missing driver the §4.8.4 census did not count.** The census exists so that the distance to a runnable image is a figure the build prints rather than something a reader reconstructs; the board clock configuration is a missing driver exactly like the other 93, and it went uncounted only because the *startup path* calls it rather than a module, so it had no seam in `np_sw02_platform_hal.h` to be counted in. A gap that the gap-counting layer does not count is the census measuring around its own blind spot. It is now `np_platform_clock_init()`, the 94th seam, trapped like the rest, and **the first platform call the image makes** — ahead of `np_hal_get_device_session_count()`, which held that position since phase 8. That ordering is not cosmetic: the boot contract genuinely breaks at the clock first. **Second, and more seriously, nothing would have caught a clock that was configured *wrong* rather than not at all.** The ARM_CM7 port defines `configSYSTICK_CLOCK_HZ` as `configCPU_CLOCK_HZ`, so the SysTick reload — and with it every `vTaskDelay`, every timeout, and the `NP_SAFETY_HEARTBEAT_MS` beat the safety MCU's watchdog is waiting on — is denominated in that one number. A core clock that is not `configCPU_CLOCK_HZ` does not fault, hang or assert; it rescales all of them uniformly, so nothing inside the system has a correct interval left to compare itself against. Two files in the tree already stated that clock and already disagreed: `FreeRTOSConfig.h` says 600 MHz, and the vendored `system_MIMXRT1062.h` says `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_CLOCK` = 528 MHz, which is what `SystemInit()` **assigns** to `SystemCoreClock` — and assigning a variable is not configuring a PLL. **The number that made this worth fixing now:** at 528 MHz the specified 200 ms heartbeat is actually sent every **227 ms**, which is still far inside the 1.5 s watchdog. A 13.6% error on the one interval the system's independent Class C safeguard watches **does not trip that safeguard** — the image would run indefinitely with every interval wrong and every existing check satisfied. A failure the existing safeguards cannot see needs its own check, which is why the check is in `main()` and not left to the watchdog. **What the check does:** calls the seam, then calls the vendored `SystemCoreClockUpdate()` — which walks the CCM and CCM_ANALOG dividers and recomputes the frequency from the registers as they actually stand — and halts unless *both* the seam's returned figure and the recomputed one equal `configCPU_CLOCK_HZ`. Both, because they are different failures: the returned value catches a driver that configured one frequency and reported another; `SystemCoreClock` catches one that reported honestly and did not achieve it. The halt is `np_platform_unimplemented()`, so a clock mismatch lands in the same designed safe state as every other unhandled condition — heartbeat stops, safety MCU cuts every stimulation enable line within 1.5 s (CLAUDE.md §4.2). **This needed no board, which is why it could be written now**; `SystemCoreClockUpdate()` was already vendored and already reads real registers. **The defect in this change's own test is the part worth remembering.** The new host test `np_app_clock_agreement_tests` probes `np_app_main.c` to confirm the boot path still contains the check. Written first as a plain `strstr()`, it **passed against a boot path with the check deleted** — because `np_app_main.c` explains the check at length in its header comment and every token the probe searched for was sitting in the prose. That is `np_bootloader_app_image_tests`' finding a second time (a text probe matching the comment that *describes* a thing rather than the code that *does* it), and it took the same fix: strip what the compiler would strip — comments and string literals both — before looking. It was caught only by deleting the call and re-running; three mutations now fail as intended. **On the stale test count:** this change independently hit `build-all.yml`'s `NP_TOTAL_TEST_COUNT` reading 30 against a real 31 since phase 8. **Phase 9 found, fixed and documented it first**, including the part this entry had not established — that the guard had been *failing* rather than merely unfired, and stayed invisible because a red `build-all` is surfaced nowhere (OI-SWCI-10). The correction is phase 9's; this change only carries the counts forward for its own new test, to 33 / 7 / 26, re-derived by enumerating ctest on the merged tree rather than by incrementing a line. **Measured, against this change's parent commit built with the same compiler** — the parent is the OI-SWCI-40 commit this branch was rebased onto, and §4.8's published `.text` 47,899 / `.bin` 48,208 came from CI's pinned toolchain, so quoting a delta across two compilers would have charged ~500 B of code generation to this change: census 93 → 94, `.text` 48,443 → 49,092 B (+649), `.data` 304 → 308 B, `.bin` 48,752 → 49,404 B (11.0% of the 440 KiB reservation), `.bss` unchanged at 162,132 B (OI-SWCI-42 untouched), 0 unresolved symbols, 0 warnings, `__isr_vector` still `0x20210000`. The growth is almost entirely the one decision to read the clock from the CCM rather than trust a constant: `SystemCoreClockUpdate()` (328 B) and the `__udivmoddi4` 64-bit division helper its fractional-PLL arithmetic pulls in (700 B), both previously discarded by `--gc-sections` because nothing referenced them. **What remains open:** the configuration step itself. There is still no `BOARD_BootClockRUN()`, because there is still no NeurOne board to configure for, and writing one now would encode guesses about the crystal, the PLL loop filter and power-domain sequencing — §4.4's argument, unchanged. OI-SWCI-41's shape has changed rather than closed: it was "nothing establishes the clock and nothing would notice"; it is now "nothing establishes the clock, the image halts rather than running on an unestablished one, and the check that will validate the driver is already in place and already exercised by mutation." The remaining work is a driver, not a decision. **Authored as Rev 13 §4.9, renumbered to Rev 14 §4.10, then to Rev 15 §4.11** — PR #310 (OI-SWCI-40) and then phase 9 (#311, OI-SWCI-39) each landed on `main` while this branch was open and took the numbers in turn; nothing in any of the three changes moved, only the numbers. The three are independent and meet only in bookkeeping: #310 compares the platform contract against its *callers*, phase 9 establishes the FlexRAM memory map, and this one adds a seam to the contract and verifies the clock.
- **Hysteresis on the ambient hard edges: Δ = 1.0 °C, anchored on the effective block, applied as a raised admission bar rather than a hold-off (`NP-THERM-COOL-001` Rev 9 §7.5, `NP-FW-POE-001` §6.1, `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2 footnote ‖, 2026-09-02):** Closes **`OI-THCOOL-16`**, raised by the Rev 6 block alignment and carried through Rev 7's shared band — a hard block at a single temperature means an ambient NTC resting on +35 could chatter start/stop. **Three independent bounds land on the same number, which is the whole argument for it.** (i) *Representation:* the shipped `adc_to_celsius()` returns whole degrees (`uint8_t`), so 1.0 °C is the finest band the sense path can express — the analog chain is emphatically not the limit at 70 ADC counts/K, ~0.04 K at 3 LSB of noise, so this is a software-table choice and is recorded as one. (ii) *Environment:* a hysteresis band suppresses cycling only when it exceeds the input's peak-to-peak excursion, and a room's own thermostat differential is 0.5–1.0 °C, so anything finer sits inside the room's oscillation and does nothing. (iii) *Availability:* the band is charged directly to the user as a wait — at 1–3 °C/h of room recovery, 1.0 °C costs 20–60 min before a denied session can be retried, and that cost is accepted, not waved away. **The obvious in-tree precedent was examined and declined:** the junction interlock's 7 °C band (`NP_NTC_CUTOFF_DEG_C` 62 / `NP_NTC_REARM_DEG_C` 55) is a *reactive fault* latch where over-cutting is free; this is a *predictive admission* gate where 7 °C would be a 2.5–7 hour lock-out, not a hysteresis band. **The mechanism is a raised bar, not a hold-off:** while latched, admission tests `ambient ≤ T_block_eff − Δ` in place of `ambient < T_block_eff`.
