That form was chosen over the naïve "deny everything until it cools" for three reasons — it is strictly more restrictive at every ambient, so `NP-FW-POE-001` §5's `min()` proof is untouched (it enters as a fourth `{0 %, 100 %}` term); it needs one boolean and one timer with no stored anchor; and it is per-protocol-correct, which matters because under `OI-THCOOL-17` a **higher**-dose protocol tolerates a **higher** ambient (34.6 °C at 120 J/cm² against 33.8 at 40), so a blanket hold-off would deny genuinely admissible sessions. **Two structural consequences, both stated rather than left to be discovered. (i) A mid-session crossing terminates the session rather than pausing it** — a resumed session is a split, partially-dosed session whose completion report would overstate delivered dose (the D-1 failure mode one level down), suspend/resume cycles *add* time-at-ceiling which is what drives CEM43, and a resume path is an automatic re-entry loop sited at the worst possible place. With no automatic re-entry anywhere, **chatter is impossible by construction** and Δ governs only the next user-initiated start. **(ii) The anchor is `T_block_eff = min(TABLE_block, POE_block)`, not the constant +35**, so the rule holds whichever way `OI-THCOOL-17` decides the per-protocol efficacy clamp — which is why 16 closed without waiting for 17. **One trap recorded because it is easy to get backwards: a margin parameter composes by `max()`, not `min()`.** Δ and `t_dwell` are therefore Class C table config and deliberately *not* in the signed descriptor; folding a descriptor-supplied Δ into the `min()` would let a stale or hostile descriptor supply Δ = 0 and erase the hysteresis while appearing to obey the rule that a descriptor may only restrict — the direction of "restrictive" inverts for a parameter that is itself a margin. **Three deliberate non-actions:** the latch does not persist across a power cycle (the dodge buys re-entry only between `T_block_eff − Δ` and `T_block_eff`, where the plain test already passes, so **it cannot cross the safety bound**, and persisting it would mean NVRAM writes for a usability latch with no safety content); **no new SHDR field** (the existing `NP_POE_OUT_OF_RANGE` reason covers it, and a new field would need a `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.1 boundary resolution first); and **no descriptor-format change**. **No envelope number moves** — the block stays at +35 and the latch can only ever restrict. Residuals, neither blocking: `t_dwell` inherits `OI-ENV-05` (60 s with a dedicated ambient NTC, ≥ 5τ_hub with the hub NTC as proxy — fail-safe either way, since self-heating reads high), and a sub-1 °C band would require the ambient path specified at 0.1 °C, which the POE block's `i16` dC encoding already assumes — `OI-POE-06` / `OI-OPR-06`. Sizing reproduced by `bun scripts/check-thermal-network.ts` §18.
- **The ambient derate holds session length fixed and refuses below the efficacy floor, rather than extending the session or ramping to zero (`NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §1, `NP-THERM-COOL-001` Rev 9 §7.4 D-4, `NP-FW-POE-001` §1.1/§3, 2026-09-02):** `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §1 had said *"linear **duty** derate T_f → T_max"* and stopped there, leaving two incompatible readings live for two revisions — a fixed-length session delivering proportionally less dose, or an extended one holding dose constant. Rev 8 raised it as `OI-THCOOL-17` and judged both readings costly. **They are not equally costly, and the deciding quantity was CEM43, not minutes.** The derate band is *by construction* the region where the face is held at the 42 °C interlock — that is what derating is for — so `NP-PWRSRC-001` §5.5's 0.25 CEM43/min at the limit applies inside it as the design condition rather than a pessimistic overlay. On that basis a routine 20-minute 60 J/cm² protocol, extended to hold dose, reaches **25 CEM43 at 34 °C and 50 at 34.5 °C — past §5.5's 40 concern line in a single session** — against **5.0 anywhere in the band** for fixed length. That is §5.5's own finding (*"the interlock caps temperature and says nothing about how long the face may stay there"*) arriving by default, as the reading of one unqualified word, rather than by anyone choosing it. **Session length is therefore fixed and dose scales with duty. The ramp is then clamped at `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.4's 10 J/cm² efficacy floor and the session is refused below it** — D-1's own principle one level down, *do not run a session that cannot work; refuse it*, because a null session is indistinguishable from a real one to the person wearing it. **One Rev 8 claim is narrowed in the course of deciding it:** §3.4's therapeutic window is 10–120 J/cm², so most of the 5 °C band derates to a *weaker* session inside the window, not a null one; only the top ~0.8 °C is pathological, which is what sized the fix at **at most 1.3 °C** off an envelope §7.2 already records as ~8 °C more conservative than the physics requires. Effective block becomes per-protocol — **33.8 / 34.2 / 34.6 °C at 40 / 60 / 120 J/cm²**. **Three consequences recorded rather than left to be discovered. (i) Rev 7's one-curve-for-every-module result survives intact** — the duty curve is unchanged and still shared by T1-A/B/C, T2-D and the intranasal probe; only *where a protocol stops walking down it* is per-protocol, so `OI-OPR-01` specifies one curve plus a **termination rule**, not a family of curves. **(ii) `OI-THCOOL-17`'s own premise is corrected in closing it.** The item said the per-protocol block *"makes `NP-FW-POE-001`'s gate need protocol dose as an input"*; the **descriptor** needs it and the **Class C gate must not have it**. Running a sub-threshold PBM session is ineffective, not hazardous, so by `NP-ENV-001` §4's own class rule it is an **Efficacy**-class bound, and siting it in SW-01 would put a clinical literature figure inside the certified boundary and re-open that boundary every time it is revised. It is a **non-dismissible Class B refusal** in SW-02/SW-03 — non-dismissible because "run it anyway" would be a choice made without the information that would inform it, and SW-02 rather than SW-03 because the session runner is on the main processor and so **Mode 3 autonomy is covered with no app present**. `NP-FW-POE-001` §1's load-bearing property is untouched: the MCU table still blocks at +35, the floor can only refuse earlier, and a descriptor overstating `dose_full_dJ` degrades its own efficacy guarantee and nothing else. **(iii) The clamp inverts dose ordering** — a 120 J/cm² protocol runs to 34.6 °C where a 40 J/cm² one refuses at 33.8 °C, so the *heavier* protocol survives the *hotter* room. Arithmetically correct (the floor binds on delivered dose and the derate is multiplicative) but it reads backwards, and it makes the only honest advice at a refusal *"a higher-dose protocol may still run"* — raised as `OI-THCOOL-18` for HFE rather than left to copywriting. It is also not free: the heavier protocol spends 2.3× the time at ceiling. **The bound is the whole distinction from the rejected reading** — that time is the protocol's own declared length, fixed at authoring time and already audited across the library by `scripts/check-thermal-dose.ts`, not a new ambient-dependent length nothing has assessed. Encoding: `NP-FW-POE-001` §3 gains `dose_full_dJ` + `duty_floor_pct` at `poe_version` 0x02 (+3 bytes), with `dose_full_dJ = 0` on non-PBM protocols keeping EEG-only and tES at their unchanged +45 envelopes. **No band endpoint moves and no safety requirement changes.** Figures: `bun scripts/check-thermal-network.ts` §17. Residuals: `OI-THCOOL-18` (HFE), `OI-THCOOL-19` / `OI-POE-07` (nothing checks `dose_full_dJ` against what the protocol commands), `OI-THCOOL-20` (T2 research arms that are sub-threshold by design), `OI-POE-08` (the floor value has no versioned owner), `OI-OPR-07` (10 J/cm² is the bottom of a 10–60 J/cm² range and may be wavelength-dependent), and `OI-THCOOL-16` rescoped to two hysteresis edges plus the unspecified mid-session crossing.

- **`ucHeap` is in DTCM, and three figures the DTCM item rested on were wrong (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 16 §4.12, phase 10, 2026-09-03 — closes OI-SWCI-42, raises OI-SWCI-45 and OI-SWCI-46):** OI-SWCI-42 asked *which buffers deserve DTCM*. **Re-measuring the record before acting on it found that three of its four claims do not survive contact with the linked artifact**, and that is half this entry's point. The **162,436 B "static footprint"** carried verbatim through §4.8.5, §4.10.6 and the item itself is `.data` + `.bss` + the **8 KiB MSP stack**: the figure came from Berkeley-format `arm-none-eabi-size`, which folds allocated `NOLOAD` sections into its `bss` column, and the stack is already placed separately by the linker script, so it was counted twice — `.data` + `.bss` is **154,244 B**. **Two of the four named largest contributors are in no image**: `np_module_map`'s `s_map` (22,536 B) and `s_nvram_scratch` (22,412 B) are dropped by `--gc-sections` because the module is unreachable from the entry point, at phase 8 and now — verified by rebuilding `100a410` — so 44,948 B of the census is sections that never shipped; it was taken over objects rather than over the artifact. And the cost it records names an **L1 D-cache that is never enabled** — `SystemInit()` enables the I-cache only and nothing calls `SCB_EnableDCache()` — so OCRAM2 access is *uncached* and the recorded cost understated it. That last one is §4.8's shape for the fourth time: **`firmware/vendor/cmsis_core/VERSION` already said so in as many words**, two statements in the tree contradicting each other with nothing comparing them. The verdict phase 8 reached survives — `.bss` in a 128 KiB DTCM overflows by a measured 31,384 B — but its arithmetic attributed the overflow to the wrong sections. **The choice was never all-or-nothing, which is what the item and both later revisits missed.** DTCM held 308 bytes of application data; of the 131,072 B that exist at `0x20000000` under the **default eFuse partition**, before any register is written, **130,764 were unallocated** while 153,940 B of `.bss` sat in OCRAM2. **`ucHeap` (65,536 B) now lives in a new `.dtcm_bss`**, taking `.bss` to 88,404 B (−42.6 %). It is the one buffer whose placement can be argued *without a profiler* on an image that cannot be profiled — it traps a few hundred instructions into `main()` — because every FreeRTOS task stack and every dynamic allocation is cut from it by heap_4, which makes its memory class a property of the **calling convention** rather than a hypothesis about a workload. The rest of `.bss` genuinely does need the bench and stays where phase 9 put it. **It needs nothing from OI-SWCI-39, and that is what answers §4.10.6 rather than overriding it.** Phase 9 declined to move `.bss` because DTCM exists only if three untested register writes work, and a failed partition with `.bss` there would present as arbitrary corruption. That is right about the 512 KiB and remains the reason the other 88,404 B stays put — but it did not separate out the 128 KiB the default fuse partition provides regardless, which is the window `.data` was already living in. `ucHeap` + `.data` occupy 65,848 B of it and leave **65,224 B**, asserted in the link as `NP_DTCM_GUARANTEED_SIZE`; and §4.10.6's corruption-vs-fault objection cannot reach it, because the MSP stack that phase 9 pinned at `0x20080000` already faults inside `Reset_Handler` under a failed partition — upstream of `.data`, `.bss` and every buffer. **Doing it properly cost a zeroing path, and that is the part that would have become a field defect.** The vendored SDK startup clears exactly one span, `__bss_start__..__bss_end__`, and `firmware/vendor/mcux_sdk/` is byte-exact under §9, so a second bss-class region is zeroed by *nothing* — and a statics-holding section nobody zeroed does not fail loudly, it holds plausible data from the previous image on a warm reset. So `np_app_dtcm_bss_clear()` runs as the first statement of `main()`, which is checkably the earliest C code in the image (`__START` is `main`, `__libc_init_array` never runs, `.init_array` is empty), and heap ownership moves through `configAPPLICATION_ALLOCATED_HEAP` — the mechanism heap_4's own comment says exists for placing the heap in a special segment — so `firmware/vendor/freertos/` stays byte-exact too. **A second finding, from auditing what else is placed by attribute: `.lpsdr4` named a region that has never existed.** `np_hd_session.c` put `s_source_power` (9,788 B) in it and said the name "names a region in the ARM linker script"; no linker script declared it, no SDRAM region or `0x80000000` address exists in the tree, and nothing configures the SEMC controller. Invisible for the same reason as the `s_map` census error — `--gc-sections` drops it. Measured with collection disabled, `ld` orphan-placed it at VMA `0x20000554`, **inside DTCM**, with a load address nothing copies from and no zeroing: a buffer annotated for 32 MB of external SDRAM would have become 9,788 B of stale DTCM on the first commit that made sLORETA reachable. The link now refuses (`ASSERT(SIZEOF(.lpsdr4) == 0)`) instead of mis-placing — **OI-SWCI-46**. **One regression only the artifact can catch, and it is the one that matters.** Reverting `configAPPLICATION_ALLOCATED_HEAP` to `0` fails *nothing*: heap_4 quietly supplies its own `ucHeap` in `.bss`, the link stays green, every linker `ASSERT` passes and the host suite passes 33/33 — measured on that mutation, `.dtcm_bss` 0 B and `ucHeap` back in OCRAM2 at `0x202315C8`, with every task stack through the uncached bus and nothing in the tree saying so. So the new CI step asserts against the **image**: `ucHeap` inside `[__dtcm_bss_start__, __dtcm_bss_end__)` and `.dtcm_bss` **exactly** 65,536 B — an equality, not a ceiling, on §4.8's seam-census reasoning — and prints the DTCM/OCRAM2 split so the distance from "all of `.bss` in OCRAM2" is a figure in the build output. It also refuses Berkeley-format `size` in a comment naming §4.11.1, because that format is how the 162,436 B error was made and is the obvious tool to reach for. Every check here was mutation-tested before commit. **Raised rather than answered:** **OI-SWCI-45** (the D-cache is never enabled — a real decision, needing MPU attributes and DMA cache maintenance, not a fix) and **OI-SWCI-46** (no external SDRAM is established, and `np_hd_config.h` specifies a further ~205 KB weight matrix for it).

- **Clinical tACS reaches all 21 driver channels: the hub wire mask is widened, and `channel_count` is validated instead of clamped (2026-09-07 — closes OI-TACS-01, raises OI-TACS-02):** The 2026-08-05 decision "T2 clinical tACS driver: 21 channels, one per cap electrode" (superseding "16-ch arbitrary waveform") was implemented for HD-tDCS — `NP_HD_DRIVER_CHANNELS = 21U`, and `k_driver_channel[]` maps all 21 cap electrodes without the aliasing that made the M1_R ring undeliverable — but never for clinical tACS itself. `np_mod_clin_tacs_params_t` carried `channel_mask_lo` (channels 0–7) and `channel_mask_hi` (8–15) and stopped there, so channels 16–20 could not be enabled over the hub wire, and every app-side encoder had followed the struct down to 16. **The fix is a wire-format change, which is why it was deliberately not attempted in the 2026-09-07 documentation consistency pass** that corrected the stale "16-ch" statements in NP-DT-001 (DI-PERF-08), NP-HEX-ZM-001, NP-HEX-ZM-ISA, NP-PWR-BUDGET-001 and NP-NPPS-REF-001 §4.12 and recorded the encoding limit in place at the three clamp sites. `np_mod_clin_tacs_params_t` now carries a third mask byte, `channel_mask_ext` — channels 16–20 in bits 0–4, bits 5–7 reserved and written clear so a later field can claim them — taking the struct from 7 to 8 bytes and moving `waveform` from offset 6 to 7. `encodeClinicalTacs()` in `app/web/src/lib/hubCompiler.ts` emits all three mask bytes; the web `NumberField` and the iOS `Stepper` take their ceiling from a new `clinicalTacsMaxChannels`/`CLINICAL_TACS_MAX_CHANNELS` = 21 in each platform's hardware-limits table rather than a literal, and the iOS stepper's `step` goes from 2 to 1 because 21 is odd and `2...21, step: 2` cannot express it. **The second half of the item is the one an author would have felt.** Nothing in any runtime validated `channel_count`, so a `.npps` file authoring 21 was silently reduced to 16 at compile time — no error, no mismatch, and a clinician left believing a montage ran that never did, the same failure shape NP-NPPS-REF-001 Rev 14 legislated against for build-time caches. All three validators (web `protocolValidator.ts`, iOS `NPProtocolValidator.swift`, Android `NPProtocolValidator.kt`) now range-check `channel_count` against 1–21 and report an out-of-range value as a hardware-source error; the encoder still clamps, but as a backstop behind that check rather than as the limit. **Guarded, not just changed:** `np_protocol_tests.c` gained `test_clin_tacs_channel_mask_spans_the_driver()`, which is the one host target that includes both `np_hub_types.h` and `np_hd_config.h` and so is where `NP_CLIN_TACS_CHANNELS == NP_HD_DRIVER_CHANNELS` is asserted — the two trees spell the count separately because the mask width is a wire contract and the hub does not include the sLORETA tree, so a change to either alone now fails the host tests. It also pins the 8-byte size and every field offset, since the encoders write those byte positions numerically and a shifted field is a silent misinterpretation rather than a build error. Four new cases in `hubCompiler.test.ts` pin the emitted bytes (all-on masks as `FF FF 1F`, reserved bits clear at every count, first-N-channels semantics, and a well-formed struct even for an over-range count); all four were confirmed to fail against the pre-change encoder. NP-NPPS-REF-001 is at Rev 15 with §4.12 and both §12 rows corrected to 1–21. **What this does NOT resolve, now tracked as `OI-TACS-02`:** NP-HW-TCAP-001, the cap wiring spec that would pin the electrode-to-channel map, still does not exist, so the firmware table remains the interim authority; and the BOM delta for the 5 additional precision current sources and their sense paths is still uncosted against the T2 BOM. Both were carried as "Open" notes on the 2026-08-05 entry; the wire format now committing to 21 channels makes them load-bearing rather than aspirational. **Not a safety change:** the T2 module drivers are still stubs (`np_mod_t2_stubs.c`), so nothing decodes this struct today, and the safety MCU's `NP_SAFETY_MAX_CHANNELS` (14) is an unrelated count — one entry per `NP_SAFETY_EN_*` enable line, with the whole clinical-stim driver being a single one (`NP_SAFETY_CH_CLIN_STIM`) — so widening the tACS electrode count does not touch the charge-density interlock's frame.

- **2026-09-08 — Generated locale files leave the repository; `locales/*.json` becomes the single committed source of every user-facing string (CLAUDE.md §17, Rev 41).** CLAUDE.md §17 has said since 2026-09-03 that canonical is the only place a string is edited, and `sync-locales.ts --check` failed CI whenever a generated file drifted from it. **That is a staleness check, not a single-source rule**, and the difference is the whole of this entry: the iOS String Catalog, the eleven web copies under `app/web/src/locales/` and the eleven `res/values*/strings.xml` were all tracked, so the same 1,420 strings were committed four times over, and each of those files sat in the working tree looking exactly like source and editable as source. **The failure that motivates this already happened once**: 26 keys existed only in the committed `.xcstrings`, 18 of them referenced by iOS source, and the NP-HFE-002 rewording of `SETUP_INSTR_ZONE_MODULES` / `SETUP_ERROR_ZONE_MISSING` was applied to the generated catalogue alone — regenerating would have reverted live copy to instructing users to listen for a bone-conduction tone the firmware no longer emits (`docs/superseded/np_fw_za_001.md`, NP-HFE-002 §7.2). A `--check` job reports that after the fact; it cannot stop the edit landing in the wrong file, because the wrong file is there to be edited. **Resolution: the three generated trees are git-ignored and regenerated at build time by each app's own build** — a `canonicalLocales` Vite plugin for web (deliberately modelled on the `canonicalProtocols` plugin that made the identical argument for the duplicated `public/protocols/` tree), the `syncLocales` Gradle task for Android, and the NeurOne target's first build phase for iOS; all three shell out to the one generator. **Where each output went, and why they differ.** Android's left the source tree entirely, into `<buildDir>/generated/res/locales` registered as a res `srcDir`, which is what a generated resource directory is for and removes any possibility of a stale `values-*` file surviving a branch switch — `res/values/themes.xml`, which is hand-written, stays where it is. Web's moved from `app/web/src/locales/` (beside the hand-written `supportedLocales.ts`) to `app/web/src/generated/locales/`, so `.gitignore` names one directory rather than a glob inside a source folder, and `i18n.ts`'s import says where the file comes from. The iOS catalogue keeps its path because `NeurOne.xcodeproj` holds a file reference Xcode resolves at project load, before any build phase runs; the phase was added to the committed `.pbxproj` **and** to `project.yml`, so regenerating the project with XcodeGen cannot drop it. **Generator output was verified byte-identical to what had been committed for all 23 files** — only location and tracking changed, no key and no string value. **`--check` is retired for `--verify-untracked`**, which asserts no generated artifact is tracked, and deliberately also guards the two retired output locations: a file re-committed there would be merged by Gradle or resolved by a stale import and win over the generated tree without announcing itself. **Three consequences carried, not discovered later.** (i) The Android and iOS builds now require `bun` on `PATH`, previously true only of the web build — `android-ci`, `ios-ci` and both compiled CodeQL legs (java-kotlin manual, swift autobuild) install it. (ii) A canonical locale edit no longer touches `app/ios/**` or `app/android/**`, so both workflows' `paths:` filters gained `locales/**` and `scripts/sync-locales.ts`; without that, a canonical change breaking the String Catalog or the Android resource merge would not have run the workflow that catches it. (iii) `web-ci` must generate before `tsc --noEmit`, which never loads `vite.config.ts` and so is the one entry point the plugin does not cover — confirmed by running it against a clean tree. Also fixed in passing: the Xcode phase was written `set -euo pipefail` and `pipefail` is not POSIX `sh`, which would have held only because macOS's `/bin/sh` is bash; the script has no pipelines and is now `set -eu`. `app/web/src/lib/i18n.test.ts` was repointed at canonical — its assertions (parses, key count, key parity across the eleven locales) are properties of the source of truth, and against the generated tree they would have restated what the generator guarantees by construction while passing just as happily on a stale copy. **Not yet reached:** watchOS, Windows and the simulator generate nothing because they read no locale file today; each becomes a further generator target when it does, not a further committed copy.
- **There is no external SDRAM, and nothing needs it — OI-SWCI-46 closed by deleting the demand (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 18 §4.13, 2026-09-08; NP-FW-HD-001 Rev 4; raises OI-SWCI-47):** Phase 10 found that `np_hd_session.c` placed `s_source_power` (9,788 B) in a section called `.lpsdr4` whose comment claimed it *"names a region in the ARM linker script"* — no script declared it, no address at `0x80000000` existed, nothing configured the SEMC controller, and `ld` orphan-placed it **inside DTCM** with an LMA nothing copies from and no zeroing. §4.12.2 made the link refuse and raised establishing SDRAM as OI-SWCI-46, scoping the answer as *"SEMC configuration plus a region"*. **It is neither, and the item closes the other way round.** **(1) There is no part to configure.** No SDRAM appears in any BOM — `NP-COST-001` has no memory line at all — and there is no schematic, pinout or timing set. SEMC initialisation needs a specific part's row/column/bank counts, CAS latency, `tRP`/`tRAS`/`tRFC`/`tRC`/`tXSR`/`tWR` and refresh interval, so writing it now would be inventing register values for silicon nobody has run — the trade `firmware/vendor/mcux_sdk/VERSION` already refuses on the clock sequence. Selecting the part is not firmware's call either: a 16-bit bus is ~40 signals at up to 166 MHz beside a µV EEG front end inside the CLAUDE.md §4.3 shielding claim, with BOM, layout and refresh-power consequences reaching §4.5. **(2) The name may not denote anything this processor can talk to.** Checked against the vendored device layer rather than from memory: `MIMXRT1062.h` declares exactly one external-memory controller, **SEMC** (`SEMC_BASE = 0x402F0000`), whose SDRAM interface is single-data-rate over an 8- or 16-bit port (`SEMC_SDRAMCR0_PS`: *0b0..8bit / 0b1..16bit*), and there is no MMDC or other DRAM controller peripheral anywhere in the header. So CLAUDE.md §4.1's "32MB LPSDR4" is either low-power SDR SDRAM (buildable, non-standard spelling) or LPDDR4 (no controller at any price). **Not resolved here — §4 is locked and this is a hardware-specification question — raised as OI-SWCI-47.** **(3) And the whole demand fits on-chip several times over, which is what actually decides it.** `s_source_power` (9,788 B) plus the weight matrix `np_hd_config.h` sizes (205,548 B) is **215,336 B**, against a measured **312,716 B free in the 440 KiB OCRAM2 staging region** and ~439 KB of unallocated established DTCM. A 32 MB part would have carried **0.64 % of itself**; the external memory was never load-bearing, and what the `.lpsdr4` attribute bought was not capacity but a link that could not succeed. `s_source_power` is now an ordinary static in `.bss` — **not** DTCM, because §4.12.3's rule stands that only `ucHeap` can be argued without a profiler and sLORETA has no better claim than the rest of `.bss`. **A correctness fix came free:** `.bss` is inside the one span `__STARTUP_CLEAR_BSS` clears, which the orphaned `.lpsdr4` never was — verified on the artifact, `s_source_power` at `0x20263f7c`. **The gate gets the timing it could not have before.** §4.12.2 deliberately omitted `KEEP()`, correctly, because the array still carried the attribute and keeping the section would have fired the assert on a buffer nothing used — but the cost was that the ASSERT could only fire once a `.lpsdr4` buffer became **reachable**, the exact condition that hid the original defect. With the section empty at source `KEEP()` is free and the gate fires when a buffer **exists**: measured on the normal `--gc-sections` build with an unreachable 64-byte `.lpsdr4` object, §4.12.2's script **links green** and §4.13's **fails the link**. `np_app_link_agreement_tests` gained a check on the `KEEP()` itself, mutation-tested (exit 1 without it, 0 with it). **A fourth finding, of the cheap variety:** three more comments — `g_proto_buf` "in LPSDR4 RAM (32 MB)", the parsed session descriptor, the parsed command — described a placement the compiler was never asked for; all are ordinary statics that have always been in `.bss`, and §4.12.1's own census had measured `g_proto_buf` there at 6,144 B in the same document that repeated the claim. Unlike `.lpsdr4` they could never have moved a byte, which is why they were free to stay wrong. **The image does not change** — `s_source_power` is unreachable and was gc-dropped before and after, every section byte-identical to the phase-10 baseline built at the same absolute path (§4.12.5) — which is precisely why the gate, and not the artifact, is the deliverable here. Host suite 33/33; cross-build green, 0 unresolved symbols. **Deliberately not done:** the 205 KB weight matrix is still unallocated (its Config-partition loader does not exist; §4.13.3 records where it goes when it does), and CLAUDE.md §4.1 / `docs/ABBREVIATIONS.md` are untouched — firmware not needing the memory is not the same claim as the hardware not having it.
- **Android gains the consent dashboard: five privileged consent methods stop being unreachable, and all five waivers are deleted (2026-09-08 — closes OI-CONSENT-01, raises OI-CONSENT-04 and OI-I18N-01):** iOS shipped `ConsentDashboardView`; Android's only consent UI was `ConsentOnboardingScreen` plus a blanket-consent toggle, so `grantClinicianAccess`, `revokeClinicianAccess`, `acceptInvitation`, `declineInvitation` and `withdrawFromStudy` were defined in `ConsentStore.kt`, unit-tested, and callable by nothing — **the CLAUDE.md Rev 37 shape at five methods instead of one**, with the consequence that a clinician grant made on iOS could not be revoked from an Android device and §6.2.5's withdrawal-granularity guarantee had no Android path at all. `app/android/app/src/main/kotlin/life/neurone/app/ui/ConsentDashboardScreen.kt` now ports the iOS screen: clinician grants (list, add, revoke behind a confirmation naming clinician and organisation), research-consent status with the L3 posture control and the §6.2 irreversibility notice, the study-participation audit trail with per-study withdrawal, and pending invitations with an accept/decline detail screen whose accept path repeats the invitation's own irreversibility notice (iOS ISC-79 parity). The stub `ConsentDashboardScreen` is removed from `Screens.kt`. **The waivers came out with it, because the gate does not let them stay:** `scripts/check-consent-reachability.ts` fails a waiver whose gap has closed, so the five `waived: { android: "OI-CONSENT-01" }` entries were deleted in the same change and the gate now reports no waiver in force. That left the self-test unable to exercise the waiver machinery at all — its two waiver cases had been leaning on whichever waivers happened to be live — so `audit()` now takes the surface table as a parameter and those two cases run against a fixture, plus a new ninth case pinning that the same fixture *without* a waiver is caught on Android. **The proof that waivers work must not vanish when the last gap closes.** **Three deliberate departures from iOS, recorded rather than absorbed.** (i) L3 posture stays a switch on Android rather than iOS's one-way "Stop pre-approving studies" button: Android could already re-grant blanket consent from this tab and removing that would have been a regression. Turning it *off* now shows the same confirmation copy iOS shows and routes through the same named `withdrawBlanketResearchConsent()`, so the §6.0 analytics teardown is explicit at the call site as well as guarded at the store's ingestion point. (ii) The dashboard's L2 category list is **read-only** — status checkboxes, not editing controls. L2 edits belong to Research Preferences and must commit through `updateResearchConsent()`, which is what carries the §6.2.5 blanket true→false transition guard; a second write path on the dashboard would have bypassed it, which is the 2026-08-16 defect re-created in a new place. (iii) The grant form shows the tier's derived can-see / cannot-see UHDR element lists (§6.1 minimum necessary) where iOS shows a use-case picker — **because iOS discards that picker's selection when it builds the grant.** `approvedElements` comes from `tier.uhdrElements` on both platforms and `selectedUseCases` is read by nothing, as are `ConsentEngine.minimumNecessaryElements(for:)` and `consentDocument(...)`. Porting the picker would have ported a control that decides nothing, into the one screen §6.1 says must state what a clinician can and cannot learn. That iOS defect is left as found and raised as **`OI-CONSENT-04`** rather than fixed here, because making the picker load-bearing changes the `ClinicianConsentGrant` schema and narrows existing grants below their tier ceiling — a consent-model decision, not a UI fix. **A localization blocker was found and fixed to get here.** Ten consent-surface format keys still carried iOS-only `%@` placeholders in canonical. `%@` matches no `{n}`, so `sync-locales.ts` copies it through verbatim to both platforms — harmless on Apple, which is exactly what `String(format:)` wants, and a **crash** on Android, where `Resources.getString(id, args)` runs `java.util.Formatter` and throws `UnknownFormatConversionException`. The ten (`CLINICIAN_GRANT_TIER_FORMAT`, the eight `DASHBOARD_*_FORMAT` keys, `INVITATION_STUDY_ID_FORMAT`) are now canonical `{0}`/`{1}` in all 11 locales, generating `%1$s` for Android and `%1$@` for Apple; **no iOS call site changed**, because positional and non-positional `%@` consume the same arguments in the same order. Ten more such keys remain on session, setup, consumable and zone screens and are tracked as **`OI-I18N-01`**, together with the question of whether `check-locale-strings.ts` should reject `%@` in canonical outright. Locale bookkeeping: `CLINICIAN_GRANT_CAN_SEE_HEADING`, `CLINICIAN_GRANT_CANNOT_SEE_HEADING` and `CLINICIAN_GRANT_RESEARCH_TIER_NOTE` added to all 11 locales (the last because the Research tier's element set is IRB-defined per study descriptor, so "nothing" would be wrong in both directions); `AND_UI_PRIVACY_RESEARCH` and two sentence-fragment keys the stub concatenated deleted from all 11, the fragments replaced by the whole `CONSENT_IRREVERSIBILITY_NOTICE`. Canonical key count is unchanged at 1420. **What is and is not verified.** `check-consent-reachability` (live + 9-case self-test), `check-locale-strings`, `check-gate-coverage`, `check-ci-scope`, `check-section-refs`, `check-platform-seam-decls`, `check-redaction-shape` and `tsc --noEmit` all pass; every `R.string` reference in the new screen was checked against the generated `strings.xml`, and every `stringResource` argument count against the generated format specifiers. **The screen is not compiled** — this environment has no Android SDK — so `:app:testDebugUnitTest` in `.github/workflows/android-ci.yml` is the first compile, exactly as the iOS half of `OI-NPPS-MOBILE-01` was left unbuilt for want of an Xcode toolchain. Consent element, category and tier labels render from `:core`'s English `displayName`, as every other Android consent screen does: `:core` is pure-JVM by design and cannot name `R.string`, which is the standing `app/android/core/` entry in `check-locale-strings.ts` PENDING_PATHS and not a new gap. **Merged across the #319 boundary.** This work was authored before "Generated locale files leave the repository" (the entry immediately above) landed, so its first two commits edited the three generated trees — the iOS String Catalog, the eleven web copies and the eleven `res/values*/strings.xml` — as tracked files. The merge takes #319's deletion of all 23: the canonical `locales/*.json` edits carry the same content, and each platform's build regenerates from them, so nothing is lost and `sync-locales --verify-untracked` passes. One consequence is worth naming because it is not obvious: the simulator bundle's `sources-sha256` is computed over a source graph that now reaches a **generated** file (`app/web/src/generated/locales/en.json`, statically imported by `i18n.ts` as its fallback catalogue), so `build-simulator-runtime.ts --check` is only meaningful after `sync-locales.ts` has run — which is exactly the order web-ci uses. The fingerprint moved twice in this branch for that reason, once for the locale edit and once for #319 relocating the file it hashes.
- **§6.1's clinician access-expansion workflow is built, and the tier setter behind it stops being able to grant access to the past (2026-09-09 — closes OI-CONSENT-02, raises OI-CONSENT-05 and OI-CONSENT-06):** OI-CONSENT-02 asked whether to build §6.1's expansion workflow or delete the `expandClinicianAccess()` that was the only part of it ever written — a public method, on both platforms, with no caller anywhere, not even a test. **Built.** §6.1 is locked and names the workflow in detail, so deleting the method would have left a locked specification with nothing behind it; and OI-CONSENT-01 closed the day before, so both platforms now have a consent dashboard to host the workflow. **The choice was not a wash, because the method was worse than unimplemented.** `ClinicianConsentGrant.approvedElements` derives from `tier`, and a tier is timeless. Setting it — the whole body of the old method — hands the clinician each newly added element over *every session ever recorded*. §6.1 requires retroactive access to be a separate decision; the method took that decision silently and took it yes, and no model on either platform could express the other answer. Building the workflow therefore had to start at the data model. **`ClinicianAccessScope`** (elements · effective-from · `includesPriorData`) makes a grant a sequence of access decisions instead of one timeless tier, and `elementsVisible(forDataRecordedAt:)` (Kotlin: `elementsVisibleForDataRecordedOn()`) is the predicate that makes the retroactive answer mean anything. A grant with **no** scopes — every grant written before this — falls back to *the tier's elements, from the grant date, prior data included*, which is exactly what a bare tier has always meant, so nothing existing is silently re-scoped; that fallback is a compatibility choice, not a decision about what an initial grant should mean, which is why OI-CONSENT-06 exists. **`ConsentEngine.accessDifferential()`** is the differential consent document — newly visible · already visible · still withheld, three sets that partition the element roster (asserted). It returns nothing in three cases, and the callers treat that as refusal, not a formatting problem: a change that adds nothing; a change that would *remove* an element, which is a re-scope and would be described by an expansion document only in terms of what is gained; and either side being the **Research tier**, whose `uhdrElements` is empty because a research tier's elements are IRB-defined per study descriptor — reading that emptiness as a set would describe a research grant as seeing nothing and a move away from one as adding everything. **`ClinicianAccessExpansionRequest`** is §6.1's persistent notification: persisted beside the grants, because one the user loses by backgrounding the app is not a notification, and still pending after a question, because asking is not deciding. It is withdrawn when its grant is revoked, and `approveExpansion` fails closed when the differential no longer holds, so a stale request can never apply a change the user was not shown. **The store surface**: `addExpansionRequest` · `approveExpansion(requestID:includePriorData:)` · `denyExpansion` · `askQuestionAboutExpansion`, and **`expandClinicianAccess` is now private on both platforms**. It is gone from `check-consent-reachability.ts`'s SURFACE for that reason and not because it was deleted — the file says so in place. Private is a stronger guarantee than the `pending` declaration it replaces: the gate can say a public method has no caller today, never that nothing may call it tomorrow, and the hazard the open item named was precisely something calling it without the consent document. **Both UIs ask the two decisions on separate steps** — approve answers only the forward-looking question, and the history question follows on its own screen with both answers as buttons of equal weight — because one screen carrying an Approve button and a history checkbox presents one decision with a modifier on it. A *from today onwards only* answer stays visible afterwards on the grant row, or the user cannot tell which of the two answers they gave. 12 new unit tests per platform, including that yesterday's sessions still show only the old tier's elements after a forward-only approval — the assertion the old implementation could not have passed. 23 canonical locale keys added across all eleven files (§17). **Deliberately not built, and raised rather than absorbed:** nothing ingests a request and nothing delivers a question, because the clinician-portal channel does not exist (`OI-CONSENT-05`); and the *initial* grant still does not elicit its retroactive posture separately (`OI-CONSENT-06`). **The Android UI and the iOS half are not compiled here** — this environment reaches no Android SDK (`dl.google.com` is refused by the sandbox's network policy) and has no Xcode toolchain, so `android-ci.yml` and `ios-ci.yml` are their first compile. `:core` was compiled and its 23-test suite run green by excluding `:app` locally; every `R.string` reference and `stringResource` argument count was checked against the canonical keys by hand.
- **The L1 D-cache stays off, and the cache state stops being something nothing checked (NP-SW-CI-001 Rev 19 §4.14, phase 11, 2026-09-08 — closes OI-SWCI-45, settles half of OI-SWCI-15, raises OI-SWCI-48):** OI-SWCI-45 was raised for a documentation defect — four places described `.bss` in OCRAM2 as reached "through the bus and the L1 D-cache" and `SystemInit()` has never enabled a D-cache — and it asked the design question underneath: should it be? **It stays off, and that is now a decision rather than an omission.** **The wrong claim was inherited, and there were five of it.** `firmware/vendor/mcux_sdk/.../system_MIMXRT1062.c` line 125 reads *"Enable instruction and data caches"* above code that enables one, with no `__DCACHE_PRESENT` branch anywhere in the file; that comment is the likeliest origin of §4.8.5, §4.10.6, OI-SWCI-42 **and** `app_imxrt1062.ld`'s own `.bss` rationale, which the item's count missed and which was still uncorrected when the item was raised. Verified on the artifact rather than by reading: `SystemInit`'s disassembly touches `SCB->CCR` (`orr #0x20000` — the **IC** bit, never `#0x10000`) and `ICIALLU`, and **no D-cache maintenance register in `SCB+0x25C..0x274` is written anywhere in the image**. One statement had it right the whole time — `firmware/vendor/cmsis_core/VERSION` — and because the divergence lives in byte-exact SOUP that §9 forbids patching, the fix has to be a **comparator**, not a fifth correction. **The decision, with its four preconditions named.** Enabling the D-cache needs: MPU region attributes (nothing in the tree calls an `ARM_MPU_*` function, so the ARMv7-M default system address map is in force and makes the whole of OCRAM2 Normal **write-back write-allocate**); cache maintenance around every DMA buffer (there is none, because there is no DMA — 94 platform traps, and even the bootloader's eMMC driver is PIO rather than ADMA); a staging handover contract — which is **already OI-SWCI-15**, open since 2026-08-09 asking in as many words to "confirm D-cache clean / I-cache invalidate happens after staging", 24 rows above OI-SWCI-45 in the same table with neither ever naming the other, which is §4.8's "two statements compared by nothing" occurring *inside the open-items list*; and a board, since the image traps a few hundred instructions into `main()` and cannot be profiled. **The value also shrank the day before the item was raised.** TCM does not go through L1 at all, so §4.12's move of `ucHeap` into DTCM put every FreeRTOS task stack and every dynamic allocation outside this decision entirely. What is left is 105,820 B of OCRAM2 data — `.bss` 88,404 + `.rodata` 17,416 — of which **58.6 % of the `.bss` half is one buffer nobody has profiled** (`s_session_pool`), against 20,480 B of already-identifiable DMA-facing buffers (`s_blob`, `g_proto_buf`, `s_uhdr_buf`, `s_shdr_buf`) that would every one inherit write-back write-allocate. **What is new is that the state is checkable.** `firmware/application/include/np_app_cache.h` states it once; `np_app_cache_assert_state()` reads `SCB->CCR` as the **first statement of `main()`** and halts into the safety-MCU cutoff on either mismatch — 28 bytes of code, and deliberately a check rather than a configuration step, because the ROM, the bootloader's PIO staging copy of this very image and `Reset_Handler` all ran before it, so an image that "fixed" CCR would be repairing the successor of a fault it had already suffered. A new host suite holds the header, the vendored startup's *code* (not its prose) and the CMSIS SOUP record against each other and probes the boot path; a new CI step censuses the **linked image**. **A second record defect, found while writing it:** §4.12.4's "the startup cannot be taught about a second bss-class region" is wrong — `__STARTUP_INITIALIZE_NONCACHEDATA` is a switch the same file already tests for, in the same `#ifdef` idiom the build exploits twice. It is still not `.dtcm_bss`'s mechanism (it initialises the `NonCacheable` section and copies an init image `.dtcm_bss` has none of), and naming it matters because it **is** the migration path the D-cache decision would take. **The regression only CI catches:** adding `SCB_EnableDCache()` to the image passes the host suite **34/34** and fails the image census, measured — 1 `DCISW` write at `SCB+0x260`. The census carries two positive controls, so blinding it fails loudly rather than reporting a clean image it can no longer read. **Raised rather than answered: OI-SWCI-48** — CLAUDE.md §4.1's "~1.1% CPU at full load" is supported by no measurement in either direction, `NP-FEAS-FNIRS-001` §3 already leans on it, and changing a locked figure on this section's strength would swap one unmeasured number for another.
- **The T2 cap map gets a controlled hardware source of truth, and specifying the conductor's rating surfaced three limits that cannot all hold (NP-HW-TCAP-001 Rev 1, 2026-09-09 — closes OI-TACS-02 half (i), answers and re-scopes half (ii), raises OI-TCAP-01…07):** `OI-TACS-02` had two halves, both inherited as "Open" notes on the 2026-08-05 21-channel decision and both made load-bearing by `OI-TACS-01`'s closure on 2026-09-07, which committed the hub wire format to 21 channels the hardware had never been specified to have. **Half (i) — NP-HW-TCAP-001 did not exist — is closed.** The document is written. §3 specifies the electrode-to-driver-channel map as the identity map (`REQ-TCAP-02`, 21 rows with electrode name, Jurcak MNI coordinate and channel), which **removes `k_driver_channel[]` from the role of interim authority**: a C array in IEC 62304 Class B firmware had been the source of truth for a hardware contract that Class C limits are derived against, inverting the intended direction. From Rev 1 the direction is that the document specifies, firmware implements, and on a disagreement the firmware is wrong. **`REQ-TCAP-03` keeps the ~60 mm non-aliasing rule live rather than historical** — never alias two electrodes within one ring radius — because §7 leaves per-channel cost unpriced and a future costing could reopen sharing; it is the rule the retired 16-channel map violated five times (Cz↔Pz, C4↔P4, T8↔P8, P7↔O1, P3↔O2), and **every one of those five reads as reasonable**, which is why §6 makes it mechanical. **Guarded, not just written:** `scripts/check-tcap-map.ts` (`REQ-TCAP-07`, gated in `tooling-ci.yml`) diffs §3 row for row against `k_electrode_names[]`, `k_electrode_mni[]` and `k_driver_channel[]`, checks the row count against **both** `NP_HD_DRIVER_CHANNELS` and `NP_CLIN_TACS_CHANNELS` — `np_protocol_tests.c` already asserts those two equal each other, so this adds the third corner, and two-of-three agreeing is exactly what `OI-TACS-01` was — and **re-derives `REQ-TCAP-03` from the coordinates** rather than trusting the map to be an identity, so the rule still bites if sharing returns. Falsified before trusted per `NP-CONV-001` §8: ten fixtures perturbing one channel, one name, one coordinate, the row count, each constant separately, a shared channel between neighbours, an out-of-range channel and an unparseable table, plus the vacuity case; and separately falsified against the real tree by reintroducing the C4/P4 alias. **Half (ii) — the BOM delta — is answered, but not as asked, and the half is under-scoped.** No silicon has ever been named (the 2026-08-05 entry's own words: *"no BOM line, and no named silicon"*), so there is no per-channel figure to multiply and inventing one would look like a costing. §7 inverts the question instead: the **materiality threshold is $305/channel** (5 channels through Pro Entry's 1.639 multiplier against its +$2,499/unit; Pro Full's threshold is $1,165, so Pro Entry binds) against a **record-derived ceiling of ≤$26.75/channel** — Pro Entry's $833 old BOM less Home Standard's $405 leaves **$428 for every T2 hardware addition together**, all of it credited to the tACS driver alone. A factor of 11 on an absurdly conservative bound: **the delta cannot flip either Pro row or materially dent either margin**, stated falsifiably so that selection above $305/channel voids it. §7.3 then finds the half **scoped to the wrong term, in the same shape as `OI-COST-07` against `OI-HUB-C08`**: if the 16-channel baseline never had a BOM line either, the uncosted quantity is the whole 21-channel stage, not the 5-channel delta. **The conclusion survives the re-scope but not by the same argument, and the document says so rather than carrying the number over**: the $26.75 ceiling located the driver inside the $428 envelope, which this premise denies, so reusing it would be circular. What survives is the threshold, which never depended on that decomposition — 21 channels at $305 is **$6,403, or 2.9–3.0× the entire Pro Full BOM floor** ($2,136–2,198), excluded by inspection. Only the scope is wrong (`OI-TCAP-04`). §7.4 names the **larger uncosted term next door that no costing owns**: `OI-EEGNET-07` decides whether the T2 cap tail is **23 conductors or 44** (`NP-HW-EEGNET-001` §6.4 — two conductors per dual-rated site if concurrent TMS and full 21-channel tES is required), a doubling of a per-unit harness for which `NP-COST-001` §2 A-1's table has no row at all. Recorded as `NP-COST-001` Rev 3 §5.1; **no figure in its §1–§4 or §8 is revised, term U is still excluded, and no price is set.** **The finding neither half asked for.** Writing the per-conductor rating required knowing whether 4 mA into one of these electrodes is permissible, and it is not answerable from the record: on `NP_HD_ELECTRODE_AREA_CM2` = 0.0962 cm², `NP_HD_MAX_CURRENT_UA` (2 mA) is **207.9 A/m² — 34.6×** `NP_HD_MAX_ELECTRODE_DENSITY_A_M2` (6.0 A/m²), and delivering 2 mA within that density would need a **20.6 mm** pellet rather than 3.5 mm, which is an EEG dimension carried into a stimulation duty; and the 40 µC/cm² per-phase limit caps a sinusoid at 3.85 µC × π*f*, i.e. **6.0 µA at 0.5 Hz, 0.48 mA at 40 Hz and 1.21 mA at 100 Hz**, so the ≤4 mA clinical tACS ceiling is **unreachable across the entire frequency band** — the binding limit is frequency-dependent and no constant in the system expresses that. Worse, `NP_HD_MAX_ELECTRODE_DENSITY_A_M2` and `NP_HD_ELECTRODE_AREA_M2` have **no production consumer at all**: the sole reference is a FAI assertion that the constant is ≤ 6.0, a tautology over a `#define`. **Not a live hazard and not a regression** — the T2 module drivers are stubs (`np_mod_t2_stubs.c`), no cap exists, and the safety MCU's charge-density interlock is an independent enforcement path this analysis does not touch — but it is a specification defect that must not reach a cap drawing. **Deliberately not resolved:** the three resolutions are a cap redesign, a clinical capability reduction, and a revisit of the 6.0 A/m² safety basis for 3.5 mm HD electrodes, which is an EE-plus-clinical decision with regulatory surface (`OI-TCAP-01`, `OI-TCAP-02`); both are recorded in `np_hd_config.h` at the constants themselves so the next reader cannot take them at face value. **Scope held deliberately narrow:** the document specifies *wiring*, not the cap — mechanics, sizing across head circumference and connector are `OI-TCAP-05`, and the T2 cap turns out to be **absent from `NP-ART-001` §2's artifact register** entirely (`OI-TCAP-06`, one miss against that register's own `OI-ART-06` completeness question), routed there rather than fixed here because adding an artifact row carries tooling, risk-register and FAI consequences. Also raised: `OI-TCAP-07`, the tACS frequency band disagreeing between CLAUDE.md §3 (0.5–40 Hz) and `NP-NPPS-REF-001` §4.12 (0.5–100 Hz, which the validators enforce). **No locked decision is changed, no published cost figure is revised, and no code behaviour changes** — 34/34 firmware host tests green.
- **`OI-CHARGE-05` closed by splitting one ceiling into two, and the charge interlock enforces for the first time (2026-09-15 — also closes `OI-FMEA-07`; raises `OI-CHARGE-06`, `OI-CHARGE-07`, `OI-FMEA-08`, `OI-FMEA-09`):** Four defects, one root cause. **The numeral 40 was plausible in two different readings, and the code had committed to neither.** All three clients computed `I(mA) × t(s) / A(cm²)` — which is **mC/cm²**, because mA × s = mC — and compared it against a constant named `tdcsMaxChargeDensityUCcm2`. The safety MCU genuinely enforced 40 **µ**C/cm². So the two sides were **1000× apart**, and the name is what hid it. **(a) The resolution is not to pick one number, because they are ceilings on different quantities.** 40 µC/cm² is a real and correct **per-phase PULSED** limit (Shannon 1992 / McCreery 1990); the mC figure is a real **per-session DC** limit. Both survive, each attached to its waveform class, and the constants are renamed to carry the unit **and the period** — `tdcsMaxSessionChargeDensityMCcm2`, `pulsedMaxPhaseChargeDensityUCcm2` — because each ambiguity had hidden a different defect. **(b) `np_charge_monitor.c` is waveform-aware.** DC channels (tDCS, HD-tDCS) accumulate `|I| × dt` across the session against **150 mC/cm² × the declared electrode area**. Charge-balanced channels (BES/tACS, VNS, cervical VNS, clinical tACS) **never accumulate** — net delivered charge on them is ~zero, so a session integral of magnitude is not a dose, and under the retired model they would have tripped in **0.4–1.0 s** at their rated currents. They get a per-heartbeat **predicate** on charge per phase (amplitude × phase width) against **40 µC/cm² × the declared area**. A channel may be both: `NP_SAFETY_CH_CLIN_STIM` carries HD-tDCS and clinical tACS on one enable bit, so classes **OR together** and both checks run rather than the last one written winning. A new 76-byte `np_safety_chan_wave_cmd_t` carries the classification and phase duration; **only the classification crosses** — both density constants stay resident on the Class C MCU, as OI-CHARGE-02 established. **The sinusoid's 2/π factor is load-bearing, not a refinement**: at the 0.5 Hz bottom of the tACS band, where the half-period is a full second and phase charge is largest, it is the difference between a protocol passing and failing, and the integer form (2000/3141) rounds the result **up** so the approximation errs strict. **A new fail-closed declaration gate** clears any *electrical* channel whose waveform class was never declared, so a lost setup frame costs the modality its session rather than its monitoring — and it needs **no heartbeat arming bit**, where OI-CHARGE-03 and -04's geometry gates each needed one: those ask a per-session question only the hub can answer, this asks a per-channel question fixed at compile time (`NP_SAFETY_CH_ELECTRICAL_MASK`), so a silent hub cannot exempt itself. **(c) The interlock was inert, and is not any more.** `np_hub_control_main.c` called the heartbeat with `current_ua = NULL, channel_count = 0`, so the MCU's accumulate loop never ran: **`OI-CHARGE-01` was logged CLOSED with only its safety-MCU half wired**, and everything DI-SAFE-01, FMEA SW01-M03 and NP-FW-BENCH-001's *"never bypassable"* said about this control was describing something that enforced nothing. Modality modules now publish each commanded current beside the `np_safety_spi_request_enable()` they already make, so the publish and the enable cannot drift apart, and the heartbeat task snapshots the array every beat. **(d) The citation chain was circular and is not any more.** `NP-DT-001` DI-SAFE-01 — a design *input* — cited *"CLAUDE.md §4.2; NP-SW-001 SW01-M03"*; SW01-M03 asserted the figure with no source; CLAUDE.md locked it with no source. Under 21 CFR 820.30(c) a design input whose source is the document asserting it is not yet a design input. `NP-DT-001` **§3.2.1** now derives both ceilings from anchors outside this repository: **DI-SAFE-01a** (40 µC/cm² per phase) gets the Shannon/McCreery citation it always should have had, unchanged in value; **DI-SAFE-01** (150 mC/cm² per session) is derived from the conventional large-pad human tDCS envelope in `docs/tdcs_database_full.csv` — 204 of its 237 studies state current, area and duration together, median 68.6 mC/cm², and the most-exposed large RCT protocol in it is Brunoni ELECT-TDCS 2013/2017 at 144 mC/cm² — with a 35× margin to Liebetanz et al. 2009's measured rat epicranial DC lesion threshold of 5,240 mC/cm². **The clinical question was weighed, not inherited.** At 40 mC/cm² the routine **2 mA × 20 min on 35 cm² pads (68.6 mC/cm²) was not available**, and 13 of the 14 shipped predefined tDCS protocols exceeded it. That is not a defect in the protocols: 68.6 is the single most common protocol in the literature and the median of the database above. At 150 the whole shipped library is admissible and the ceiling still binds at roughly 31 minutes of 2 mA on a standard pad. **This was NOT resolved by inflating declared pad areas** — those are physical facts about the hardware, and OI-CHARGE-04 exists to stop software assuming them. **Found while doing it, and fixed:** all three clients compared with `>` where the safety MCU trips at `>=`, so a protocol landing exactly ON the ceiling was one the app signed and the device then cut — the OI-CHARGE-04 fidelity defect in its smallest form. Both comparators now match the enforcer. **`OI-FMEA-07` closed in the same pass** (NP-FMEA-001 Rev 7): §3.3 re-authored against the module that exists and **risk scores CHANGED**, which Revs 5 and 6 deliberately did not do. The two ADC failure modes are replaced by the hazard they were standing in for — delivered current diverging from commanded — and FMEA-M03-03 keeps its **S5/P2 = 10 UNACCEPTABLE-initial** score with the cause corrected to *commanded current never reaching the monitor*, mitigated now by controls that exist. The module is named a **commanded-dose** control and the substitution's real justification is stated for the first time: **signature independence, not privacy** — a measured value cannot be signed in advance, so a Class C cutoff keyed to one would inherit Class B's integrity. `OI-FMEA-04` closed NOT APPLICABLE (no ADC to bench-validate). **Verified:** firmware host suite **36/36**; web **319/319** across 13 files (a new `pulsedPhaseCharge` suite plus the rescaled tDCS suite), `tsc` clean, production build clean; Android `:core` **compiles and 256/256 tests pass**, including all eight new and changed charge tests — run under a JDK 21 toolchain override because this environment has no JDK 17, CI still builds 17; locale, section-ref, redaction-shape and consent-reachability gates all pass. **Not compiler-verified:** iOS — no Swift toolchain here, so `ios-ci.yml` is its gate; the diff was reviewed against each declaration by hand, which caught one real error (`block.modality` → `block.modalityType`). The hub sources are ARM-cross-only and compile in no host test, so each was additionally syntax-checked with `gcc -Wall -Wextra` against the real include set — that caught a genuine `-Waddress-of-packed-member` unaligned-pointer hazard in the new per-phase call, fixed by copying into an aligned local. **Four successors raised rather than absorbed:** `OI-CHARGE-06` (the 150 is derived but not regulator-reviewed, and a current-density criterion may belong alongside it), `OI-CHARGE-07` (three product-electrode areas are PROVISIONAL firmware constants, never measured; the BES pad is the one that matters, at 64% of the per-phase ceiling at 0.5 Hz), `OI-FMEA-08` (both requirement documents specified a 95% ramp-down margin neither implementation has), `OI-FMEA-09` (FMEA-M03-02's residual rating is conditional on a cross-check that is specified but not built — recorded explicitly, because an unstated dependency is exactly how Rev 5's unbuilt control went unnoticed).

- **`OI-CHARGE-04` closed by authored electrode geometry, not by picking one of the two numbers (2026-09-09):** The 40 µC/cm² tDCS charge-density ceiling was computed against two different electrode areas — iOS/Android `tdcsDefaultElectrodeAreaCm2 = 35.0` (DHF Rev 11, "a standard tDCS sponge pad") in the app pre-flight, `NP_ELECTRODE_AREA_CM2 = 25` (OI-CHARGE-02) in the safety MCU that actually stops the session. Never a safety hole; a fidelity defect, and the app was the permissive side. **Three findings corrected the entry that raised it, and two of them changed which resolution was right.** (a) **The divergence was ~2.8×, not the 1.4× recorded**, because the app used a different *model*, not just a different number: `NPProtocolValidator` divided by `35 × electrodeCount`, summing every electrode's area into one denominator, where charge density is per electrode — the full session current passes through each electrode of a pair. 70 cm² against 25 for one pair (a session running to ~36% of its authored duration, not 71%), 210 cm² for three (~12%). **This is why option (1), harmonize the constant to 25, was rejected: it would have left 2× of the 2.8× in place.** (b) **Option (2) had already been three-quarters built.** The raising entry costed a per-protocol authored area as "real firmware work … the signed-protocol wire format and the Class C charge monitor"; OI-CHARGE-02/-03 had already delivered `np_safety_chan_limit_cmd_t.area_mcm2[14]` — every channel, not just HD-tDCS — and a `np_charge_monitor_set_channel_area_mcm2()` generic over channel index. What was missing was only that nothing populated the tDCS slot. (c) **Three app sites carried the 35, not the two the entry counted**: `app/android/core/.../NPHardwareLimits.kt` had it with no divergence note at all. **Resolution — option (2).** `np_mod_tdcs_params_t` gains `electrode_area_mcm2` (uint16 milli-cm², 6 → 8 bytes); `NP_HUB_PROTO_VERSION` 0x0002 → 0x0003, so a stale descriptor fails as `NP_HUB_ERR_BAD_VERSION` at header verification rather than as an opaque `INVALID_ARG` at command dispatch; `np_session_runner.c` scans tDCS commands and populates `area_mcm2[NP_SAFETY_CH_TDCS]` exactly as it already did for HD-tDCS montages, taking the **smallest** declared area when several tDCS commands appear, since the accumulator is shared across the session; `np_charge_monitor_geom_gate()` now covers TDCS. **The gate's second heartbeat bit is the load-bearing design choice.** `NP_SESSION_STATUS_GEOM_REQ_TDCS` (bit 3) is a *separate* bit from OI-CHARGE-03's `GEOM_REQUIRED` (bit 2) because the gates are per-channel: one shared "geometry required" flag would have meant a session declaring tDCS geometry gated off **clinical tACS**, which shares the `CLIN_STIM` enable bit and declares no geometry of its own. Both bits ride every heartbeat, so a lost frame disarms neither. **A tDCS command that declares no area is refused, not defaulted** — the hub rejects `electrode_area_mcm2 == 0` outright and the gate holds the channel off — which is the OI-CHARGE-03 fail-closed precedent applied to the channel where the two sides disagreed. **Neither 25 nor 35 changed, and that is the point.** 25 stays the MCU's fallback for channels declaring no geometry, which tDCS is no longer one of; 35 became the app's *authoring default* for a standard sponge pad — now transmitted and enforced rather than assumed at one end only. So the DHF Rev 11 record stands, where option (1) would have required a change note abandoning it. **Web gained the check it never had:** `hardwareLimits.ts` carried `tdcsMaxChargeDensityUCcm2: 40.0` and checked nothing against it for want of an area, so the web could author and compile a protocol iOS and Android rejected. **Verified:** 34/34 firmware host tests (three new safety-MCU tests pin the tDCS gate, the gate independence from CLIN_STIM, and per-session re-arming); 309/309 web tests, including six new charge-density tests that pin the *model* — that adding electrode pairs never relaxes the check, and that a smaller declared pad is stricter — and two `.npps` round-trip tests; all nine CI gates. **Not compiler-verified:** this environment has no Swift toolchain and no Android SDK, so `ios-ci.yml` and `:core:testDebugUnitTest` are what compile those changes; every new symbol was checked against its declaration by hand, and the iOS/Android validator tests that asserted the old summed-area math were rewritten to construct the boundary they always claimed to be testing (the old `chargeDensityBorderlineValid` fixture passed only *because* of the defect: 2 mA for 20 min is 34.3 µC/cm² across 70 cm² but 68.6 µC/cm² per electrode). **Raised, not fixed, while closing this: `OI-CHARGE-05`** — the 40 µC/cm² ceiling is ~1000× below every protocol in `docs/tdcs_verification_ledger.csv` (Nitsche & Paulus 2000 is 8,571 µC/cm², Batsikadze 2013 is 68,571), gives a 35 cm² pad a 1400 µC budget that 2 mA exhausts in 0.7 s, and cannot fund the 30 s ramp the firmware itself enforces as a minimum. The literature states this limit in **mC/cm²**, exactly 1000× larger. It is a CLAUDE.md §3 locked invariant cited by DI-SAFE-01, SW01-M03 and four other documents, so it was left alone and routed to Regulatory / Safety SW. OI-CHARGE-04's plumbing is indifferent to the outcome: the density constant lives on the Class C MCU as `NP_CHARGE_LIMIT_UC_CM2` and never crosses SPI. **The strongest evidence for OI-CHARGE-05 is a side effect of this change and is reported rather than suppressed:** the fourteen shipped predefined tDCS protocols were given honest `electrode_area_cm2: 35` declarations, and **thirteen of them now fail hardware validation** (51.4 to 102.9 µC/cm²; the one survivor is the only 1 mA protocol in the set). They were already being cut off by the safety MCU at ~0.7 s of a 20-minute session under the old 25 cm² budget — the shipped library has never been runnable on device — so this is the defect becoming visible, not a new one. They keep their declarations and fail loudly; inflating the declared areas or shortening evidence-based durations to make the ceiling satisfiable would restore exactly the silent-truncation failure OI-CHARGE-04 exists to remove.

- **CORRECTION to the `OI-CHARGE-04` entry above (2026-09-09, same day):** That entry's account of the code change stands unaltered — the wire field, the version bump, the per-channel geometry gate on its own heartbeat bit, and the reasons neither 25 nor 35 changed are all as written. **What it got wrong is the `OI-CHARGE-05` finding it reported at the end**, and the error is worth recording because it inverted which side of the app/enforcer boundary is defective. **(i) The 1000× error is a divergence, not a shared understatement.** The entry said the 40 µC/cm² ceiling is ~1000× below the evidence base and implied both sides suffer from it. In fact all three clients compute `I(mA) × t(s) / A(cm²)`, and `mA × s = mC`, so **that expression yields mC/cm²** and is compared against 40 — the app has been enforcing **40 mC/cm²**, the clinically recognised human tDCS figure, with only its message label reading "µC/cm²". The safety MCU genuinely enforces 40 µC/cm². **So the app and the enforcer are 1000× apart, and the app's ceiling is the defensible one** — which also means the dominant app/enforcer divergence was never the 2.8× area-and-model gap OI-CHARGE-04 closed. **(ii) The literature citations were correct numbers under a wrong label.** Nitsche & Paulus 2000 is 8.57 mC/cm², reported as "8,571 µC/cm², 214× the ceiling"; against a 40 mC/cm² ceiling it **passes comfortably**. Only the 2 mA protocols exceed it — Batsikadze 2013 at 68.6 mC/cm² — which is a real and debated clinical limit, not an arithmetic artifact. **(iii) The "already being cut off at ~0.7 s" claim was false.** `np_hub_control_main.c:164` calls the heartbeat with `current_ua = NULL, channel_count = 0`, so the MCU's accumulate loop never runs: **the charge interlock is inert today.** `OI-CHARGE-01` is logged CLOSED but only its safety-MCU half was wired. The shipped protocols were not being truncated on device; they would be, the moment the hub half lands. **(iv) Two findings were missed entirely** and are now in `OI-CHARGE-05`: the same cumulative `|I| × dt` accumulator is applied to BES/tACS, VNS, cervical VNS and clinical tACS, which are charge-balanced biphasic and therefore accrue ~zero net charge — a session integral of magnitude is not a dose for them, and per-phase is the meaningful quantity (which is what 40 µC/cm² is genuinely the right ceiling for); and the inert interlock in (iii) means DI-SAFE-01, FMEA SW01-M03 and NP-FW-BENCH-001's *"never bypassable"* currently describe a control that is not enforcing. **None of this changes OI-CHARGE-04's code**, which is why that entry's technical account is left standing rather than amended: declared geometry is correct under any ceiling, and the density constant stays resident on the Class C MCU. **What it does change is the priority**: the 1000× divergence and the unwired interlock are both larger than the defect OI-CHARGE-04 was raised for. Full diagnosis, including the waveform-aware resolution shape and the 2 mA × 20 min consequence, in `OI-CHARGE-05`.

- **§6.3's per-project study workflow gets a device-side ingestion gate, and the invitation stops being something the device is handed (2026-09-10 — closes OI-CONSENT-03, raises OI-CONSENT-07):** `OI-CONSENT-03` recorded that `addInvitation()` had no caller outside a unit test on either platform, and that §6.3's per-project contact workflow — NeurOne reviews a study, generates the eligible list, sends a personalised invitation the device must receive — had no sync layer and no descriptor fetch behind it. **Resolved by building the device's half, not by finding the method a caller**, on the same reading that decided `OI-CONSENT-02`: §6.3 is locked and names the workflow step by step, and the one piece of it that existed was **worse than unimplemented**. `addInvitation(StudyInvitation)` accepted a *finished* invitation — approved element set, the plain-language “what they CANNOT see” list, and the irreversibility notice — on trust from whatever called it, and in doing so took four decisions silently and took each in the affirmative: that §5.3 step 1's signature on the study descriptor need not be checked; that §5.3's **k≥10 and ≥1-week date-rounding floors** need not be met, which only the device can enforce because §5.3 puts the anonymisation on the device; that the user's own L1/L2/L3 state need not be consulted; and that **the party asking for access may write the sentence describing what it cannot see**, which drifts from the element list by construction the moment either changes. It also held invitations **in memory only** — no persistence key, no `save()` on any of the three methods — while `acceptInvitation` wrote a participation record that *did* persist, so after every restart the audit trail and the inbox disagreed, and the same descriptor could be ingested and accepted a second time. **What enters the device now is the descriptor.** `StudyDescriptor` (studyID · title · categories · requested elements · `kAnonymity` · `dateRoundingDays` · `issuedAt` · `signature`) is §5.3 step 1's object; the `StudyInvitation` the user reads is **derived** from it on-device once the signature verifies, and `cannotLearn` is the complement of `approvedElements`, computed rather than asserted. Only `studyTitle` remains server-supplied text — it names a specific study the way a part number names a part (§17) — and the irreversibility notice is `CONSENT_IRREVERSIBILITY_NOTICE`, the §6.2 copy, resolved from a locale key at render (which is what ISC-79's “notice for that specific study” had been standing in for). **`ConsentEngine.admit()` is the gate, and its order is load-bearing:** signature first, so nothing a descriptor *claims* — its categories, its element list, its anonymisation parameters — influences any later step of an unverified one; then §5.3's floors, which are facts about the study; then L1, because §6.2.1 makes a contact method the shared precondition for **all three** delivery paths and not only the one that asks a question; then a per-study already-decided check, because §5.3 and §6.3 step 6 make withdrawal block future descriptor processing and re-presenting a study is the device forgetting an answer the user gave; then posture. **The posture is the finding that made this a data-model change rather than a wiring job.** §6.2 locks that an L3 user “still receives per-study *engagement* notifications, **not consent requests**”. So the same study reaches an L2 user and an L3 user as two different objects, and **the default on silence is opposite**: an unanswered consent request means *not participating*; an unread engagement notification means *participating*, because L3 already answered. One shape for both — which is what `StudyInvitation` was — has to pick one of those defaults for everyone, and it had picked the L2 one, so an L3 user would have been re-asked a question they had told the product to stop asking, and counted out of a study they were in. `StudyInvitation.Posture` holds both: an engagement notification records participation **at ingestion**, and its “decline” is a withdrawal through the existing `withdrawFromStudy`, which is also §6.2's “can opt out per-study”. Accepting one is refused — it was never a question, and would file a second participation for a study the user is already in. **The rest of the store surface:** `ingestStudyDescriptor` returns a `StudyDescriptorAdmission` naming its refusal reason, because “nothing appeared” is the one outcome a user cannot tell from “nothing was sent”; §6.3 step 4's **third response** exists (`askQuestionAboutInvitation`, leaving the invitation open — asking is not deciding, the rule `askQuestionAboutExpansion` already held for §6.1); invitations persist under `np.consent.study-invitations`; `recordParticipation` is idempotent per study; and the participation record carries the **real descriptor hash**, produced by the verifier from the bytes it checked, instead of the literal string `"pending"`. **Both dashboards render the posture rather than leaving it to be inferred from the buttons**, and 12 canonical locale keys were added across all eleven files (§17). `check-consent-reachability.ts`'s SURFACE loses `addInvitation` and gains `ingestStudyDescriptor` (`pending`, `OI-CONSENT-07`) and `askQuestionAboutInvitation` (`ui`); 15 methods, no waiver in force, gate and self-test green. **Deliberately not built, and raised rather than absorbed — `OI-CONSENT-07`:** there is still no descriptor fetch, no signing key and no outbound channel for the question. **But the inbound end is no longer open by default,** which is the substantive difference from the item this closes: `StudyDescriptorVerifier`'s only implementation is `RefusingStudyDescriptorVerifier`, injected by default, so **every device today refuses every descriptor as `verifierUnavailable`** and a transport cannot be wired up without also supplying §5.3's signature check. That makes skipping the check unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged. **Not decided here, and named so it is not read as decided:** whether a blanket *withdrawal* should permanently bar all future descriptors independently of the user's current consent state (§6.0's “stops ALL research data flows”) — today the gate refuses on `!hasAnyResearchConsent`, which is the conservative reading and covers withdrawn-everything and never-consented alike, but distinguishing “never asked” from “said stop” needs a field `ResearchConsentState` does not carry and is a policy decision, not a code tidy. **17 new unit tests per platform** (iOS 28→45, Android 23→40), including that a forged descriptor asking for k=1 is refused as forged and not as out-of-range — the assertion that pins the check order. **`:core` compiled and its 212-test suite run green here** by building it as a standalone Gradle project; **`:app` and the iOS target are not compiled here** — `dl.google.com` is refused by this sandbox's network policy so no Android SDK is reachable, and there is no Xcode toolchain — so `android-ci.yml` and `ios-ci.yml` are their first compile, and every `R.string` reference and `stringResource` argument count was checked against the generated `strings.xml` and `Localizable.xcstrings` by hand. **No locked decision is changed.**

- **2026-09-13 — CLAUDE.md Rev 43: the always-loaded core drops from 38,680 to 34,153 bytes (−11.7%); no design decision changed.** Fourth application of the Rev 33 core/subsidiary split, taken under one criterion: **a figure that cannot be quoted from the core without first opening another file does not belong in the core**, because there it can only be misremembered. Four relocations. **(i) §17 → `docs/reference/localization.md`** (~3.9 KB, the largest): the core keeps the rule, the surface table and the five write-time invariants (text in `locales/*.json`; the three per-platform files are git-ignored build outputs, never edited or committed; module-level tables hold keys; `MODALITY_<ID>_NAME`/`_DESC`; firmware exempt) and now says to read the detail file **before adding a key** — the generator mechanics, iOS two-hook requirement, `bun`-on-`PATH` consequence, placeholder/plural rules, not-translated list and `PENDING_PATHS` reach are §17.1–§17.6 there. The rule's trigger — any non-firmware code generation — is unchanged. **(ii) §2.1's BOM / COGS / GM% columns + the ★ box contents → `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.1**: the core keeps name, tier, retail in force and modalities included (what identifies a configuration; those names have 231 uses across the tree) plus the invariant that every T1 row is GM-negative and every figure is a floor excluding term **U**. Rev 40 had kept the table because "a cost or margin question usually starts there"; that holds for the identity columns and fails for the money ones, since §2.1's own rule forbids quoting any figure without first reading `docs/np_cost_001.md`. §2.1a's illustrative figures went with them; the core keeps the shape of the finding (break-even binds before margin, both Pro rows profitable today, `OI-COST-08`, `OI-COST-09`). **(iii) §4.3, §4.4, §4.5, §4.7 → `docs/reference/hardware-detail.md`**: per-layer shielding dB, fit-system ranges/materials, the mode/draw/PD/runtime table, status-LED behaviour. §4.1, §4.2 and §4.6 did not move — §4.2 is a live constraint and the most-cited hardware section.
