ECR-EMMC-002 now covers the Config column. Re-verified at 512 with 0 violations across 513 interrupted runs; the 256 case is kept as a must-fail test. Still open: lookahead_size, block_cycles, name_max/attr_max against EMMC-FS-01.
- **OI-LFS-10 CLOSED — the Config littlefs instance adopts EMMC-FS-01's remaining values; ECR-EMMC-002 APPLIED, NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 2 → 3 (2026-09-24, principal; NP-SOUP-LFS-001 Rev 6 §13.9, DHF Rev 85).** Supersedes the Rev 2 NeurOne choices `lookahead_size` 512, `block_cycles` 500 and unset `name_max`/`attr_max`: the Config instance now uses 64, 200, 64, 256 and states `metadata_max` 4,096, as EMMC-FS-01 prints. Why: agreement with the controlled specification — none of the four was a power-loss hazard, and Rev 2's reasons for its values were sound but outweighed. `inline_max` falls to 256 B; `ukmd.rec` stays inline. NP-FW-EMMC-001 is amended by `editscripts/patch_emmc_ecr002_prog512.py` to print `read_size`/`prog_size` 512 in all three columns, which makes EMMC-FS-01 consistent with EMMC-UHDR-05; the superseded 256 is retained in each cell. All three littlefs instances now equal the specification exactly.
- **OI-LFS-11 CLOSED — UHDR session logs are one file per session (2026-09-24, NP-SOUP-LFS-001 Rev 7 §13.10, DHF Rev 86).** Supersedes `np_log_backend.h`'s one append-mode log file per partition, which littlefs's 2 GiB `file_max` would have capped at under a third of UHDR. Decided: the layout NP-FW-EMMC-001 already specified — UHDR `/uhdr/sessions/<count>` per session (EMMC-UHDR-12/-13), created exclusively and never reopened (a reused count must not append to or truncate an earlier session's file); SHDR stays one file because its 512 MiB partition cannot reach `file_max`. The session logger increments the count at session start (EMMC-SHDR-09) and steps past existing files; persisting the count is raised as OI-LFS-12. New platform seam `np_log_hal_part_close()`, census 98 → 99.
- **NeurOne's eMMC block-device `erase()` is a no-op; littlefs `block_cycles` stays as specified (2026-09-24, NP-SOUP-LFS-001 Rev 8 §13.11 — the host-decidable half of OI-LFS-07).** littlefs's contract leaves an erased block's state undefined and an eMMC has no erase-before-program requirement; the power-loss sweeps pass with an erase that never touches the medium. This removes upstream #1083's erase-group question. OI-LFS-07 stays open for the silicon tests HW-LFS-01…05.
- **OI-LFS-12 CLOSED — the device session count lives in the Config partition as a replicated record (2026-09-24, NP-SOUP-LFS-001 Rev 9 §13.13, DHF Rev 89).** Implements EMMC-SHDR-09 (*incremented in Config partition at session start*). Committed before each session's UHDR file is created, loaded at boot; `np_hal_get_device_session_count()` (OI-HUB-MAIN-03) retired, census 99 → 98, because the count is first-party data in a first-party store. The cervical fault summary now records the current session's count.
- **OI-LFS-04 / OI-NVRAM-10 CLOSED — options A and B together: per-tile drive is bounded optically in hardware (2026-09-24, Safety + Hardware Engineering; NP-SOUP-LFS-001 Rev 10 §13.14, NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 13 D-9, DHF Rev 90).** The question was whether anything but firmware and the 62 °C thermal cut bounds a tile's emitter drive; the answer was no (§13.12). **Decided:** `REQ-TDRV-01` — CH_A/CH_B on-current regulated to a fixed hardware reference, sized so worst-case on-irradiance is ≤ 400 mW/cm². `REQ-TDRV-02` — a gate-path limiter holding conduction ≤ 50 % over any window ≥ 250 ms, so the average is ≤ 200 mW/cm², the CW ceiling, with no emitter data needed. Option C (accept the thermal cut) is not taken. `OI-HEXTILE-23` decided (regulated). Supersedes §4.3.1's plan for CW as DC at ~75 mA: under B, CW is ~50 % PWM at the cap (`OI-HEXTILE-25`). **Decided, not built** — implementation and verification are `OI-HEXTILE-24`, and `RISK-FWHUB-10` stays open until then.
- **Every thread in the thermal/power group terminates in heat, not watts — `OI-PWR-01`, `OI-SINK-02`, `OI-SINK-05`, `OI-POE-09` and `OI-PWR-11` CLOSED; the TMS coil gets its first electrical specification (2026-09-15, NP-PWR-THERM-001 Rev 1, GitHub Issue #336).** Issue #336 grouped five threads on the stated grounds that *"resolving any one of them alone just moves the unspecified quantity somewhere else."* With `NP-THERM-SINK-001`'s `SPEC-SINK-01` finally supplying that quantity, the grouping turns out to be a finding: **four of the five were being argued as supply questions and every one of them terminates in a rejection limit.** **(1) The PBM ceiling in the unit the governor needs.** `NP-PWRSRC-001` §4.1 put the sealed-cavity ceiling at **27.6–49.1 W** from a model with no N in it. Under `SPEC-SINK-01` it is **31.4 W across the whole lattice, 8.9 W at N = 6 and 17.9 W at +35 °C** — and since `NP-THERM-CFD-N1-001` §6 puts authored montages at N = 5–37, the number that matters is **3.1× below the bottom of §4.1's band with a spreader and 5.9× below it without**. §4.1's band is the fully-lit corner of a surface, quoted as a property of the assembly. **CLAUDE.md §4.5's *T1 peak ~45–50 W* is therefore an electrical peak the assembly cannot spend** (38.4 W device draw fully distributed, 15.9 W at N = 6): a 45 W brick already covers it, and the 65 W rung is the T2 *intent* signal §2.2 says it is and nothing else. **(2) `OI-PWR-01` CLOSED, not narrowed.** `NP-THERM-CFD-N1-001` §11 recommended NARROWED with a stated precondition — *"it should not gate anything that `OI-N1-02` does not gate first"* — which was met when `OI-N1-02` closed on 2026-09-08. Its analysis is adopted in full and only its disposition changes: nothing remains under its number that is not carried by name by `OI-R1-01` (mesh independence), `OI-R1-02` (THERM-1b bench) or `OI-SINK-01` (the spreader, BLOCKING), and a fourth name pointing at three owned items invites a mesh CFD to be sequenced against a term that no longer binds. **(3) `OI-SINK-02` discharged by pairing, not overwrite.** It asked for `R_SINK_DEFAULT` 0.5 → 1.08 and recorded why `NP-THERM-SINK-001` would not do it — *"it would silently move every published figure in `NP-THERM-CFD-N1-001`"*. Both requirements are met by carrying both values: `R_SINK_SPECIFIED = 1.08` is added alongside, and `rSinkNote()` prints at every report line naming either that 0.5 is N1's published value and is superseded. **The defect was a superseded number being readable without its label, not a constant having the wrong value.** **(4) `OI-SINK-05` — the per-protocol split does not survive its own re-run.** N1 §6a reported *4 inadmissible at any heatsink / 14 heatsink-recoverable* over 23 protocols, computed at `R_sink` 0.5 with the external film double-counted. Re-run against `SPEC-SINK-01`: **22 of 22 zone-fixed protocols are thermally bound (§6a found five power-bound), 3 are inadmissible at any rejection resistance, 4 are recovered by the SPREADER rather than by a heatsink, and 21 of 22 hold no tiles at all on the bare shell that ships today.** N1 §6a's phrase *"recoverable on the heatsink alone"* has no referent after `SINK-D-2`; what they are recoverable on is `SPEC-SINK-04`, a lamination process and a BOM line against a margin-negative T1, which is `OI-SINK-01`, **still BLOCKING and more so after this**. `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §9.3 consequence 2 — that aggregate thermal risk *"cannot"* bind because the power envelope permits ~6 tiles — is now wrong for **every** authored protocol. **(5) The T1 wall-plug path is CLOSED, not deferred.** `NP-PWRSRC-001` D-1 was stated reversibly *"on evidence, via `OI-PWR-01`"*, and §4.3's escape clause required a **materially higher** ceiling to make even one rung of the supply ladder spendable. The specified ceiling is **lower**, and lower in the direction that matters most — at the montages the library authors, not at the fully-lit corner nobody runs. D-1, D-2 and §7's source set are adopted as decided. `OI-PWRSRC-07`'s transient qualification (70–125 W for an isolated session) was separately refuted by `NP-THERM-CFD-N1-001` §7 and must not be quoted. **(6) CLAUDE.md §2.2 needs no change, and the reason is recorded rather than left as an absence.** The issue's premise — a ladder keyed to *peak draw* must move when the ceiling moves — is right, and the consequence is the opposite of how it sounds: **a charger ladder keyed to peak draw and a governor denominated in sustained watts are different quantities that were never in conflict.** The appearance of conflict came from §4.5 carrying only one of them. §2.2 is untouched; what needs the thermal-bound column is §4.5. Two consequences do follow: no marketing copy may read the ladder as a **capability** ladder (at T1 the 65 W rung is above the ceiling twice over — `OI-PWRTH-04`), and §2.2's EU note (*"any PD-compliant charger must work"*) turns out to be nearly free, because a 30 W brick loses nothing at any authored montage. **(7) The TMS coil has an electrical specification for the first time — `SPEC-TMS-01…05`.** `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §4 found TMS absent from the electrical model entirely and raised `OI-PWR-03` for *an actual coil-inductance and capacitor-bank calculation* in place of its E ∝ B² scaling. Delivered: **figure-8, two 25 mm wings in series, 10 turns each, L = 18.2 µH, `R_ac` 11.7 mΩ, Q 69; a 28 µF / 1,600 V bank; 36.0 J/pulse at 0.5 T; a 142 µs series ring; `I_peak` 1,989 A; dB/dt 22.1 kT/s; 169 W of average recharge at 10 Hz biphasic-with-recovery.** It **reproduces §4.1's scaling estimate at both ends of the field range from inputs that estimate shares none of** — 1.4 J at 0.1 T against ~1–2 J, 36.0 J at 0.5 T against ~16–40 J, 423 W and 134 W monophasic against ~400 W and ~130 W — and `check-power-envelope.ts --validate` pins those correspondences so the specification cannot quietly stop being the thing §4.1 estimated. **Biphasic-with-recovery is specified rather than left open**, because monophasic needs 430 W of device draw and no USB-C PD class delivers it; the topology is what makes the row expressible as a PD contract at all. **(8) The coil's own thermal wall is (1) at the other tier, and it is the reason #336's grouping was right.** Q 69 means **9.2 % of the stored energy is dissipated in the winding per cycle — 3.30 J/pulse, 33 W during a 10 Hz train**. The adiabatic headroom of a 200 g coil assembly from 25 °C to the **same 42 °C applied-part limit the PBM tiles are held to** is 1,700 J, i.e. **516 pulses against a 3,000-pulse protocol (5.8× over) and 1.2× over for a single iTBS block**. Given a number, **the supply is the easy half** — 176 W is an ordinary 240 W EPR contract; what is not ordinary is 33 W of I²R at the scalp with **no cooling path specified anywhere** and with CLAUDE.md §4.3 requiring the window beneath it to be a **non-conductive** CFRP window, which forecloses the obvious metallic spreader exactly as `SPEC-SINK-04`'s graphite film must be cleared against the same stack. **`OI-PWRTH-01`, BLOCKING.** **(9) `OI-PWR-02` quantified, confirmed and re-marked BLOCKING.** `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §4.2 compared 0.1–0.5 T against a clinical figure-8's ~1.5–2.0 T and said the target *"may be too low"*. **That comparison is not like-for-like in either direction.** In NeurOne's favour, the induced field goes as **B·ω** and this coil rings in 142 µs against ~250 µs, so it converts **1.8× more of each tesla** into induced field — a factor §4.2 charges it for and it does not owe. Against it, and larger: **a 25 mm wing falls off far faster with depth than a 45 mm one** (0.48 against 0.76 at 20 mm), which §4.2 does not charge it for at all. With both credited and only the ratio quoted, **0.5 T reaches 17 % of the reference coil's maximum cortical field and is 4.1× short of 120 % RMT**. Closing it by field alone needs **2.06 T → 613 J/pulse → 2,886 W**, which no PD class, mains accessory or head-worn thermal path in the programme reaches. **So the TMS supply question has two answers 17× apart, and which is right is not a power question at all**: §9's T2 + TMS row is written at the lower answer (**~176 W, 48 V/5 A, 240 W EPR**) and **labelled**, never averaged between them. **(10) `OI-PWR-04`'s third option assessed explicitly, and declined for a fourth.** §4.4's second PD sink on the hub was the lead candidate and its **whole case was scoping the Mode 3 loss to the modality that forces it**. At 176 W nothing scopes it — no power bank sources 176 W, so TMS is outside Mode 3 by arithmetic whatever the inlet count is — while `OI-PWR-12`'s cost (a 5 A switched aggressor at the occipital arch, inside the assembly whose **measured** shielding is the primary technical claim) stands unchanged. **Resolution: put the PD inlet on the TMS driver's own enclosure**, where §8.4's 33 W and `SPEC-TMS-02`'s 1,600 V bank already are. The head-worn hub stays single-inlet, so CLAUDE.md §1's wired-first USB-C and T1 Mode 3 are untouched, `OI-PWR-12` does not arise, the path stays SELV behind a certified brick, and `OI-PWR-13`'s dual-source arbitration is unneeded for the head-worn assembly. Note also that **a shared single 240 W EPR rail is feasible now and was not before** — §4.3 rejected it against an *unbounded* requirement, not a large one. `OI-PWRTH-07` (passive vs active applicator) decides between the two. **(11) `OI-PWR-11` decidable at last.** CLAUDE.md §2.2 ships Pro Full two 65 W bricks against a §4.5 row negotiating one 100 W EPR contract, and §4.4.2 listed three readings the document set could not distinguish. **2 × 65 = 130 W against a 176 W requirement — short by 36 %** — so **reading 2 (a dual-inlet architecture assumed and never specified) is REFUTED**, which means §4.4 was a proposal rather than a specification being rediscovered. Reading 3 survives only for a 1170 nm applicator (65 W covers 44–46 W and nothing at TMS); reading 1 (an undocumented spare) survives and is the only reading consistent with both §4.5 and `SPEC-TMS-04`. The residual choice is commercial, not electrical — `OI-PWRTH-03`, with `OI-PWRSRC-15`. **§2.2 is not modified.** **(12) `OI-POE-09` CLOSED in both halves — the one thread in #336 that is not about heat.** `NP-THERM-COOL-001` Rev 11 withdrew a Rev 10 claim that an efficacy-floor refusal *"sets the §6.1 latch … exactly as a thermal denial does"*, because that mechanism is specified nowhere. **(a) Ownership: two independent latches, one per IEC 62304 class.** The shared-latch option buys `NP-FW-POE-001` §6.1's *"one boolean and one timer"* property and pays for it by making a **certified** object writable from an **efficacy** computation — and §7.4.4's own argument for siting the clamp in SW-02 is that an efficacy computation inside the Class C boundary re-opens it every time `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.4's floor is revised; a **write** into Class C state is that same coupling through a narrower aperture, not an escape from it. Cost: exactly two booleans and two timers, bounded and known. **What replaces the withdrawn claim, stated so it is not assumed a second time: a floor refusal sets the SW-02 efficacy latch, a distinct object from the §6.1 Class C hysteresis latch; the two never write to each other; admission requires both clear, composed at the admission test in §5's existing `min()` shape.** **(b) Sizing: the band is chosen in dose — `Δ = max(1.0 °C, 50/D °C)`**, i.e. **re-admit only at ≥ 2.0× the efficacy floor**, composed by `max()` exactly as §6.1 requires margins to enter. Δ = 1.0 °C came from `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §7.5.1 on three grounds — ADC representation, thermostat differential, re-arm wait — **none about dose**; carried onto this edge it happens to buy 1.8×–3.4×, defensible in every row and chosen in none. A **pure** dose rule is unimplementable above 50 J/cm², because `OI-POE-06` records that the shipped `adc_to_celsius()` returns whole degrees, so a specified 0.42 °C would silently become 1.0 °C with nobody having chosen it. The `max()` is what makes the rule chosen where the sense path can express the choice and safe where it cannot. It changes behaviour **only at or below 50 J/cm²** (40 J/cm² re-arms at 32.5 °C rather than 32.8 °C — *more* conservative, and in the direction §7.4.5 already warns reads backwards), touches no Class C state, and cannot widen any thermal bound because +35 °C is not SW-02's to move. `OI-POE-06` stays open and becomes slightly more valuable: at a 0.1 °C ambient path the dose rule binds unaided and the `max()` goes inert. **(13) What #335 gets, and what it cannot get.** `P_thermal(N, ambient, spreader)` is the quantity `OI-PWRSRC-12` asks for and the form #335's watts-denominated governor needs, with three properties it must carry rather than flatten: it **rises** with N (a tile-count governor gets the sign wrong; a watts governor ignoring N gets the magnitude wrong by 12× between N = 1 and N = 80), it is **spreader-conditional** per `SINK-D-1`, and it is **ambient-dependent** in a way `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2's flat +35 °C gate cannot see (`OI-N1-08`, still open). **Its constants are not available**: `OI-SINK-01` is BLOCKING and `OI-HEXTILE-02` has selected no emitter, so `TILE_W`'s 25.0 W/tile and η_wp 0.35 both enter multiplicatively. **The governor's form is decidable today; its constants are not** — `OI-PWRTH-08`. **(14) One consequence that falls out of CLAUDE.md §4.2 alone, and that nothing in the set states.** §4.2's TMS interlock gates EMF cancellation off 5 ms before each pulse and holds it 50 ms — **55 ms per pulse**. At 10 Hz that is **55 % of the train**; above **18.2 Hz** it is continuous; and iTBS's intra-burst rate is 50 Hz, so for the whole of every burst it is off. Not a safety finding — the gate exists because the Helmholtz coils must not fight the pulse — but a **claim** finding: CLAUDE.md §1's *"only consumer brain wearable with measured shielding"* has its active half switched off for most of a TMS train, the passive stack being unaffected, and **a claim qualified nowhere is a claim that will be made without the qualification** (`OI-PWRTH-05`). **Nine decisions PWRTH-D-1…9 (six to principal), nine open items `OI-PWRTH-01…09` (one BLOCKING, plus `OI-PWR-02` re-marked BLOCKING), five risk rows. No locked section modified; no firmware, app or protocol changed; no figure in a released document rewritten.** Adds `scripts/check-power-envelope.ts`; adds `R_SINK_SPECIFIED` and `rSinkNote()` to `scripts/check-thermal-multitile.ts`. See `docs/np_pwr_therm_001.md`.

- **2026-09-15 — CLAUDE.md Rev 47: the header's revision digest and every §2 figure leave the always-loaded core; no design decision changed.** The core went from 38,093 to 36,119 bytes (−5.2%) — a smaller return than Rev 43's because a stub carrying an invariant costs most of the bullets it replaces, and §2's four surviving rules are the bulk of what was there. Fifth application of the Rev 33 core/subsidiary split, under Rev 43's criterion (*a figure that cannot be quoted from the core without first opening another file does not belong in the core*) plus one this revision adds: **a narrative that exists in full elsewhere does not belong in the core either, because a digest of it is a second source of truth about why a decision was made** — Rev 41's argument about generated locale files, applied to prose about the past. **(i) The header's Rev 41–46 digest → `docs/reference/claude-md-revision-history.md`**, which already held every one of those claims in full and with its reasoning. The digest's only distinct content was its *selection* of six revisions, an editorial judgement nothing recorded, and it decayed accordingly: the Document Map's row for that file still read *"Rev 33–42"* four revisions after Rev 46 was written. The header now says where the history is, that none of it is summarised, and that it must be read before assuming why an invariant reads the way it does; the history file states it is the only copy and that a summary must not be reintroduced. The `**Revision:** N (current)` stamp stays — that is the file's own version, not a narrative. **(ii) §2's figures and tables → `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.1–§2.3.** Rev 43 kept name, tier, retail-in-force and modalities as *"what identifies a configuration"*; **half of that does not survive inspection.** The Retail column is a figure set governed by §2.1's own rule that no figure may be quoted without first reading `docs/np_cost_001.md` — the exact condition Rev 43 used to evict the cost columns beside it — and the Modalities column restates §3, itself a pointer to `docs/reference/modality-stack.md`. `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.1 **already repeated both columns verbatim** *"so this table stands on its own"*, so the core's copy was never the only copy. **What survived is the tier mapping**, which is not a figure and is not derivable from §1 (§1 defines T1 as FDA-exempt wellness and T2 as a 510(k) target but never says which configuration is which): one prose line, six names grouped by tier. A **Tier column was added to the reference table** so the move destroyed nothing. **§2 is a pointer section that is deliberately not empty.** It keeps four rules, none of them a number, each constraining code written by someone who would never open a commercial file: §2.2's *keyed to peak draw, not price* and *any PD-compliant charger must work — the app informs, never blocks*; §2.3's *only authenticated consumable* and *every consumable prompt is measurement-triggered, never calendar-triggered*. §2.1's pricing guardrail and §2.1a's *break-even binds before margin* stay because they are what makes a cost answer safe to give, and the header's live constraint 1 already depends on the first. **Each rule was also written into the receiving file, and one of them had to be:** §2.3's measurement-triggered invariant **existed nowhere but CLAUDE.md** — `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.3 carried the consumable table with no statement of the rule governing it, and `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.2 states the analogous rule for *reminders*, not consumables — so relocating §2.3 without carrying the sentence would have deleted an invariant `docs/np_acc_priority_001.md` cites by section number. **Carrying it surfaced a contradiction that was previously split across two files and is NOT resolved here:** the consumable table's audio cup foam names *"Calendar reminder"* as its trigger, which the invariant forbids. `docs/np_acc_priority_001.md` raised it and declined to resolve it on the ground that inventing a seal or compression proxy is the design work the invariant protects; the rule and the row are now adjacent with the contradiction flagged, and choosing between a measurement trigger and a reasoned exception remains a principal decision (Product + FW). **(iii) Every "retired" framing left the core**, on the ground that nothing in this repository was ever published or used externally, so no outside reader needs telling what a thing used to be. Three occurrences, each **reframed rather than deleted**, because in each the routing or the rule was work the retirement narrative only decorated: the risk-file Document Map row now gives the RISK-01…26 ID range to grep rather than the register's status; the `docs/superseded/README.md` row — the core's only pointer to those files, so deleting it would have orphaned them — now names the live hazard (*"you have a figure or a file and need to confirm it is the current one"*) instead of *"tracing why something changed"*; and **§16 was inverted rather than trimmed**, from *"Retired term: Health Data Record (HDR)"* to the rule itself — **never name a health data record "HDR"**, because the term does not say whose record it is, which is the one thing §5 turns on (title accordingly drops "CHANGES"). **Two additions the old wording lacked:** the prohibition is now scoped, since `HDR` meaning a binary *header* (`blob(n) = HDR(8) + …`, LittleFS and NVRAM) is a different word and explicitly fine — which the old *"replaced throughout all documents"* contradicted for seven live uses; and **the rule has a live violation**, found while inverting it: `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.2 specifies predictive-maintenance Phase 2 as a *"fleet-trained LSTM on HDR sensor trajectories"* — the retired term in the record sense, inside a locked section's detail file — and `docs/np_fw_emmc_002.md` §G quotes it back verbatim. **NOT fixed here:** the phrase is quoted as specification wording in two places, so the correction (`SHDR`, per §5.2's own *"SHDR-based"* heading) must change the source and both quotations together, which is an edit to a locked section's detail file rather than a CLAUDE.md revision. Recorded as found. **Nothing broke:** only `scripts/check-section-refs.ts` reads CLAUDE.md, and only its top-level numbered headings; §2 and each of §2.1 / §2.1a / §2.2 / §2.3 is retained as a stub, so the inbound citations still resolve. No design decision changed, no figure was rewritten, no locked section's substance was altered. Narrative: `docs/reference/claude-md-revision-history.md` Rev 47.

- **2026-09-15 — the retired term `HDR` leaves §5.2's Phase 2 specification; `SHDR` was already the answer elsewhere in the document set (follows the CLAUDE.md Rev 47 entry above, which recorded this as found and deliberately did not fix it).** CLAUDE.md §16 prohibits naming a health data record *"HDR"* because the term does not say whose record it is. One live violation survived in the record sense: `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.2 specified predictive-maintenance Phase 2 as a *"Fleet-trained LSTM on **HDR** sensor trajectories"*, and three places quoted that phrase back — the same file's Phase 2 gap paragraph, `NP-FW-EMMC-002` §G, and its `OI-EMMC2-13` row. **Resolved to `SHDR`, and the resolution was not inferred here.** §5.2's own heading is *"Predictive maintenance system (SHDR-based)"*; its gap paragraph already reasoned in terms of *"roughly thirty **SHDR** fields"*; §H's characterisation cohort contributes into `shdr_accel_records`; and **`docs/ABBREVIATIONS.md` had independently written the same sentence as *"LSTM trained on **SHDR** sensor trajectories"*** under its LSTM entry, citing CLAUDE.md §5.2. So the document set already agreed on SHDR in every place except the one sentence that was the source of the other three, and `ABBREVIATIONS.md` is the evidence rather than this entry's judgement. **All four occurrences were changed together**, because three of them are quotations and a quotation that no longer matches its source is a worse defect than the term it preserves. **One stale attribution fixed in passing, and it was the reason the quotations had drifted out of sight:** `NP-FW-EMMC-002` §G and `OI-EMMC2-13` both attributed the phrase to *"CLAUDE.md §5.2"*, but CLAUDE.md §5.2 has not contained it since Rev 43 relocated the phase list to `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` — a reader following the citation to verify the quote found a §5.2 that does not carry the words. Both now cite the section **and** name the file that owns the text. **No engineering content changed**: Phase 2's data source was always the device-condition record, the term was written before the UHDR/SHDR split existed, and `OI-EMMC2-13` remains RECOMMENDED, NOT ADOPTED. **NP-FW-EMMC-002 was not revision-bumped** — the edit corrects a quoted term and a citation target, alters no requirement, threshold, class or open-item disposition. **Noticed and NOT fixed, because it is unrelated to this correction:** that document's front matter reads **Revision: 3** while its Change Control paragraph still says *"NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 2"* and its revision-history table has **no Rev 3 row at all** — so what Rev 3 changed is unrecorded. That is a document-control defect needing whoever made the Rev 3 change, not a terminology pass.

- **2026-09-15 — supersession bookkeeping: `OI-CONV-07` raised, precedence between the three registers stated, and two stale facts corrected. No document was deleted and no design decision changed.** Reviewed `docs/status/document-register.md` and `docs/superseded/README.md` for retirement bookkeeping. **The word counts did not fall, and were never the defect** — a register of superseded documents cannot say less about supersession without ceasing to be the register; what was wrong was four specific things. **(1) The dedup follow-up was flagged in four file preambles and tracked nowhere.** `document-register.md`, `pending-decisions.md` and `completed-decisions.md` each carried prose saying a dedup pass against the DHF *"is flagged as a follow-up"*, and CLAUDE.md's Document Map said the same — with no ID, no owner and no row, which is not tracking. Raised as **`OI-CONV-07`** with its owning entry in `docs/np_conv_001.md` (beside OI-CONV-01–06) and a row in `pending-decisions.md` §13.3 (Structural — accepted, managed); all four preambles now cite the ID. **(2) Precedence among the three registers was undefined.** `document-register.md`, `np_dhf_001.md` and `superseded/README.md` each record location + successor for the retired set, populated by relocation rather than by one owner, and nothing said which wins when they drift. Stated in the interim, in all three status preambles: **the DHF is the source of truth, this register may be stale, and where they disagree the DHF wins.** Recorded on `OI-CONV-07` that `superseded/README.md` is deliberately **not** a dedup target — it carries retirement *reasoning* (what partly survived, why a document was kept) the other two do not, so only the location + successor columns are redundant. **(3) Four register rows named a successor that no longer exists under that name:** the design briefs read *"SUPERSEDED by R5"*, an `R`-prefixed letter revision retired by `NP-CONV-001` §4.1 on 2026-08-11 when document revisions became integers. Now `NP-DB-005`. The *files* are untouched — `OI-CONV-06` closed 2026-08-11 deciding explicitly not to renumber `NP-DB-001`…`005`, because that would collapse five §820.30(j) design-record entries; only the register's name for the successor was stale. **(4) `superseded/README.md`'s retention argument led with a duty whose clock has not started.** It opened by retaining *"because `21 CFR §820.30(j)` requires the design history to be reconstructable"*, with the live inbound citations mentioned second. But `NP-CONV-001` §4.0.4 (BINDING) makes retention a **two-limb disjunction** — referenced anywhere, *or* a process/legal duty — either sufficient alone; and §820.180, which §4.0.4 relies on for the second limb, runs from **release for commercial distribution**, which has not happened (pre-tooling, T1 FDA-exempt wellness, T2 a 510(k) *target*). The README now leads with **limb 1, which is satisfied and verifiable by `grep`**, notes that the §820.180 duty binds the DHF this archive will join rather than today's disk, and says that reading **changes nothing about what stays** because limb 1 is independently met. **This is the answer to "nothing here was ever published, so can it go": the premise is true and does not unlock deletion**, because the operative limb is citation, not publication — so the README now states that a deletion proposal is a `grep`, not a judgement about whether the project shipped. **Deliberately NOT done:** §4.0.4's blanket *"question 2 is already answered, and the answer is yes"* is left as written. Qualifying it is a change to a BINDING convention section and needs `NP-CONV-001` revised by its owner, not a note in an archive README. **Also corrected:** the README asserted *"`NP-RISK-001`'s ~38 inbound citations"*; the true figure is 63 occurrences across 18 files, so the count had drifted and could only drift again. Replaced with the property that actually matters — every inbound citation resolves to `np_risk_001.docx` **by rule, not by lookup** — plus the `grep` to reproduce any count, per the house practice of producing figures rather than transcribing them.

- **2026-09-15 — `NP-FW-EMMC-002`'s Rev 3 revision-history row was misfiled into the open-items table, not missing; moved, and the two stale statements around it fixed. This CORRECTS the 2026-09-15 entry above, which reported the row as absent.** That earlier entry said the document's *"revision-history table has **no Rev 3 row at all** — so what Rev 3 changed is unrecorded."* **That was wrong, and the way it was wrong is the finding.** The row existed, fully written and accurate, about fifty lines further down the Change Control section: it had been appended after `OI-EMMC2-14` as the last row of the **"Open items created by this document"** table. Both tables are three columns wide, so `| 3 | 2026-09-13 | … |` parsed and rendered without complaint — as an open item whose **OI-ID was "3"**, whose **Description was a date**, and whose **"Blocking for" column held the entire revision summary**. Nothing flagged it, because nothing is wrong with the Markdown; two adjacent same-arity tables are indistinguishable to a parser and nearly so to a reader scrolling past. The earlier entry was written from the revision-history table, which genuinely ended at Rev 2, and did not scroll far enough — the same mistake the layout invites. **Resolution: the row was moved verbatim into the revision-history table, above Rev 2** (the table is newest-first), and its content was not rewritten, because it was already correct — it matches both §C.3's inline *"Rev 3 correction"* note and the `NP-FW-NVRAM-001` Rev 2 entry in this log. For the record, Rev 2 → 3 changed exactly one thing: **§C.3's UKMD record stopped naming a Config partition byte offset (`0x1000`) that LittleFS owns and may relocate, and became the named file `ukmd.rec`** — `EMMC-FS-01` mounts LittleFS over all 4,096 blocks of the 16 MiB Config partition, so no offset inside it was ever honourable. Raised as `OI-NVRAM-02` (`NP-FW-NVRAM-001` Rev 1 §3.3(b)), decided by its Rev 2 **D-22**. Provably safe because **no code ever used the offset**: `np_uhdr_key.c` has always reached the record through `np_uhdr_hal_config_read_ukmd()`/`_write_ukmd()`. Struct layout, the 192-byte size, the `NP_UHDR_UKMD_RECORD_SIZE` static assertion and the §C.4 unlock sequence are unchanged; no privacy, classification or safety claim in §A–§H is affected; the `NP-FW-EMMC-001` companion clause changes stay deferred as `ECR-EMMC-001` because that document is a `.docx` (`OI-CONV-04`). **Two stale statements fixed alongside it.** (i) The Change Control paragraph opened *"This delta document (NP-FW-EMMC-002 **Rev 2**) is under change control"* against front matter reading Revision 3 — now Rev 3. (ii) **The DHF disagreed with itself about the document's current revision**: `docs/np_dhf_001.md` lists NP-FW-EMMC-002 twice, in §5.4 and cross-listed in §5.13, and while the §5.4 row read Rev 3 / 2026-09-13 the §5.13 row still read **Rev 2 / 2026-08-12**. Since the DHF is the declared source of truth for design records — and `OI-CONV-07` has just restated that it wins over `document-register.md` — a DHF that contradicts itself is worse than a stale register. The §5.13 row now reads Rev 3 / 2026-09-13 and its section-by-section contents list carries the §C.3 correction beside §G and §H. **Bolding convention preserved rather than invented:** in this document's revision table bold Rev/Date marks the current revision (Rev 2 was bold, Rev 1 was not), so Rev 3 takes the bold and Rev 2's Rev/Date markers were unbolded — formatting only, no wording in the Rev 2 row touched. **Not changed, and worth knowing:** the front matter's References line lists `firmware/shdr/` and `ci/shdr/shdr_fleet_schema.sql` Rev E under *"added at Rev 3"*. That is literally true of when the reference entries were added, but both support §G/§H, which are Rev 2 content — a reader could take it to mean Rev 3 touched §G or §H, and it did not. Left as written rather than reworded, since the phrase is accurate about the reference list and rewriting a released document's front matter to clarify an inference is not this correction's business.
- **2026-09-15 — `OI-ACC-02` closed by reading the code instead of the table, and CLAUDE.md §2.3 gained a definition rather than an exception (CLAUDE.md Rev 48; `NP-ACC-PRIORITY-001` §5 row 14 and §8):** the audio cup foam's replacement prompt was recorded `Calendar reminder` on a `6–12 months` interval in `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.3, against CLAUDE.md §2.3's locked *"all consumable prompts are measurement-triggered (§5.2), never calendar-triggered."* `NP-ACC-PRIORITY-001` Rev 1 raised it and **deliberately declined to resolve it**, on the ground that inventing a seal or compression proxy is design work and not a documentation edit. **That refusal was upheld, not overturned — no trigger was invented — and neither of the two outcomes the item offered was taken**, because both presuppose that the table describes the product. **It does not.** `app/ios/NeurOne/Models/ConsumableInventory.swift` and `app/android/core/.../ConsumableModels.kt` already trigger the foam on a **session count** delivered by the hub's SHDR-class `CONSUMABLE_STATUS` characteristic — a measurement the device takes and logs (CLAUDE.md §5.1, *"device session count (unsigned integer, no timestamps)"*). The calendar existed only in the row's prose, and **it could never have been anything else:** the headset has no battery, no coin cell and no `VBAT` rail (CLAUDE.md §4.5 — USB-C throughout, Mode 3 from a power bank), so the i.MX RT1062's SNVS RTC has no backup domain and wall time is lost on every disconnect (`NP-FW-EMMC-002` §H.3.1, the same reasoning that denominated the §H characterisation window in records rather than days). A device-side calendar trigger is **unimplementable**, not merely forbidden — which is why the contradiction was never between the invariant and the product, but between two documents. **The defect that was real is the threshold's provenance.** `150` was back-derived from `6–12 months` through an unvalidated *assuming daily use* assumption, and did not match its own stated derivation (`~180`) on either platform; nor did it follow the convention the neighbouring rows follow, the hydrogel tips (45, midpoint of 30–60 sessions) and VNS pads (30, midpoint of 20–40 sessions) taking the midpoint of a **session** range while `150` sits below the bottom of its own calendar-implied 180–360. That is precisely the defect class `NP-FW-EMMC-002` §G.2 records for `NP_ACCEL_DROP_THRESHOLD_G` and `NP_ACCEL_MAINT_THRESHOLD` — *"chosen before any hardware existed and … never compared against a device that failed"* — and it takes that document's remedy rather than a new one: the number is **relabelled an unvalidated placeholder** on both platforms and carried by **`OI-ACC-04`**. **The value is deliberately unchanged.** Re-deriving it would re-derive from a derivation just declared void, and would move both platforms' test suites and the prompt frequency of a $24 revenue-generating consumable on withdrawn reasoning; the alternative (270) is stated in the disposition so an override costs one edit. **No seal or compression measurement is obtainable from the current sensor set, and that is a physical finding rather than a cost one:** the foam is non-conductive and sits in no circuit; **there is no microphone anywhere in the design**, so no acoustic seal test exists; and the one audio impedance channel — `np_mod_audio_hal_mesh_impedance()`, itself an unimplemented HAL stub (`OI-AUDIO-08`) whose return value is discarded at its only call site — measures a **conductive mesh**, not the foam beside it. **The null is bounded by what was searched** — the four candidate contact/presence channels enumerated in `NP-FW-BENCH-001` §4.1 plus `np_sw02_platform_hal.h` as of this revision — and a part added to either list reopens the question; this is stated as "none in that enumeration", never "none can exist". **What the exposure count cannot see is recorded with it:** a foam torn, contaminated, or compression-set in storage or by a second user, and any wearer whose session length departs from whatever `OI-ACC-04` eventually assumes. The degradation mechanism is time-under-compression; session count is a proxy for it, not a measure of it. **CLAUDE.md §2.3 (Rev 48) therefore gained a test, not an exception.** *"Measurement-triggered"* had no test, so a row passed by having a Notes cell that sounded like a measurement — which is how the foam row survived from the table's first revision until a ranking exercise happened to read it. §2.3 now states that a prompt qualifies in exactly one of two ways and the row must say which: a **condition measurement** of the part (hydrogel tips: impedance trend), or an **exposure count** of the quantity driving the part's degradation **with that mechanism named** (VNS clip pads: electrochemical degradation from VNS current, 20–40 sessions — the only row that satisfied this half before today). It further states that **the trigger's kind and the threshold's provenance are two claims**, and that a threshold back-derived from a calendar is a calendar prompt wearing a session count — without that clause the sharpening would have *laundered* the foam row into looking compliant while its number stayed void, moving the defect from visible to certified. **The clause is scoped explicitly to consumable replacement prompts, and that scoping is load-bearing:** `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.2 carries the broader sentence *"All reminders measurement-triggered, not calendar-triggered"*, and reading the new definition onto it would delete two controls that have no measurement to substitute — the **3–5 year Tier B fluxgate service visit** (`docs/reference/durability-maintenance.md`; scale-factor drift is not self-detectable, and it is the maintenance action behind CLAUDE.md §4.3's shielding-attenuation claim) and the **$1,800/yr T2 annual calibration visit** (`NP-PWRSRC-001` **D-16**, the ISO 14971 information-for-safety re-acknowledgement point, which D-16 already rules CLAUDE.md §6.1's decision-support document out of replacing). **Two claims made when the item was raised are false, were false before this revision, and are corrected rather than inherited:** (i) *"every other row in that table names a measurement (impedance trend, driver impedance, authentication, session count)"* — the interface-cover, S3 Rx and T2 service rows name none of the four, and **no Notes cell anywhere in the table names a session count**, because the session counts live in the **Interval** column, so the enumeration was wrong about which column it had read; and (ii) *"the one calendar-triggered consumable reminder in the document set"* — the mesh frame (`Annual`), the covers (`Annual / as lost`) and the S3 insert (`12–24 months`) are all calendar-denominated in that same column. The second correction matters more than the first, because **the mesh frame is the worse case**: its Notes name a measurement that does not exist, so it passed inspection by sounding right while shipping nothing at all (`OI-ACC-05`). **Three successors, each narrower than the item they replace:** `OI-ACC-04` (the foam threshold — closed by a compression-set figure at the cup's clamping pressure plus a stated session-duration assumption, or by denominating the count in cumulative worn ticks, which is implementable without a clock as an ordinal odometer but needs a new SHDR field and its own UHDR/SHDR classification); `OI-ACC-05` (the mesh frame — implement `OI-AUDIO-08`, add a fifth consumable kind and widen the 4 × uint16 `CONSUMABLE_STATUS` characteristic, or restate the row honestly as the foam's now is); and `OI-ACC-06` (**§2.3's rule has no gate**, alone among invariants of its class — §5.1 has `check-redaction-shape.ts`, §6.2 has `check-consent-reachability.ts`, §17 has `check-locale-strings.ts` — and a `check-consumable-trigger.ts` would fail on four rows today, so it needs that file's `PENDING_PATHS` discipline of exempting on the record rather than skipping silently; the S3 Rx row can never pass, its real trigger being refraction change, which is user biology and therefore UHDR and permanently outside NeurOne's reach). **One consequence outside this item's scope, recorded and not acted on:** `OI-ACC-03` (the cervical tcVNS gel pad, which has no consumables entry and therefore no trigger) becomes cheaply closable on the template this revision supplies, `docs/reference/modality-stack.md` already recording 20–40 sessions for the pads and the VNS clip row already naming their degradation mechanism. **Changed:** CLAUDE.md §2.3 + header (Rev 47 → 48); `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.3 (disposition block, foam row, mesh-frame row); `docs/np_acc_priority_001.md` §5 row 14 and §8; `docs/status/pending-decisions.md`; comments only in `ConsumableInventory.swift`, `ConsumableModels.kt`, `ConsumableTracker.swift`, `ConsumableTracker.kt`. **No constant's value changed on either platform, no price, BOM, COGS or GM% figure moved, no accessory rank moved, and no firmware was touched.** No QMS/CAPA record opened — development mode, no hardware, no bench data, and no shipped device holds a foam count.
- **2026-09-20 — every manufactured artifact now has an owning specification document, and no artifact FAI became writable (GitHub #332; `NP-ART-001` Rev 3, `OI-ART-04` discharged into `OI-ART-09`):** `NP-ART-001` §4's principal finding since 2026-08-11 was that **nine of fifteen manufactured artifacts had no owning specification document**, several of them **shipping T1 modalities** whose governing specification was a one-line roster entry in `CLAUDE.md` §3. Six documents were issued against it, all Rev 1 and all **DRAFT**: **`NP-HW-AUDIO-001`** (A11 audio cup assembly), **`NP-HW-NASAL-001`** (A12 intranasal bilateral Y-probe and hygiene sleeve), **`NP-HW-VNSCLIP-001`** (A13 auricular VNS/HRV clip), **`NP-HW-CVNS-001`** (A14 cervical VNS electrode assembly, cable and connector — the absence cited by name in `NP-ART-001` §3.2, `NP-FAI-001` §2.1.1 and `NP-RISK-002` RISK-25), **`NP-HW-TMS-001`** (A15 TMS focal figure-8 coil, written against `OI-PWRTH-01` and `OI-PWRTH-09` as `OI-ART-04` required) and **`NP-HW-TACSDRV-001`** (the 21-channel clinical tACS driver stage, **registered as new artifact A16** — `OI-TACS-02`'s remaining half). **What each document is, and is not.** Each carries (a) every requirement the existing record already binds, with the citation on the row, (b) the interfaces as shipped firmware and baselined tooling already fix them, and (c) an enumerated statement of what is *not* specified, routed to open items. **No dimension, material, emitter, exposure limit, force, constant, price or locked decision was created or changed by any of them** — `NP-ART-001` §4's closing sentence (*writing a specification for the intranasal probe is engineering work, not documentation work*) stands, and is the reason none is more than DRAFT. **The consequence is stated as plainly as the change:** `NP-FAI-001` §2's F1 and F2 fail on all six, the writable-FAI count stays at **1 of 16** (`NP-FAI-HUB-001`), and **G2 pre-tooling completeness is still not declarable**. What did change: **every artifact has an owning document**; **`OI-RISK2-02` is unblocked** with per-artifact hazard-input lists supplied and the analyses still owed (`NP-RISK-002` Rev 3 §4.1, `NP-RM-001` §8 in-doc note B); **`OI-FAI-07` is re-scoped rather than closed**, `NP-FAI-CVNS-001`'s F1 failure moving from *absence* to *DRAFT status* (`NP-FAI-001` Rev 3 §2.2, which also names `NP-FAI-TACSDRV-001` as a new named absence, `OI-FAI-08`); and each blocked checklist now has a document enumerating, by ID, the engineering decisions standing between it and F1/F2. **Five findings the artifacts' absence had been hiding**, none of them new gaps and all of them newly *visible* — the register's own thesis arriving from the other direction: **(a)** `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` F-01 is **BASELINED** and its saddle radius, insert-pad placement and ≤ 2 N detent are dimensioned against intranasal-probe features no document states, so a tool at baseline waits on a part with no geometry (`OI-NASAL-01`); **(b)** the intranasal probe has **no exposure ceiling of its own** — `CLAUDE.md` §3's hard-limits table has a *PBM scalp* row and a *PBM deep* row and **no intranasal row**; the 25 % duty ceiling is inherited from the cranial tiles by a firmware comment (*"same ceiling as zone modules"*) and bounds duty, not irradiance; the 42 °C limit is realised by an NTC per zone that the probe does not have; and the tissue is mucosa while `RISK-03`'s uncommissioned engagement is scoped to 660/808 nm **at the scalp** (**`OI-NASAL-02`, BLOCKING**, routed to extend that instruction rather than open a parallel one per `NP-REG-PBM1064-001` §2); **(c)** the **only authenticated consumable cannot be enforced as single-use** — `np_mod_ins_hal_auth_check()` returns a `bool`, the optical and resistive codes are naturally *type* codes, nothing describes a consumed state, no counter exists, and a per-unit serial would raise a UHDR classification question before it could be logged (`OI-NASAL-04`), which touches hygiene and the primary MRR line at once; **(d)** the **T2 21-channel qEEG's A1/A2 linked-ear normative reference sits on the auricular clip's stimulation pads**, a consumable whose stated degradation mechanism is electrochemical attack by the VNS current itself, with a replacement trigger covering only the stimulation duty and a `[500 Ω, 5000 Ω]` contact window that cannot see a pad that is a poor µV-scale reference (`OI-VNSCLIP-01`); **(e)** **A14's cardiac interlock takes its R-peaks from A13's PPG sensor** (`NP-FW-CVNS-001` §6.2, *"co-located in the clip mount"*) — so the control that mitigates RISK-25 and is presented to FDA as the *"key safety differentiator from predicate"* depends on a **T1 accessory** being worn and working, through a consumable degraded by unrelated use, and that dependency appears in no device description, no control set and no IFU (`OI-CVNSHW-03`). **Four further absences recorded rather than filled:** `CLAUDE.md` §4.2's TMS row is titled *Coil protection* and names **no patient-facing control** — no dose, train, inter-train interval or contraindication limit exists anywhere in the set (`OI-TMS-03`); there is **no acoustic requirement** for the TMS coil click and no acoustic ceiling elsewhere to inherit (`OI-TMS-02`, `OI-AUDIOHW-01`, `OI-AUDIOHW-02`); there is **no electrical-safety case for a 28 µF / 1,600 V energy store** on a head-worn device, `CLAUDE.md` §4.5 and `NP-PWRSRC-001` being written for USB-C PD (`OI-TMS-04`); and the audio cup mesh frame's **40 dB RF** figure is a shielding claim on a **user-replaceable** part that appears in no `CLAUDE.md` §4.3 layer, no `docs/reference/hardware-detail.md` §4.3 row, no `NP-BIB-EMF-001` audit row and no `NP-DT-001` `DI-PERF-22` entry, while its fouling trigger is an unimplemented HAL stub (`OI-AUDIOHW-03`, with `OI-ACC-05`). **Two PROVISIONAL, never-measured electrode areas** that the Class C per-phase charge interlock divides by (`OI-CHARGE-07`) are now owned by the hardware documents that should state them — the auricular clip pad at 0.5 cm² (`OI-VNSCLIP-02`) and the cervical collar pad at 2 cm² (`OI-CVNSHW-04`) — and `NP-HW-VNSCLIP-001` §5.2 records that VNS sitting at 3 % of the ceiling is **not** a reason to defer the measurement, because the defect is a Class C safety argument with a hardware input and no hardware source. **`NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` adds one more:** no **compliance voltage** exists anywhere, and neither does a T2 cap electrode impedance — one decision, and a **precondition** for `OI-TCAP-03`'s silicon selection rather than a consequence of it (`OI-TACSDRV-02`); its siting is undecided between an own board, Hub PCB Rev C and the posterior aggregation node (`OI-TACSDRV-01`), and **if it lands on the hub board, A16 folds into A9 and the row is struck** — recorded so the fold is a decision rather than a disappearance. **Two corrections of record:** `NP-ART-001` Rev 1 and Rev 2 marked A13's risk-register column ✅ against the `NP-RISK-002` §4 row stating the clip's hazards are *not assessed at all* — a named absence read as coverage; and §4's *"four of those nine are shipping modalities in the T1 flagship configuration"* miscounts a list of three, the fourth artifact in the accompanying Rev 1 history row (the TMS coil) being T2. **Revisions issued:** `NP-ART-001` Rev 3 (A16 row, §4.1, OI-ART-09), `NP-FAI-001` Rev 3 (§2.2, OI-FAI-07 re-scoped, OI-FAI-08), `NP-RISK-002` Rev 3 (§4.1, OI-RISK2-02 unblocked and extended to A15), `NP-FW-CVNS-001` Rev 5 (§9's reason moves from absence to maturity; **no constant, limit, criterion, test procedure or firmware behaviour changed**), `NP-HW-TCAP-001` Rev 2 (pointer only), `NP-RM-001` §8 in-doc note B. **No firmware, app, protocol, locale or script was touched; no price, BOM, COGS, GM% or margin figure was quoted, set or revised, and `OI-COST-10`'s sequencing constraint is untouched.**
- **2026-09-20 — `OI-BIBEMF-08` discharged: Layer 4's requirement is stated in dB at last, and the layer supplies 1 % of it (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 1, GitHub #362).** `NP-BIB-EMF-001` §7.3 found that Layer 4 of the CLAUDE.md §4.3 stack — carbon-loaded EMI absorber foam — was **the only layer whose benefit had never been stated in any measurable form anywhere**, its whole justification being the phrase *"cavity-resonance suppression"* repeated in three documents, while its cost was known exactly (**18 % of the outward thermal path**, blocking two fixes to a **BLOCKING** `OI-SINK-01`). It placed a burden on EMC: state a requirement in dB against a named source and band, or lose the layer. **The burden is discharged, and the answer is neither branch §7.3 anticipated.** **(1) The source was one document away the whole time, and it is not a radio.** §7.3 looked for a radio because `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3a bounds the RF concern to 6 GHz Wi-Fi and says the radios live in the hub — the right bound for *ingress*, the wrong one for this question. §7.3's own caveat held the answer: `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2 puts **18 STM32G071 cluster controllers** on L1 **inside** the envelope, with a 400 kHz I2C tree, 80 PWM LED drivers and the ADS1299 SPI bank, and §9.6 of that document had already written the consequence in terms — *"a Faraday cage does not protect the EEG electrodes and fluxgates that share the enclosure with the source."* The emission that matters is **edge rate, not clock rate**. **(2) The band is 420 MHz – 3 GHz, and its lower edge is a property of the wearer** — the lowest circumferential mode of the scalp-to-Pd-liner shell is **506 MHz at a 52 cm head and 422 MHz at 62 cm**, so one adult SKU sweeps 84 MHz of resonance across the population. Nothing in the document set recorded that the shield's resonant frequency depends on who is wearing it. **(3) The requirement is stated and testable:** `REQ-CAV-01` (E ≤ 0.5 V/m, derived from `SH2-DRC-16`'s 5 µVpp budget via **RF demodulation** — a contributor `REQ-EMI-05`'s feed-forward model **cannot** subtract, because it predicts commanded current and not an offset that moves with posture) and `REQ-CAV-02` (loaded **Q ≤ 20**, i.e. **≥ 26.2 dB** of peak-field reduction at resonance). **(4) Layer 4 supplies 0.26 dB of that, and the reason is structural rather than a property of our foam.** For a lossy slab on a conductor `Z_in = j·η·tan(kd)`; in the thin limit `η·k = η₀·k₀` **exactly**, because `η ∝ 1/√ε_r` and `k ∝ √ε_r`, so `Z_in → j·η₀·k₀·d` — **purely reactive, independent of the loading.** At 3 mm the foam is **λ/217** at the lowest mode, and swept to an absurd ε_r of 6 − j12 it still reaches only 2.71 dB. This is why every thin commercial RF absorber is iron- or ferrite-loaded, and magnetic loading is exactly what `REQ-EMI-10` and `NP-BIB-EMF-001` §7.8 forbid in this enclosure. **(5) The wearer's head is the absorber, by 49.8 dB.** Dry skin — the *least* lossy tissue that could bound the cavity — presents **53.9 Ω/sq over 36.5 % of the boundary**, taking Q from 409 to **1.32**. That is not a suppressed resonance but an absent one, and **every state in which the sources are energised is a state in which the head is inside the cavity.** **What this settles now, without waiting for anyone:** there is **no electrical requirement on the Layer 4 station in band**, so it may be specified on **thermal grounds alone** — which unblocks `OI-THCOOL-04` and the EMC half of `OI-THCOOL-15` (its mechanical half is untouched and still gates). Two stale statements are corrected with it: `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §2's *"chosen for cavity-resonance suppression in dB"* and `OI-THCOOL-04`'s *"currently specified in dB only"* were **both false — there was never a dB figure**; and §6.3's proposed ***conductive*-filled absorber elastomer** is the wrong material on three grounds (a conductive fill makes a **reflector**, not an absorber; it collides with §6.9.1's own electrically-insulating and non-magnetic constraint on the gap pad landing at that same station; and `REQ-EMI-10` prohibits it). The filler must be **ceramic** — BN/AlN/Al₂O₃, i.e. §6.9.1's own "ceramic-filled silicone" — and nothing lossy need be added at all (`REQ-CAV-03`). The 0.075 → ~0.02 m²K/W target is unaffected; only the chemistry is. **What is NOT settled, deliberately: Layer 4 is not deleted.** Deleting it changes a **locked** §4.3 decision and CLAUDE.md §1's *"5-layer"* wording, which is a published claim (`competitive-position.md` §176), and a **first-order calculation is not entitled to do that on its own** — the same limit `NP-BIB-EMF-001` §9.4 observed. The recommendation (delete) is routed to the principal in `pending-decisions.md`, gated on two new sweeps — **`EMF-1a`** (S₂₁ inside the closed assembly, absorber fitted vs. removed) and **`EMF-1b`** (the same sweep with a head phantom) — plus `EMF-1c`/`EMF-1d`, all four on the `EMF-1` fixture that was already committed and has never run. A renumbering caution is recorded with it: if the layer goes, **`L5` must not silently become `L4`**, because every existing citation of "Layer 4" would then quietly mean port filters. Five open items raised, `OI-EMCCAV-01…05`; `OI-EMCCAV-03` makes `OI-HUB-C19`'s 24 V boost siting an EMC decision, since a switching converter inside the envelope would be an order of magnitude above anything in the source table. `OI-EMCCAV-02` narrows but does not clear `RISK-SINK-03`: an exterior graphite spreader cannot affect cavity Q, so `OI-SINK-01`'s EMF clearance reduces to ELF eddy loading of the Helmholtz actuator plus external RF — it stays **BLOCKING**. Arithmetic in `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` (`CI-Kind: report`), 14 published anchors, fails on drift. `NP-BIB-EMF-001` → Rev 2 (§7.3a added; §7.1 verdict row and §9.3 updated; Rev 1's §7.3 text retained unedited per `NP-CONV-001` §7, because it was outweighed by a calculation rather than refuted, and the burden it placed is what produced the answer). **`DI-PERF-22` now carries a figure for every layer**, which is the defect the issue was titled after. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted** — `EMF-1`, `EMF-3`, `RISK-20` and `OI-THCOOL-06` all unchanged and open.
- **2026-09-20 — CORRECTS the 2026-09-20 `OI-BIBEMF-08` entry above: Layer 4 is not a removal candidate, it is a mis-justified layer, and deleting it would have made the thermal path WORSE (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 2, `NP-BIB-EMF-001` Rev 3).** The entry above closes with a recommendation to **delete** Layer 4, routed to the principal and gated on `EMF-1a`/`EMF-1b`. **That recommendation is withdrawn.** The RF finding it rests on is untouched and unchallenged — the layer supplies **0.26 dB of the 26.2 dB** `REQ-CAV-02` needs, and its *stated* benefit stays refuted. What was wrong is the **thermal half**, which `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 1 inherited from `OI-BIBEMF-08` and never checked: **it assumed that removing the layer recovers its 18 % of the outward path, and never asked what would occupy the 3 mm station afterwards.** It would be stagnant air, and **air is a worse insulator per mm than the foam** — **0.115 m²K/W against 0.075, 54 % worse** — both conductivities taken from `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §2's own table, which is to say the refutation was sitting in the same table the 18 % came from. Through to the outward path: today **0.410**; **delete without closing the gap 0.450** — *a regression, the exact opposite of the intent*; delete with the outer bowl **re-lofted 3 mm 0.335**, but that is a shell tooling change `NP-REV-SHELL-001` gates (mould, rim latches, cluster-clamp travel, blind-mate boss); **ceramic-filled substitution 0.355, with no tooling change and no claim change.** So substitution lands within **0.020 m²K/W** of the best case for free, and deletion wins only if the re-loft actually happens — which reverses the ordering the entry above assumed. **Three further reasons the deletion was wrong, none of them weighed the first time.** (1) **The foam is the compliant member.** It takes up a ±0.5 tolerance stack across a curved 5–7 mm gap and preloads the cluster clamps and rim latches; its compressibility is **precisely why `OI-THCOOL-15` exists** — the gap pad cannot press against it. A non-compressible ceramic-filled part **keeps that budget and unblocks the pad**; an empty gap keeps neither. (2) **Nothing is priced.** The phrase *"negative BOM cost"* in the entry above was an assumption, and this document set's own `OI-BIBEMF-07` records that **the shielding stack has no BOM line at all**. (3) **Substitution leaves the published claim and the renumbering hazard untouched** — CLAUDE.md §1's *"5-layer"*, `DI-PERF-22` and `competitive-position.md` §176 all stand. **New `REQ-CAV-04`: the station is retained and re-specified**, and its justification of record changes from *"cavity-resonance suppression"* — which `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §6 refutes — to **tolerance compliance and a thermal path across the inter-bowl gap**. **That reclassifies the finding rather than softening it: Layer 4 is the same shape as Layer 2** (`NP-BIB-EMF-001` §7.2 — a layer held in place by dependencies its documentation never named, while its stated rationale was the one the evidence could not support), so the per-layer audit now has **two** instances of that defect and **zero** removals. §7.1's verdict row moves from **FAILS THE TEST** to **Keep, on other grounds**. The standing cost-justification principle is satisfied by **correcting the justification, not deleting the part** — a layer kept for a reason it does not serve is the defect `OI-BIBEMF-08` was raised about; a layer kept under a corrected justification is not. The deletion option is **retained and costed** in `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.2 with the re-loft marked **not optional**. **Two limits of the original analysis are also now on the record.** **`OI-EMCCAV-06`** — the 420 MHz – 3 GHz band's upper edge is derived against the **internal** source only; the 31.1 dB roll-off is a property of the cluster controllers' digital edges and says nothing about **external 6 GHz Wi-Fi ingress through the parting-plane seam**, which is the case `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3a and `RISK-20` are actually written against — and **above 3 GHz the foam is no longer electrically thin** (11–41 dB there), so the §6 verdict does **not** transfer to it. Tissue is lossier at 6 GHz so the head should damp it harder, but **the case is not worked**; `EMF-1a` should sweep to 6 GHz. **`OI-EMCCAV-07`** — nobody has budgeted the heat that moves into the **outer bowl** (mu-metal + Helmholtz coils) when outward resistance falls, against `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2's **SOFT** temperature envelope and `REQ-EMI-11`'s coil-drive→field transfer function; it applies to the substitution as much as to a deletion. `OI-EMCCAV-04` re-scoped: **`EMF-1a`/`EMF-1b` no longer gate anything**, since substitution does not depend on them — they are what would make `REQ-CAV-04`'s justification *measured* rather than calculated. `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` gains the §7 thermal comparison and six anchors (16 → 22), so the air-for-foam penalty is pinned and cannot drift. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted** — `EMF-1`, `EMF-3`, `RISK-20` and `OI-THCOOL-06` all unchanged and open.
- **2026-09-20 — CORRECTS the two 2026-09-20 `OI-BIBEMF-08` entries above: the case for KEEPING Layer 4 rested on two costs that do not exist and one function the foam does not have. The recommendation returns to DELETE, with the 3 mm bowl re-loft BINDING (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 3, `NP-BIB-EMF-001` Rev 4).** The entry immediately above withdrew a deletion recommendation and reclassified Layer 4 as a *mis-justified* layer to be kept and re-specified — *"the audit's second Layer 2"*. **That reclassification is withdrawn.** Its three grounds do not survive contact with the record. **(1) "A shell tooling change" — there is no tooling.** `NP-REV-SHELL-001` is **"DRAFT — open review; no item signed"**, CLAUDE.md's header puts the whole programme in **pre-tooling design phase**, and `OI-ART-01` **already requires** `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` to be re-scoped or superseded because its F-01 still describes the retired 5-colour zone-slot scheme. A 3 mm re-loft is a **CAD edit riding along with a re-scope already owed**. **(2) "A published claim" — nothing is externally published.** `docs/reference/competitive-position.md`'s own source note says its copy *"should be re-verified before publication"*, and its shielding line already carries a ⚠ recording that two of its words are unearned. *"5-layer"* is **an internal string in a design document**. **(3) "The foam is the compliant member" — that was an inference, and the record contradicts it.** `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a specifies the inter-bowl preload path as **over-center lever-throw cluster clamps with per-module spring plungers**, one actuator per cluster (`MECH-2`). The foam is *incidentally* compressible — which is precisely why it obstructs `OI-THCOOL-15`'s gap pad — and an obstruction is not a function. **What survives, and it is the durable contribution of the withdrawn entry, is its arithmetic — re-cast as a condition on HOW to delete rather than an argument against deleting.** Vacating a 3 mm station does not delete its resistance; it fills it with **stagnant air at 0.115 m²K/W against the foam's 0.075, 54 % worse per mm**. So: today **0.410** · **delete without closing the gap 0.450**, still a regression · **delete with the 3 mm re-loft 0.335**, the best figure available · ceramic-filled substitution 0.355. **`REQ-CAV-04` restated: delete the station, and the re-loft is BINDING — the two move as one change.** The 0.020 separating substitution from deletion is **not worth buying back**: once ceramic-filled the station's *bulk* resistance is ~0.002 (3 mm at k ≈ 1.5), so that 0.020 is almost entirely **contact resistance**, the price of the interface a compliant pad necessarily creates — deletion removes the interface, not just the material. **And this is the cheapest moment the decision will ever be available**: no mould cut, the shell tooling spec already open for re-scoping on unrelated grounds, no external copy depending on the number. **The reclassification is also withdrawn on its own terms: Layer 4 is NOT a second Layer 2.** L2 is held by three real, undocumented dependencies (`NP-BIB-EMF-001` §7.2 — the Helmholtz magnetic circuit, the degraded-mode fallback, possibly IEC 61000-4-8). L4's candidates for the same role turned out to be **an unbuilt mould, an unpublished sentence and an inferred function**, which is what a layer looks like when nothing holds it up. §7.1's verdict row returns from *Keep, on other grounds* to **DELETE**, and L4 is **the audit's one removal**. **One question remains and it is ME's, not EMC's — `OI-EMCCAV-08`, against `MECH-2`:** once 3 mm of incidental compliance leaves the gap, do the over-center clamps and spring plungers still take up the ±0.5 tolerance stack across the curved span? If yes → delete and re-loft. If no → the station returns as a **thin ceramic-filled pad sized by that tolerance stack**, not by the 3.0 mm an RF justification picked (~0.355, `REQ-CAV-03`). **And one measurement could still change the answer, which the earlier entries did not flag as decision-relevant: `OI-EMCCAV-06`.** The 420 MHz – 3 GHz band's upper edge is derived against the **internal** source only and says nothing about **external 6 GHz Wi-Fi ingress through the parting-plane seam** — the case `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3a and `RISK-20` are actually written against — where the foam is **no longer electrically thin** (11–41 dB). **`EMF-1a` should be swept to 6 GHz, absorber fitted vs removed, before the re-loft is cut into CAD.** `EMF-1a`/`EMF-1b` otherwise gate **confidence, not the decision**, because §6.1's result is structural: they can only show the layer does *less* than 0.26 dB, never more. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted** — `EMF-1`, `EMF-3`, `RISK-20` and `OI-THCOOL-06` all unchanged and open.
- **2026-09-20 — The four dependencies holding EMF Layer 2 are now written into the document that owns it, and the Layer 4 clamp question answers itself the other way (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 4; `hardware-detail.md` §4.3).** Two things, both following from the entries above. **(1) `NP-BIB-EMF-001` §7.2's documentation defect is closed.** For four revisions `hardware-detail.md` §4.3 justified Layer 2 by *"15–25 dB ELF magnetic"* — **the one benefit §4 of that document cannot support** (WHO residential ELF is 2,000–18,000× below any reported effect, and EEG, unlike MEG, needs no external magnetic suppression) — while omitting every dependency that actually holds the layer. §7.2 called that *"a documentation defect, and it is why the layer looked removable"*, and nobody had fixed it. **Four dependencies are now stated in the layer's own entry as D1–D4.** **D1 is decisive:** `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3.1 reason 2 co-designs the Helmholtz coils with the mu-metal as **one magnetic circuit**, so the calibrated quantity in the active loop — the **coil-drive → field transfer function** — is defined *with the mu-metal present*. Removing L2 does not subtract dB from a total; it **invalidates the actuator characterisation on all three axes**, taking `REQ-EMI-05`'s feed-forward subtraction and `REQ-EMI-11`'s recalibration with it. The active half would need **redesign, not re-measurement**. **D2:** `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2 makes the active-cancellation envelope **SOFT** *because* the passive shield is always present — remove L2 and a soft bound becomes a hard one, re-opening `NP-ENV-001`'s operating range. **D3:** L2 carries the shell's magnetic-continuity architecture (one unbroken bowl, because magnetic shields leak at butt-joints), which is what `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §89's TMS-window routing and `OI-THCOOL-06` (**BLOCKING**) exist to protect. **D4:** IEC 61000-4-8 power-frequency magnetic **immunity** may require it — device immunity, not user protection — and `regulatory-strategy.md` §8 **still omits IEC 60601-1-2 entirely** (`OI-BIBEMF-04`, which gates any L2 removal). Plus the claim arithmetic: L2 supplies the 15–25 dB half of the combined 35–45 dB ELF figure, against an active loop `OI-PWRTH-05` records as **off for most of a TMS train**. **The contrast with Layer 4 is the point:** L4's candidate dependencies turned out to be an unbuilt mould, an unpublished sentence and an inferred function; **L2's are four real ones, and D1 alone is decisive.** **(2) `OI-EMCCAV-08` is answered, and in the opposite direction to the way it was raised.** It asked whether the cluster clamps could still take up the tolerance stack once 3 mm of compliance left the inter-bowl gap. **The clamps lose no space at all.** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2 allocates the clamp **3.0–4.0 mm** of lever features *inside L1* plus the **5–7 mm** Gap for travel (**8–11 mm** total), and the absorber sits at station **L2, outboard of the Gap**. Deleting it moves the Pd liner and everything outboard **3 mm inward together**, so the Gap is preserved, L1 does not move, and **only the clamp's counterface changes** — from compressible foam to **rigid Pd/mu-metal/CFRP**. That is an **improvement**: an over-center lever reacting against a compliant counterface has degraded throw authority and an over-center point that **drifts as the foam takes compression set** across repeated bowl separations for module replacement. **And the tolerance argument inverts.** The foam is not only a compliance provider, it is a **±0.5 tolerance CONTRIBUTOR**: the clamp load path (features ±0.3 · Gap ±0.5 · absorber ±0.5) **shrinks from ±1.30 to ±0.80 worst-case — 38 % — on deletion**. The earlier entry counted the first and missed the second. `OI-EMCCAV-08` is therefore **narrowed and no longer gating**, its residual being `MECH-2`'s anyway: confirm the per-module spring-plunger stroke covers ±0.80, *a strictly easier requirement than today's ±1.30*. **`OI-THCOOL-15` closes outright as a side effect** — its central question, *"what the pad compresses against"* (§221 above), **ceases to exist** once the pad presses on the rigid bowl. **New `OI-EMCCAV-09`:** the 5–7 mm Gap is itself **an allocation, not a derived requirement** — `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a specifies the actuator only functionally (over-center lever throw, one-handed, low input force, `RISK-22`) and **no throw, stroke or plate-lift figure exists anywhere in the document set**, which is the same defect shape as Layer 4's one level down. **Also recorded: the deletion's full search-and-replace hazard.** §8.4 lists the **16 documents** carrying an EMF *"5-layer"* string and flags **four that must NOT be touched** — `DI-USE-05`, `DO-HW-01`, `NP-RISK-003` §41 and `durability-maintenance.md` §18 all say *"five-layer keying"*, the **retired RISK-15 zone-module scheme**, a different five-layer entirely. And **`NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2 is the one edit that is not cosmetic**: its *"passive 5-layer shield always present"* is the evidence for **D2** above, so editing it blindly is how a real dependency gets deleted by a find-and-replace. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-20 — `FLUSH-1` (principal direction): the cluster lever is flush when closed and throws only with the bowls separated — which removes *travel* from the inter-bowl Gap allocation and re-opens the largest unclaimed thermal lever in the document set (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a; `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 5 §8.5).** **The second half was already a fact in the record with its consequence never drawn.** `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.2: *"The module cluster clamps sit in the inter-bowl gap and are **reached by unclamping the bowls**."* §5.5: *"During a module swap the bowls are open."* **So the lever's swept volume is a bowls-OPEN volume.** The first half is the new binding requirement: the lever must be **flush or sub-flush in its closed position**, within the 3.0–4.0 mm of L1 outer-face features, and retain there — a packaging constraint on the closed geometry, not a new mechanism, since the over-center throw already supplies a stable closed detent. **What this corrects.** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2 allocates the inter-bowl gap as *"**inter-bowl clamp travel** + blind-mate boss + labyrinth lip"* at **5–7 mm**. Under `FLUSH-1` the first term is **not an assembled-state requirement at all** — the throw happens with the bowls apart and the gap closes only after the lever is home. `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.3 had already found the 5–7 mm to be *"an allocation, not a derived requirement"* (`OI-EMCCAV-09`, raised as the same defect shape as Layer 4's one level down); **`FLUSH-1` says what it was mis-allocated for.** What actually sets the assembled gap is the **closed** lever footprint (18 clusters, minimised by `FLUSH-1` by construction), the labyrinth lip, the fluxgate sensors on the inner bowl (§5.3.1), and the blind-mate sensor/coil boss — **and the boss is a standalone posterior-centre feature** (§5.3c), so it constrains the gap **locally at the occiput, not across the vault**. **A locally-relieved gap — deep at the boss, shallow over the lattice — is therefore available, and was foreclosed only because "travel" implied a uniform swept clearance everywhere.** **The prize, and it dwarfs the Layer 4 deletion the entries above are about.** The inter-bowl gap is **stagnant air at 0.231 m²K/W — 56 % of the entire outward path and its single largest term** (`NP-THERM-COOL-001` §2). At k = 0.026 **each millimetre is worth 0.0385 m²K/W**, so **narrowing the gap 2 mm recovers more than deleting the entire 3 mm Layer 4 absorber (0.077 vs 0.075)** and 3 mm recovers **0.115** — half again as much. Outward totals: 6 mm **0.410** (today) · 5 mm 0.372 · 4 mm 0.333 · 3 mm **0.295**. **Against `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §6.1's sealed-recirculation proposal** (0.231 → 0.067, recovery 0.164, requiring a **motor inside the sealed cavity**), narrowing to 3 mm reaches **~70 % of the prize with no motor, no power draw, no moving part and no BOM line**. **They interact rather than compose** — a narrower gap is a smaller volume at higher flow resistance to stir — so they must be **traded, not stacked**, and §6.1's 0.067 would have to be re-derived at whatever gap `MECH-2` lands on. Both §2 and §6.1 of that document now carry the alternative. **Dimensioning the assembled gap is `MECH-2`'s and the thermal case is `NP-THERM-COOL-001`'s; `OI-EMCCAV-09` is re-scoped to carry the hand-off.** **The EMC position is unchanged and narrow:** the gap is inside the Faraday envelope either way (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.2), narrowing it moves no aperture, and `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §4.1's 420 MHz lower edge was already computed across a head-size span far larger than any contemplated gap change. `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's Gap row is marked **UNDER REVIEW** with the corrected contributor list. `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` gains the gap model and six anchors (25 → 31), pinning the per-mm figure against drift. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-20 — The boss is the only real inter-bowl Gap constraint, it should project OUTWARD, the fluxgates constrain nothing, and `OI-THCOOL-06` has been carried as open in error since 2026-08-30 (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 6 §8.6; `NP-BIB-EMF-001` Rev 5).** Four findings, all following from `FLUSH-1` in the entry above. **(1) The fluxgates need ZERO gap, and the previous entry's §8.5 table was wrong to list them.** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2 places them in the **"Inner-bowl socket wall + FPC channel"** station (2.0–2.5 mm), `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3(c) puts them *"on the inner bowl, **near the scalp**"* — the far side of L1 from the Gap — and §5.1 lists them among the inner bowl's contents. **This contradicts §221 of this log**, which states *"§5.3c's fluxgates sit there"*, in the inter-bowl gap. Likely reconciliation: the magnetic **keep-out** reaches into the gap while the sensor **body** does not — preserving §221's no-continuous-gap-pad conclusion but **not its stated reason**.
