Material, because **a keep-out is a planform exclusion and only a radial constraint sets the Gap** (`OI-EMCCAV-11`). **(2) The boss is the only real Gap constraint and it is undimensioned.** What crosses it: **216 interface pins in 20 tail groups** plus the fluxgate/coil harness, in **four segregated contact groups with independent returns** (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §4.3, `REQ-EMI-05`), **blind-mated as the bowls draw closed**, so it needs real mating depth — wipe, lead-in chamfer, and the ±0.4 lateral / ±0.5 Z blind-mate tolerance. **No figure exists anywhere in the document set**, and §4.3 already says the layout *"must be settled before MECH-1 cuts the posterior boss"*. `FLUSH-1` makes it urgent rather than new: it is now **the one thing left setting the Gap**. **(3) The boss should project OUTWARD, and nothing requires inward.** Outward **decouples the vault Gap from the boss entirely** — `FLUSH-1`'s whole prize — for a local emboss at the occiput centreline, *"where the internal harness gathers (Boa arch / neck attach)"*, which is the bulkiest part of the assembly; it also marginally **increases** exterior area, mildly helpful against `NP-THERM-SINK-001` §6's finding that the exterior already sits **6.1 K above ambient with every tile idle**. **The magnetic cost is real but largely sunk.** The outer bowl carries the mu-metal that `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3(d) requires **unbroken** and that `hardware-detail.md` §4.3's **D3** makes load-bearing; mu-metal loses permeability when work-hardened and **cannot be re-annealed after lamination** to PETG and CFRP. **But the boss is already "one aperture"** — `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §4.3 routes the entire module interconnect through it precisely to avoid opening a second — so continuity is **already interrupted at that exact spot**, and the accepted treatment is a **mu-metal chimney collar**, which is **itself a projection**. **The question was never whether something projects at the boss, only which way:** inward it consumes Gap everywhere its shadow falls and competes with the harness; outward it consumes profile where hardware already sits. **`OI-EMCCAV-10`, time-boxed by `MECH-1`.** **(4) A status error, propagated through five documents, is corrected: `OI-THCOOL-06` has been CLOSED since 2026-08-30.** Its owning document, `NP-THERM-COOL-001`, closed it by **D-2** — it was BLOCKING only on the pneumatic loop's penetration of the posterior boss, and §6.9 put that loop out of scope. **`NP-BIB-EMF-001` Rev 1 §1 and §7.6 carried it as open/BLOCKING anyway**, `hardware-detail.md` §4.3 repeated it, and `NP-EMC-CAV-001` inherited it through **four revisions**, including in entries in this log. **The owning document wins; all four are corrected.** `EMF-1`, `EMF-3` and `RISK-20` are unaffected and remain open, so §7.6's aperture-limited finding stands on the other three. **But the closure trigger — *"reopen only if the loop is revived"* — is too narrow**, because the measurement is *"bench-measure ELF magnetic leakage through a **mu-metal chimney collar at the posterior boss**"*: it is about **a formed collar**, not about what passes through it. An outward-embossed boss needs exactly that bench number, so `OI-EMCCAV-10` recommends reopening it **against the collar rather than against the loop**. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-20 — `OI-THCOOL-06` REOPENED against the posterior-boss collar, and `BOSS-1` DECIDED: the boss projects OUTWARD (both principal direction; `NP-THERM-COOL-001` Rev 12, `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3(c), `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 7).** Two directions, taken together, and the second is what makes the first binding. **(1) `OI-THCOOL-06` is reopened — against the COLLAR, not the loop.** The 2026-08-30 closure (by D-2) was **correct on its own terms**: the item was BLOCKING only on the pneumatic loop's penetration of the posterior boss, and §6.9 put that loop out of scope. **Its reopen trigger was not.** *"Reopen only if the loop is revived"* scoped the item to a **consumer** rather than to the thing measured — **ELF magnetic leakage through a formed mu-metal chimney collar at a penetration** — and two things now need that number, neither pneumatic. **(a) The collar exists either way:** `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §4.3 routes the entire module interconnect, **216 pins in 20 tail groups** plus the fluxgate/coil harness, through that **one aperture**, so mu-metal continuity is interrupted at the boss whatever its geometry, and `hardware-detail.md` §4.3's **D3** makes that continuity load-bearing for Layer 2. **(b) An outward emboss adds a distinct question:** drawing mu-metal over a local dome **work-hardens it**, with **no re-anneal possible after lamination** to PETG and CFRP. Scope is **ELF, below ~100 Hz, where waveguide-below-cutoff does not apply** — a fluxgate/Helmholtz bench, **not** the VNA sweep `EMF-1a`–`EMF-1d` use — riding the `EMF-1` fixture as **`EMF-1e`**. The 2026-08-30 closure row and the original text are **retained struck-through** per `NP-CONV-001` §4, and §6.1's, §6.9's and §8's *"no longer needed"* / *"closed with it"* statements are marked superseded **in place** rather than rewritten. **(2) `BOSS-1`: the boss projects OUTWARD, and only as far as it needs to** — a **local emboss** at the occiput centreline, planform the boss footprint, depth the **minimum** the blind-mate stack requires (contact wipe + lead-in + the ±0.4 lateral / ±0.5 Z tolerance). **Not a round number and not a global allocation.** **What it buys: the inter-bowl Gap's last local constraint is gone.** Three removals in sequence — `FLUSH-1` took out *travel* (the lever throws only with the bowls separated), `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.6.1 took out the **fluxgates** (never in the Gap; they sit in the inner-bowl socket-wall station), and **`BOSS-1` takes out the boss**. What remains setting the assembled Gap is the **closed lever footprint and the labyrinth lip**, so `OI-EMCCAV-09` is re-scoped from *"which contributor binds?"* to *"how thin can the lever close?"* At **0.0385 m²K/W per millimetre** on the largest single outward thermal term, that is `FLUSH-1`'s full prize unlocked. **Outward is not special pleading on this bowl:** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2 already records that *"coil formers add local thickness only"*, so local outward thickness variation is an **existing feature class** here and `BOSS-1` adds one more instance. **Two keep-outs bind the emboss, neither a blocker:** it must stay inside the existing **Boa-arch / neck-attach exterior volume** — outward is free only while it hides in hardware already there, and breaking that envelope makes it a new exterior feature with `NP-HW-FITOVER-001` consequences — and it must **not intersect a Helmholtz coil former**, because `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §174 makes former geometry *fixed, because calibration depends on it*, and that calibration is the coil-drive → field transfer function which **D1** identifies as Layer 2's **decisive** dependency, re-run per configuration by `REQ-EMI-11`. A former is a **keep-out to route around**, not an obstacle. **The two directions interlock:** `BOSS-1` commits to the geometry whose magnetic cost `OI-THCOOL-06` bounds, which is what turns that measurement from **advisable into needed** — it is now **BLOCKING on `OI-EMCCAV-10` and on MECH-1 cutting the posterior boss**, the same time-box `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §4.3 already sets. `OI-EMCCAV-10`'s **direction half is closed** by `BOSS-1`; its **measurement half is `OI-THCOOL-06`**. **One geometric note recorded against `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's TOTAL row:** the Layer 4 deletion re-lofts the outer bowl **3 mm inward globally** while `BOSS-1` embosses it **outward locally at the occiput** — different locations, no conflict. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted; no figure in the thermal document changed.**
- **2026-09-20 — The inter-bowl Gap dimensioned: ~3 mm, and the closed lever is NOT what sets it — the 18 cluster controller boards are, and they were on no contributor list anywhere (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 8 §8.7).** Asked to dimension the Gap to the closed-lever footprint. **That question has a trap in it.** Under `FLUSH-1` the lever is flush with L1's outer face, inside the **3.0–4.0 mm** of L1 outer-face features `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2 already allocates — **flush means it contributes ZERO Gap** — so dimensioning to it would give ~0, which is wrong, because something else is in there. **`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §4.1 assigns L1's *gap-facing* face to "**cluster controller components, incl. the STM32G071**; clamp plates."** The 18 controller boards sit in the Gap, and they appear on **no** contributor list — not `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.5's, not §8.6's, and not `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's Gap row. **This is the third correction to that list in three revisions and the first that ADDS a contributor rather than removing one** — the first three (travel via `FLUSH-1`, the fluxgates via §8.6.1, the boss via `BOSS-1`) all removed. **The floor, derived from package types the record actually names** so the heights are JEDEC maxima rather than guesses (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §10.1, `NP-HW-HUB-001` §8.1): STM32G071 **UFQFPN32 0.60 mm** · PCA9548A **TSSOP-24 1.20** · TMUX1308-class **TSSOP 1.20** · zero-drift TIA op-amp **SOT-23-5 1.45 — the tallest** · ~400 **0402/0603 passives 0.55**. Taking the clamp plate to **pocket** over the parts: cluster PCB **0.8–1.0 mm (ASSUMED — not stated anywhere)** + tallest part **1.45** + assembly/tolerance clearance **0.5–1.0 (ASSUMED — not stated anywhere)** = **2.75–3.45 mm**, against **5–7 mm** today. If the plate instead **sits over** the parts rather than pocketing, add its thickness — also unstated; §8.1 of that document puts carrier and plate on *"opposite sides of the same cluster footprint"* with the plate lifting to release modules, which is consistent with either. **Thermal value:** 3.5 mm → outward **0.314** (recovers 0.096) · **3.0 mm → 0.295** (recovers **0.115**) · 2.75 mm → 0.284 (recovers 0.125), against today's 0.410. **So the answer is ~3 mm, not ~0 — and it is set by a 1.45 mm op-amp package, a PCB thickness nobody has written down, and a clearance nobody has derived.** **`OI-EMCCAV-09` is re-scoped a third time** to carry the four missing inputs, all `MECH-2`'s: **(1)** cluster PCB thickness — 0.8 vs 1.0 moves the floor 0.2 mm ≈ 0.008 m²K/W; **(2)** clamp plate thickness; **(3)** whether the plate pockets over the components or sits above them, **the single largest swing**; **(4)** the **outer bowl's inner-surface PROFILE tolerance**, which is **nowhere in the record** — §2's stack gives thicknesses (CFRP 2.5 ±0.3) but no **form** tolerance over a ~190 mm dome, and that is what sets the clearance term. **One consequence for the gap pad, and it partly restores a cap this log set.** §221 above capped gap-pad coverage at **22.7 %** on three keep-outs: cluster-clamp actuators, fluxgates, and bowl separation for service. **Two of the three are now wrong** — the actuators are flush under `FLUSH-1`, and the fluxgates were never in the Gap at all (§8.6.1). **But §8.7 supplies a keep-out §221 never named, and it is bigger than either: the 18 cluster controller boards themselves.** **§221's conclusion therefore survives its reasoning being twice wrong** — recorded plainly rather than quietly, because a conclusion that outlives its stated reasons is exactly the shape this programme keeps finding in Layer 2, Layer 4 and now here. Recomputing the cap needs the **cluster board footprint**, which `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2 does not give. **`OI-EMCCAV-12`.** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's Gap row corrected to name the controller boards. `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` gains the floor model and five anchors (31 → 36). **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-21 — `PCB-1` (cluster board 0.80 mm) and `PLATE-1` (the clamp plate pockets), both principal direction: two of the inter-bowl Gap's four missing inputs closed, and the floor firms to 2.75–3.25 mm (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2, `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a, `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 9 §8.7).** **`PCB-1`: the cluster controller board is 0.80 mm nominal, ±0.08 (±10 %), 4-layer rigid-flex.** `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` Rev 2 costed this board (*"small 4-layer + rigid-flex tail, $1.80"*) and gave it **no thickness** — which turned out to matter entirely outside that document, because §4.1 puts the board on L1's **gap-facing** face and its thickness is therefore a term in the **largest single outward thermal resistance**. **0.80 rather than the default 1.6 mm:** the usual reason to run 4-layer at 1.6 is self-support, and this board does not need it — §8.1 makes the carrier *"fixed — laminated into L1, never moves"*, so the polymer bowl supplies the stiffness — and **0.80 is a stock rigid-section thickness for 4-layer rigid-flex**, carrying no fabrication premium. **Not 0.60 mm**, which constrains dielectric and copper-weight options on a 4-layer stack-up that carries a **24 V rail at up to the whole vault feed** (§5.4); the residual 0.20 mm is worth ~0.008 m²K/W and **is not worth buying without a fab DFM review**. **`PLATE-1`: the clamp plate POCKETS over the controller components.** Its seated plane is set by its **land areas**, not by the tallest part, so the closed-state stack is **max(component plane, plate plane)** rather than their **sum**. **Result:** component plane = 0.80 + 1.45 (the tallest named package, a SOT-23-5 zero-drift op-amp) = **2.25 mm**; **Gap floor 2.75–3.25 mm** against **5–7 mm** today. A 0.8 mm plate sitting *over* the parts would give 3.55 mm, so **`PLATE-1` is worth 0.031 m²K/W** on top of **`PCB-1`'s 0.0077**, and the plate's own thickness now only governs if it exceeds the 1.45 mm component plane. **One cost is recorded rather than glossed, and its mitigation is free.** `PLATE-1` **removes plate section**, and `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a caps the cluster at 7 tiles precisely because an 8th raises **plate-mode deflection ×3.07** — which that section calls *"the one that matters"*, the per-module spring plungers existing because *"a rigid plate over a curved cluster could not seat evenly"* — against a plate already carrying **34.2–57.0 N** across 19 contacts on a 6-tile plate (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1.5). **The pocket geometry must not spend the margin the 7-tile cap bought. But the cluster controller board's POSITION within the cluster footprint is unspecified in both owning documents** — sited **off mid-span** the pocket removes section where the bending moment is low and the structural cost is near zero; sited **at the centre** it removes it at maximum moment under a distributed load. **Specify the position before the pocket geometry; ribbing around the pockets is the fallback, not the first move.** **`OI-EMCCAV-13`**, gating the pocket geometry. **Two inputs remain in `OI-EMCCAV-09`**, both `MECH-2`'s: **(a)** the clamp plate's structural thickness, which now only binds if it exceeds 1.45 mm, and **(b) — the one that actually binds — the outer bowl's inner-surface PROFILE tolerance**, which is **nowhere in the record**: `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's stack gives thicknesses (CFRP 2.5 ±0.3) but **no form tolerance over a ~190 mm dome**, and that is what sets the clearance term — the whole of the remaining 0.5 mm uncertainty, worth **0.019 m²K/W**. **One EMC note, raised not decided:** the plate is a part in the cavity, directly over the noisiest silicon in it, and **neither its material nor its conductivity is specified anywhere**. A conductive pocketed plate would be a local shield over §3's named emitter — but it sits near the fluxgates, so `REQ-EMI-10` governs and it is not a free win. `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` gains three anchors (36 → 39). **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-21 — `BOARD-1` (principal direction): the cluster controller board sits at the cluster's PAN-FACING EDGE, on the cluster's own mirror axis — not at its centroid. Four independent constraints select the same position (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2, `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a, `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 10).** The entry above recorded that **`PLATE-1` removes plate section exactly where `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a says not to** — that section caps the cluster at 7 tiles because an 8th raises **plate-mode deflection ×3.07**, which it calls *"the one that matters"*, against a plate already carrying **34.2–57.0 N** across 19 contacts on a 6-tile plate — and flagged that the board's **position** was **unspecified in both owning documents**, making it a free mitigation nobody had used. **It is now specified, and the mitigation is as free as predicted:** at the plate **edge** the bending moment is minimal, so `PLATE-1`'s pocket costs nearly nothing, and **where the board falls into the inter-tile gap, no pocket is needed at all.** **Ribbing around the pockets remains the fallback and is no longer the expected case.** **What makes this a rule rather than a preference is that four constraints select the position independently.** **(1) Plate structure**, above. **(2) Tail length** — all **18** cluster tails converge on the **PAN at the occiput centreline** (§4.2), so PAN-facing siting is the **shortest run for every cluster**: less rigid-flex, lower cost, fewer bend-radius constraints (§8.3). **(3) `REQ-EMI-06` loop area** — N1 is a **tree from the PAN**, never a ring (`REQ-EMI-09`), and §9.3 computes loop area **over a 200 mm cluster feed**. **Loop area is linear in feed length**, so shortening the feed shortens the loop by the same fraction — direct margin on the **≤25 mm²** limit §9.5 calls mandatory rather than a refinement, and margin on the self-field §9.5 exists to subtract. **(4) `SYM-1` preserved without an exception clause** — siting on the **cluster's own** mirror axis keeps the six self-symmetric midline clusters self-symmetric and lets the six lateral mirror pairs mirror by construction, rather than requiring a carve-out. **`REQ-EMI-01` is unaffected:** the board stays inside its cluster footprint, so no electrode-mux or TIA lane lengthens — the analog path is still muxed at the cluster and digitised at the PAN. **One packing conflict is recorded rather than glossed, and it is `MECH-2`'s layout question, not an architectural one.** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §3's **cluster-clamp bosses also sit in the inter-tile gaps**, where they *"add mid-span stiffness to the scalp-facing plane without costing any module coverage"* — and for a **midline** cluster the mirror axis meets the PAN-facing edge **on an inter-tile boundary**, which is exactly where a boss wants to be. **The board and the boss compete for that gap, and pretending the rule lands cleanly everywhere would be wrong.** `OI-EMCCAV-13` is **closed on the structural question** with the packing residual retained. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-21 — `PACK-1` (principal direction): the board-vs-boss packing conflict DISSOLVES rather than trades, because the two want different RADII on the same axis — one hex edge apart (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 11 §8.8, `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §3).** `BOARD-1` in the entry above left one residual: `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §3's **cluster-clamp bosses also sit in the inter-tile gaps**, and for a midline cluster `BOARD-1`'s mirror axis meets the PAN-facing edge on an inter-tile boundary. **Asking the two what they actually want resolves it without either giving ground.** The **boss** wants an **internal** vertex — §3 has the bosses *"add **mid-span** stiffness to the scalp-facing plane"*, and internal is the whole point. The **board** wants the **perimeter** — `PLATE-1`/§5.4a make that where the pocket costs least, and outside the plate footprint it costs nothing. **`PACK-1`: the boss takes the PAN-facing internal vertex; the board takes the outer end of the SAME seam; both sit on the cluster's mirror axis, one hex edge apart.** **The geometry is exact, not approximate.** For a flower centred at C with petals P_a and P_b straddling the PAN direction, the vertex shared by C, P_a and P_b lies **on the axis at one circumradius**, and the P_a/P_b seam runs outward from it by **one edge length** to the cluster perimeter — and on a regular hexagon **circumradius = edge length = W/√3**. So: **boss at a = 23.09 mm** from the cluster centre, **board at 2a = 46.19 mm**, **clear separation exactly one hex edge = 23.09 mm**. Two independent sources agree: `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a calls **23.09 mm** *"one hex edge, W/√3"*, and `hardware/np_socket_map.json` carries `moduleWidthMm: 40` with `rowPitchMm: 34.64` = 40·cos30° — a consistent 40 mm hex lattice. **Three consequences.** **(1) Neither party leaves the mirror axis, so `SYM-1` survives for both** — midline clusters stay self-symmetric, lateral pairs mirror by construction, no exception clause. **(2) The shared seam IS the board's tail route** to the PAN, which is what `BOARD-1`'s loop-area and tail-length arguments already wanted — the rule is self-consistent rather than three rules in tension. **(3) — and this is the one that matters — 23.09 mm is NOMINAL, and the scan-grounded lattice does not hold nominal everywhere.** The socket map is **measured, not idealised** (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §3.4, PROVISIONAL pending REG-1/ACT-1), and its in-row spacing runs **38.6–41.0 mm over the mid-vault (rows 5–8)** but falls to **27.5–28.3 mm at rows 1, 3 and 10** and **24.0 mm at row 11, the rim**. One hex edge therefore falls from 23.09 mm to **~13.9 mm at the tightest cluster**. **So `PACK-1` is comfortable over the mid-vault and TIGHT at crown and rim, and the packing check must be run at the tightest cluster rather than at nominal.** Where the intra-cluster run is too short, **the board continues outward into the INTER-cluster seam** — which is free, because the neighbouring cluster's own boss is at *its* internal vertex, not its perimeter. That keeps the board on-axis and `SYM-1` intact, rather than moving the **boss** off-axis, which is the move that would cost the symmetry. **What is still owed is the same input `OI-EMCCAV-12` already names: the cluster board FOOTPRINT is not given in `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2**, so 23.09 mm — or 13.9 mm at the rim — is a **centre-to-centre budget, not a verified fit**, for a board carrying an STM32G071, a PCA9548A, two mux banks, a TIA op-amp, a gain switch and ~400 passives. **`OI-EMCCAV-14`**; `OI-EMCCAV-13` is now **fully closed**. **One method note worth keeping:** a first pass at this took naive nearest-neighbour distances across the whole socket map and read a 19–39 mm spread as a pitch discrepancy against the 40 mm nominal. It was not — the map's own `rowPitchMm: 34.64` = 40·cos30° confirms the lattice, and the spread was an artifact of comparing in-row and cross-row neighbours on rows of differing width. **The real finding is narrower and survives: the lattice is at nominal over the mid-vault and compresses at crown and rim.** **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-21 — CORRECTS the `PACK-1` entry above: the lattice does NOT compress at the rim, and the governing constraint was never a measurement — it is the part (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 12 §8.8).** The entry above published a caveat that *"the lattice compresses at crown and rim"*, with one hex edge falling to **13.9 mm at row 11**, and directed the board-vs-boss packing check to the tightest cluster. **Withdrawn.** **The tile is ONE UNIVERSAL 40 mm MOULD, type-agnostic, identical shape** — `NP-ART-001` A1 (*"one universal 40 mm mould, all tile types"*), `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §627 (*"tiles are type-agnostic: any type inserts into any socket"*) and `NP-DT-001` `DI-USE-05` (*"all sockets share one identical shape"*) all state it, **because the modules are interchangeable.** Interchangeability makes hexagon uniformity an **invariant, not a preference**: every tile is the same size by construction, so **adjacent socket centres are 40 mm apart ON THE SURFACE everywhere** — they cannot be closer, the parts would overlap — and **`PACK-1`'s 23.09 mm boss-to-board clearance is the tile's own edge length, hence IDENTICAL at all 18 clusters.** There is no per-cluster sweep to run; that is the point of interchangeability, and it **strengthens** `PACK-1` rather than qualifying it. **What the withdrawn caveat measured was a 3-space CHORD, not an on-surface distance.** The socket map gives xMm/yMm/zMm on a **doubly-curved scanned surface**; at row 11 the z-coordinate swings **21.6 mm across four sockets** as the rim turns over, so the chord badly understates the spacing — a 25 mm chord for a 40 mm geodesic implies a local radius of **~13 mm**, which is the rim fold, not a compressed lattice. Over the mid-vault, where curvature is gentle, the same chords read **38.6–41.0 mm** and the error is invisible. **That is exactly why it passed review, and it is the SECOND time this lattice was mis-measured the same way** — the entry above already records an earlier naive nearest-neighbour pass that read a 19–39 mm spread as a pitch discrepancy. **Same root cause both times: treating 3-space distances between socket centres as on-surface distances.** A guard is now written into `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` **at the point of use**, rather than left as a lesson in a log nobody greps before measuring. **And the real variation runs the OTHER WAY, which is upside nobody has counted.** Congruent flat hexagons **cannot tile positive Gaussian curvature without opening gaps** — that is *why* inter-tile gaps exist at all, and why `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §3 can site the cluster-clamp bosses in them *"made free by the lattice gaps"*. **The gaps widen where curvature is highest, and both the boss (`PACK-1`) and the controller board (`BOARD-1`) live in those gaps — so crown and rim have MORE clearance than the mid-vault, not less.** Quantifying it needs the **surface model** (`scripts/extract-helmet-surface.ts`), not the socket list. **`OI-EMCCAV-15`**, raised as upside rather than risk. `OI-EMCCAV-14` re-worded to drop the withdrawn rim figure; the footprint gap it names is unchanged. **`PACK-1`'s conclusion is unchanged: boss at the PAN-facing internal vertex, board at the perimeter end of the same seam, one hex edge apart, on every cluster.** **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-21 — CORRECTS the entry above again: the module surface is MEDIAN-CURVED, not flat, and its curvature mismatch is already bounded and already allocated (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 13 §8.8).** The entry above closed with an argument from *"congruent **flat** hexagons cannot tile positive Gaussian curvature without opening gaps"*, concluding that the inter-tile gaps widen with curvature and hand `PACK-1` free clearance at crown and rim — raised as `OI-EMCCAV-15`, upside nobody had counted. **The premise is false and the item is withdrawn.** **`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §3.1 committed "Option A (rigid, MEDIAN-CURVED 40 mm hexagon)"** — a single **compromise curvature**, `R_m ≈ 87 mm`, **3.1 mm dome depth** at W = 40, chosen to minimise the discrepancy between module and site curvature **across all socket positions**. That is exactly what §3.1's *"worst-case mismatch"* column tabulates, per candidate tile width (0.75 mm at W = 34, **1.04 mm at W = 40**, 1.15 at 42, 1.38 at 46) — the compromise-curvature analysis was already in the record, complete, with a committed option. **And the residual already has a home:** §3.1 states *"worst-case 1.04 mm at 40 mm is absorbed by the PDMS window standoff + a ≤0.8 mm compliant gasket; most of the vault sees ~0.25 mm."* **So the mismatch is taken up RADIALLY at L0, not in-plane as gap width.** There is no uncounted free clearance — the quantity exists, is small, is bounded, and is allocated. **`PACK-1` is unaffected, and the curvature makes it more exact rather than less.** Its 23.09 mm is the module's own hex edge: **23.094 mm measured as an arc** on the R = 87 mm module surface, **23.026 mm as a chord** — a **0.068 mm (0.29 %)** difference, immaterial against a 23 mm packing budget, and **identical at every cluster** because every module is the same curved part. **Nothing decided in this thread spends the L0 mismatch budget:** `PCB-1` and `PLATE-1` sit on L1's **gap-facing** side and `BOSS-1` on the **outer bowl**, while the standoff and gasket are at **L0**, scalp-facing — different stations, no interaction. **A root cause worth naming, because this is the third correction in this geometry area and it is NOT the same failure as the previous two.** The Rev 11 and Rev 12 errors were **mis-measurement** — 3-space chords read as on-surface distances, twice. **This one was re-deriving from first principles a question the document set had already answered** — §3.1 had the table, the committed option and the budgeted residual, and none of it was consulted before reasoning about tile curvature. The guard in `scripts/check-cavity-q.ts` now carries **both** lessons at the point of use: measure on the surface, **and read §3.1 before reasoning about tile curvature at all**. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-21 — `CURV-1` (principal direction): the module SOCKET seating surface, and its contact-array datum, carry the SAME compromise curvature as the module — `R_m ≈ 87 mm` (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §3.1; `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` `REQ-SKT-01` addendum; `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2 socket-wall row).** The entry above established that the module face is **median-curved, not flat** — `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §3.1's committed Option A, one compromise radius chosen to minimise site-vs-module mismatch **across all socket positions**. That fixed the *module* side and nothing fixed the *socket* side, which is where the interface actually is. **`CURV-1` closes it, and it is forced rather than chosen:** modules are **interchangeable** — any type into any socket (`NP-ART-001` A1, `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §627, `NP-DT-001` `DI-USE-05`) — so one module curvature admits exactly one socket curvature, and the compromise was already taken above; there is no second compromise to make. **The reason this is load-bearing and not a cosmetic follow-through is the contact array.** The module↔socket interface is a **compression contact with no connector** (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §9.1), seated by the §5.4a clamp plunger, and **`SH2-DRC-08` specifies wipe ≥ 0.30 mm** over ≥1,000 cycles. `REQ-SKT-01` lays the 19 contacts out as **two staggered rows, 9 + 10 at 2.00 mm pitch — a ~18 mm span** — and across that span the `R_m` sagitta is `87 − √(87² − 9²)` = **0.467 mm, 1.56× the ENTIRE specified wipe budget**. A flat socket floor under a curved module back puts that difference straight into contact height: **the end contacts leave compression, or the middle ones bottom.** The sagitta exceeds the wipe at every span from 18 mm upward, so no plausible array geometry escapes it — this is not a tolerance that can be absorbed by re-laying the pads. **Two consequences that are not restatements of the decision.** **(a) The contact-array datum follows the curved surface, not merely the pocket floor** — curving the pocket while leaving the pads on a flat datum reproduces the identical error one level in. **(b) `SH2-DRC-08` must be executed on a CURVED pair**: wipe and contact force measured on flat coupons would not be testing this interface at all. That changes **how an already-specified bench test is run**, not what is tested, and it is cheap only because no tooling is cut. **It does not foreclose Option B — it supplies what Option B assumed.** §1's future path records Option B as *"semi-flex tile + **shell-socket-defined curvature**… a module swap into the **unchanged socket interface**"*; `CURV-1` states exactly the socket curvature Option B would hold constant while the tile flexes to it. **Nothing in this thread's other decisions is disturbed:** `PCB-1` and `PLATE-1` sit on L1's **gap-facing** side and `BOSS-1` on the **outer bowl**, while `CURV-1` is the **scalp-facing seating surface** — different stations, no interaction, and `PACK-1`'s 23.09 mm is unchanged (it is the module's own hex edge, already measured on the R = 87 mm surface). **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted; no revision bump — like `FLUSH-1`, `BOSS-1`, `PLATE-1` and `PCB-1`, this is a principal direction recorded in the owning section of an unrevised document.**
- **2026-09-21 — `NP-PROC-FPC-001` corrected to Rev 4, and the emitter decision it is supposed to unblock is untouched (GitHub #333; discharges `docs/status/pending-decisions.md` §13.2e(g) items 4 and 6; `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` Rev 8 → Rev 9).** §13.2e's 2026-08-16 pass corrected the electrical premise under `OI-LED-W1` and left six residual items; two of them were document corrections it declined to make, on a parenthetical reading of standing practice — *"(`.docx` — not hand-edited here, per standing practice)"*. **That practice does not exist in the form the parenthesis implied.** `np_tool_shell_001.docx` carries an in-place PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED banner dated 2026-08-18; the set's actual rule is that a `.docx` correction **retains** what it replaces, not that it is never made. Applied on that precedent. **What Rev 4 corrects.** §2.6.2's CRITICAL FINDING is **withdrawn and retained**: its load-bearing clause named *"the same **15V** VLED rail"*, and D-6 / `OI-HUB-C17b` set `VLED` = **24 V**, at which a ~3 V AlGaAs part is not categorically incompatible — at 7–8 series it lands on residuals `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.1.1 already accepts, leaving a per-tile string-count cost and a **3.00 < V_f < 3.20 V dead band**, which is a rigidizer-layout and RISK-08 question and not a driver-rail one. Five SFH 4703AS figures corrected from the ams-OSRAM *Produktdatenblatt* v1.1: **I_F max (DC) = 1000 mA, not 2000** — the 2 A row was `I_FSM` **surge** at t_p ≤ 200 µs / D = 0.005, the same error class as GH CSSRM5.24's "700 mA" optical binning current in §2.6.1 — so the margin is **5.6×, not 11×**, and because 1000 mA is a *DC* rating bounding any duty up to CW, **§13.2d's residual pulse-rating item for this part resolves PASS**; *"Vf = 3.55 V @ 1 A"* is **not a datasheet value** (typ 3.3 V / max 4.0 V at 1 A, 10 ms); **centroid 810 nm** added beside the 820 nm peak, which is the wavelength `docs/reference/competitive-position.md` already markets, matched on the manufacturer's own centroid specification; and *"(DigiKey, Mouser stocked)"* replaced with **DISCONTINUED**, recorded at the strength of three vendor status fields and **explicitly not a PCN**. §2.1 gains the statement §13.2e(g) item 6 asked for, plus the consequence the bare statement omits: **on the current-hogging axis the ±0.10 V bin degrades gracefully; on the string-construction axis it does not** — relaxing it removes the premise that a fixed N exists — so a §2.5 "use-as-is" disposition at ±0.15 V is now a string-length decision too. **Three bookkeeping defects found while applying it.** (i) The document's header read *"Revision: B"* while its own §8 history already carried a **Rev C** row dated 2026-07-28; every active-set citation said **"Rev 1"**, which is `NP-CONV-001` §4.1's `A`=1 mapping applied to the wrong letter. It was at Rev 3; this issue is 4, and `NP-DT-001` DO-HW-03, `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001`, `NP-ART-001` §5 and the document register are corrected with it. (ii) §13.2e(a) and the CRITICAL FINDING both cited **§2.3** for the V_f estimate table; it is **§2.1**, and §2.3 has no forward-voltage row. (iii) `OI-HEXTILE-02` cited the binning precedent as §4.2 and `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001` cited §4.3 and §3.1 — none of those subsections exists; §4 is the Hirose connector and §3 is the BCR421W. **One new input, at the strength it was obtained and no higher.** Re-running the Option C search without the V_f filter — residual item 3 — found a family both prior passes missed: the **Luminus SST-06-IRD-810 / SST-10-IRD-810**, vendor-described as high-power IR emitters with a *typical 810 nm centroid* in a ceramic SMD package, which is the exact part class §13.2d concluded does not exist in-window. They are **dual-junction**, which is both why their V_f is elevated and why a V_f filter drops them. **No figure is datasheet-verified**: `ams-osram.com`, `download.luminus.com` and `www.mouser.com` are all blocked by the network egress policy, so residual items 1 (the `IF = f(VF)` curve-read at 120–180 mA) and 2 (an EOL PCN) **cannot be discharged from a session at all** and are marked so, to stop the next pass re-attempting them. The lead is recorded as a lead in §2.6.3 and in `OI-HEXTILE-02`. **What did not happen, which is the point of the entry.** **No emitter was selected.** `OI-LED-W1` is a decision with three named owners — SAB on the science, Regulatory Counsel on RISK-03 scope and the published "810nm" claim, EE Lead on cost — open since 2026-07-28, and **no research or correction pass can close it**. `OI-LED-01`, `OI-LED-07`, `OI-HEXTILE-02` and RISK-03 all remain open; `NP-FAI-HEXFPC-001` stays unwritable, `OI-HUB-C08`'s term **U** stays uncosted, and GitHub #9's *"part numbers locked in BOM"* stays unticked. Two passes have now improved the facts the owners decide against; the decision is still the thing that is missing.
- **2026-09-21 — the Luminus lead is verified against the manufacturer's datasheets, and the two findings that matter are about NeurOne's own documents rather than the part (GitHub #333; `NP-PROC-FPC-001` Rev 4 → Rev 5; `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` Rev 9 → Rev 10; raises `OI-HEXTILE-22`).** **Corrects the entry above it in one respect and confirms its caution in another.** That entry recorded the Luminus SST-06/SST-10-IRD-810 family as an unverified lead because the egress policy blocked every vendor document, and said no figure in it was usable. The datasheets (`PDS-003021 Rev 02`, `PDS-003022 Rev 01`) were then supplied and read. **Two of the search-derived figures were wrong** — `V_f` max is 3.4 V not 3.2 V, and SST-10 offers 90°/130° lens options not one angle — which is exactly what recording them as a lead was for. **The part, verified.** λ_c **810 nm** typ on an **ACTIVE** part, clearing §2.3 on package, θ_jc (5.3 / 8.2 °C/W), DC current (1.5 / 1.0 A) and pulse handling with margin, and **meeting §2.1's ±0.10 V bin as a standard shipping condition** — Luminus bins in 0.2 V `V_f` increments, so the bin §2.1 calls *"tighter than commodity specification"* needs no special agreement. **The `OI-LED-01` curve-read was performed** for this family: `V_f` = **2.82 V** (SST-10) / **2.85 V** (SST-06) at 150 mA, read off each datasheet's `ΔV_f` vs `I_F` trace and self-validated because the trace returns −0.005 V at its own 350 mA reference. That is **below** `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.1.1's 3.00–3.20 V dead band — N = 8 gives 22.58 V, 1.42 V residual, 5.9 % overhead — and it is the **first candidate ever put through §8.1.1 on a datasheet value rather than a design target**. **Three requirement failures, recorded and not waived:** `T_j` max **115 °C** against §2.3's ≥ 125 °C; **neither datasheet publishes an L70 figure at all** against §2.3's ≥ 80,000 h; and the **shipped wavelength bin spans λ_p 800–830 nm**, whose lower 8 nm is below the 808 nm window, so a PO against the stock bin can ship out-of-window parts though the typical is inside it. **FINDING 1 — §2.1's bin is critically sized against §8.1, not comfortably inside it.** A ±0.10 V bin is 0.2 V wide; 0.2 V across N = 8 is **1.6 V of spread in `N · V_f`**; §8.1's entire residual window is `(24 − V_dropout) − 22.4` = **`1.6 − V_dropout`**. So a fixed N = 8 across a full bin **requires a driver dropout of zero**, and of the three bins this part ships in, only the lowest clears the 24 V functional ceiling across its width — while falling under the 22.4 V thermal floor at its low edge. **Not a Luminus property**: any supplier's ±0.10 V bin does this at N = 8. It is `OI-HEXTILE-18` and `OI-HEXTILE-19` arriving as a number instead of a warning, and it shows the two are one question with the bin width — all three spend the same 1.6 V. **FINDING 2 — `OI-HEXTILE-22`: §4.1's 3.80 mm lattice pitch was never checked against the footprint of the emitter that sits on each site.** A triangular lattice puts the 60° neighbour at **(1.90, 3.29) mm**, so an axis-aligned square package must be **≤ 3.29 mm**. Luminus is 3.45 mm square (3.65 at maximum material, needs ≥ 4.21 mm); **SFH 4718A (3.75 mm) needs ≥ 4.33 mm and SFH 4703AS (3.85 mm) needs ≥ 4.45 mm — both above §4.1's own 4.04 mm ceiling at 91 sites**, so for them it cannot be bought with the 1.2 mm boundary clearance. **Only L1IZ-0850 (1.9 × 1.37 mm) fits — and that is the 850 nm part, the one that fails the wavelength window.** The wavelength decision and the lattice-pitch decision are therefore coupled, which neither §13.2d nor §4.1 had noticed; `NP-PROC-FPC-001` §2.3's package requirement (*"SMD 2835 or equivalent"*, i.e. 2.8 × 3.5 mm) already exceeds 3.29 mm on its own 3.5 mm axis. **One more input recorded without acting on it:** the part's radiant flux at 150 mA is **~220 mW against §4.3's 95 mW design target**, so §4.3.1's *"by construction"* landing on R-4's 400 mW/cm² ceiling moves — the same irradiance arrives at ~65 mA or at fewer sites, which interacts with `OI-HEXTILE-20`. **NOTHING IS SELECTED.** A verified candidate is not a selection. `OI-LED-W1` still needs SAB, Regulatory Counsel and EE Lead; `OI-HEXTILE-02` now waits on `OI-HEXTILE-22` as well; `OI-LED-01` is discharged for this family and untouched for the three parts it names by name; `OI-LED-03` and `OI-LED-05` gain a third supplier to ask. `NP-FAI-HEXFPC-001` stays unwritable and `OI-HUB-C08`'s term **U** stays uncosted.
- **2026-09-21 — §2.3's `T_j max ≥ 125 °C` is a screening filter, not a derived limit; and the same trace found the 1064 nm sibling computing against the wrong junction temperature (GitHub #333; `NP-PROC-FPC-001` Rev 5 → 6; `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001` Rev 1 → 2, raising `OI-PBM-HW-08`).** **Follows the entry above**, which recorded the Luminus family as failing three §2.3 requirements. One of the three was traced, because a recorded failure is only useful if the requirement behind it means something. **It does not.** `125` appears **nowhere else in the document set as a temperature** and has no firmware referent, while the chain that *is* derived runs **42 °C scalp face (IEC 60601-1 applied part) → 62 °C junction throttle (50 % current, SW01-M04, `np_thermal_interlock.c`) → 65 °C junction cutoff → ~70 °C PTC → 85 °C STM32G071 die cutoff** (`CLAUDE.md` §4.2, FMEA-M04). The design never intends the junction above 65 °C and three independent controls stop it before 70, so the row's 63 °C of headroom is traceable to no thermal analysis — **125 °C is the commodity datasheet convention for high-power LEDs**, and the row reads as *"be a normal high-power LED"*, exactly like its neighbours MSL 3 / RoHS / `If_max ≥ 350 mA`. **What Rev 6 does and does not do:** §2.3's Notes cell now carries the provenance and the consequence — a sub-125 °C candidate is a **screening waiver to be recorded, not a safety finding** — and points at the row above, **θjc**, as the derived one, since `NP-THERM-BEZEL-001` §4.1 identifies the junction-to-face drop as *"the real THERM-1 requirement, and it is a face-temperature spec, not a junction spec."* **The requirement is NOT relaxed, no candidate is re-scored, and retiring or re-deriving it is a Hardware Engineering decision this pass does not take.** The practical effect is ordering: of the Luminus family's three failures, **the missing L70 figure is the one that genuinely blocks a PO** (no substitute exists) and the **wavelength bin** is the one that can ship out-of-window parts; `T_j` is the soft one. Also marked stale, while retaining it, the pre-§2.4 warning that pulse current *"has not yet been confirmed"* — `OI-LED-04` closed that on 2026-07-28 and §2.6.3 has since re-confirmed it twice. **`OI-PBM-HW-08`, found by the same trace.** `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001` §3.2/§3.7 compute against *"maximum junction temperature 42°C (IEC 60601 scalp surface limit + 20°C junction-to-case)"* — the parenthetical describes `42 + 20 = 62` and then labels the result **42**; the sentence contains its own correction. The arithmetic then uses **ΔT = 20 °C** (effective junction 45 °C), matching neither 42 nor the **62 °C** the interlock actually permits. At the correct **ΔT = 37 °C**: V_f drift becomes 55.5 mV and §3.7's *"no derating required"* **survives**; peak wavelength becomes **11.1 nm → 1075 nm**, which is **6 nm outside** the ±5 nm band, and that conclusion **fails**. **Both paragraphs were self-contradicting before any temperature correction** — *"shift < 6 nm — remains within ±5 nm band"* (6 > 5) and *"1070 nm maximum. Remains within … (1059–1069 nm range allows the shift)"* (1070 > 1069). **Stated with its likely resolution, because the correction should not be over-read:** the ±5 nm band is justified as matching the Nd:YAG literature wavelength, and the 1064 nm claim **has since moved** — `OI-HEXTILE-21` and commit `f8806dd` re-authored `clinical-03` against the **1060–1080 nm** Alzheimer's band (Grade A), which 1075 nm satisfies with margin. So the probable fix is re-deriving §3.2's band against the target the claim is now made in, not rejecting the emitter — **a decision, and `OI-PBM-HW-08` does not take it.** The DHF row for that document also read a flat *"SUPERSEDED"*, which reads as *"ignore §3"* when the 2026-07-28 supersession is scoped to module count, BOM and kit pricing; corrected to **PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED** with the scope named, because §3's component requirements are live and are what `OI-PBM-HW-07`/`-08` act on.
- **2026-09-21 — §2.3's `Tj_max ≥ 125 °C` is retired outright, and the entry above it had the disposition wrong (GitHub #333, owner direction; `NP-PROC-FPC-001` Rev 6 → 7; `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` Rev 10 → 11).** **Corrects the entry immediately above**, which traced the requirement to no derivation and then **left it in place** as a screening filter, recording that a sub-125 °C candidate would be *"a screening waiver to be recorded"*. **That disposition was wrong, and the reasoning that produced it was the interesting mistake:** having established that the figure was traceable to nothing, the pass treated the question as *how should a failure against it be scored* rather than *should it be a requirement at all*. The owner's direction settles it — **a figure nothing requires should not be a requirement, and unnecessary constraints should not be added.** **Why it is not a harmless leftover.** §1 of that document reads: *"All specifications in this document are MANDATORY unless marked ADVISORY. A purchase order that omits a mandatory specification is non-conforming."* So an undrived number in §2.3 is not inert — it rejects usable parts and manufactures false failures, and it had **already done exactly that** to the §2.6.3 candidate at `T_j` 115 °C, a part with 53 °C of headroom over the throttle and 50 °C over the hardware cutoff. **Retired, not relaxed to ADVISORY**, because ADVISORY still leaves something to screen against; the objection is to the constraint existing, not to its force. **NO SAFETY CONTROL IS LOST, and this is the part worth checking rather than assuming.** Junction temperature is bounded by **42 °C scalp face (IEC 60601-1) → 62 °C junction throttle at 50 % current → 65 °C junction cutoff → ~70 °C PTC / thermal fuse → 85 °C STM32G071 die cutoff** (`CLAUDE.md` §4.2, FMEA-M04, `np_thermal_interlock.c`) — every one of them a firmware or hardware interlock, none of them a specification a supplier can be held to. The procurement ground is covered twice over by rows that **are** derived: **θjc < 15 °C/W**, which drives the junction-to-face drop `NP-THERM-BEZEL-001` §4.1 calls *"the real THERM-1 requirement … a face-temperature spec, not a junction spec"*, and **L70 ≥ 80,000 h**, which is where junction temperature actually reaches reliability, being specified *at* a junction temperature. Reflow survivability sits separately in the Package row (peak 245 °C, 30 s). **The row is retained in place and marked RETIRED** rather than deleted, per the `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` F-01 precedent, so the retirement is auditable — it must not appear on a purchase order or in a candidate assessment. **Consequence for #333:** the Luminus SST-06/SST-10-IRD-810 family now has **two** open requirement failures, not three — the **missing L70 figure**, which has no substitute and genuinely blocks a PO, and the **wavelength bin** reaching 8 nm below the 808 nm window. **A related candidate is flagged and NOT acted on:** `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001` §3.2's **±5 nm** wavelength band is the same shape of question — tighter than the target the 1064 nm claim now rests on (1060–1080 nm) and already generating a compliance failure at 1075 nm. It differs in that it **is** derived from something (Nd:YAG literature matching), so it is a re-derivation rather than a retirement, and `OI-PBM-HW-08` owns it. **No emitter is selected and `OI-LED-W1` is untouched.**
- **2026-09-21 — the Tj_max retirement is generalised into a binding rule: a requirement must be required (`NP-CONV-001` §7.1, `CLAUDE.md` §18, Rev 49 → 50; raises `OI-CONV-08`, first pass run).** **Follows the two entries above.** Owner direction, stated as a general rule rather than a one-off: *nothing should be a requirement if it isn't actually required; never add unnecessary constraints.* §7.1 states the two-question test — **what fails if this is not met**, and **where is that traceable** — and the corollary that caught the founding case: **a Notes cell that restates the requirement is not a derivation** (*"operating headroom above the 62 °C throttle"* says what the number is *for*, not what makes it *that* number). Disposition is **retire, not downgrade**, because ADVISORY still leaves something to screen against; and **retire is not delete**. **The scope limit is written into the section deliberately**, because a rule licensing the removal of requirements is dangerous in a device programme: it reaches only constraints **NeurOne invented**, never an externally imposed limit (IEC 60601-1's 42 °C, IEC 62471 MPE, the Shannon/McCreery charge ceilings), a hazard control, or an ISO 14971 disposition — and **"I could not find the derivation" is a reason to look, then to raise an open item, never to retire.** **First-pass audit, `OI-CONV-08`.** Method: scan tables with a Rationale column, flag numeric thresholds whose rationale cites no standard, no `NP-*` document, no §ref and no derivation, hand-filter. **Three clusters**, each now carrying a task: `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001` §3.3 (six emitter rows — and two are more than unsourced: `≥ 45 mW` radiant flux is **derived to 31.5 mW then uplifted 43 %** with no basis, and `≥ 10 %` WPE may be **unmeetable**, since `OI-HEXTILE-21` puts η_wp at ~4.8 % for 1064 nm); §4.1 (four PD rows, **plus a factual error that outranks them** — `Spectral range 900–1700 nm` is justified as *"Covers 808nm CH_B"* and **900–1700 does not cover 808 nm**, which matters because PD1/PD2 are the dose-metering path and CH_B is a shipping T1 channel); and `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` (`≤2 N` dock retention, `≤6 mm` louvre slot naming *"finger-probe safety"* but not the clause — the IEC 61032 Test Probe 11 finger is 12 mm — both **tooling-blocking**). **One document checked and cleared, and worth pointing others at: `NP-HW-CVNS-001`** carries an explicit *derived?* Yes/No column per requirement with citations, which is exactly what §7.1 asks for. **The sweep is explicitly NOT complete and its gaps are recorded rather than implied**: the `.docx` set was not scanned at all — and §7.1's founding case lived in one, so that is the highest-yield gap — prose requirements were missed, and a rationale citing a document **passed the filter without confirming the document derives the number** (`NP-HW-TMS-001` REQ-TMS-02–05 against `SPEC-TMS-0n` is the named case). **The distinction `OI-CONV-08` turns on:** a requirement that is **wrong** is not one that is **unfounded**. `NP-PROC-FPC-1064-001` §3.2's ±5 nm band is derived (Nd:YAG literature matching) but tighter than the 1060–1080 nm target the 1064 nm claim moved to — **re-derivation, not retirement**, owned by `OI-PBM-HW-08`. Only the unfounded ones are retirement candidates.
- **2026-09-22 — Socket array TOPOLOGY: three alternatives recorded and NOT adopted, one degree of freedom found unallocated and TAKEN (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §5.1.6a, `ALT-SKT-A…D`).** `CURV-1` (entry above) fixed the array's *datum* and `REQ-SKT-01` fixes its *outline*; a follow-on question — could 19 contacts go in three rows instead of two, and is there an arrangement that only improves things — found that neither fixes the **topology**, and that the row-count framing was the wrong frame. **The governing identity subsumes it:** on the spherical module cap, depth below the pole is `z(ρ) = R_m − √(R_m² − ρ²)`, a function of **radius from the module axis alone**. So the array's height variation is set by its **radial spread**, not by row count, span or aspect ratio — and **any iso-radius locus has ZERO variation, exactly, at any size.** At `R_m = 87`: today's 9+10 gives **0.471 mm** over a 0–9.04 mm spread; 7+6+6 gives **0.224 mm**; a single arc at ρ = 16 gives **0.000**. **`ALT-SKT-A` (three rows, 7+6+6) is recorded and NOT adopted** — it halves the variation but spends a third of `SEAT#`'s lever arm (9.04 → 6.24 mm) on the one interlock guarding §5.1.3(a)'s *silent* dose failure, which is the wrong currency; two constraints are written down for whoever takes it — **7+6+6 never 6+7+6** (the symmetric layout is C2-symmetric, so the array stops mis-keying itself and §7.1's asymmetry would need a separate feature — and 6+7+6 is the one anyone reaches for first), and the middle row is landlocked for escape routing. Its trigger is `SH2-DRC-08` on a curved pair showing a deflection window tighter than the wipe implies. **`ALT-SKT-B` (outboard `SEAT#`) is the structural observation:** the two geometric objectives belong to **different pins** — eighteen want small radial spread, `SEAT#` alone wants a large radius — and they conflict *only* while all 19 share one outline, so splitting them improves both (0.161 mm and 1.99× the lever arm, or 0.373 mm and 1.66× in the minimal two-row version). It is **not free**: it puts a pad inside the per-tile gasket's ingress path against `SH2-DRC-11`/`FAI-IPX-05`, with radius as the dial. **`ALT-SKT-C` (one constant-radius arc) is the only topology that reaches exactly zero**, is statically balanced under §5.4a's central plunger, and turns on a supplier question nobody had asked (`OI-SHELL2-14`) — spring-pin blocks in this class are rectangular grids, and an arc is a custom pin field on the wearing half. **On the record it was raised against: the set contains NO prior consideration of a perimeter or partial-perimeter pin arrangement for this interface.** The ring-shaped concept in the record is `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` **D-1**'s 5-ring centred-hexagonal **emitter** lattice (tile face, not contacts); `NP-HFE-002` §7.3's "perimeter completeness" is a visual tile-type encoding. **★ What IS taken, because it is free: `ALT-SKT-D` — net-to-position assignment.** **Which net lands at which position is specified nowhere** — `REQ-SKT-01` gives the outline, `SH2-DRC-05a` checks span/mis-key/`SEAT#`, `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.3 orders mating by **pad-length stagger in Z** not position in XY, and the §7.2 pin table is a *numbering*. That unallocated freedom costs nothing to state and cannot make anything worse, and it matters because `z(ρ)` is worst at the ends and flat mid-array while the pins differ enormously in how much they care. **Rule of record: position by tolerance to contact-resistance drift, not by pin number** — `VLED`×3/`PGND`×3 (redundant *by rule*, §5.1.5) and the digital nets take the END positions; the singleton sense nets whose drift is a dose or µV error (`PD1_K`, `PD2_K`, `AGND`, `NTC`, `ELEC`/`ELEC_SHLD`) take the CENTRE; `SEAT#` keeps an extreme by function. Two constraints it must not break: each `VLED` keeps its **broadside `PGND` partner** (`REQ-EMI-07`/§9.3 cancellation) and the three pairs stay **contiguous** (splitting them worsens the current imbalance §5.1.5 noted 3-way paralleling does not fix). Folded into `SH2-DRC-05a`; `SH2-DRC-05b`'s pin-for-pin diff is what keeps it from decaying. **Three open items raised, and two are gaps in the EXISTING design rather than in any alternative.** **`OI-SHELL2-12`: `SH2-DRC-08` states no working DEFLECTION range** — force, wipe and cycles only — so both `CURV-1` and §5.1.6a had to compare a sagitta against the **wipe** figure because it is the only published length at this contact. **Wipe is a proxy being used past its warrant** (a sliding distance during mating, not the usable compression window), and until the window is a number no topology decision here closes on evidence; `SH2-DRC-08` is amended to report it, on a curved pair, and not to back-derive it. **`OI-SHELL2-13`: `SEAT#` is a SINGLE-POINT detector and `SH2-DRC-10b` asks it a whole-interface question** — one point sees lift at itself, and a tilt about an axis through it lifts the core while it reads home, which the §5.4a plunger permits in any direction. **That hole is in the design as it stands; no alternative created it and `ALT-SKT-B` moves the point without closing it.** Closing it costs contacts, which is the one budget §5.1.5 and RISK-22 already closed — so either the criterion narrows to the directions one point can see, or the count reopens under §5.1.5's rule. **`OI-SHELL2-14`** is the arc supplier question. **Also newly noticed and stated as unspecified: spring-beam ORIENTATION**, which decides whether a middle row has room to cantilever and is therefore prior to `ALT-SKT-A`. **`REQ-SKT-01`'s outline is unchanged; no locked section modified; no revision bump; no measurement asserted.**
- **2026-09-22 — §7.1 gains the counter-example that stops it reading as a deletion rule (`NP-CONV-001` Rev 7 → 8; `OI-CONV-08` gains a disposition guide).** **Follows the entry above**, which stated the rule from a single instance: `NP-PROC-FPC-001` §2.3's `Tj_max ≥ 125 °C`, searched, found unsourced, **retired**. One instance of *"unsourced → retired"* teaches the wrong generalisation, and the counter-case was already in the repository — `NP-EMC-CAV-001`, merged to `main` on 2026-09-22 and written **independently of this rule**, which makes it better evidence than an example constructed for the purpose. **The symptoms are identical.** `NP-BIB-EMF-001` §7.3 calls EMF **Layer 4** *"the only layer in the stack whose benefit has never been stated in any measurable form, anywhere"*, its entire documented justification being one phrase — *"cavity-resonance suppression"* — repeated across three documents. That is §7.1's *"a Notes cell that restates the requirement is not a derivation"*, arrived at from the other end of the document set. **The searches returned opposite things.** `Tj_max` had nothing upstream: no figure elsewhere in the set, no firmware referent, and a derived chain (42/62/65/~70/85 °C) made entirely of interlocks rather than supplier specifications. Layer 4's requirement was **constructible, one document away** — the source is not a radio but the **18 STM32G071 cluster controllers inside the envelope** (`NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2), so the emission that matters is **edge rate, not clock rate**; the band is **420 MHz–3 GHz**, whose lower edge is a property of the wearer; and `REQ-CAV-02` is **loaded Q ≤ 20 = 26.2 dB**. **So the outcomes invert.** The `Tj_max` requirement is retired and nothing failed, because the constraint was never real. Layer 4's requirement **stands**, and the **component** fails it by two orders of magnitude — **0.26 dB supplied against 26.2 needed, 1.0 % of its own stated job**, while the wearer's head supplies 49.8 dB (`REQ-CAV-04`: delete the station). **The lesson §7.1 now carries as a table: the disposition is decided by what the search returns, never by the symptom** — do the search first, and expect both endings. **And it converts the scope limit from a caution into a demonstration.** §7.1 already forbade retiring on a failed search (*"I could not find the derivation" is a reason to look, then to raise an open item, never to retire*). Layer 4 shows the cost of breaking it: retiring that requirement for want of a derivation would have deleted **the one instrument that later measured the layer at 1 %**, and an 18 % outward-thermal-path cost would have been retained on the strength of a phrase. **No rule changed** — the two-question test, retire-not-downgrade, retire-is-not-delete and the scope limit are all unaltered; only the worked examples and `OI-CONV-08`'s disposition guidance are added.
- **2026-09-22 — `OI-ACC-03` closed (principal): the cervical VNS gel pad is replaced on a measured failure, not enforced as single use; its price is deliberately empty; and the app now tells the wearer which pad location failed (`NP-ACC-PRIORITY-001` Rev 4; `NP-HW-CVNS-001` Rev 2; `NP-REG-CVNS-001` Rev 2; `NP-APP-ROADMAP-001` Rev 4):** the pad was a specified T2 consumable (`NP-REG-CVNS-001` §2.4: *"Gel pad 5-pack; single-use per electrode contact surface"*) that appeared in no consumables table. `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.3 now carries it. **The decision:** *"single-use"* means the pad is **guaranteed to work for one use**, not that it must be discarded after one. **As long as a failing pad is detected, single use does not need to be enforced** — so no authentication and no use count. That closes `NP-HW-CVNS-001` `OI-CVNSHW-05`, and restates `NP-REG-CVNS-001` §2.4 (Rev 2), because *"single-use"* on a medical device label reads as *"do not reuse"*. **Detection is what the decision rests on, and it exists:** every cervical session is preceded by a per-electrode impedance check against ≤ 5.0 kΩ at 1 kHz (`REQ-CVNS-06`, `NP_CVNS_IMPEDANCE_MAX_KOHM`), the safety MCU withholds enable when it fails (CLAUDE.md §4.2; the hub's reading is advisory, `OI-CVNS-HUB-09`), and a pad lifting mid-session is caught within 500 ms (`REQ-CVNS-08`). That is a **condition measurement**, the first of CLAUDE.md §2.3's two admissible kinds, with a threshold of its own safety provenance — and it is the C3a blocking mechanism `NP-ACC-PRIORITY-001` §5 row 9 already named, so the rank is confirmed, not moved. **Checked, because the decision leans on it: a worn pad that still passes cannot overdose.** The per-phase ceiling divides by the declared 2 cm²; at `REQ-CVNS-04`'s maxima (2 mA × 1000 µs = 2 µC) the commanded density is 1 µC/cm² against 40, so contact would have to shrink below 0.05 cm² before the ceiling is at stake, far past any impedance that passes 5.0 kΩ. The residual reuse hazard is **skin**, so `OI-CVNSHW-07`'s biocompatibility basis now covers repeat application. **Price and GM%: not set** — `OI-COST-10` forbids a price before `OI-HEXTILE-06`, and no COGS exists; the empty cells are the record. **One premise from the `OI-ACC-02` entry (2026-09-15) is corrected rather than inherited:** it said this item had become cheaply closable on the VNS-clip template, `modality-stack.md` *"already recording 20–40 sessions for the pads"* — that is the **auricular** clip's PDMS pads (modality ⑥), and a pad with a condition measurement needs no interval at all. **The refusal now reaches the wearer by location (app side of `OI-ACC-07`).** A refusal with no words is not a prompt (CLAUDE.md §5.2), and the principal required the message to say **which pad location** failed, ideally on a graphic. iOS and Android read a new optional characteristic, `CVNS_PAD_STATUS` (`4E455550-0013-…`, NOTIFY 4 B — failed-electrode mask, which check, and the side of the neck of each electrode's pad), and show an alert naming the side (*"a gel pad on the left side of your neck failed its contact check"*) above a neck diagram that lights it. **The side is supplied by the hub, never inferred by the app from an electrode number** — the hub knows the montage (`electrode_config`: bilateral = one pad each side, unilateral = both on one side) and its own wiring. **No helmet graphic was lit, because there is nothing to light:** the pads sit on the neck module, not in a helmet socket, and neither app draws the helmet (sockets are a text list). The diagram is of the neck, drawn as the wearer sees themself in a mirror, labelled Left/Right in words, and hidden from screen readers because the message already names the side. Classified UHDR (derived from tissue impedance; the side reveals the montage) in `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.1 — display only, never persisted, never SHDR. `CVNS_PAD_STATUS` is added to `NP-APP-ROADMAP-001` §5 (Rev 4). **`OI-ACC-07` stays open for the hub side (FW + EE):** no firmware GATT server exists yet (`OI-WA-03`) and `NP_CVNS_FAULT_IMPEDANCE` is defined but never raised; the bilateral electrode-to-side mapping waits on `OI-CVNSHW-01`; the two pads of a unilateral montage cannot be told apart until `OI-CVNSHW-06` gives the module a geometry, so a single failure there is named by side only. Mode 3 is handled in the user doc: a wearer who sees the red light without the app connects the phone and starts again from the app. The app is not yet told about a fault that happened while it was disconnected, which matters for a cardiac cutoff. The IFU text for pad replacement is proposed in `NP-REG-CVNS-001` §7.4 (reuse while accepted; replace the pad the app names; without the app, connect the phone and start again from the app; discard a torn, dirty or non-sticking pad; never share). **Changed:** `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.3; `docs/reference/modality-stack.md`; `docs/reference/data-architecture-detail.md` §5.1; `docs/np_acc_priority_001.md`; `docs/np_hw_cvns_001.md`; `docs/np_reg_cvns_001.md`; `docs/np_app_roadmap_001.md` §5; `docs/status/pending-decisions.md`; `docs/status/document-register.md`; `docs/np_dhf_001.md`; `locales/*.json` (ten `CVNS_PAD_*` keys, all eleven locales); iOS `CervicalPadStatus.swift`, `CervicalPadAlertView.swift`, `GATTCharacteristics.swift`, `NeurOneGATTManager.swift`, `SessionView.swift` + tests; Android `CervicalPadStatus.kt`, `GattUuids.kt`, `GattParser.kt`, `NeurOneGattManager.kt`, `SessionScreen.kt`, `MainActivity.kt` + tests. **No requirement, constant, limit, price or rank changed; no firmware touched; CLAUDE.md not touched.**
- **2026-09-23 — a cervical VNS cardiac cutoff survives power loss, is held for the person who triggered it, and withholds cervical VNS only; offline faults are explained when a phone connects (`NP-SW-FAULTMSG-001` Rev 2; `NP-FW-CVNS-001` Rev 6; `NP-RISK-002` Rev 4; `NP-APP-ROADMAP-001` Rev 5):**
  - **Decisions (principal, 2026-09-22).**
    - **P1**, persisting the lockout in the safety MCU, over P1b, which would have banned cervical VNS in Mode 3. P2–P4 accepted.
    - *"The CARDIAC status should only block the things it was meant to block and no more."*
    - The cutoff is set only for the user who triggered it; other users are not affected, and the same user is assumed unless the app says otherwise.
    - A blanket warning is shown so that users do not switch IDs to get round a block.
    - The principal chose the app's individual profile as the user identity, per-user records enforced in the safety MCU, and a warning at connect with a confirmation.
  - **Why it mattered.** RISK-25 lists the 30 s lockout and app confirmation as mitigations, but both were RAM state. The headset has no battery, so an unplug and re-plug in Mode 3 restarted cervical stimulation after a cardiac cutoff with no confirmation (`OI-FAULTMSG-01`).
  - **What changed.**
    - CARDIAC used to sit in the all-channel cutoff mask, so a cervical cardiac cutoff also stopped PBM, audio and every other modality. It now withholds `NP_SAFETY_EN_CVNS` only.
    - The safety MCU keeps an append-only, fail-closed record log in flash (SW01-M09).
    - The apps explain offline faults from the user's own records, gate a cervical restart on reading them, and warn every profile when *someone* on the device has an outstanding cutoff. That warning is a deliberate disclosure; it never says who.
  - **Not done.**
    - The hub does not build `CVNS_FAULT_STATUS` and has no GATT server (`OI-WA-03`).
    - Silicon, the ARM build, Swift compilation and the Android `:app` module are unverified.
    - RISK-25 is not re-scored.
  - **No threshold, latency, lockout duration, SHDR field, price or CLAUDE.md text changed.**
- **2026-09-23 — RISK-25 re-scored: S5 × P2 = ALARP, not "LOW / MITIGATED" (`NP-RISK-002` Rev 5 §4.3; `NP-FW-CVNS-001` Rev 7 §13; `NP-FMEA-001` Rev 8), at the Quality Lead's direction; ratings approved by the Quality Lead (interim: Steve Hickman, CEO) 2026-09-23:**
  - **Trigger:** the `NP-SW-FAULTMSG-001` Rev 2 design change.
  - **The recorded rating was wrong three ways:**
    - The severity was scored S4, while `NP-RM-001` §4.1 names this exact harm as its S5 example.
    - "LOW" is not a rating the `NP-RM-001` matrix can produce at S5; the best possible is ALARP.
    - The residual rested on `NP-FMEA-001` FMEA-M05-06's lockout "persisted to battery-backed RTC backup registers". The headset has no battery, so that control never existed. That is how the power-cycle gap (`NP-SW-FAULTMSG-001` F1) passed review.
  - **Post-change:** S5; initial P3 (UNACCEPTABLE); residual **P2**, because no control is yet verified on hardware; target P1 (still ALARP). A §4.4 ALARP justification is now recorded.
  - **The four new hazards:**
    - Torn-flash NMI: **ACCEPTABLE**. Every NV read runs with all enables off, so the hang fails safe; the loss is availability.
    - Update-path erase of the NV pages: **ALARP**.
    - Blocked person switching profiles: **ALARP**. The residual of the principal's per-user decision.
    - Auricular VNS left available after a cervical cutoff: **ALARP (provisional)**, a clinical question.
  - **Raised:**
    - `OI-RISK2-05`: other FMEA §3.5 M05 rows, and §2's IWDG, describe controls the safety-MCU code does not contain.
    - `OI-RISK2-06`: the auricular VNS clinical question.
  - **No requirement, threshold, code or CLAUDE.md text changed.**
- **2026-09-23 — the hub now builds and publishes the cervical offline-fault summary (`NP-SW-FAULTMSG-001` Rev 3 §9.6):**
  - **The store.** A Class B module (`np_cvns_fault_summary.c`) keeps the last 8 cervical fault stops with the user named at the time, plus the last-named user. They persist as a CRC-checked UHDR blob, so a Mode 3 fault survives the power bank being unplugged.
  - **The frame.** It builds `CVNS_FAULT_STATUS` exactly as the apps parse it, and a host test byte-compares it with the apps' fixture. The frame carries only the active user's records and the unattributed ones, and it is published on change from the heartbeat.
  - **The writes.** `ACTIVE_USER` and `CVNS_REENABLE_CONFIRM` are validated and forwarded.
  - **Wiring.** The cervical module records each fault with its real kind at session end.
  - **Attribution.** The hub's attribution follows the safety MCU's: it changes when the user is forwarded between sessions, not when the app writes.
  - **Not done:** the BLE GATT server (`OI-WA-03`) and the UHDR storage glue (`OI-LFS-05`) are the three remaining platform seams (census 94 → 97), so nothing reaches a phone on hardware yet. The worst-case duration of the save in the heartbeat task is unmeasured.
  - **No requirement, threshold or safety-MCU code changed.**
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-ACC-04` re-scoped (principal): the audio cup foam's replacement prompt measures loss of seal only, and comfort and hygiene replacement is the user's call.** Comfort and perceived hygiene are what a wearer notices directly, and the foam can be replaced voluntarily at any time, so **no prompt is owed for them — by design, not as a gap**. The prompt covers loss of function, which for an ear pad is loss of seal (bass level, passive isolation). **Target trigger: a condition measurement (CLAUDE.md §2.3 kind 1)** — the in-cup (feedback) microphone of the noise cancelling the programme intends to add for session quality; adaptive ANC already estimates the driver-to-mic path, and a leak shows as a low-frequency drop. Foam wear is read as a decline in the best seal across sessions (one poor reading prompts a reseat), including the compensation adaptive ANC spends; the score is presumptively UHDR and stays on-device. **Consequences:** the same-day first re-scope's gate on `OI-AUDIOHW-05`/`-06` is withdrawn — the threshold becomes a seal loss in dB derived from what a session needs, not a compression-set figure; the worn-tick route is moot; `150` stays as a labelled placeholder until the microphone exists, because nothing measures the seal today. **Raised `OI-AUDIOHW-08`** (`NP-HW-AUDIO-001` Rev 2): add ANC with an in-cup mic, which reverses the record's *no microphone anywhere in the design* and carries privacy, acoustic-safety (`OI-AUDIOHW-01`) and placement consequences; microphones put no current, light or field into the assembly, so `REQ-AUDIO-12` is not triggered. Recorded as a candidate, not adopted: the same mic may detect mesh fouling as a high-frequency loss (`OI-ACC-05`). **Also raised the same day, `OI-ACC-08`:** `CONSUMABLE_STATUS` has no firmware producer, no increment rule, and no path for *Mark replaced* to reach the hub. **Changed:** `docs/status/pending-decisions.md` (`OI-ACC-04`, `-05`, `-08`); `docs/reference/commercial-model.md` §2.3 foam row; `NP-HW-AUDIO-001` Rev 2 (§6.1, §8, revision history); register and DHF index rows; comments only in `ConsumableInventory.swift` and `ConsumableModels.kt`. **No constant, price, BOM, COGS, GM% or locked CLAUDE.md decision changed; no firmware touched.** No QMS/CAPA record — development mode, no hardware.
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-NASAL-01` closed by its second branch: the baselined hub probe dock is recorded as GATED on the probe's geometry, not given invented dimensions (`NP-HW-NASAL-001` Rev 2, `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` Rev 2, `NP-FAI-HUB-001` Rev 2):** `OI-NASAL-01` read *"supply the four values, or record F-01's geometry as gated."* The four values — Y-junction body OD, probe-tip envelope, PD-window location/finish, authentication-contact location — cannot be supplied honestly: each follows from a selection not yet made (`OI-NASAL-06` emitter, `OI-NASAL-03` sleeve and PD, `OI-NASAL-07` cable), and a figure written now would be the invented number `NP-FAI-001` §2 F2 and `NP-CONV-001` §7.1 forbid, with a baselined tool cut to it. So F-01 is gated: HUB-MDR-01…03, FAI-HTOOL-01 and FAI-HUB-09/-10/-20 now carry the gate, and `NP-FAI-HUB-001` §0's statement that the saddle radius was dimensioned is corrected. The values are carried forward as **`OI-NASAL-09`**. **The hub mould is not released** — `OI-HTOOL-06` stays blocked, now visibly. **`OI-HTOOL-07`** records a recommended, not executed, route to release it without waiting for the probe (probe-contact surfaces into the bonded silicone inserts; the hub allocates a pocket envelope the probe designs to). **No F-01 value, material or BOM line changed; the ≤ 2 N detent's provenance stays with `NP-CONV-001` OI-CONV-08 (c); no CLAUDE.md text changed.**
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-ART-07` closed: `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` owns every tethered cover on the hub, and `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` F-02 is retired in place (SHELL Rev 3, HUB Rev 3, `NP-FAI-HUB-001` Rev 3, `NP-ART-001` Rev 5).**
  - **Decision.** Hub port covers are specified by `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` F-02 alone. Any one of three grounds would decide it:
    - SHELL §1 lists "control hub tooling" as out of its own scope, and its §9 sends hub anchor posts to a separate document, which became HUB. So SHELL F-02 was hub geometry inside a shell spec that excludes the hub.
    - HUB's ≤ 20 mm free-length tether is the hazard control for `RISK-HUB-01` (a tethered cover drawn into the hub fan), verified by `FAI-HUB-23` [BLOCKING]. SHELL has no reach limit.
    - HUB is BASELINED and has an issued FAI checklist. SHELL is releasable for F-04 only.
  - **Retired, not deleted** (CLAUDE.md §18). SHELL §2.2, SH-06…08, FAI-CV-05/06 and the F-02 rows of §2–§4 stay verbatim under a banner (`editscripts/patch_art07_tool_shell_f02_retirement.py`). The wrong back-reference in HUB had already been corrected on 2026-08-18.
  - **Corrects the 2026-08-18 entry's "2.5×".** SHELL's 50 mm is a loop *circumference* (§2.2). A loop spans about half its circumference, so its reach is ≈ 25 mm, about 1.25× the limit. The conclusion stands: exceeding a hazard-control maximum disqualifies it at any ratio.
  - **What the retirement exposed.** SHELL named the right ports and HUB named the right tether. HUB F-02 lists USB-C and a "DFU/service" port that no other document describes (DFU runs over USB-C, `firmware/bootloader/src/np_dfu.c`). It leaves out the hub accessory port(s) that A12, A13, A14 and the mastoid pad connect to. Those covers ship as `NP-ACC-PRIORITY-001` rank 11, and `docs/reference/service-network.md` §8.3 sells them as magnetic where HUB specifies compression-fit. Deleting the duplicate outright would have left those covers with no tooling document at all.
    - Raised as `OI-HTOOL-08` (port inventory and retention method) and `OI-HTOOL-09` (HUB's 1.0 × 0.5 mm boss is borrowed from an alignment boss and has no derivation against `FAI-HUB-19`'s 10 N tether pull).
    - `FAI-HUB-14` is `[GATED: OI-HTOOL-08]`. `FAI-HUB-23` and HUB-MDR-05 now apply to every tethered cover on the hub.
  - **Not reopened.** The ≤ 20 mm tether maximum.
  - **No geometry, threshold, price or CLAUDE.md text changed.**
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-FWHUB-01` CLOSED: transcranial PBM gets a socket dispatch path, and the path ships closed at the power gate (`NP-FW-HUB-001` Rev 2, DHF Rev 68).** `NP-FW-HUB-001` Rev 1 found that no transcranial PBM protocol could run: the PBM driver sat only in the five retired zone slots the parser rejects, and socket-addressed commands were dropped by `dispatch_command()`.
  - **Built:** `src/np_socket_dispatch.c`, the socket-indexed registry `OI-HUB-SOCKET-01` asked for, beside the slot registry with no fallback between them. A socket drive command drives every named socket or none — mod-type, params, placement against the live module inventory, power, and driver faults with rollback. It owns the one `NP_SAFETY_EN_PBM_CRANIAL` bit, releasing it only when no socket is active, or at once, with everything stopped, when a stop fails.
  - **Decided — the power governor ships refusing every load.** `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §9.3 requires a concurrent-power governor in the session runner; while no socket command could reach an emitter that was latent. `scripts/check-pbm-power.ts` reads 20 of 23 predefined transcranial protocols over the 40 W emitter budget, and the governor cannot be written yet (`OI-HEXTILE-09`, blocked on `OI-SESPWR-03`; emitters unselected, `OI-HEXTILE-02`). A plausible approximation would encode design targets as limits. So `np_pbm_power_admit()` refuses everything, and the test suite asserts that the definition which ships does. **Transcranial PBM therefore still does not run; the reason is one function body (`OI-FWHUB-09`, blocking) rather than a missing registry.**
  - **Raised, not absorbed:** `OI-FWHUB-10` (no socket-path telemetry or dose metering), `OI-FWHUB-11` (`HUB-REQ-C05`'s per-cluster gate not commanded), `OI-FWHUB-12` (the PBM I²C stub's five-slot bound).
  - **No Class C code, enable-word layout, wire format or protocol version changed.**
- **2026-09-23 — Multi-modality at one hex-tile socket: the principal accepted all six recommendations of `NP-FW-MMSOCK-001` (P-1…P-6), and P-5 is built (`NP-FW-MMSOCK-001` Rev 2, `NP-FW-HUB-001` Rev 3, DHF Rev 71).**
