  - **P-1 — T1 tES stays on conventional pads outside the lattice.** No T1-B pod (≤ 1.02 cm²) can carry any of the 14 shipped tDCS protocols under DI-SAFE-01 (8.0–24.0 cm² needed). The T1-B electrode is recording-first and stimulates only for tACS where a pod fits the per-phase ceiling (≳ 8–10 Hz at 1 mA) — enforced by the Class C ceiling against the pod's area, with no separate frequency rule. `OI-MMSOCK-01` closed. **No T1 tES pad hardware is specified anywhere** — `OI-MMSOCK-11`.
  - **P-2 — tES socket target, after P-1**, now scoped to tACS on the T1-B dual-rated pad; not built (`OI-MMSOCK-10`).
  - **P-3 / P-4 — PBM may share a T1-B with EEG, and with tES** once tES is socket-addressable.
  - **P-5 — Class C change C-1 taken now, pending SW-01 review.** `NP_SESSION_STATUS_GEOM_REQ_BES` (bit 4) and a third fail-closed geometry-gate arm for BES/tACS; the area is the fixed pad constant (`OI-CHARGE-07`'s rule), so **no wire-format change**. No enable-word bit or frame byte moved.
  - **Found and fixed while building P-5:** the runner sent electrode areas only in sessions containing tDCS or HD-tDCS, so VNS, cervical-VNS and BES areas were dropped elsewhere and the MCU enforced 25 cm² — **50× looser than designed on the auricular clip**. Every computed area is now sent (`REQ-FWHUB-37`); the scan is host-tested (`np_chan_decl_tests`).
  - **P-6 — two electrodes per T1-B, one recording-only, one dual-rated.** Propagation to `NP-HEX-ZM-001`, `NP-HW-EEGNET-001`, `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §7.2 and `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` (socket 19 → 20 contacts) is `OI-MMSOCK-12`, before socket tooling.
- **2026-09-23 — `REQ-CAV-04` TAKEN (principal, GitHub #391): EMF Layer 4, the carbon-loaded absorber foam, is DELETED, and the 3 mm re-loft of the outer bowl moves with it as one change. The EMF stack is now 4-layer passive + active (CLAUDE.md Rev 51 §1, §4.3; `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 14 §8.2).** This takes the recommendation the four 2026-09-20 `OI-BIBEMF-08` / `NP-EMC-CAV-001` entries above converged on. **Why:** `REQ-CAV-02` (loaded Q ≤ 20 over 420 MHz – 3 GHz, ≥ 26.2 dB) is real and still binds, but the layer supplied **0.26 dB** of it while the wearer's head supplies **49.8 dB**, and it cost 18 % of the outward thermal path. **The re-loft is binding** because vacating the station without it fills 3 mm with stagnant air (0.115 vs the foam's 0.075 m²K/W): outward path 0.410 → 0.450 without it, **0.335** with it. The radial stack goes **30–35 → 27–32 mm**, the Gap and L1 do not move, and the cluster clamp's counterface becomes rigid Pd/mu-metal/CFRP with its tolerance stack falling ±1.30 → ±0.80. **Rules that bind from here:** Layer 4's number is retired and held (Layer 5 is not renumbered); *"five-layer keying"* is the retired RISK-15 scheme and was not touched; the only documented way anything returns to the station is `OI-EMCCAV-08` (`MECH-2`) — a thin electrically insulating, non-magnetic ceramic-filled pad (`REQ-CAV-03`) sized by the ±0.80 stack. **Closed by it:** `NP-THERM-COOL-001` `OI-THCOOL-04` (nothing left to specify) and `NP-THERM-SINK-001` `OI-SINK-07` (no datasheet to read); `OI-THCOOL-15`'s counterface sub-question is answered (the rigid bowl). **Raised:** `OI-THCOOL-21` — re-baseline the thermal network at 0.335 (the scripts still carry the foam; conservative on scalp safety, understated on capability). **Still open and not gating:** `OI-EMCCAV-01`, `-02`, `-03`, `-04`, `-06`, `-07` (now live: more heat lands in the outer bowl beside the mu-metal and Helmholtz coils), `-08`, `-11`, `-12`, `-14`. **The shell re-loft rides `OI-ART-01`'s `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` re-scope (GitHub #331).** No measurement asserted: `EMF-1` has still never run, and the 35–45 dB ELF / 40–60 dB RF figures (unchanged — Layer 4 contributed to neither) remain design targets.
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-TACSDRV-06` DECIDED by the principal: the T1 → T2 upgrade path is a new unit, and the modules a purchaser bought carry over to it (`NP-REG-UPG-001` Rev 2 §7.0; `NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` Rev 4; DHF Rev 73; CLAUDE.md Rev 52).** The principal took all five points of `NP-REG-UPG-001` §7.1 as written. **No T1 unit is converted by the depot, a partner or the user, and none is upgraded piecemeal.**
  - **Reasons, as given:** (1) purchasers reuse any modules they bought; (2) the regulatory issues of conversion and user installation are avoided; (3) a rolling upgrade cannot get round the T1 → T2 price differential. The analysis had weighed neither (1) nor (3). Rev 1 §6.2 had defaulted to *"T2 ships complete with T2-released parts"*, and carry-over is now an objective the design must deliver.
  - **`REQ-UPG-01`**: a T1-identity unit never enables a T2 modality (cervical VNS, clinical tACS / HD-tDCS, 1170 nm, TMS) or unlocks a T2 software feature, whatever is attached. It is refused in the partition that owns the enables, fails closed, and is presented as F4. Reason (3) is what makes it a requirement: T2-D, cervical VNS and possibly the qEEG cap fit interfaces every T1 unit has. **`REQ-UPG-02`**: the tier identity is written once, at manufacture, and no field path writes it. **`REQ-UPG-03`**: every T1 module interface is kept on every T2, and interface changes land on both tiers or neither. The first to be bound is `OI-MMSOCK-12`'s 19 → 20-contact socket. **None of the gates exists yet** (`OI-UPG-01`, sharing D-9's mechanism and `RISK-PWRSRC-10`).
  - **Settled by the decision:** T1 carries no populated T2 content, meaning one ADS1299, no A16 content and no compliance supply. T1 bowls omit the TMS window and every T2 bowl carries it, pending `OI-UPG-05`'s layup-vs-mould confirmation. No service tier changes a unit's tier. Pro Entry → Pro Full stays inside T2. `OI-TACSDRV-01`'s dependency is discharged. `OI-UPG-03` is closed as moot.
  - **Opened or re-scoped by carry-over:** carried modules become 510(k) components, so until counsel answers Q2 (now *on what conditions*, with the A13 clip named because it feeds A14's cardiac interlock) they are **tier-common part numbers built under the QMS from first production** (`OI-UPG-04`). Product owes a T2 offering for buyers who already own modules, and no price may be set before `OI-HEXTILE-06`. A module UID seen on two devices links their SHDR records (`OI-UPG-07`), so none enters an SHDR upload until that is decided. *"Your modules carry over to Pro"* is not published until counsel answers Q1 and Q2.
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-EMCCAV-03` closed (GitHub #400): siting the 24 V vault boost is an EMC decision, and the condition now lives on `OI-HUB-C19`'s own row (`NP-HW-HUB-001` Rev 7 §7.4, §11; `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 15 §3.1, §10).** No decision changed: the boost stays **on the Hub PCB, provisional (principal 2026-07-30), outside the helmet envelope**. What is recorded is the gate on a revisit — **siting it inside the envelope (the PAN, the one named alternative) is not accepted until `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §4 (band) and §6 (the head meets `REQ-CAV-02`) are re-derived with the boost as a source.** Why it must gate rather than merely inform: `REQ-CAV-02` is an allocation against the in-envelope sources of §3 (the cluster controllers' edges); the head's 23.6 dB margin under it leaves **3.6 dB** against a source ×10 stronger in field — the reading of §3's *"an order of magnitude"* — which is inside the first-order model's own uncertainty; and with Layer 4 deleted (`REQ-CAV-04`) the head is the whole of the damping, so a stronger internal source must be answered at the source. **Not a new requirement** — it is `REQ-CAV-00`'s existing *"fastest edge inside the envelope"* re-derive trigger, written onto the row that owns the decision. **Not computed, and why:** how far above the controller edges a boost would actually sit needs its switching frequency, switch-node edge time and hot-loop area (none in the record; they arrive with `OI-HUB-C19`'s part selection) and an amplitude for the controller edges (§3 gives only rise times). `NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` Q3's compliance-supply switching stage already follows `OI-HUB-C19`'s Hub-PCB rule.
- **2026-09-23 — The locale set is made ready for translation: the last printf conversions leave canonical, and a translation ledger holds #191's two rules (closes `OI-I18N-02`, raises `OI-I18N-03`; GitHub Issue #191).** #191 asks for every locale to be translated and verified by a native speaker, as late as possible before shipping, and for later string additions never to modify an existing translation. **No string was translated in this change, on purpose.** Nobody here can give the native-speaker verification the issue requires. A machine translation committed now would also look verified: once real translation began, the no-modification rule would protect it. That is worst for the 61 keys `_metadata.json` marks `legal_review_required` (consent, age-gate and BIPA copy). What this change did instead is the two prerequisites. **(1) `OI-I18N-02`.** The fourteen keys that still carried `%d`, `%.1f` or `%1$d` are canonical `{0}`/`{1}` in all eleven locales. Every locale still held the untranslated English, and the conversion script refused any value that was not, so no translation could be touched. Each call site formats its own numbers: nine iOS sites wrap integers in `String(…)`, `AdaptiveAdjustmentsCard.offsetLabel` pads with zeros itself, and Android's `HistoryScreen` passes `"%.1f".format(it)`. `checkInterpolationSyntax` now rejects every printf conversion in canonical, not only the Apple `@` form. The self-test proves both directions, and putting `%d` back made the gate exit 1. **(2) The ledger.** `locales/_translations.json` records each verified (locale, key) as two hashes: the English it was translated from, and the value that was verified. Each (locale, key) is untranslated, translated, stale or tampered (localization.md §17.7). The ledger code lives in `check-locale-strings.ts`, so its self-test stays hermetic, which `check-gate-coverage.ts` rejected the first layout for. The gate now fails on a locale value that differs from the English with no entry, or that has changed since it was verified. A stale entry is not a failure, because an English edit must never wait on a translator. Eight self-test cases cover every state. `scripts/translations.ts` is the only writer. `export` issues only untranslated and stale keys. `import` requires `--verified-by`, and refuses a changed source, a placeholder mismatch, legal copy without `--legal-reviewed`, and any overwrite of a current translation without `--replace`. `fill` copies an English edit into untranslated locales. The modality names are marked `"translate": false` in `_metadata.json` (§17.3). Export, import, stale detection, a hand edit, and an unverified paste were each tried on a scratch copy of `locales/`. No ledger file is committed yet; the first `import` creates it. **Found while building it: `OI-I18N-03`.** Russian and Arabic need plural categories (`few`, `many`, `two`) that the equal-key-set parity rule and web's three-way `tPlural` cannot hold. They fall back to `other` without crashing but are grammatically wrong. Those two locales are blocked until it is fixed; the other eight are not. **Not verified by compiler:** no Swift toolchain or Android SDK here. `tsc -p tsconfig.scripts.json`, `check-locale-strings` (live and 47-case self-test), `check-gate-coverage`, `check-ci-scope`, `check-section-refs` and `sync-locales --check` pass.
- **2026-09-23 — Locales carry their own CLDR plural categories, so Russian and Arabic can be translated (closes `OI-I18N-03`; GitHub #191; CLAUDE.md Rev 53).** The ledger change earlier the same day found that Russian (`one / few / many / other`) and Arabic (`zero / one / two / few / many / other`) could not be translated correctly. The parity rule required every locale to carry exactly en.json's key set, so `ru.json` could not hold a `_FEW`. Web's `tPlural` also chose only `_ZERO` / `_ONE` / `_OTHER`. A Russian translator would have had to pick one form for 2, 5 and 21 alike. **The rule now:** a locale carries every en.json key, and may add only its own CLDR categories on a family en.json defines. Anything else extra still fails. An extra category is translated from the family's `_OTHER` (its ledger source hash is taken of that text) and has no English form, so it can exist only as a verified translation. The gate fails an extra category with no ledger entry, which keeps #191's rule that nothing unverified lands. **Per surface:** `check-locale-strings.ts` gains `localeParityErrors`, `sourcesFor`, `localePluralCategories`, `isUnusedPluralMember` and `pluralExamples`, with 18 new self-test cases (68 total). It also gains `placeholderMismatch`, which lets a plural member leave out the count `{0}` only when its category matches exactly one count. A generator run on a scratch copy exposed the need: the Arabic dual "سطران" ("two lines") carries no number, while Russian `one` (1, 21, 31 …) must keep it. `sync-locales.ts` is unchanged; both its generators already emitted every category a locale carries, which the Arabic Android `<plurals>` confirmed. Web `tPlural` delegates to a pure `pluralKey()`: an explicit `_ZERO` for 0, else the `Intl.PluralRules` category, else `_OTHER`. Six new Vitest cases cover it, and the web parity test was rewritten to the new rule. `scripts/translations.ts` issues the missing categories with `pluralCategory` and example counts (Russian `other` gets fractional examples, since no whole number selects it). It skips English members the language never selects, keeps allowed extras through `fill`, and reports per-locale key counts in `status` (ar 1531, ru 1517, zh-Hans 1496). Exercised on a scratch copy: a Russian `_ONE / _FEW / _MANY / _OTHER` import, a clean gate, extras kept by `fill`, and an unverified `_FEW` paste rejected. **CLAUDE.md §17's table said "all 11 locales carry the same key set"; it now states the plural exception (Rev 53).** **Visible consequence:** until they are translated, Chinese and Indonesian show English `_OTHER` text for a count of 1 on web ("1 lines"). That is correct CLDR selection over untranslated text, and Android and Apple already did it. The simulator bundle was regenerated because its fingerprint covers `i18n.ts`. Web `tsc`, 327 Vitest tests, the Vite build, `check-locale-strings` (live and self-test), the scripts type-check, `sync-locales --check`, `build-simulator-runtime --check`, `check-gate-coverage`, `check-ci-scope` and `check-section-refs` all pass.
- **2026-09-23 — `OI-EMCCAV-11` (GitHub #404): the fluxgate body is on L1 and never in the inter-bowl Gap. This CORRECTS the stated reason of the 2026-08-31 gap-pad entry above (line 221, *"§5.3c's fluxgates sit there"*), and it withdraws the keep-out reconciliation that the 2026-09-20 entry proposed, because the record specifies no keep-out (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` Rev 4 §5.3(c); `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 17 §8.6.1).** The 2026-08-31 entry is not edited; this entry corrects it. **(1) Settled by the record: body location.** The fluxgate magnetometer body is on the inner bowl (L1), inside `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's "Inner-bowl socket wall + FPC channel" station. Every document that sites it agrees: `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.1, §5.3(c), §5.3.1 and §5.7; `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2, §3.2 and §4; `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §4.3 and `REQ-EMI-10`; `NP-HW-EEGNET-001` §5.4. *"Near the scalp"* in §5.3(c) is comparative (inner bowl rather than outer), not a scalp-face siting. The 2026-08-31 entry and `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §6.9.1 both came from one analysis, and both cited §5.3c for a claim §5.3c never made. **The siting is now stated once, in `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3(c)**; `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` (Rev 2) and `NP-THERM-COOL-001` (Rev 15) cite it instead of restating it. **(2) The Gap's contributor list is unchanged.** It already excluded the fluxgates, and they stay off it under any keep-out reading, because a keep-out is planform and only a radial obstruction sets the Gap. **(3) The 2026-09-20 entry's "likely reconciliation" is withdrawn as unsupported.** It assumed a magnetic keep-out that reaches into the gap, and **no fluxgate keep-out is specified anywhere**: no radius, volume or planform, and no part, package, count or site pattern to derive one from. Every fluxgate protection in force is a **material** rule (`REQ-EMI-10`; `REQ-NET-04`; §6.9.1's insulating, non-magnetic pad). **So the 2026-08-31 entry's no-continuous-pad conclusion now rests on bowl separation and on the 18 cluster controller boards (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.7.3, `OI-EMCCAV-12`), and not on the fluxgates.** Its cluster-clamp reason had already fallen to `FLUSH-1`. **(4) NOT decided, and left to EE: is a planform keep-out needed?** Reading A says the material rules suffice, so gap-pad coverage (#405) has no fluxgate term. Reading B says a keep-out of radius *r_ko* is needed, and its coverage term *N_sites × π r_ko²* cannot be computed from the record. The only candidate justification for B is **thermal**: pad heat shifting a sensor's offset, per `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2's temperature-drift finding. It is recorded as a hypothesis, not a finding. **(5) A new open condition:** no fluxgate part is selected, so the fit of a 3-axis sensor in the 2.0–2.5 mm station is unverified. A gap-ward protrusion would make the fluxgates a **local** radial Gap contributor at their sites. Both (4) and (5) stay open under `OI-EMCCAV-11` (EE + ME). No dimension changed, no locked section modified, no measurement asserted.

- **2026-09-25 — `RISK-SHELL-03` polarity RESOLVED: `NP-RISK-004` §2.2 adopted by the principal (GitHub #437; DHF Rev 100). `OI-RISK4-01` and `NP-CONV-001` `OI-CONV-01` closed. The score is NOT re-assessed (`OI-RISK4-06`).** **What was decided:** the `SAFE_EN[n]` "polarity inversion" that `OI-CONV-01` raised on 2026-08-11 was never two conventions for one line. It is **two nets joined by an inverting buffer**, and the only statement of that buffer in the tree was a Class C source comment (`np_gpio_mgr.c:5-7`, *"Asserting a GPIO LOW enables the downstream stimulation driver via an inverting buffer"*). The safety-MCU pin keeps `PBM_CRANIAL_EN#` (active-low, open-drain, pull-up), and **no firmware identifier changes**. The hub-PCB buffer output is the new active-high **`PBM_CRANIAL_PERMIT`**. `SAFE_EN[n]` stays active-high and is **not** renamed to `SAFE_EN#[n]`. Each cluster's 24 V load switch conducts only on `PBM_CRANIAL_PERMIT` AND `SAFE_EN[n]`, so SH2-DRC-13's "defaults LOW" is the safe state. **Made binding as `NP-HW-HUB-001` HUB-REQ-C06** (§7.2.5, realised under OI-HUB-C22): (a) the buffer is specified in hardware; (b) the pull-up and the buffer share one rail, because otherwise losing the pull-up rail alone reaches *enabled*; (c) every active-high enable receiver has a pull-down, and the buffer does not back-power; (d) the AND. **Naming rule added to `NP-CONV-001` §1.1: across an inverter the two sides take different stems, never the same stem with and without `#`.** This line had already lost its `#` once, in `NP-FW-HUB-001`'s References (fixed there at Rev 10). **Found and routed, not fixed:** `NP-FMEA-001` OI-FMEA-01's pass criterion ("all stimulation GPIO LOW") passed on the *enabled* pin level. It is restated at Rev 11, and the same wording in §3.2, FMEA-M01-01 and FMEA-M02-05 is left for Quality. `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` SH2-DRC-13 named the Safety MCU as `SAFE_EN[n]`'s commander, contrary to HUB-REQ-C05. It is restated at Rev 5 and unblocked in `NP-REV-SHELL-001` Rev 2. `NP-SW-CI-001` OI-SWCI-27 now covers the pull-up's supply domain (Rev 23). **Still open, deliberately:** the re-score of RISK-SHELL-03 is a hazard-analysis act, so it is `OI-RISK4-06`, and **CRITICAL stands until it closes**. That item must first decide whether an **enable-pin read-back** is a required control. None exists today: `np_hal_pin_read()`'s only caller is the SPI NSS watch, so a pull-up open or pin shorted LOW, with the buffer powered, reaches *enabled* undetected. That is the pin-level half of `NP-HW-HUB-001` OI-HUB-C21. `np_safety_config.h:8`'s "reset *drives* HIGH" wording (really "the pull-up *holds* HIGH") is Class C source and was not edited.
- **2026-09-25 — `NP-FMEA-001` Rev 11: every enable-line level restated at the safety-MCU pin (GitHub #437). This follows up the `RISK-SHELL-03` entry above, which left §3.2, FMEA-M01-01 and FMEA-M02-05 for Quality; the principal directed the fix.** The FMEA had written the cutoff as "drive all stimulation GPIO LOW" throughout. At the pin, which is active-LOW open-drain per `np_safety_config.h:7-8`, LOW is the *enabled* level. Ten statements across §3.1, §3.2, §3.5, §3.6 and §3.7 are restated in place, and a new §3 note fixes the convention: **levels are stated at the pin, where HIGH = disabled**. The original wordings are listed in the Rev 11 row. The worst was §3.5's *"asserts the cervical VNS disable GPIO LOW (active-low enable)"*, which as written drives the cardiac interlock's enable to its *enabled* level. **No failure mode, score or mitigation changed.** **Found and raised as `OI-FMEA-11`, not fixed:** three mitigations cite code that does not exist. FMEA-M01-01 cites an ISR cutoff bypassing the bitmask; the real path is `granted_mask` → `np_gpio_mgr_apply()`. FMEA-M01-05 cites `np_gpio_enable_init()` and a boot read-back self-test; neither exists. FMEA-M02-05 cites `np_gpio_enable_cutoff_all()`; the real path is `np_hal_gpio_init()`'s `BSRR` preset. Their residual scores are to be re-derived by Quality against the code as built, and are not to be relied on until then. M01-05's missing read-back is the same gap as `NP-RISK-004` OI-RISK4-06.
- **2026-09-25 — `NP-FMEA-001` OI-FMEA-11 CLOSED at the principal's direction as interim Quality authority (GitHub #437): FMEA-M01-01, -M01-05 and -M02-05 re-authored against the code, and their residuals CHANGED.** The three rows had scored their residuals on controls the safety MCU does not contain: an `IDLE→ENABLED→FAULT` state machine, 100% branch coverage of the GPIO module, an ISR cutoff bypassing the bitmask, `np_gpio_enable_init()`, `np_gpio_enable_cutoff_all()`, a boot read-back self-test and a hardware IWDG. **Decided residuals:** M01-01 S2×P1 → **S4×P1 ACCEPTABLE** (overwrite-not-accumulate `granted_mask`, per-interlock in-iteration clears, and an un-gated `np_gpio_mgr_apply()` of all ten pins every iteration). M01-05 S2×P1 → **S5×P1 ALARP** (BSRR preset before MODER, init writes all disabled, no grant before heartbeat and signature; no read-back, and the pre-instruction window rests on the pull-up alone). M02-05 S2×P1 → **S5×P1 ALARP** (`check()` and `apply()` run every iteration, and the cutoff lands within one iteration; a **hung main loop is backstopped by nothing** because no IWDG is configured). **Method:** a residual keeps its harm severity unless a control limits the harm, following the Rev 8 precedent for FMEA-M05-06, and scores are read from the NP-RM-001 §4.3 matrix (S5×P1 = ALARP). **Both ALARP justifications are recorded as incomplete**, because each has a feasible further reduction not yet decided: an enable-pin read-back (`NP-RISK-004` OI-RISK4-06) and an IWDG (`NP-RISK-002` OI-RISK2-05, now carrying the dependency at Rev 6). **Verification built, no firmware behaviour changed:** `np_gpio_mgr_tests`, the eleventh Class C host target, is the first host coverage of `np_gpio_mgr.c` and `np_spi_watchdog_check()`. It has 18 assertions, and five injected mutations (a transposed bit, an inverted pin, a skipped init write, `>=`→`>` on the timeout, an OR-accumulating grant) are each caught. CI safety count 10 → 11, total 48 → 49. **Not re-derived, and routed to OI-RISK2-05:** §3.2's "hardware TIM" watchdog claim (the code uses a SysTick counter) and FMEA-M02-02/-04's IWDG. §4's module summary now shows SW01-M01 and SW01-M02 as ALARP.
- **2026-09-25 — `NP-RISK-004` OI-RISK4-06 DECIDED (principal): a cranial enable read-back is a required control, with an independent actuator. RISK-SHELL-03 re-scored CRITICAL → HIGH (GitHub #437).** **Decision:** option "cranial net + PAN cut", chosen over read-back on all ten enable nets (+10 I/O, which forces a larger safety-MCU package under OI-HUB-C20), detection with no actuator, and no read-back. Recorded as `NP-HW-HUB-001` **HUB-REQ-C07** (§7.2.6). (a) A safety-MCU input senses **`PBM_CRANIAL_PERMIT`** at the buffer output, **not the MCU pin**: an open pull-up leaves the pin floating, and a pin-level read-back sees nothing past the pin. (b) A **separate output, `VAULT_FEED_EN`** (active-high, pull-down, fail-off), drives a series switch on the 24 V vault feed, independent of the permit path. This realises `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §6's whole-vault Class C "PAN feed cut", which until now **no signal, GPIO or firmware implemented**. (c) At boot the feed stays off until the permit reads disabled. (d) Each iteration, a permit reading HIGH while commanded disabled, on two consecutive reads, cuts the feed, latches a fault and withholds grants until a power cycle. I/O ~23 → ~25. **Re-score (NP-RISK-004 §2.3):** CRITICAL was assigned to a *systematic* cause (two documents specifying opposite polarities, so every unit energises at power-on), and correcting the specification removes it. The remaining random single fault now needs a second, independent fault, but the control is specified, not built or verified, so the score is **HIGH**, the same class as RISK-26. **Target MEDIUM** on OI-HUB-C22 (schematic), OI-HUB-C23 (firmware and host tests, new) and bench fault injection. **Not covered:** a hung safety-MCU main loop (`NP-RISK-002` OI-RISK2-05, no IWDG), and the other nine enable lines, whose FMEA-M01-05 ALARP justification must now be written as it stands. **No firmware changed in this step.**
